## Chapter 1: run of the mill

### Summary

It starts with a visit.

But, boy, oh, boy, it does not end there.

### Text

It starts with a visit.

---

The aftermath of the battle was—is—brutal.

The hotel is in shambles. Razzle and Dazzle are no longer ands. Sir Pentious is gone, deceased, well and truly and *permanently*. There are members of the cannibal town lying strewn on the ground, dead. Lute had looked—*devastated*—at Adam’s death, and that—that doesn’t feel *good*, even if she knew what the cost of a victory would be. And Alastor—Alastor’s gone, missing in action, impossible to reach or contact and she’s besides herself in worry, just another slew of blood in the already full bucket of hers that is starting to become too much, too much, too much.

It’s only Husk and Niffty’s even tempers and lack of concern that keep her from tearing the hotel apart, tearing apart the rubble, in a mad search for a radio demon that had called up favor after favor to help her, help them, save them—who put himself on the line for them, and she had—*they* had—already lost Sir Pentious, she can’t lose—she *can’t*—

“Charlie?”

“Dad,” she gasps, and the tears flowing down her cheeks are hot, burning even higher with the heat of embarrassment at being caught when she had been trying to not be seen, trying not to be found like this, but—

“Charle’, why are you crying?” His voice is so soft, caring, protective, his steps quick as he crosses the room in hurried strides to pull her onto the bed and wrap her up in his arms where she knows no harm will, can, would dare, come to her, and it’s warm and comforting and *safe*. And—it has her thinking of a violent shield, of the slew of corpses caused by its protection alone, of the mass of power that had enveloped her, enveloped them, protected her, protected them, and then not a *word* afterward, not a *crackle*, a hiss, a shock, a *shadow*—

Alastor and Lucifer don’t get along.

Nothing she can say will change that.

No level of concern will alter whatever animosity is sitting between them.

She can already imagine what her father must think. Alastor, going down at Adam’s hand, leaving the hotel defenseless, leaving *her* defenseless. He must be livid, his opinion dropped even lower than it already had been, and she doesn’t know how to explain, how to vocalize, how to elaborate that it was all her fault because none of them had thought—none of them had spared a second to think—that he might, that he could maybe, possibly, potentially need backup.

She hadn’t been able to do a thing against Adam, had almost lost until her father had saved her—*her*, Hell’s princess, daughter of an angel, had almost fallen to a shit-stain like *Adam*—

And they had left him to Alastor?

Alastor, who was as mortal as all of them when faced with an angel?

*Alone?*

What if he never comes back? What if this—what if this was his last straw? What if the connections, their family, this found family he had boasted so proudly in front of her father wasn’t worth the cost of his life?

If he wasn’t dead, was he just done with them?

She thinks, chest rattling, heart hurtling around gruesome, heavy sobs, grasping at the back of her father’s coat and letting herself feel as small and unsure as she’s felt since she returned from heaven, that she is an awful, awful, terrible, horrible person for thinking that she’d prefer him to be dead than to have ditched them.

*“Charlie…”*

“He’s still gone, dad,” she finally breathes, this whisper of a breath amidst hacking cries that have her throat aching and nose running, and she knows she’s gotten snot all over his shoulder for sure but she also can’t be bothered to care about that right now considering—“He’s gone, and I—I didn’t do anything. I didn’t help him. All my—all my power and I didn’t, I couldn’t—he was alone, and he’s just—”

“This is about *Alastor*?”

Her father is a great man.

Great at changing his form? Absolutely.

Great at disguising his tone, though? Not at fucking all.

She draws away, scraping the cuffs of her sleeves over her face roughly until it bursts in splotchy, uneven red marks, snorting something ugly and gross back up her nose as she wipes at stray strings of mucus that drip down her lips. “I know,” she rattles, arms wrapping around herself uselessly, a poor mimicry for the embrace she just extracted herself from, “I know you don’t like him, but he—he gave me the information I needed. He—he called in friends, favors, got another overlord to help. He put himself in the line of fire for us, for *me*—I—”

Her father sighs, this tired, exhausted thing, like he’s drawing her burden of emotion upon himself, scooting closer to where she had drawn away to with a small shift, turning his body to face her.

“Look—this—*Al*—he’s a tough dude or something, right? I’m sure he’s fine. Probably slinked away to lick his wounds or something. Isn’t that what overlords do?”

“Well, yes, but—*dad*—” She feels restless, angry, furious—at herself, at Heaven, at the angels, at Alastor who, to her knowledge, hasn’t even done anything wrong yet and yet her ire for him burns still—“He’s an *overlord*. And if he’s—if he’s hurt—if he got hurt badly enough to have to flee, if he,” she huffs, frustrated, raking her fingers through her hair, avoiding the concerned gaze of her father because she doesn’t—she doesn’t need anyone being concerned about *her*. She’s *fine*. She’s *alive*. She had people fighting by her side the *whole time*. She didn’t have the hardest job. “We’re royalty, dad. I’m your *daughter*. And yet, I—he had to fight my battle for me. I put him in that line of fire, and now—it’s not *just* his life, dad. It’s his reputation, his connections, his desire to continue to help us—I asked him to take on those risks. *I* put him there and asked him to pay the price. *Alone*. And now—“

“Charlie.”

Her laugh is breathless, manic, hands clawing at her throat, lungs shrieking around a breath that won’t fucking reach—“If something happens to him, if he loses something, *anything*, because of this—that’s—that’s on me. Dad, that’s on me. I—and he’s gone and I can’t even fucking apologize, dad—I don’t—I can’t—*dad*—“

“Charlie—*Charlie!* Hey, breathe—Charlie, breathe—*breathe!*”

---

Alastor returns close to the end of the remodeling of the hotel, a bright smile on his face, like nothing had happened, like no time had passed, like he had just gone on a little stroll after the battle and had only just returned from smelling the proverbial roses, and the relief is so strangling that she doesn’t think twice as she whirls and swings her arms around him and squeezes with all her might, a relieved sob she’s helpless to stop from escaping shuddering past her lips.

“My, my, my, look at all this wonderful work you all did—yes, indeed! Wonderful work, indeed!” Crimson eyes swing to meet Lucifer’s displeased frown, and Charlie draws away haltingly, anxious, and yet Alastor’s smile doesn’t even twitch, gaze sliding away cooly. “Yes, quite His Highness’ style, don’t you agree?” The gaze dips down to meet hers and—“It suits you well, Charlie.”—it’s soft.

The tears come unbidden, and, frankly, she can’t bring herself to care about boundaries and all the ones she’s clearly violating with Alastor because he’s here and he’s real and he’s *alive* and she’s so, so, *so* sorry—a guilt that hadn’t left her even after Emily had come to visit with Sir Pentious, redeemed and in white and gloriously alive—because what was that kind of victory without one of the people that made it all possible?

*“Alastor!”* She sobs, and she doesn’t care if she sounds like a child right now because this—*this*—this is everything she needed. The last member of her family back, safe and sound.

“Why, my dear, if you keep crying like that, you’ll flood the place and ruin all the work you just did.”

“I didn’t—I wasn’t sure you were coming back,” she hiccups, face buried in his side, trying to drown herself in ostentatious red that she thought she wouldn’t ever get the opportunity to see again. “I thought—I wasn’t sure what to think, you were just—*gone*—and I—I am so, so, so, so, *so sorry*, Alastor. If I had—if I had known—if I had *thought*—”

“All is fair in love and war, spitfire, settle down.”

“Sir!” Niffty’s excited little squeal has her drawing back, stumbling into the protective grasp of her father’s arms as Niffty clambers her way up Alastor’s body and loops her arms around his neck, eye shining with joy and unshed tears, and Charlie’s momentarily blindsided with the realization that she wasn’t—she wasn’t the only one that missed him, that was worried sick about him.

Even Vaggie looks relieved—delighted even—and that’s saying quite a lot.

“Darling! Don’t you look spiffy as always?”

“I stabbed him for you, sir, don’t you worry. I made sure he suffered.”

“Didn’t see you coming?”

“Not at all!” Her beam is big and wide, and Alastor’s grin is a delighted thing, high and charming, fingers snapping as a small crown of flowers, wilted as they are, materialize in his hand, gently bestowing it upon her head with all the adoration of a father looking at his child—or, well, his pet, since it was Alastor, although she’d never pretend to understand what goes on in his head when it comes to those under his purview.

“I bequeath thee Slayer of Filth,” he declares, and Niffty squeals, jumping off his shoulders to run to Husk and Angel, showing off her crown, wielding her knife—thankfully not angelic—as she runs to tackle the bugs that have, inconceivably, survived and returned with the same vengeance as everything else in hell does.

It makes her heart flutter, her soul weep at the softness, at the home that Alastor has built here with them, a home she hadn’t seen, hadn’t realized, until he risked it all to help them.

She doesn’t see the way her father’s eyes narrow behind her.

Alastor’s return marks the turning point—for her mood, for her drive—and Emily’s visit finally settles into her head and starts to make sense. Sir Pentious was alive—he was redeemed—he was proof! Proof, at last, that this can work, that what she’s doing is *real*, that it can mean something, that it can change things! She wasn’t a failure! She was a success, a visionary. She had done what had been thought to be impossible.

And then the visit happens.

---

Lucifer’s in his penthouse at the hotel when they arrive, peering out one of the many, many, *many* windows as the sound of voices drifts up to him, loud and raucous, and a quick peer out shows three figures approaching, exiting a limo that is puffing out pink smoke from the open door almost harder than the moth-man smoking it, the poor vehicle itself seeming to be hacking a lung.

They make his stomach twist more than Alastor, he thinks, briefly, for an illogical moment—not from fear, but from disgust—and he’s quick to carve open a portal to the main lobby, stepping through to see the radio demon already there, the corners of his smile twitching, like it can’t decide the breadth it wants to be at, red eyes glowing. It’s almost unsettling, although not intimidating, and Lucifer doesn’t bother announcing his presence as he walks closer, tuned into the rumbling of static coming from the demon.

Does he like the guy? Not at fucking all. Can he acknowledge that clearly the dude has a stake in his daughter’s hotel and would defend it? Unfortunately.

“Are they guests or trouble?”

“What guests would leave the car running,” is what the demon deigns to answer him, stepping forward, eyebrow twitching, and the windows darken as a barrier springs forth around the hotel—excessive, in Lucifer’s humblest of opinions seeing as no obvious threat is detectable right now, and certainly no fucking threat that matters with *him* around—and Alastor melts into the shadows, reemerging, presumably, somewhere past the door.

He can’t hear anything past the barrier, and, suddenly, it makes a little more sense—it’s certainly a greater degree of care than he expected. It *is* early morning, and they seem to be the only two awake for the day, although Lucifer wouldn’t be surprised if the little critter was running around somewhere doing this and that already—she seemed like a busy bee.

It’s—considerate, to block out the noise so that the others could continue to sleep.

Or—he thinks, making another portal to bypass the barrier Alastor has raised, exiting into the cool morning air behind their guests, quizzical gaze trained on the demon his daughter shed so many tears over—it’s insurance.

“To what do we owe the pleasure before operating hours,” Alastor greets, cheery and chipper and downright fucking beaming despite the early hour. Lucifer himself is barely awake, having been waiting for his coffee to make—the greatest thing that his sinners have ever brought down to hell, in his astute opinion, and it’s one of the only things that kept him from slipping fully into woes because, wow, what a holy invention! Beautiful! Amazing! Pure good! But his luck sucks and it’s still steeping upstairs, waiting for him to go and drink it, and he’s certain it’ll be cold and bitter by the time he gets around to it, lips pursing into a frown as he bemoans the loss of a good cup of joe.

Ugh. Cup of joe.

His love for coffee had almost floundered when he found out that there were places in hell where a cup of joe meant a very real Joe being in the coffee, and boy, oh, boy, did he want to off himself personally at that discovery. No, sir, no, thank you! It is home brewed for him forever, thanks very much.

And boy, oh, boy, is he *fucking distracted*, half asleep as he is, because why is that what he’s thinking about instead of paying a-fucking-ttention—

The one with a screen for a head falters in his steps for a split second, like he hadn’t expected Alastor to be standing there—which, truly, seems quite fair, seeing as, to his knowledge, the man hasn’t done much since his return, besides puttering around the hotel and making Lucifer consider banishing the radio he has in his room *for enjoyment* because he happens to like to listen to the odd song here and there when he’s working, but—there’s nothing enjoyable about waking up at two in the fucking goddamn morning to *Sunshine, Lollipops,* *and Rainbows* playing from it, obnoxiously loudly, just for the amusement of the stupid fucking piece of shit overlord who’s wearing too bright of a goddamn color for this time of day when he hasn’t even had his fucking coffee, never mind that he himself is dressed in bright ass white even though he’ll fucking die on the hill that it’s actually off-white verging on cream even though it’s white, it’s fucking white, he *knows* it’s white—

Okay, deep breaths, deep breaths.

Clearly he needs a caffeine boost for the day.

Truly, the nectar of the gods.

“Alastor, Alastor, Alastor—didn’t think you’d show your face again so soon with so much confidence,” the television screen laughs, voice obnoxious and grating, and Lucifer wonders briefly, self-consciously, if he ever sounds like that because, wow, how annoying. He thinks he’d prefer the radio to this. At least the radio is—kind of charming? Certainly easier on the ears than—what is that?—*desperation*?

A head tilt is all the deer demon graces them with, eyelids rising just the barest bit, like a warning, or a batting of innocence.

“Last we saw, you were getting cut down by an angel and tucking tail,” the man cackles, and there’s a surge of static that reaches even him where he’s perched on top of the car, observing, curious.

It’s one thing to watch the demon around his daughter, someone he’s obviously trying to curry favor with for whatever reason. But to see him around others—now, that’s a show he needs to watch, needs to analyze, needs to *dissect*.

When Vaggie had tugged him aside privately after Alastor’s return, whispering hurriedly that the demon and his daughter had made a deal, a deal she didn’t know the contents of because Alastor had left his end *vague* as *fuck*, he had been ready to rain hell on the demon, strangle him, kill him, end the fucking deal before it can ever be called due. But—Charlie’s tears sprung to mind, and their tentative, healing bond, and this dream of hers coming to fruition that a certain radio head was too woven into—

He couldn’t.

So, instead, he decided he’d stick around, relocate, dictate his bureaucratic methods from a new office for the most part in the hotel and only return to his palace as needed, to keep an eye on the situation, to keep an eye on *him*, so that he’d be there when the bill comes due, to save Charlie from whatever it may be in whatever way he saw fit. And, if he got lucky, maybe he could suss out just what kind of flavor of favor it would be before it was even called in, see if it was something he could do himself, get her off the hook without her needing to ever be reeled to the surface.

So, he sat.

And he watched.

And he listened.

And he picked at his nails because he was gonna fall back asleep at this rate, damn—

“Time really has done you in,” the moth-man drawls, dragging another lungful of smoke from his pipe, pink smoke swirling out past his lips like hearts, fluttering in the air.

Alastor’s fingers twitch and summon his staff in a whirl, the brief breeze wafting the smoke away from him in a motion that seems effortless and more just for appearances than any actual aggravation or desire to fight, even though his smile tightens, his shadow splashed against the barrier behind him, melding into the dark green and smoke, and yet he can see it lengthen even in the weak morning light that’s covering everything in shades of pale rose blush.

“Time has an effect on us all, I’m sure,” Alastor croons, a laugh track playing behind him. “Have you looked in the mirror recently? I know this great lady—she’ll help you drop ten pounds before you can finish signing your name—ha, ha, ha!”

*“You little—”*

“Val,” the television coaxes as the smoke puffs out, a clear sign of aggravation.

“Oh, dear, touchy subject—I do apologize,” Alastor sings, swaying as his fingers flitter over the bow of his smile, lashes lowering. “I forgot your beau there also has some self-confidence issues. Topics of changing format must be a point of heavy contention, I’m *sure*.”

“Shut up, you static piece of shit. At least we all have the decency of not running away. So much for the ‘powerful radio demon’. The hotel suits you, truly. You’re a *real* has-been.”

Alastor’s brow twitches.

He can’t see it, but he’s certain the television demon is grinning if the victory in his voice is anything to go by, taking arrogant steps forward, going to stand toe to toe with Alastor, his hands clench into fists.

“I’m *embarrassed* to have ever wanted you to be a part of us. Look how the mighty have fallen,” he drawls, and one of the hands flashes up to grab a smiling face in electric claws, and Lucifer almost rises, almost does something—he won’t sit by and let his daughter cry again—but the radio demon’s gaze only lowers more, ears twitching in the direction of the moth-man that has started to go around and edge closer, smoking as the wings on his back flutter, small little gusts that drive the plumes forward.

The third member of the team just seems to be standing there, phone camera trained on them, and it’s—oh.

*Oh!*

*That’s* what this fucking is?

A fucking public humiliation, public execution, intimidation, grab for power bullshit show?

Are they fucking serious?

Seriously, sometimes he just—he doesn’t know what Charlie sees in these people because, seriously, are they fucking *serious*? They are the fucking *worst*. They owe this hotel and *everyone* in it (including Alastor, loathe as he is to admit it) their *lives* for the extermination being cancelled—for all future exterminations potentially being cancelled, if negotiations go well, and yet they’re—

“…*it’s not* just *his life, dad. It’s his reputation, his connections, his desire to continue to help* *us—I asked him to take on those risks*. I *put him there and asked him to pay the price*. Alone. *And now*—“

Motherfucker.

“Always knew the radio demon was a *coward*.”

A laugh track plays from Alastor, distorted and haunted.

“That angel should’ve finished the damn job.”

*“Oh?”* Alastor’s gaze flicks to the moth-man who has edged himself around his side, and Lucifer wonders what the fuck is powerful and humiliating about a gang up. Isn’t that more embarrassing? “Have you come to try your hand, then?”

“Heh,” the television huffs, amusement coloring the breath of air as electricity sparks along his body. “Nah…”

Pink smoke snares a shackle around Alastor’s neck and wrists.

“Just gonna send a message.”

The girl filming blows a bubble from the gum she’s chewing.

Huh. Alastor doesn’t really show up well in film, does he? Is that just a thing? Does he even know he’s being filmed? Is he just always a fucking blob on camera?

That’s kind of—

Funny?

He doesn’t know.

The bubble pops.

*“Ladies and gentlemen!”*

It all happens faster that Lucifer can process.

Because he’s half asleep.

Not because he’s not competent.

He’s just sleepy.

It’s early. Sue him.

By the time he had shown up for the fight with the angels, Alastor had been long gone, apparently having been cut down by Adam, from what he’s gleaned from the conversation. He hadn’t actually gotten to see the man against anyone who could actually present a challenge—clearly he had gone overkill on the loan sharks when he had been there, blowing off steam, as he had said. But this—oh, this is so much worse—better?—than that had been.

Restrained as he is, as power cords lunge at him with intent to stab, Alastor raises his microphone to his mouth, and his voice booms across the hell-scape, demonic and chilling in an effortlessly charming way that Lucifer doesn’t think he’s ever mastered even as the ruler of Hell—nowhere close to the way his own comes out as clear aggression every time he’s riled up to this point—cool poise maintained even under such stringent circumstances.

“*A brief broadcast to all—and a* reminder—”

“Are you seriously fucking broadcasting right now?

The television demon lunges forward, claws swiping through the air, but Alastor smoothly wraps himself deeper into that Val dude’s chains, spinning away and spinning back out as the television head stumbles past, sharp boot coming up and kicking the demon with what seems like minuscule force—seriously, probably not enough to make a soccer ball reach its goal—straight into the barrier, an audible crack ringing out.

*“No interruptions, please. That’s quite rude of you.”*

“Don’t go getting cocky now,” the Val guy hisses, yanking on the chains, and Alastor’s smile twitches as he’s yanked forward, smoke blown in his face, large moth wings unfurling and flapping with aggression, clawed hands grabbing at an antler and yanking down, destabilizing him in every way possible, then—“Vox!”

“Got it!”

Power cords slash forward, electrical current powerful enough to singe the air with the smell of ozone before even making contact, and Lucifer jolts forward, wings unfurling—

Alastor’s a thing of fluid movement, kicking vicious and hard into the gut in front of him, Val doubling over for a split second, but that’s all that’s needed for the radio demon’s antlers to triple in size and his legs to open in an effortless split, yanking the hand grasping his antler down in a vicious yank of his head with a sickening crack of his neck, and—*“Val!”*—leave the moth demon to get skewered through with razor sharp, heavy duty power cords, pumping an aggressive amount of electrical current. The scream is agonizing, the chains breaking and hand falling slack as he’s fried, one cord straight through the eye, face burnt from the inside out, and Alastor’s antlers shrink out of the grasp as he palms the ground and swings his legs around and gets out of the way in seconds before the current can transfer to him, Val’s body falling forward—

The girl’s phone clatters to the ground as she runs forward.

*“Val!”*

“You fucking bastard!”

*“A reminder to all,”* Alastor breathes, not sounding the slightest bit winded, brushing off dirt from his suit with a dismissive hand. The scream is echoing in feedback all across hell, the speakers reaching to such a level that, had the barrier not been up, it would have surely woken everyone in the hotel up, if the primary source didn’t do that already. *“Do not* mess *with the* Radio *Demon*.”

“I’m gonna fucking *fry you*, you complete fucking piece of dog shit crap shoot—I will shit on your fucking *mom—*”

The business end of the microphone stand stabs straight through the screen as Alastor dodges power cords, shadows lunging and restraining electrified talons for the split second necessary for him to shatter screen and—“*Do wish the audience a good morning, Vox*.”—with a blast of radio frequency, the television gives a warbling, distorted scream, speakers wailing in echo, metal and wires and burnt plastic shattering and exploding behind him, before he’s, too, slumping forward, power fried, brain—is there a brain in there?—mush, like as if Alastor had blown it out with a shotgun shell.

The girl—she flings bottles of potions at Alastor, alters his suit into a straitjacket, scrambles away with all her might, but it’s clear to see that she’s more of the brains than a fighter, and Alastor smiles meanly as he lifts the microphone to his mouth.

*“Wishing you a very good morning, sinners, this is Alastor.”*

The girl spits at his feet as he pauses in front of her.

*“Stay tuned.”*

Her body slumps as a manifested shadow slams her head into the ground.

Crimson eyes and an ever-present grin turn to meet Lucifer’s gaze as his shadows pluck the bodies from the ground and draw closer, Vera Lynn crooning over the speakers, a shadow taking the phone that had fallen and crushing it into powder.

“Enjoy the show, Luci’?”

“Lu—*Luci*’?

Alastor laughs, but something about his smile seems strained, his eyes cold.

A hand flutters to his throat where the chain of smoke had sat, making as if he’s fixing his bowtie, but Lucifer can read the gesture for what it is, holding the door for his shadows to pile the bodies into.

“Lucifer is much to grandiose a name for a—what was it Angel said? Short king?”

Lucifer gawks.

“Quite fitting, I believe.”

“I—you—”

Alastor’s eyebrow lifts.

“What the actual fuck?”

A black tentacle wraps around his waist, yanking him off the roof of the car with not a shred of delicacy, and he squirms out of it immediately, wings flapping in a physical manifestation of his irritation, glaring as the limb disappears back to where it originated from, the car pulling away from the hotel as the barrier drops.

“I do certainly enjoy my broadcasts to be longer, but, what can I say? A sour mood ruins a good performance,” he sings, turning away to return to the hotel.

Lucifer blinks before stumbling to catch up to him, falling into step with ease, the deer’s steps nowhere near long enough to leave him in the dust, and he wonders idly if it’s done on purpose.

“Seems like you’ve been in a sour mood since you returned.”

The smile twitches, brows furrowing, even though all the man does is hum, opening the hotel doors and striding inside.

From the back, Lucifer thinks, the man looks tired. Tired and stressed. Not even the high puffs of his suit can hide the slump of his shoulders, pulled back tight as they are by hands clasped behind his back. His shadow, he notes, is frowning—disappearing as soon as his eyes linger a second too long on it. It’s—it’s a feeling he’s familiar with.

Faced with his own mortality? He can’t say he relates. But—he figures being faced with an immortality of being alone for—*forever*—is a close enough comparison.

Fuck.

*Fuck.*

He’s really going to do this, isn’t he?

Charlie is so lucky he loves her this much.

“How about,” he wants to throw up, “some coffee?”

His voice does not squeak at the end. He is the ruler of hell. He is an angel. He is a king! It did not squeak, no matter what anyone says!

The radio demon perks up with a brush of static, turning to the kitchen with a flourish, smile spreading into something that seems a little more genuine and a little less vicious. “Coffee! What a great idea!”

Sinners, help him.

“Mhm,” he hums, trailing behind, eyes narrowing in consideration, “fabulous.”

Maybe this would be a more reliable avenue for keeping Charlie safe from the consequences of that deal. If he can—if he can *stomach* the radio demon enough to get just—just close enough that he can discern what makes him tick, what has him so tired, what has soured his mood so much that he would kill, however impermanently, three demons that hadn’t posed much of a threat to him *clearly*—then maybe, just maybe he can get at whatever Alastor may ask before he even has a chance to bring it to Charlie.

Yes.

This was a solid plan.

And not at all because he was bored.

No, sir.

He is above that.

Totally.

## Chapter 2: tea time

### Summary

Vaggie almost regrets telling Lucifer about the deal. Almost.

Alastor is charming, a gentleman, a showman that can be and became everything they could ever want and need—made himself essential, indispensable, impossible to get rid of, like the very bugs in the hotel. And then, the opportunist that he is, he went ahead and made himself a home in their hearts, however bizarrely, however insanely. But, and she knows this as well as she knows how to breathe and how to fight and how to fly, that he is, at root, a master manipulator. She knows this. She knows this for a fact. But—even so—even knowing that—

She sighs.

She almost regrets telling Lucifer.

But, really, she doesn't regret anything at all.

### Notes

listen y'all- y'all need to understand- i've been doing this shit for so long, so fucking long- like, genuinely probably longer than anyone probably expects from me- and i've been so ridiculously busy that i haven't written in ages, but- i forgot what it's like to be part of an active fandom and wow, this is crazy. thank you so much- am literally so happy you guys are enjoying this so much.

do not look at me for a plan, though, because there isn't one.

for right now, we're just trying to stay a chapter ahead and seeing where this story takes us.

truly, the weirdest 5+1 i've ever done.

enjoy!

### Text

Right, okay, if Lucifer is honest, he kind of, maybe, potentially, totally, put the whole ‘keep an eye on Alastor’ thing on the back burner.

In his defense, though, shit’s gotten real fucking busy.

There’s proof now—proof that redemption is possible, that it’s a viable avenue, that it *works*, and that means that heaven and hell have to meet quite a bit to discuss what this sort of arrangement *means* when it comes to hell and its overpopulation problem—when it comes to heaven and its plans.

He doesn’t go to the meetings—of course not—him and heaven don’t mix well—but he hears all about them with the rest of the members of the hotel whenever Vaggie and Charlie return and helps brainstorm new pitches, tries to figure out how to keep heaven on their side. Between that and his own duties in managing, well, the entirety of hell, Alastor falls to the wayside on his long, long, *long* list of duties.

The man acts as irritating and persistent as always, no real point of concern at the moment, from what he can see, but as soon as he realizes his attention’s been slipping, he zeroes back in on his target whenever he has access to him, watching his movements closely with no small amount of consternation, feeling his temper tick with almost every breath the demon takes.

Truly, since their shared coffee that one morning when Alastor had temporarily incapacitated the overlord trio apparently known as the—*V’s?*—he hasn’t seen much of Alastor to begin with. And, seriously, he can’t tell if that’s a better or worse name than the fucking *Radio* *Demon* on the imagination scale. At least the tall, dark, and red all over overlord hadn’t named himself. But those three stooges had decided on their name, and that’s just—wow.

And he thought *his* ideas were bad.

What a confidence boost.

And, really, it’s not that bad having the guy be absent. For all of Lucifer’s plans, the absence of the radio demon could work to his benefit, really—potentially. Maybe he’d find his needs somewhere else and have no need of Charlie anymore. Or, maybe, he’d be gone so long that when the time came, Charlie wouldn’t be as attached to him anymore and know how to turn him away. Yeah! That would be great! Perfect! The best way this could possibly turn out! This absence—it isn’t *annoying*. It’s great! Downright spectacular.

Even so, he considers just asking the overlord plain out what the fuck he wants with his daughter, but, truly, he doubts he’ll get any sort of a straight answer, just the fucker tap dancing on his last nerve, so he doesn’t bother trying. But, fuck, wouldn’t it be easier? It’d be so much easier. It would save so much fucking time and he wouldn’t have to play this stupid fucking guessing game of, ‘Will he? Won’t he?’—if he could just get that shit fuck to fucking answer a question with a straight fucking answer—*fuck!*

Okay, so maybe this *is* a little annoying.

“So even royalty can have a bad day, huh?” The bartender comments idly, cocking an eyebrow as Lucifer’s head meets the countertop unceremoniously, hat flopping off his head and toppling over to the other side of the counter to be saved by the cat’s smooth reflexes, grunting in appreciation when it’s set down next to him.

“Definitely been more than a day,” he snorts deprecatingly, dragging his head up to scrub his hands over his face and try and convince himself that he can be a perfectly functional, multitasking, butt kicking king when, really, all he feels like is like he’s spread too fucking thin and like he’s stressed all the time and like his answer should be at this bar but it’s not and, regrettably, his answers will not be in the glass the bartender is setting down in front of him, even if it is a nice gesture. He drinks it silently, grateful that it’s at least whiskey and not *beer*—rubbing his fingers over his temples and debating just sloughing off to his palace for some time away from—well—*everything*—

It feels very much needed—

“Between you and Alastor, I’m starting to think that exhaustion is contagious.”

Eh?

Exhaustion?

Contagious?

*Alastor?*

He perks up pathetically quickly. “What do you mean?”

The cat demon cocks at eyebrow at him but gracefully doesn’t point out his attitude change, wiping at a glass, as if he’s just looking for something to keep himself entertained with because Lucifer’s the only one at the bar, the spider demon having already left awhile ago, so there’s not a damn glass dirty aside from the one with his drink in it—seeing as the man chugged from the bottles like a heathen. He can’t imagine what the bill is like for how much drink ends up down this guy’s gullet. Clearly, this man is just trying to find something to do. A nervous tick, maybe? Not that it matters. It’s not the data he’s looking for or cares about, anyway.

“You both burning the candle at both ends or something?”

“I hadn’t realized he was also tiring out.”

Husk shrugs his shoulders dismissively, setting the glass down, swinging the towel around in his hand. “Ain’t my place to comment, but seems like it. You got something on your mind, your Highness?”

“I’m—” he should tread carefully. King of hell or not, this man was under Alastor’s claws. Albeit not a threat to himself, if too much is spilled to the bartender, if the bartender gets in trouble or injured because of him—because he learned the wrong thing, says the wrong thing, divulges the wrong thing—

Husk is not an overlord. He is not the king of hell. He is a soul under contract.

He should steer clear of putting him in a tough spot.

The man’s eyebrow cocks. “Hm?”

He’s so nice. That’s so nice of him. Go, him—he’s a ten out of ten. The most merciful ruler. Truly, he should be more popular.

“I just,” he inhales deeply, gaze focusing on a little spot on the counter where the grain of the wood whorls. “I’m worried. About—about Charlie.”

“Something wrong with the princess?”

“No, no, not to my knowledge, I just—I worry.” His finger taps idly against the side of the glass, gaze drifting to the snake coiled around the hat placed on the counter next to him, teeth worrying at his lower lip. “I thought maybe if I—if I could help—if I could get along with—with everyone—it would help her feel—more at ease?”

Husk hums. “You mean Alastor?”

His cheeks burn for no good fucking reason, and he worries the alcohol will somehow spontaneously combust from the heat as he takes a sip to hide the embarrassed grimace of his expression and the annoyed tick at the corner of his mouth. The laugh that tinkles out of the man tells him he’s not very successful on any of those fronts.

“Look,” the bartender sighs, folding the towel and setting it to the side, finally giving up fiddling with his hands to brace his body casually against the bar, “dirty little secret about Al—”

Lucifer can’t help but lean forward a little bit.

“—he’s real easy to get along with.”

He sags.

Now, that’s just bullshit.

Easy to get along with—the guy hated him the second the door opened! He’s not *blind* or *stupid*. He remembers the way those eyes pinned him, followed him, narrowed in dislike and suspicion, and, yeah, Lucifer hadn’t liked him either—the fuck was that smarmy dude doing being so close to his daughter?—but that’s not the issue! Alastor *never* liked him, before they had even exchanged words. How is that easy to get along with?

“Oh, don’t give me that look. I’m being serious,” the cat huffs, looking off to the side with an expression that’s both pissed and amused. “As far as demons go, he’s pretty okay. Make him laugh, be a miserable sack of shit around him, don’t be a sorry excuse of a person, and he’s right as fucking rain. Easy man to get along with. Even better if you share some of his interests. And don’t have a dick.” The cat’s eyes slide over to him, flicking up and down in the most threadbare of consideration. “You’re definitely out of luck on that last one.”

“Yeah, I think that ship has sailed.” It has more than sailed. In fact, it sailed and then got shot by a cannon ball and lost its main sail and then had the hull split in half and then died a watery, dismal death as it sunk into the fucking abyss of ships that have already fucking sailed and split and sunk dismally, *thanks*. There’s no undoing the complete clusterfuck that was their first encounter, an entirely mutual dislike that has now made this whole fucking plan of his embarrassingly difficult.

A problem of his own making? Absolutely! But a problem nonetheless.

Honestly, when he thinks of it, maybe he had played right into the demon’s hands perfectly. This is truly working exactly into Alastor’s benefit without the demon having to even do a thing. It’s amazingly convenient for him. It truly is almost like he—

He pauses.

Oh my god, he’s got to be kidding him.

He totally—fucking—*did!*

It’s impressive that his alcohol doesn’t boil from the heat of his rage at the realization that he fucking played straight into the piece of shit’s hands because he *doesn’t like him*. He doesn’t like him and made it damn clear and now he can’t get *close to him*. More than that, he can’t act out either or flaunt his power because *Charlie* knows he doesn’t like him. And Charlie *cares* about him. The demon had built himself a useful shield he never has to personally deploy with his *daughter,* and he did that all before making a fucking deal—the *bitch*—

“Well, ain’t this place about fixing shit? Fix his first impression of you.”

The only thing he’s going to be fixing is that dumbass smug look at that stupid deer’s face when he gets his hands on him—he’ll make his bout with Adam look like a children’s party, not to fucking worry—

“How?”

A shrug is his answer, and he wants to pull his hair out.

Knocking the rest of his drink back instead, he sets the glass on the counter, waving off the question of another, watching the man go about cleaning the glass, seeming happy to have something to do again. In lieu of not having a glass occupying his fingers anymore, he rakes them through his hair, eyes pressing shut in the mental anguish of trying to figure out how the fuck he’s gonna get anywhere close to the stupid radio lord—to tear him a new asshole or befriend him, he hasn’t decided yet.

He’s so fucking screwed.

Charlie’s so fucking screwed.

This whole thing is so fucking screwed.

He was an angel, the highest ranking seraphim, the closest to divinity himself. He’s the fucking king of hell—fucking royalty—the strongest of his denizens—not a fucking detective, sleuth, bounty hunter or some bullshit with both the talent, patience, desire, and know-how to understand and want to understand the fucked up psyches of those he’s searching for.

He has enough problems from his own psyche, thanks very much. He’s all set on understanding anyone else’s.

He just—

He just wants his daughter to be okay… That’s all. Was that—too much to ask?

The bartender sighs. “Alright, I’m gonna level with you. Al ain’t gonna like you, flat out, to begin with.”

He wants to bang his head on the counter again.

“I thought you said to fix it!”

“Sure, yeah, but you’re also the main thing he dislikes.”

Lucifer blinks. “What, does he just have something against amazingly handsome individuals with a world class smile?”

Husk stares at him.

Lucifer beams.

“Top notch, right?”

“*Mhm*, definitely.”

That was most, mhm, definitely sarcastic. He’s detracting points of fondness from his mental list of the hotel members when it comes to this little cat, no matter how helpful he’s trying to be. His smile is the best, absolutely charming—he was the most beautiful angel! He knows it’s worth something, dammit! Don’t lie to him! Just look at Adam! The dude was dog food compared to him.

“Alastor ain’t a fan of anyone stronger than him. So long as he’s how he is and you’re you, chances are you’re not gonna get along ever.”

“Motivational. Really. Thank you. I feel *so* much better.”

“Doesn’t mean you shouldn’t try, though. Guy gets along with the other overlords just fine.”

“They don’t hold a candle to angelic power.”

“And Al ain’t—“ the cat pauses, freezes, grits his teeth and looks away. “Look—you wanna get along with him? Make him laugh. At your expense, not at your expense, at fucking paint drying, whatever. Easiest way to get on his good side is to amuse him. *That’s* how you charm him—you make that fake ass smile of his real.”

Huh.

That was—surprisingly good advice.

All he needed to do was lure him in by making him laugh—tempt him with humor.

Simple enough.

He was hilarious.

He could do this in his fucking sleep.

---

In the spirit of redemption and becoming better and not lying to herself or others anymore, Vaggie almost regrets telling Lucifer about the deal. *Almost*.

Really, if she had any idea what things would feel like once the radio demon was back, if she had bothered to understand her own complicated mess of feelings that had lit up inside of her when Adam had turned their sights on them, Alastor nowhere to be found—especially after witnessing how easily the first angel had gotten rid of Sir Pentious—and the sheer elation that had filled her and startled her so badly at his return, maybe she wouldn’t have said anything. And, truly, even now as she sits and tries to parse through it during the handful of instances where she’s alone and not running around with Charlie trying to market the hotel as viable to heaven again, trying to figure out the true mechanics of redemption when they don’t really understand *how* Sir Pentious made it to heaven—if death is a requirement, or if the form of death matters, if an executioners blade would do the same thing, or if that was a risk they were even willing to take with their guests when they weren’t sure how this worked—she doesn’t really understand her feelings.

Alastor was—*is*—a master of manipulation.

That’s his thing, really.

He controls by fear, but, more than that, Vaggie also knows that he controls by *charm*.

Alastor is *charming*, no doubt about it, as loathe as she is to admit it. And that charm opens doors for him with people that would otherwise probably not give him the time of day. There had been a time where he hadn’t been taken seriously, according to Mimzy, a time when people had scoffed and turned away from him, considered him small and weak, thought he was incapable of doing any of things he later went on to do. He had played the charming game, presumably—if he was so easily dismissed, how else had he gotten near the overlords he took down? It wasn’t like he was in hell for very long before he abruptly rose to power. He couldn’t have possibly terrified them.

So he was charming—suave—a real gentleman when he wanted to be, polite and kind and unleashing a slew of endearments that would make even the most composed of people swoon—and Vaggie didn’t doubt that he had charmed Charlie into a deal, that he had charmed *all* of them into one without even having to breathe a word.

Alastor had somehow, as the most hidden, closeted, obscure part of their little found family, done his job so perfectly that he conned them all into caring about him, had entrenched himself so firmly in their lives that his absence had felt like a physical wound that no amount of optimism could fill, a visceral pain in the same way Sir Pentious’ had felt.

It was because of that—because of the understanding of this even if the rest of her feelings remained incomprehensible—that she had decided to confide in Lucifer, the only person who, out of all of them, had not fallen for Alastor’s whims and flights of fancy. He hated the guy and he loved Charlie and that made him objective in a way that Vaggie no longer felt entirely confident that she could be. She had—she had thought she *hated* Alastor, despised him, especially when he swooped in to make a deal when Charlie was finally alone, at her most vulnerable, when Vaggie couldn’t be there to help her.

He had been waiting for that opportunity from the start—she knows it.

She remembers when he first showed up to the hotel. She remembers how he quickly suggested a deal—a deal that didn’t go through because Vaggie had warned against it. Charlie wasn’t an idiot, but she was also kind and trusting and desperate for her dream to come to fruition, and maybe—maybe if she hadn’t said anything, Charlie would’ve taken the deal. Maybe Charlie would have gone through with it in her desperation, especially after suffering such a critical blow.

They had been desperate, feeling doomed, backed into a corner.

And then Alastor had come and offered aid.

And then there was replacing Vaggie’s role in the hotel—another thing she’s certain Charlie hadn’t picked up on, but she had. Niffty was—Niffty was great, but—Niffty was also only one person, demon, gremlin, *thing*. What was the difference between her and Vaggie? The only difference, really, was that Niffty was under Alastor’s claws. And Husk? Well, Husk had already revealed his true purpose—the confidant of the hotel, drunk and morose and the perfect ear to listen to their woes that they all kept falling for every single time even after they heard how much he *knows*—and—

He’s also Alastor’s.

The hotel was run to Alastor’s benefit, to make himself and the employees he provided indispensable. He took his job seriously, if a little lackadaisically, helping when instructed—with the exception of technology—and being an overall essential tool that, really, she’s not sure they could divest themselves of—

Until *Lucifer*.

That had been the *only* time she’s ever seen the man upset in any sort of way that matters. Everything else—it’s posturing, a show, a gimmick, a play of intimidation to remind them of the power imbalance between himself and them. Sir Pentious had been that every time he attacked—*Vaggie* had been that the first day Alastor had shown up, shoved to the side and played for laughs, ridiculed for his amusement, to exert his authority and power.

But Lucifer—Lucifer wasn’t just some paltry sinner or fallen executioner he could intimidate. Lucifer was a King, a seraphim—fallen or not—an all powerful ruler with the exact power wheelhouse to put Alastor six feet under again without needing an executioners weapon to do it. And yet, what had irritated him hadn’t been any of that—no, Vaggie knows it’s not that simplistic. Maybe because she already went through it herself, she could see it so clearly then, but it wasn’t ever about just his *power*.

It was that Lucifer was Charlie’s *dad*.

And he had the power—the *metaphorical* power—to replace Alastor.

And that was what Alastor hadn’t wanted.

And it’s not something she understands, really—she can’t really pretend to when, really, it’s not like Charlie would ever dismiss him or ask him to leave, especially considering he had already done so much for them, but the mere possibility of it had been enough to spark Alastor’s ire, and *that*—despite her sudden mess of complicated feelings for him—is *scary*.

Whatever reason he has for being here, no matter how genuine he seemed with Mimzy, no matter how much he’s helped and how much he’s done for them—no matter that he was there for Charlie when she couldn’t be, even if he took advantage and struck a deal that, even so, according to Charlie, followed her strictly set limitations (even if she knows that a limitation like that is garbage to a man like Alastor, a mere insect to his masterful games)—no matter any of his contributions and how her heart hurt when they thought he had been killed or her relief and happiness when he reappeared beside them—no matter any of that!

She knows he’s dangerous.

And, as fond as he might be becoming of them, Alastor had a *reason* for coming to their hotel, and she knows it wasn’t just for entertainment. The problem is that she doesn’t know the truth for *why* he came. And with Charlie in this deal—this deal that could mean anything—there’s a world of opportunity even with the limitation of not causing harm—he’s more dangerous than ever. She knows it. And it’s terrifying—it terrifies her—it—

And then he brings her tea.

While she’s sitting there, tearing her hair out going through the notes she’d taken at heaven’s meetings, trying to make sense of the confusion of heaven that had made being an executioner a much easier job because heavens politics are *una mierda*—he brings her tea, something that she knows he doesn’t like, something she knows he doesn’t drink, something he’s made just for her, deliberately going out of his way for *her*—and she knows she’s not impartial anymore, that her heart isn’t as steeled against him as it was before.

And that’s—even more dangerous.

So, she almost regrets running to Lucifer, almost regrets doing the one thing she knows Alastor wanted to prevent. But it’s only *almost.*

She doesn’t regret it at all.

---

Alastor—was—fucking—*impossible!*

Like, actually, truly, entirely impossible—not just to make laugh, but to fucking track down. The man that Lucifer had previously thought would be so fucking difficult to avoid is actually scarcer than the fucking guests in this place, a veritable shadow in a hotel he’s supposed to be managing.

It’s incensing, frustrating, grating and destructive and enough to have him flinging his chair at the wall just for something to let his anger out on because time is ticking—Charlie’s time is ticking—and he has to—he needs to get to know this guy, get him to relax, get him to turn to *him* when the deal comes due because he has so much more to offer than his daughter and is so much harder to manipulate when it comes to his sinners and is just—

He needs to be the one the bill comes due on.

That’s *all* he needs. Just—make himself the one that the deal shifts to—take his daughter out of the equation. It’s so simple. So fucking simple.  But he can’t *do* that if he can’t fucking find the demon, so—

He groans, staring despondently at the duck he had been working on, desperate for something to do with all the energy inside of him, to distract himself from what a complete and utter failure he is as a father to not have been able to be there for his daughter enough to prevent such a deal from being made. If he had just been here, if he had suspected, if he had been paying attention, present, just fucking there—

He knew he should’ve built this hotel a lot fucking smaller.

Charlie’s not an idiot, he knows. He doesn’t doubt his daughter. And she—she seems certain about the quality of a man that Alastor is, but—even if he could be persuaded to believe such a thing before, *now* is different. Death, grievous injury, a *fall*—in whatever context—changes people.

It changed him.

It changed Lilith.

It changed the world.

Alastor had been in a sour mood since the battle, had made himself scarce now in the days after where his presence would not just be expected but appreciated considering his absence immediately post-fight when before—before he made it seem like he would detest to be anywhere else.

So, why? Why now? Why is he now absent?

It’s—worrying.

Worrying?

Upsetting.

Upsetting, frustrating, distressing, worrisome, fucking anxiety inducing.

There was more to this, more to this whole thing, some crucial part that he’s missing when it comes to this man, but—

It seems like no one else knows either.

Any questions trying to pry into the man gets him nothing but a few dismissive comments on the surface level of his personality, the same repeated story of his past deeds in hell to reach his overlord status, and the continuous sense of confusion and uncertainty from everyone as they realize someone they had worried for still remains an enigma.

They’re living with a veritable stranger—a dangerous stranger—and trusting him with their lives and well-being.

That should be—terrifying.

They know the literal ruler of hell better than a fucking *deer*.

That’s damning.

Husk is useless, what little information he might have that others aren’t necessarily privy to kept under lock and key, barring the one almost slip he made the other night that he seemed to think Lucifer hadn’t picked up on, but—Lucifer’s not an idiot.

Alastor’s not *what?*

The questions continue.

And his absence remains grating.

After all, its been *weeks*.

It’s only as he’s walking down the halls of the hotel, turning the small duck over in his hands, debating if maybe he should start going out into the city on his manhunt or just cave and summon the fucking demon, violation of privacy be damned—he’ll just be at the same fucking square he already is, anyway, if the guy decides to get pissed about it—that it hits him. Alastor’s not what? Not, not, not, not, *not—*

*“Alastor ain’t a fan of anyone stronger than him. So long as he’s how he is and you’re you,* *chances are you’re not gonna get along ever.”*

*“Motivational. Really. Thank you. I feel so much better.”*

*“Doesn’t mean you shouldn’t try, though. Guy gets along with the other overlords just fine.”*

*“They don’t hold a candle to angelic power.”*

*“And Al ain’t—“ the cat pauses, freezes, grits his teeth and looks away.*

He stops in his tracks.

*“They don’t hold a candle to angelic power.”*

Why was Alastor hiding?

Why was he staying far away?

Why was he still slinking in the shadows, missing like he had been after the battle?

Was it—could it be—

*“They don’t hold a candle to angelic power.”*

That *motherfucker*.

“Niffty!” He shouts as he enters the common lobby where everyone seems to spend their spare time when they’re not aching for some privacy, barely noticing the multiple sets of eyes that turn to look at him, anger boiling to a fever pitch that he doesn’t even recognize Charlie standing off to the side with Husk, vision narrowed to a tunnel that scans the floor for the little cyclops cretin so that he can squeeze out of her the information of her boss’ location out of her because he is *well past* playing the patient game.

He’s never met a poser bigger than himself, that *shit*.

“Your Highness,” comes a giggle from just behind him, and he whirls to pin her with a burning glare, wings unfurling in his fury, eyes dyed red.

He had seen enough of his daughter’s tears for an eternity.

“Where is Alastor,” he hisses, and the cyclops blinks, pupil narrowing for a moment before she skitters to the side, as if trying to look for the man, peering around the room, his fury spiking higher at her playful obtuseness. He’s not in the mood for niceties right now—these fucking sinners—always getting on his nerves, never straightforward, too fucked to recognize real danger until it’s strangling them—and strangle them he will if they keep testing his fucking patience; it is not known to be *infinite*—“*Where* is he?”

“Snapping at a young lady is hardly fitting of someone of your station,” comes drawled from behind him, static burgeoning from the shadows, the tinges of genuine upset in the cadence of the words, and when he whirls, he sees the flicker of reality stuttering around the figure, but his own anger is too bright to suffer paltry tricks, “your Highness.”

“I am going to tear your asshole, you *bitch*—“

“Dad!”

“That’s almost better than what he said to Adam.”

“Angel!”

He’s too far gone to spare them a thought, lunging forward and snaring his claws in the pristine bowtie, hand tearing a portal into the air, wings lashing with a gust that rattles the windows as he propels them through, tail lashing it shut behind them as they whip past.

*“Dad!”*

Alastor’s smile is a strained thing as he melts out of Lucifer’s grasp and manifests a few paces away, far enough to have the advantage of distance but not so far to have cornered himself, and yet it is all meaningless in the face of Lucifer’s rage, his daughter’s tears playing in a reel in his mind’s eye. He won’t watch her spill anymore over this waste of space again, not if he has any say over it.

“What has got your tail in such a twist? Such aggression in the presence of your daughter—it’s highly unlike you.”

“Cut the shit, bitch. You’re not healed yet, are you?”

The smile freezes.

His own teeth grit into a vicious grin.

*Bingo*.

“I should’ve noticed sooner, but I don’t care nearly enough about you to pay that much attention.”

“My, my, you could have fooled me with all the whispers I’ve been hearing,” the radio demon muses, posture tense and yet his tone is frivolous, a beautiful exercise in control, and yet still not enough for Lucifer’s assessing gaze, and he knows he’s right—right about everything.

He really should have seen it sooner.

Angelic power...

Alastor might be powerful, to the point where he was entrusted to the all important job of keeping Adam occupied, which he had resoundingly *failed* at, but, at the end of the day, he was just another sinner, just another wayward soul in the depths of hell, all his strength and power and regeneration ability useless in the face of angelic power—as vulnerable as every other sinner in the Pride Ring. The fact that he managed to walk away from it, to play at normalcy and jubilance in such a state *for so long* is a testament to his infallible control, to his power—more internal than external—and to his stupidity, his pride, too cocky to request aid when no one here even held his fleeing against him to begin with.

If anything, wouldn’t the knowledge of his injury gain him more points here? Show that he didn’t flee out of fear, out of pride, out of malice, but, rather, because he was too injured to keep going—because he had tried his best?

Any injured soldier would be removed from the field to receive treatment so that they could fight another day—be it an infantry man or a sergeant or lieutenant.

Why *hide it?*

“I knew there was something off about you.”

The demon hums, steps to the side, and Lucifer’s wings flare, teeth baring.

Alastor is his prey right now, in every way with meaning, and he had played nice already. He was still playing nice now, technically, getting the man to show himself through the use of Niffty instead of just dragging him into the light unwittingly. He was being a goddamn saint right now, truly. But his patience is fraying. And he can already feel himself on the verge of tearing that stupid suit off the deer to get at wherever the stupid fucking wound is and get this *over with*.

“Show me.”

“I assure you, your assessment is fallible.”

*“Show me!”*

The room quakes.

The corners of those lips twitch, falter, and the shadows expand and contract, drawing closer as if in defense, red tipped gloves flinching just slightly, and if there was a second of hesitation more, he would truly lose his fucking *mind*—

“Your intentions?”

He freezes.

His—his intentions?

Mother of fucking *fuck*—

He’s angry—too angry—angry in the way that only Alastor seems to make him—too angry to think rationally to any extent that matters, tail lashing furiously behind him, point raking vicious against the floor. It had taken Adam attacking Charlie to get him to this level of rage previously, and yet this demon, this circus act of a man, had gotten him there by simply being absent, being quiet. Could such a thing make any level of sense? What the fuck was wrong with him?

Just another thing to add to the long, long, long, long, *long* list.

He inhales shakily, shoulders lifting and falling with the effort, the flame of his anger sputtering, gas turned down, feed gone, and his tail lashes and then hangs, hand smoothing over the front of his suit as he breathes out, fixing his gaze, still burning coals, onto Alastor’s form. For all of the man’s cockiness and abrasive attitude, he’s not stupid. His confidence and cool is a carefully crafted front, his ability to confront Lucifer without fear a hard-won control over his instincts, but, right now, he can see—those ears pinned back, the smile strained, the microphone stand crossed over the front of his body in a silent defense—that this demon is overly aware of the difference in their power, of their status.

It’s almost—heady—finally being respected by someone so adversarial, and he has to take another breath to calm the staccato of his heart, jaw unclenching.

“To heal you,” he finally gets out, and his mouth feels stuffed full of cotton somehow, like it’s both too dry and yet drowning in saliva, and he brushes the back of his hand over his lips to check for drool he knows isn’t there, wings tucking close as his rage finally lowers to a tolerable simmer.

The radio demon stares, uncomprehending.

Yeah, fucking get in line, bitch.

“I won’t have Charlie crying over you again when your ass croaks because of this.”

The tension bleeds.

Good one, Luci’. Finally got them on the same page. Fucking finally.

His phone is ringing off the hook, the door knob rattling, but he ignores it, motioning to his bed, chest still heaving around breaths too hard to be because of the minimal effort he exerted—just pure remnants of his unfettered rage that he had only just tempered. It’s—embarrassing, to have lost his cool so completely so easily over something that could have been just a simple talk. Truly the weeks of frustration had caught up to him, more than he had expected them to.

It’s embarrassing—so embarrassing.

Again—another one for the long, *long* list.

Eternity is truly too fucking long. He’s losing his mind.

He only moves to the door when the radio demon settles himself on the edge of the bed, staff disappearing with a wave of his hand, unlocking it and narrowly avoiding Charlie barreling in, placing a calm hand on her shoulder and urging her to stop, the crowd of onlookers huddled behind her not much of a help.

“Dad, what the hell—why did you grab Alastor? What happened?”

He falters.

It’s—should he say anything? Should he divulge details? It’s technically not his place to say—the dumbass had gone to too many lengths to hide this bullshit. And yet—he also doesn’t really feel like lying or protecting or excusing his arrogant actions. But then, again—

*Ugh*. He’s still being nice, isn’t he?

Fuck.

He misses when life was apathy and rubber ducks. That was nice. He wants to go back to that.

Minus the absence of Charlie. Her being back in his life was a treasure.

The rest of this, though?

He’s all set, thanks.

“Everything’s fine, hon, I just—needed to talk to Alastor for a moment, and he’s been a little hard to get ahold of.”

“But you were so *mad*—”

“Oh, you know, just a little theatrics to draw him out. That’s all,” he laughs, feeling awkward and ill-fitting in his suit under his daughter’s concerned gaze, shame and embarrassment a peculiar cocktail in his stomach that he is all too familiar with.

He hates it.

“Don’t you have a radio?” Angel snorts.

Husk smiles slow, syrupy amusement pulling at the edges of his face.

Lucifer’s eye twitches.

Of fucking course. Thanks so fucking much for the help, Husker. Much fucking appreciated.

He’s gonna put fucking demon catnip in his bottle the next time he catches the guy chugging away and take notes on every embarrassing fucking thing he says. That’ll show him.

“Well, I still have business to attend to, but rest assured, Charlie, there’s nothing wrong. Not at all! Just some talks, that’s it. Yup. Entirely it. Ha, ha, ha—okay, good talk, bye, now, love you—”

The door slams shut.

Shadowed tentacles squirm in the air for a moment before dissipating.

Being nice *sucks*.

The damage, when he finally gets to survey it after much, much, much too much exercising of patience he doesn’t possess, waiting for the demon to reveal it,  is far more extensive than he had thought it would be. On the surface, it hadn’t seemed like much, just a jagged scar across the sinner’s torso, but when the green, shadowed threads binding it shut were snipped by a careful claw, the skin sagged open, flayed, and the depth that he could see inside the sinner—

He debates briefly if his stomach is strong enough for this, the claw dipping further and slicing at another row of threads he hadn’t been able to see deeper inside, the open maw of the chest cavity facing him an agony to look at even more than it would have been to endure it, he’s sure.

Credit where credit’s due, he must reluctantly admit.

Keeping up appearances with this—getting into a fight with three overlords like this—

His mood would be piss poor, too.

He doesn’t speak as he steps forward, but the eyes on him are wary, static crackling through the air as blood drips, and he can see the pulsing of a heart that had been narrowly missed, the incredibly narrow chest having truly suffered through the most heaven sent stroke of luck to be in the state it’s in and yet still alive, the most vital of parts intact, and Lucifer wonders what deals Alastor has made in his life to have such blessed luck when it came to his survival because—he really should be dead.

The fact that this blow from Adam had failed to kill him is baffling. The fact that he had been walking around afterwards like nothing had happened even more so.

Angelic power was not something to be trifled with lightly—even less so for a demon.

It’s with hesitant eyes on him that he reaches forward with his hands and begins the arduous process of healing, wings flaring wide in concentration, horns emerging, tail lashing before he wraps it around to restrain it, eyes glowing. It’s a good thing that Alastor had undone his own magic binding his wounds because healing—especially a wound of this extent, especially on a sinner, no less—was no simple task, and even the remnants of magic not his own is enough to have his brow furrowing in irritation as he chases the vestiges of it away to let his own take root.

There’s so much, though, he thinks, even as he toes away what’s around the most prudent parts of the wound, power leeching into the very cells of the body, fighting through every stitch he makes, every binding of blood and viscera and tendon, against the foreign magic that feels so—aggressive? Caged? This wild, violent thing that claws at the flesh and tries to seep into and undo him as he works, like it’s actively working against Alastor getting healed, and that’s—that’s irritating? Confusing? It doesn’t make fucking sense.

Alastor is a quiet patient, for all of his reluctance to be one to begin with. He’s silent and calm, doesn’t wince despite the very clear agony this must be causing, and his smile shrinks into something smaller, still present but not as aggressive, ears pinned back being the only sign of his pain, eyelids lowered. He stares at Lucifer’s shoulder with a focus that would have made him flush in either anger or embarrassment in any other circumstance, obediently keeping his head out of the way without needing to be told.

He makes work easy.

Even if the magic inside of him is another matter altogether.

It takes *hours*—hours it shouldn’t have taken if that fucking vicious thing inside of him hadn’t been fighting him tooth and nail the whole way—and he is *beyond* exhausted at the end of it, not so much physically as he is mentally, the focus required of such intimate and delicate care taking all of the stores of energy he had in him for the day.

There had been gentle taps at the door during the course of the treatment, and yet he had ignored it all, incapable of stopping or even acknowledging the sounds because he knew that if he stopped, he’d lose all focus and have to leave his job incomplete. And, really, he didn’t much fancy his odds of getting the radio demon back in his clutches for something like this any time soon.

It was—a lot.

Alastor is quiet even as Lucifer finally stumbles away from him, just a small sway of a few inches before a tug of his tail halts him, legs juddering only to find that his tail had been wrapped tightly around Alastor’s leg, and yet he’s too tired to find embarrassment in it, instead just swaying back forward and to the side to collapse onto the mattress, tail loosening as his horns recede and his wings furl closed and his tail retreats.

Fuck, his eyes hurt.

A small crackle of static, amazingly muted for the demon’s usual showmanship, has his head tilting out of the folds of the duvet to peer with one eye at the demon still staring ahead at the wall. He can see those ears twitching from this angle, the slight sway of black tipped locks as the head bows forward, gloved hand rising to trace the line of the scar left behind, tender and inflamed and yet wholly healed, the corners of his mouth twitching spastically.

“You have my gratitude.”

“Yeah, mhm, thanks,” he groans, turning on the bed to lie on his back, staring at the demon long enough that the head finally turns to face him, exhaustion making his tongue lax and his manner subdued, the light of hell’s sun gone with the emergence of night, “what shit ass deal did you make that it fought me so hard the whole time?”

The static stops.

The room stills.

He *seriously* needs to stop talking to Alastor when he’s tired.

It’s getting embarrassing.

He considers briefly that he may have grievously overstepped.

He considers languidly that he’s also too fucking tired to care.

He considers that he really needs to put, like, a gag on himself that’s too complicated for him to get off when he’s exhausted to keep this from happening again.

He’s trying to get closer to the dude, not scare him away!

Anger doesn’t come, though, like he thought it might, his irritation having spiked preemptively, Alastor simply staring for a moment, fingers rubbing over the scar again as if in consideration before he’s looking away, pulling his shirt back over his shoulders, doing up the buttons one by one, seeming to find comfort in the task—much like his bartender and his glasses.

It’s fascinating, in Lucifer’s eyes, to find such comfort and joy in the mundane acts of life. He’s spent so much of his life dreading even the most basic aspects of the day to day—getting up, showering, brushing his teeth, getting dressed, walking to do work, sitting down, laying down, waking up at all—

To find comfort in the necessities is just—bizarre—to him. Fascinating. Enviable.

He wishes he could do that.

“A necessary one,” is what is finally spoken, the static filter absent for once, the voice soft and clear as day, the most vulnerable of admissions that he’s ever seen from the demon. It’s not so much a willingly put down wall as it is an exhaustion induce one—he can’t imagine how tired Alastor must be after enduring all that pain when he himself feels ready to sleep for the next week.

He can imagine it, he thinks, the kind of circumstances that might make a man like Alastor make such a disadvantageous deal.

And yet that terrifies him more.

For as subdued as he was right now, Lucifer knows.

He can tell.

Alastor is an animal—cornered and threatened—*afraid*—the most vicious of sinners put on a leash, locked in a cage, restrained and limited, left at such mercy that whatever held him rebelled viciously at him getting healed, left him open to an attack that Lucifer is now not so certain he would have fallen to otherwise—and his daughter was tied to that. She was tied to a being who had been so powerful as to, presumably, shake the foundations of the Pride Ring, and was now—muzzled, held back, sealed.

And there was nothing more dangerous than a cornered animal, prey or predator.

And when it’s a predator turned prey…

This—this is worse than he could have ever imagine. Truly.

Oh, Charlie. What has his daughter gotten herself into?

### End Notes

## Chapter 3: duck duck goose

### Summary

Alastor had done the very thing that Angel has spent too long, embarrassingly long, being too afraid to even properly fantasize about, gone ahead and fulfilled every stuttered, fracture dream he has ever had and just—just made it a reality. Just like that—like it was easy.

And then he fucking kept quiet about it.

What a dick.
