### Text

Lucifer wants to say that things between him and Alastor had changed after the whole healing session, but to say that would be a lie.

Between them? Nothing had changed.

It should have—*considering*—but it didn’t.

But for Lucifer? Everything had changed.

It shouldn’t have—*considering*—but it did.

And it scared him? It terrified him..

And it should have—*considering*—and it did.

And, really, it was the only part of this whole clusterfuck that made sense.

And it should have—*considering*—and it did.

---

He was running out of patience—out of patience and out of *time.*

He’d taken a gamble this time, taking on the V’s while injured, banking on them being too proud and mainly non-combative overlords, ruling with entertainment and favors rather than any actual demonic strength, looking for a quick jab they can take at his reputation to boost their own without costing them their carefully crafted images of control—so weak that they not just hadn’t but *couldn’t* challenge any of the presently seated overlords, their only viable chance being the only one who had been absent for years, Alastor himself. And it had paid off—it had gotten rid of them, for a time, and their survival was something he could frame as wanting them to experience the humiliation of being defeated and having to endure the after effects, enacting karma for their similar intentions with him.

As if he would let them humiliate him like that. Not *them*. Even at his weakest, at his most damned, he would rather perish himself than let *them* take him down.

It had another benefit, an additional upside—the angles he plays always plural—because getting them out of the way meant that their contracts, their territories, were up for grabs.

And on top of the drama of having sinners wreaking havoc in their areas, chipping away at their land in desperation to rise to status themselves and stop being canon fodder, it also left those under their contracts *open.*

Defeat for an overlord is *perilous*—both for reputation and for *power*.

And he needs that gap in time to test his theory, to see if there’s a way—the chance to discover his back door.

But the effort proves futile for the meanwhile—contracts already gone and overwritten by his own, a powerful demon with no territory of his to claim going through and taking up souls that had been previously owned, squashing hopes of freedom. Although, he supposes, that’s not exactly true anymore, is it? He has a territory now, for all intents and purposes—a home base, if he felt so inclined to frame it that way, at the hotel. Funny, how that works. He deliberately picks canon fodder, those demons at the bottom rungs who won’t be so easily identified, won’t be missed if they were to disappear, by the three overlords when they finish regenerating, those that take less power to override the contract on just to see, just to find out, if it’s possible—

Can a contract be undone by a being more powerful? Can it be overwritten?

Could someone like Charlie—someone much stronger than himself, even if she doesn’t use her strength frequently, if at all—overwrite his contract? Could she set him free?

He can only hope.

But, regardless, right now, he’s playing the waiting game, and, while normally a patient man, Alastor’s time is running out. Mortality is a fickle thing to play with, and his desperation mounts with every day that his chest burns, resisting his healing, glacial in his progress of trying to burn the holy presence out of himself.

It shouldn’t have happened.

It *shouldn’t* have *happened.*

The constraints of his deal are too extensive, too limiting. He hadn’t realized that his own defense would be an expense of it, a crucial thing he’d trade. When the deal had been crafted, it had seemed like a minimal detail—even if he couldn’t obviously defend, he could evade, dodge and weave and get out of the way. Defense was more than just a brittle shield, and he could adapt—he *could.* He was a strategist above all else, a careful man. There was no corner he couldn’t talk himself out of, no cell he couldn’t swindle the key to. So long as he had the gift of gab, he could extract himself out of any situation—even if it cost him a few things here and there. That had been how this deal had happened, too.

But he was thinking too small.

Never before had that kind of noose been around his neck—thinking too small—oh, the nonexistent humanity.

Relief had blinded him, and now—now he’s got a lynch mob coming after him because he hadn’t spared a thought that the threats would suddenly get bigger than just the sinners of the Pride Ring. He had thought of his safety in regards to hell, in regards to threats he *knows,* and neglected to think about those he doesn’t—about those with abilities too wide and powerful for him to simply sidestep out of the way of.

And his defense is gone.

And with the debate from heaven—a debate that would’ve been over already if they were going to cave, not that he’d say as much to Charlie—he knows it won’t be the last threat they face on this scale.

Which means he either has to extract himself from the deal or extract himself from the hotel—and leaving the hotel is not an option, less so now than it was before.

He should’ve known, should’ve realized, should’ve thought. This deal, this godforsaken deal, this soul he’s sold—a deal that forces him to protect interests that aren’t his own and ban him from taking ownership of himself—it had been on the exact scope the threats now are. He should’ve thought ahead, thought bigger, but panic—even *he* is not immune to its effects.

And his life—his ability to move and breathe and live to fight another day, find another way to reclaim what is his—is something he would choose over and over and *over* again, over anything else.

He can change nothing—*do* nothing—if he’s dead.

If the hotel had failed, if it hadn’t succeeded, if it had just remained an amusing little game that he could enjoy watching while hiding himself away behind a defense that was so perfect that no one would even see it as such, then everything would’ve been fine. It would’ve been grand. He would’ve had time, would’ve had patience—would have maybe even had the capability to find a home there, find comfort, find a family that he had never had the opportunity to know before since his mother.

He could have had a life—a real life—one unlike even his one on earth.

But the chessboard’s expanded, the stage lights coming on outside of the spotlight, and hell is no longer the only player he has to contend with despite his confinement to it. His pawns have to expand, his game has to expand, and, if he wants to come out of this alive, his power has to expand, too.

And he can’t *do* that while in a stranglehold that wants to see him struggle and suffer and endure a demise that has the potential to be worse than his one on earth.

How cruel.

He’d be almost proud if it wasn’t him being served on a platter.

He’s the frog that didn’t know he was being boiled alive until the water was already too hot to escape—the deer that didn’t see it was being hunted until after the shot was fired.

But he’s no quitter.

Unfortunately, though, he’s also no paragon of patience—not now—and waiting for the overlords to regenerate to see if a deal can be broken, if a deal overwritten after death can be maintained—if he just needs amass enough power to pull this off—can he get out, can he be free, can he be himself again—his *own*—no more kowtowing to a master he doesn’t respect or enjoy—

Waiting is hell.

And hell comes with complications.

He’s so focused on planting seeds, tests, experiments—keeping track of pawns so that he can kill them off if they’re failures, making sure they don’t blab when the time comes before he can check on them, making sure they’re a wide range of grunts of different power levels, just in case—he’s so focused that he loses sight of the hotel and it’s inhabitants for a moment—and now that’s strung with it’s own little trysts of unexpected twists.

He’s kept his eye on the goings on with the debate with Heaven, the curiosity of the affair—he can see how this will go down, and he’s not optimistic. There’s no actual path to redemption yet, as optimistic as Charlie is. It’s only his continued insistence that has kept the doors of the hotel shut all this time even though he’s certain there are sinners interested now, more than before. Because he knows that it would just be a provision of false hope.

Maybe going through his own desperate phase of hope that seems so tremulous and out of his grasps has made him more understanding to the plight of the lowly sinner.

*Ha*, what a scheme.

It is a cruel, cruel thing to hope in a time of desperation only to have it stripped away—even worse so when it comes from someone with the best intentions, with the kindest front, with the heartfelt words that are meant fully and wholly when spoken and yet end up being as reliable as a piece of tissue paper in the face of a hurricane.

What was it they said?

The road to hell is paved with good intentions?

He can see how Charlie has no idea of how true those words ring.

On top of the heaven-born issues, there’s the fallen-born issue—the king and ruler of hell himself.

Healing him was—a ploy. He knows this. He’s watching the board, seeing the piece move, and yet it makes no sense to him, what it could mean, what the intention is behind it, what is gained from healing him. Perhaps—Charlie’s peace of mind? No, that makes no sense. Had it been that, all Lucifer would have to do is just ensure that Alastor not die from the wound. If he died from something else, it wouldn’t be Lucifer’s concern, it wouldn’t be something he could have prevented. But, no—he had *healed* him, *insisted*  on it.

And then there came the fact that now he knows about the deal.

It makes him want to laugh and cry in equal breaths, the continuous levels within which this deal is determined to fuck him over in rapid succession. The healing wasn’t just glacial—it *wasn’t happening*. Even now, with angelic healing, with the wound closed, it still aches and smarts, still feels like it’s trying to tear itself apart from the inside. It’s the smallest of silver-linings that his magic is responding better inside of him now after the healing, but it means little when it’s being fought against in the same way Lucifer’s had been—to the point where it had drained the most powerful being in hell so completely.

How determined was this deal to leave him defenseless?

He was no lawyer, no connoisseur in legalese, but he had made enough contracts to work his way through them. To be unable to ‘defend his own interests over theirs’ had been an expected line, one he hadn’t debated strongly. After all, the whole point of the deal was to have a puppet—of course his own interests would be waylaid. If it was that or his life, defense seemed like a small sacrifice.

He hadn’t considered, though, the implications of the fact that his greatest interest was himself.

He had been played—on a greater scale than he had realized until he was faced with a situation where his greatest interest turned to himself, un-aligning itself from the contract holder’s desires for a moment, and he couldn’t defend it.

Until Adam, he had been doing the impossible, fending off executioners in a way no other overlord had ever managed before—a grand shield around the entire hotel and battleground, initiating a larger slaughter of executioners than any overlord could ever claim to have managed—that Lucifer himself can ever claimed to have managed.

And then Adam had broken the shield—a not entirely unexpected occurrence; it hadn’t frazzled him, hadn’t shaken his nerves. In the end, he was doing what was necessary, defending the hotel, defending their interests—he was *in line* with the *deal*.

Until Adam’s beam.

Until he had to defend, deflect, in interest of himself.

Then it had all gone wrong.

And it was that one second that had nearly cost him *everything*.

And then Lucifer had healed him. And then Lucifer had found out about the deal. And then Lucifer had gained *nothing*.

And he doesn’t understand.

What was the point? What was the angle? What was won from that interaction, from that insistence, from that initiation of contact?

He’s tired—plumb sick and tired—of playing a game he can’t see the whole board of, tired of being reduced to the same states he was in before in the roaring twenties when he wasn’t allowed on the board at all. He was tired, tired, achingly tired—and he refused to be a plaything to anyone anymore. He had played along for long enough.

For more than enough.

He sighed, eyes pressing shut.

Waiting was hell.

---

Valentino is in a *foul* mood by the time he gets back from wherever the fuck he and the other two V’s had fucked off to for a handful of weeks.

Word on the street was that the radio demon had done a broadcast in the wee hours of the morning the day the three disappeared and had killed the three for daring to try something at the hotel—although, really, Angel’s not sure how much he believes that. The biggest downside of radio is that it’s a live medium, and in the aftermath of *whatever* the fuck had happened, the power had gone down in Pentagram City, all electronics fritz-ing, no videos to back up or prove the story true, only whispers on the streets from those that had already been awake or woken in the morning early enough to catch the short broadcast.

It was truly too brief for most of hell to have a solid lock on the story, but the whispers were enough to fan the flames of fear in the denizens of hell that the infamous Radio Demon’s broadcasts would commence once more, that, once again, souls would be plucked off the streets and the foundation of the city shaken again as screams fill the air.

None of them had realized the true scope of mercy those seven years of absence had been—and even his short six month return of peace had been—until the possibility of a comeback had demons wishing Execution Day had actually been carried out in full because at least death at an angel’s hands was quick.

Spending his time in the hotel during the whopping month he ends up getting off due to the unexpected sabbatical those three took, he doesn’t hear much of what’s going on outside, instead spending his time chatting with the others or watching old movies in the lobby on the singular old television Alastor allows on the premises, enjoying the mundaneness of life far more than he should, only hearing the occasional broadcast scream during the odd hours of the evening or dawn.

He figures that, considering the very live, very public roasting session that had gone down between Vox and Alastor months ago, that any battle that may have gone down between the four would be widely known and recognized—and, moreover, something Alastor himself would be celebrating over. After all, there were no bodies or sign of a scuffle outside the hotel that day—he should know; he fucking lives there—and the demon had made no indication of what, if anything, had taken place at any point during the handful of moments Angel managed to catch sight of him—usually when he was on his way out (seriously, he was the only one absolutely thriving within the power outage while the rest of them tried to figure out how the fuck to entertain themselves in the absence of their phones and tech while Charlie and Vaggie worked tirelessly with heaven) or in the kitchen, cooking the occasional meal for all of them.

But, sadly, not all good things last, and the power comes back on around midday after a handful of weeks, and Angel’s phone began ringing incessantly with messages and calls and demands to get his ass down to work.

It had been nice while it lasted.

And Valentino? He’s in a mood, an absolutely horrendous fucking thing that makes just being in the same room as him intolerable, let alone in his direct sights—having fear licking a vicious line down his spine, leaving his nerves fried even as he tries to ground himself in his newfound independence and determination to not be this *thing’s* toy anymore.

He looks haggard even if fully put together, tapping at his glasses—a new pair—brow permanently furrowed, as if in latent pain rather than just plain old fury, and the set is *tense* as a consequence, hell-born and sinners alike treading as if on glass as they move quickly to do the jobs they’ve all gotten an unexpected month off from, every small mistake—from stumbling over a wire or taking too long to focus the camera—resulting in Valentino’s teeth gnashing furiously and his temper climbing.

Angel’s belongings are tossed to the wayside in his dressing room, forgotten in favor of just getting this scene done and over with as fast as possible so that he can *go*, escape that fucking look that’s promising murder if anyone steps a further toe out of line—and he hates Valentino, would happily fucking shoot his ass dead, but he’s also not suicidal and, really, after so long out of the studio, he doesn’t really want to start again with the abuse so soon. He would rather film than die, so film he does, smartly not looking over to where the moth-man sits in the producer’s chair, expression tight.

It’s only after Valentino leaves in a huff, the crew beginning to shut down the set, that the whispers start up, people checking finally charged phones desperately for what went down before the power got cut, what happened in the early morning that had caused such a vacuum in the power sphere for so long.

And, oh, shit, is it so worth it—

The videos are being taken down, left, right, and center, and yet they all clamor desperately to view the re-uploads before they can be taken down by a Vox that’s clearly tirelessly working to sanitize and control the narrative of a shit-show that the three had walked into, even if the conclusion isn’t shown, the camera dropped after Valentino’s takedown, and yet that’s all he needs to see to have him saving the video, screenshooting comments, trying to preserve as much evidence as he possibly can until he can get this video on a tape where Vox will never be able to touch it because, fuck, this is better than any sort of drug—a high unlike any other.

The V’s had been killed—slaughtered—and hell hadn’t even *known*, really, a delayed gratification unmatched by even the best of scenes, and the internet is *rolling* with laughter, amusement, schadenfreude for the suffering of the three up and comers. The last thing on the video is Alastor’s haunting voice and then a black tentacle, static erupting around the screen before it goes black with a crack.

It’s delicious.

And it’s also confusing as fuck, when he considers it, debating back to the month previous, trying to determine if he’s seen any hair or hide of the radio demon where he even looked smugly pleased of himself. Clearly he and Vox have some history—*clearly* it didn’t end well, if Vox is anything to go off of—and clearly Alastor isn’t a fan of the V’s, be it just for professional reasons or personal ones. He should be *gloating*—he’s never stayed his delight before.

So, what gives?

Velvette’s social media is flooded with questions asking about the fight faster than she can delete them, hell having already moved into making gifs and photosets, reaction images, and it’s gruesome and morbid, and Angel has never run back to the hotel so fast, desperate to hook up his phone to his computer and burn this whole thing onto a CD or tape or *something* because it is, plain and simple, pure gold.

Alastor had—he had done what Angel could barely bring himself to even dream of for years.

It’s—

His frantic energy slows, the transfer bar loading on his screen, taking his delirious joy with it, the wetness of the scene he just shot lingering on him, leaving him feeling dirty, used, and all too aware of the fact that despite all of this, despite the fact that Valentino had *died*, he was still under his thumb. There was—there was really no freedom for him, was there? Not unless—not unless he killed Valentino permanently…

Even the thought has him shivering and looking at the shadows in his room like he’ll find the overlord suddenly hiding there, just waiting for him to think of something so dangerous so that he can exact his revenge. He’s under contract—he can’t attack him. His soul would be forfeit then.

Would he—would he even be able to be redeemed if his soul wasn’t even his own?

Was there—would there *ever* be freedom for him?

He slumps.

He should know better than to question these things, but—hope is infectious. It’s leeched into his very being and, after Pentious, he can’t help but think, can’t help but hope, can’t help but *dream*, about a future where he’s—where’s he’s his own again, free.

He shakes his head as the computer beeps its success and he hurriedly removes the disc before it can become corrupted while Vox goes on his cleanse, gazing down at the little circle of pure gold that he’s holding. If this—if this is the closest he gets to that dream, then he supposes that’ll have to be good enough. He’s got a pretty good life in hell, after all, all things considered. There’s not that much to complain over now.

And—and maybe it can get better.

Before they didn’t even know if redemption was possible.

Who knows—maybe a claimed soul *could* be redeemed and restored ownership of their soul.

There’s so much that they don’t know about this whole thing.

He’ll—he’ll dare to dream, he thinks, leaving his room in a whirl, disk in hand, to go share it with Husk and literally *everyone* because it needed to be seen and relished and *enjoyed* by more than just him. Maybe Charlie’s blind optimism was contagious or something, but he—he wanted to dream. He wanted to live. He wanted to celebrate. The V’s had been knocked down a peg—*Valentino* had been knocked down a peg—and that was absolutely *everything*.

For some reason—and, really, he can’t imagine *why* he doesn’t consider this a possibility seeing as it’s a free hell and whatnot—he doesn’t expect to see Alastor in the lobby, reclined on the couch, expression frozen as his long fingers grasp a glass of bourbon, seeming unconcerned with the time, his free hand turning over a—what is that, a *duck*?—absentmindedly.

*Okay*, a little creepy, a little quirky, a little freaky—all very Alastor things, to be fair, but also just slightly off enough to be concerning.

The duck is certainly new.

He casts a glance to the bar desperately only to find it empty—shit—before turning his gaze back to to the frozen overlord.

Was he—was he okay?

Should he say something?

Fuck—okay, okay—this was fine, this was great. He owes the guy a huge thanks, anyway. He’s got pure cinematic *gold* in his hands because of him. He might as well vocalize his gratitude, right? Right.

Yup.

Any second now, Angel.

Get your *shit* together, Angel.

Fucking say something, Angel.

Fuck.

Okay—

“*Hey*, Al—”

Good start—

“Ah, just the man I was waiting for!”

“Motherfucker!”

The speed with which Alastor’s neck cracks and rectifies itself is always haunting, unsettling, downright fucking terrifying—everything about the guy is terrifying right now—but the smile is wide on his face, glass set aside on the table, looking—*entirely untouched*, actually, now that he looks closely. Are there even any lip stains on the rim? Was this weirdo just sitting with a fucking ducky and a glass of alcohol he had no intention of drinking?

What a fucking weirdo.

He means—he already knew this dude was off his rocker, but—this is next level. Wasting perfectly good alcohol? For shame.

“I would never,” Alastor dismisses with an easy wave, the ducky tossed in the air for a moment before being caught once again, fingers wrapping around it with more flair than is strictly necessary for a fucking *duck*.

Tickling the ivories? Maybe.

Mister rubber-duck-fuck goose, though? No.

Seriously, what the fuck.

“I heard your boss is back in town,” Alastor comments, eyelids lowering abruptly, long lashes fanning wide, interest in his gaze—for information rather than anything Angel has to offer—and he reclines into the cushions, gesturing with the duck—seriously, that’s going to drive him crazy—for Angel to take a seat, something he acquiesces to haltingly, disk spinning around his finger.

Was this Alastor’s angle? Whittle at him to find out how the V’s are doing? It makes sense, of course. They’d take precautions against his particular brand of skills, wouldn’t they? There would be no radios around them. They’d keep things hush-hush. And Alastor playing the angle of someone who has an inside source who also happens to hate one of them with a passion isn’t exactly unprecedented or unexpected. Even so, though—

He’s shocked.

Flabbergasted.

Just—

Wow.

Unbelievable.

“*Yeah*,” he drawls, leaning back against the cushions, spinning the disk around again before thinking he should probably stop risking damaging it considering what slice of absolute heaven’s on it. He wants to keep that video forever.

He refuses to deign the prying with more of an answer than that, though. He likes Al just fine, as much as he can like someone he barely fucking knows, but—the man’s heartlessness could know no bounds sometimes. It was—it kind of hurt. They’d gone through so much together already—they had thought he was *dead*—and yet… And then, knowing—knowing Husk has the same regrets as him, about selling his soul to someone powerful, someone who makes his life unbearable—it puts him on edge, beyond on edge.

For however much he can enjoy and tease Alastor, he finds himself brutally reminded consistently that, at the end of the day, Alastor was an overlord. People in power always seem to be cut from the same fucking cloth.

“How are you doing?”

What.

“What?”

Alastor’s brow lifts.

Angel just gapes.

“Well,” the duck twirls in his fingers, a singular long digit stroking at its—hat? Seriously, a fucking hat?—as he speaks, “I had to send dear ol’ Husker on an errand, and it occurred to me that you might, mm, need an ear?” Crimson eyes swivel to peer at him, duck raising to press its beak coyly to Alastor’s cheek, his expression subdued—as much as that wide smile can allow. “Until he’s returned, I’m more than willing, if you feel the need.”

“I—*huh?*”

Angel is hallucinating.

He has to be. It’s the only thing that makes sense. Maybe Val drugged him somehow, put some shit in the lube or something fucked up like that because what the fuck—what the *fuck—*

“Of course, I’m also more than willing to just sit here in silence. I’m not particularly fond of hearing such sordid details, but, well—I can imagine, first day back and all…”

“Um,” his voice comes out as a strangled squeak, and the duck looks like it’s mocking him, as if laughing at him for stealing its sound—and, seriously, he’s beefing with a fucking duck right now? In his own head? “It was fine?”

Today is a fever dream.

“Splendid!” The duck flies before being plucked out of the air gently, Alastor’s free hand picking the glass off from the side table and passing it over to Angel. “A welcome back gift on Husker’s behalf.”

He blinks.

“I had hoped it would be timed well for him to join you on your trip back, but it seems your session was shorter than previous ones. I hadn’t expected that. My apologies.”

“I,” he accepts the drink, peering at it, seeing it clear and untampered with, swirling evenly with not the slightest grain or ill hue, smell clear and soothing, as bourbon should be, “um, yeah—he, uh—he wasn’t in a good mood.”

Alastor’s grin spreads, and finally, *finally*, it feels like the man is gloating, gaze darting off to the side, humming softly. “No, I suppose he wouldn’t be, no.”

“Apparently he got iced for a month.”

“Interesting,” Alastor muses, lips pulling wider, expression dark. “You’ll have to tell me when your next shoot is; I’ll make sure to send Husker with you. I can’t imagine he’ll return to good spirits any time soon…” And the duck in his hands is at eye level with him now, his bright red eyes peering at it, and—

“Okay, seriously, what the fuck is this thing with the duck? Where did you even get a duck? *Why* do you even *have* a duck?”

Alastor blinks, turns his face from Angel to the duck and then back to Angel. And then back to the duck. “Oh, you mean this little thing?”

Angel stares.

Alastor blinks.

He tosses back the drink.

Doing this shit sober is ass.

“That *is* a duck, yeah.”

A laugh track plays, Alastor straightening his posture to twirl the yellow toy around, looking like a kid in a candy store, cradling it in his hands. “This, my dear friend, is not *just* a duck. It is a performer!” He taps the little top hat on the duck with more fondness than Angel has ever seen him exude, dragging his thumb over the little bowtie on the thing.

It looks—ridiculous.

And he’s not just saying that because he’s apparently decided that today’s the day that he beefs with inanimate objects as stupid as a fucking duck! It just looks ridiculous, okay? Actually ridiculous.

“I found this delightful little thing sitting in the hallway putting on a show!”

A what.

“Al—sweetie, baby—”

A sharp burst of static.

“—terrifying overlord, uh—did you, uh, pour yourself one too many drinks behind the bar or something waiting for me?”

Alastor blinks.

Angel frantically types one handed into his phone for anyone to get their ass over here to deal with this man.

“No, I don’t think so!”

“Mhm, mhm, and I ain’t saying y’ ain’t convincing, but—you did also just say that this, uh—“

“Duck.”

“—mhm, yeah, the duck—*rubber* duck—a fake, not alive, very much *inanimate*, duck,” he gives the man a pointed look to which he just gets a beaming smile for his troubles, “was putting on a performance. In the hallway.”

“It was positively delightful!”

For fuck’s sake.

*“Right*…”

“Although, I must admit, I haven’t managed to figure out how to have it repeat its little show, but you can bet your bottom dollar, I’ll make sure to show you once I do.”

“Mhm, mhm,” if anyone could fucking hurry up, please, “that sounds *great*, Al—a bona fide blast, just, uh—”

“Okay, I’m here! I’m here! What’s on fire? Where’s the problem? What’s wrong with Alastor? What do you mean he’s lost his marbles? Is everyone okay?”

Angel is out of his seat in a flash, running to where Charlie had barreled down the stairs, chattering a mile a minute, Lucifer hot on her heels, looking frazzled and worried and wielding a—is that a coat rack? The girl can summon a fucking trident, and she brought a *coat rack*?

Okay, clearly today’s on crack.

“Okay, great, you guys deal with him. I’ve done my bit. Actually, I’m technically a guest—this is above my pay grade. I shouldn’t have done shit.”

Alastor blinks from where he’s still sitting on the couch, rubber duck standing flat on his hand, staring at him soullessly—as inanimate objects do. Since they’re—y’know—obviously—not alive. *Alastor*.

Clearly this is a psychotic break.

It’s the only thing it can be. Clearly almost dying finally got to him and he’s cracking.

The dude’s always been weird, but this is—this is odd even for him.

Was he going to think the coat rack Charlie’s wielding is alive next? Would he think that it came down here to serenade him or something? Fucking hell—

“I assure you my mental faculties are all intact, thank you—”

“Is that a—“

“It’s a duck,” he sighs, scrubbing a hand over his face as both Charlie and Lucifer blink at him. Lucifer’s eyes are wide. “Smiles here thinks it’s alive—says it was ‘putting on a show’.”

Alastor sighs, lifting his head as he lowers his hand, leveling Angel with the most deadpan look his smiling face can manage, one of his ears twitching in what seems to be disdain. Or annoyance. Or anything except a, ‘thank you so much, Angel, for getting me the help I need,’ the asshole.

“It *was* putting on a show.”

“In the hallway?”

“Yes.”

“With no one else around?”

*“Yes.”*

“You see this shit?” He turns to Charlie, pointing accusingly with the three hands that aren’t holding his precious disk. “Fix this.”

“Um, actually,” Lucifer pipes up, looking sheepish, laugh coming out staccato and awkward as he tugs at the collar of his shirt. “He’s not—”

“Oh, shit! Alastor! Fire, *fire*—*dad*, fire!”

Angel whirls, staring at where the Radio Demon, one of the most feared overlords in hell, is sitting, staring, dumbfounded, on a burning couch, with a rubber duck in his hands, his coat on fire.

“*Oh* my god—Alastor! Oh shit, oh shit, oh shit,” Charlie shrieks, yanking off her coat and *fanning at the flames*—seriously, did this girl learn nothing from that time on Valentino’s set?—trying to get Alastor to stand up and get off the couch that is literally on *fire*—

And this *isn’t* a psychotic break?

Yeah, *sure*—and Angel is a virgin.

Big fucking laugh.

And, as if on cue, Alastor begins to cackle, delighted peals of laughter amidst the chaos while Lucifer stares in abject horror, Angel right fucking there with him, while Charlie makes everything *worse*, and Alastor is—Alastor is *laughing*. The whole fucking room is laughing, actually, laugh tracks spitting out of every radio in the building, his shadows where they flicker on the wall bouncing jovially with his mirth, his shoulders shaking and eyes scrunching, ears twitching as he downright giggles, clutching the rubber duck to his chest with all his might as he shakes.

“Alastor, this really isn’t funny right now, you’re on *fire*!” Charlie screams, her own coat now in blazes—which she smartly tosses aside only to set *another* couch on fire, and Alastor looks two seconds away from wetting himself, bowing back into the flames as he curls up and laughs and laughs and laughs—

Angel would just like to say that he most definitely fucking called it—

*“Dad!”*

“Huh? Oh, right—”

A burst of magic comes forth from where Lucifer stands, putting out the fire and restoring the room to order in barely a second, golden stars dotting the edges of his magic as it settles into the foundation of the hotel again, and all that’s left is Charlie heaving for breath in the aftermath of blind panic, and Alastor fucking *dying* on the couch.

“See what I mean?” He whispers conspiratorially to Lucifer who jumps, as if he’s caught off guard, face bright red, and—huh? Why is it red?—and then looks away, rubbing at his nose with a little sniff, clearing his throat when that doesn’t seem to be enough of an effort at nonchalance. “You good there, short stuff?”

“Wha—pft, *yeah*, totally, never better—*never better*, mhm, just, uh—um—where did you say you found that thing again, uh, *Al?”*

Alastor’s still fucking laughing, his previous cackling at least reduce to errant bursts of chuckles and giggles that leave him pressing his lips tight into a close lipped smile in an attempt to stave it off, and—

Actually, when he thinks about it, he doesn’t think he’s ever seen the guy laugh like that. Not that kind of genuine, open mouthed, can’t be tamed laughter that had him ignoring literally being *burned alive* in favor of just letting it all out—he’s never seen that.

He wishes he had been recording that.

That would’ve been almost equally as valuable as the disk still in his hand—which he then smartly tucks into his chest fluff, just in case Alastor decides to wreak any more havoc again.

“It was in the hallway, lying around on the floor, putting on a little show,” he laughs merrily, setting the duck on the table. Once it’s on the table, it does a little backflip and then lets out a burst of fire, then a little backflip, then a burst a fire, and it just keeps doing that, one after the other, until finally it stops and just sits. “Isn’t it delightful?”

“You—”

“Your Highness, you ain’t looking so hot…”

“Yeah, okay, thanks, Angel—you—uh—*Al*—”

Why does he keep saying his name like that? That’s weird as fuck. This whole thing is weird as fuck.

Charlie tosses him a look, and he’s grateful to see he’s not the only one confused as shit by—*whatever* this is—happening in front of them.

“—you—you like it?”

“You don’t?” Alastor laughs, picking up the duck, this time pointed away from himself, and with a small green spark, it starts its performance all over again in the palm of his hand, and Lucifer looks like he’s malfunctioning, stepping with all the grace of a fucking penguin on gasoline covered ice—seriously, it’s like he’s holding a rock between his asscheeks, what the fuck is that—closer to where Alastor’s seated, lips pursed into this tight, severely upside down look while his eyes are all wide and shining and—

“Oh, my god, is he going to cry,” he whispers, hisses, looking frantically over at Charlie who also looks back, equally as lost, scurrying over to him, her eyes almost as wide as her father’s. “Is your dad *seriously fucking crying—“*

“I don’t *know—”*

“—over a *duck*?”

*“I don’t know!”*

Okay, that’s it, he’s mature enough to admit it. He’s beefing with that duck. What the fuck is up with the duck? It flips and spits fire and what—*what*? All his jokes, and this is what gets this strawberry looking motherfucker to crack his facade? A fucking *duck*?

His literal *job* is to entertain—what the *fuck!*

“You—you know, I, uh—I made that.”

Alastor blinks, turning with the duck just as its spitting fire, smile bright, eyes glimmering in delight as Lucifer jumps back with a yelp, narrowly avoiding singeing his suit.

Alastor’s ears twitch.

The fucker’s clearly enjoying this.

“Is that so?”

“Yeah—uh, yeah, yeah, it is, uh, it is *so—um—”*

“I suppose you’ll be wanting it back, then?”

“*What?* No! No, no, no, no, no—ha, ha, ha! No, that’s—uh, you can—you can keep that.”

Alastor’s neck cracks.

“Do you not like it?”

His tone is ominous.

“No! No, I—I like it, it’s—it’s cool, it’s cute, I just—I meant, I—”

“Charlie,” he whispers, reaching over and clutching her shirt, eyes wide—in either horror or delight, he can’t tell—

“What?”

Lucifer inhales deeply, as if stealing himself, shoulders by his ears, before it escapes him in a rush with the words, “I’d *like* you to have it.”

*“I think your dad has a crush on Alastor.”*

*“What!”*

“*Oh*,” comes a low voice from behind them, and they both shriek, jumping away from the staircase where Husk is leaning on the railing, looking equally as shocked by their shrieking, wings fluttering irritably. “Geez, what’s with all the yelling…”

“How long have you been standing there,” Charlie cries, hands grasped over her heart.

Angel shares her sentiment.

“Since the fire?”

Damn.

“Wait—what, ‘*oh*’? What’s, ‘oh’?”

Husk blinks. And then grins, tilting his head away as his ears twitch. Yet another asshat having a grand ol’ time right now. No wonder those two ended up together. “Well, you know, everyone likes to talk to the bartender,” he singsongs, and Angel wants to smack him, glaring furiously while Charlie just gawks. Husk laughs. “Your dad’s been asking about him for weeks. Didn’t peg their little squabbles as a ‘having the hots’ thing, but, yeah, this makes sense.”

*“Really?”*

There are literal hearts coming off of Charlie’s head. With fucking glitter and shit. Geez.

It’s actually insane how big her heart is, now that he thinks about it. Wouldn’t most kids be—he doesn’t know—some sort of upset that their dad might be interested in someone *other* than their mom? Shouldn’t she be, like, reminding him of all the good memories with Lilith or something, and maybe encouraging her dad to find love *not* in a cannibalistic overlord? Or something?

Instead, she looks ready to throw them a wedding right this second.

And all because of a *fucking* *duck*.

He looks at the literal king of hell’s tail lashing from side to side behind him, beyond his control.

Fuck it. He needs a drink.

---

*Victory!*

His laugh is manic, delighted, a wild, unrestrained thing in the comforts of his own room, a success fueled exaltation of delight that feels better than anything else has in a long, long, seriously, depressingly long time—teeth gnashing as he bows forward, catching himself on the edge of his bed.

He did it.

He fucking did it.

He got that bitch to laugh.

He got him to laugh and he gave him a gift, a gift he fucking *likes*—and when was the last time someone *actually* liked anything he made? When was the last time that someone enjoyed and appreciated the small, unimpressive, wholly self-indulgent shit instead of just the big, the showy, the fucking monument to the gods shit? When was the last time he had felt like that—filled with *pride*, so hot that it felt like it would burn him from the inside out, molten delight through his veins, leaving him spastic and uncoordinated in the wake of it? When was the last time someone had looked so—so *overjoyed*—at the *simple*?

His heart lurches, and he grits his teeth around the unfamiliar feeling, nose scrunching.

It’s not important right now.

What’s important is that between the laughing and the gift and the whole healing a massive angelic injury thing, he’s got to be on some sort of a good side, and that’s two fucking steps down the right path, and victory has never tasted so sweet. It’s an addicting, heady thing, filling him up inside until it feels like there’s no room for any of the bad thoughts and feelings and reluctance to take up, and it is *glorious*.

*When* was the last time he felt like this?

He needs to stop focusing on that.

“Ha! Take that depression! Part fucking two, bitch,” he shouts at the empty room, eyes bright and crimson and snarling.

This was all going so much better than he expected—slow, but at least it was going *somewhere*—and somewhere was better than nowhere, and he really had thought it would go nowhere and he’d be a failure all his life, but, no! He succeeded!

He fucking did it!

Lilith was *so wrong* about him!

Look at him now, bitch!

*Ha!*

Okay, maybe no calling his ex-wife a bitch, even in his head. Lilith was great, mhm, mhm—she literally gave him Charlie which is, like, the greatest gift ever, and he should be eternally grateful and worship at her feet or whatever—not like she had feet anywhere near him for him to worship seeing as she fucked off to who knows where, who knows when—

But, *anyway*—

“Okay, okay—I just gotta keep this momentum going. Right, right, right—don’t lose that progress, Luci’. Lock it in.”

Yeah, lock it in. Lock it in *how*?

Fuck.

He’s healed the guy; he made him laugh. The fuck was the rest on Husk’s list? Power? Being pathetic? Something, something, general interests?

*Fuck*.

His head hurts.

He should’ve been paying better attention.

But okay, okay, he can do this. He can figure it out. General interests are out, right? He doesn’t know jack-shit about Alastor, so he can’t do fuck-all with that. But power—power he has in spades, not any he’s planning on sharing with that man, but—it’s *something*. And being pathetic?

He excels at that.

He, uh, *also* has that in spades.

Ha, ha, ha.

“Alright,” he grins, tail lashing as he tosses his hat to the side, shrugging off his coat. “Let’s play, Alastor.” His chuckle is dark. “The hunt’s only just beginning, *deer.*”

The shadows laugh.

### End Notes

hehehehehe alastor and lucifer's game is becoming a bit of a gossip source for everyone else lol (everyone, expecting them to kiss. meanwhile, luci and al, trying to one up each other.)

this chapter is literally on crack and it is, literally, the most lighthearted thing i've written for this whole story, and if the ideas i have floating around in my head come to fruition, this really might be the greatest level of jubilance we might get for a minute.

am at the whims of these lil dudes- so sorry!

hope you enjoyed!

let me know your thoughts in the comments!

## Chapter 4: yellow belly

### Summary

As far as bosses go, Alastor isn't the worst. Truthfully, he's probably one of the better overlords to be contracted to, the kind that doesn't make deals on a whim but because of actual goals, desires—the kind that wouldn't have been endeared to Husk's particular brand of accruing sinners at a table, spinning cards and fast hands.

Honestly, Alastor isn't the worst boss.

But that doesn't mean he's jonesing for the man to get some new employees.
