### Text

It takes a whole two weeks for Lucifer to realize that while he might be an all around very—uh, *quite*—pathetic person, he’s not the exact *flavor* of Alastor’s preferred pathetic specimens, a little tidbit given to him by Niffty who is apparently *embarrassed for him?* He seriously hadn’t realized he could reach new lows, but, lo and fucking behold, hell wasn’t deep enough and he got a pick axe and got to fucking digging.

Another one for the list—what a laugh.

How the fuck was there a specific flavor for being a pathetic loser? He had to be—what? Attempting to be better? But he was! And Alastor hated him regardless! But *no*—of course that wasn’t enough, no—he not only had to be trying, but he had to be *failing*. And, unfortunately, seeing as his greatest goal, focus, and thing he’s attempting towards is be close with his daughter, to heal their relationship, and he’s apparently *succeeding*—which, like, honestly, good for fucking him; it’s about time he fucking gets it right, thanks so much, he’s feeling quite proud—it means that his brand of pathetic-ness is just *not good enough* for Alastor.

Which is just—a weird thing to now be insecure about, but he’s fucking managing it now. He was clearly getting too full of himself—*really* needed that harsh drag into reality—thanks *so* *much*, Niffty—

Said no one *ever*.

“How do I,” he trails off, gaze drifting to the side, fingers grasping at each other in a desperate bid to not fiddle, lips pursing. He was making such progress on the Alastor front. Or, well, he had thought he was doing well. It had taken a month to get there, sure, but it was something! But at this rate—if every step was going to take him an entire fucking month to get through, he could very well be out of time by then. He had to hurry up, and yet he had no clue how the fuck to manage something like that.

“He sees right through you,” Niffty giggles, wide eye tracking a bug across the wall in delight. She’s holding a massive needle in her hands, practically bouncing, her smile almost as large as Alastor’s.

Their resemblance, in mannerism if not in looks, is actually astounding.

Why the fuck does he need *his* daughter if he’s clearly got one of his own?

Fucking weirdo.

“What—uh, what do you mean,” he coughs.

He can’t be—he can’t be *that* obvious, right? There’s no way. He subtler than that, he’s sure. He fucking lived with Lilith, was married to her—the literal queen of making him have to bend over backwards to try and find the right way to phrase if she could please just do one fucking thing for him, and, no, he’s not ordering her, it’s a request, they’re a couple, why the fuck is he doing everything, it is not his fault that they have a kid now and that this shit is hard and if she could just fucking help instead of seeing every request as a bid for subservience when he just wants to be a fucking team, that *bitch*, after *everything*—

No.

*No*.

He loved her.

He *loves* her.

He’s just—it was a stressful time.

And he’s not really known for handling stress all that well.

Case in point—

Niffty lunges at the bug, moving almost faster than his eyes can track, stabbing it straight through with a resounding cheer. She then turns with a very pointed look in her singular eye, and Lucifer’s shoulders slump.

Yeah, okay, maybe he’s obvious as fuck.

*Fuck*.

“If you want to be friends with him so badly, I can help!”

He sits up straight. “What—really? You’ll help me?”

She bounces, pulling a bag out of her skirt pocket and dropping the bug in, cooing at it softly. “Of course!” The bug gets tucked away. He does his best to not shudder and is resoundingly sure he’s failed. “He’s nice! Everyone should get to experience that!”

Nice.

Yup.

Mhm.

That’s—exactly how he’d describe Alastor.

Totally.

How’d she guess?

“Okay,” he beams, almost matching her energy with his own anxiety, pulling his shoulders back. “What do I have to do?”

Niffty, as it turns out, is absolutely fucking *unhinged*, and there is nothing in hell that terrifies Lucifer more than his own mind, but Niffty—Niffty comes at a close fucking fourth spot because what the actual *fuck*—

*“Just talk about your feelings!”*

*“I’m sorry, what?”*

*“He loves that!”*

“*Ha, yeah*, again, *what*?”

Yeah, so—absolutely the fuck not—not happening at all! Not in this lifetime—ha, ha, ha! No, sir! Talk about his feelings? To fucking Alastor? What a cosmic fucking joke. Life really thinks it’s a comedian, huh? That’s actually hilarious. Like, actively hilarious.

Who knew the cyclops had *jokes*?

Did she pick that up from the demon?

At least hers are better.

He’s laughing all the way to the kitchen, a smattering of giggles that he can’t quite swallow back, constantly wiping at the errant spray of spittle that comes from the unexpected little burst of joviality, when he catches whiff of the most enticing aroma emerging from past the door.

Making the kitchen a closed off section had seemed the most logical with the redesign of the hotel, especially if it was meant to grow in size. Eventually there would be a much bigger number of people to feed of various diets, and having the whole open concept was just weird—even if Charlie felt that it would bring everyone closer together and not have anyone isolated in the common areas.

No—that was—just *no*. He loves his daughter—very much—but, yeah, fuck no. Kitchen, separate—it’s the only that makes sense.

The only downside of it, though, was that with it having been built under the same guide as everything else—*bigger* and *better*—it was a very lonely space for their grand total of two—wait, no, *one*—guest and two employees. And then just the addition of the trio of him, Charlie, and—uh—Al—astor. Built with the intention of having it be modern and streamlined, made for the most ideal of conditions when cooking for a large group of people, it’s safe to say that he is absolutely shocked—veritably rendered mute—when he opens the door (and he doesn’t remember making it an actual door with a knob, had designed it as the standard swinging doors in most restaurants, to facilitate the easier movement of employees because that’s what makes the most fucking *sense, again*) and is transported into an entirely separate fucking era.

The walls are a comely yellow color, easy on the eyes and yet vivaciously, adding a life to the room that had been lost under steel appliances. The floor’s patterned tile, a white and black checkered design that forms flowers of black in tiles of white, matched with cream cabinets and wooden tabletops, lacquered and polished to an enviable shine, fridge old fashioned and teal, truly something out of a vintage magazine, and it’s just—

There are flowers hanging around the windows, little baskets of them, and more scattered around, a fruit bowl on a circular table by one of the multitude of windows that lets the rosy light of hell seep in, and it looks more like a kitchen that belongs in a house rather than in a hotel, but he finds he—he doesn’t hate it—doesn’t hate it even as he picks up on the tell-tale glowing edges of a certain interior design impaired deer’s magic at the upper corners of the walls, his light fixtures seeming to be the only thing the deer hadn’t taken issue with, although he had certainly change the bulbs to warmer tones.

What an asshole.

Speaking of assholes—

The smell in the kitchen is divine, mouthwateringly good, better even than the look of the place, a rich blend of spices and broth that has him salivating, and he had originally been coming here just for something to do, to grab an apple to keep his hands busy and to have a walk and see if *doing* something while in such a good mood would help him at all, but now that he is here, he *needs* to get himself some of whatever that is. He’s fairly certain he’ll die if he doesn’t. Absolutely certain, actually. Like, *so* positive.

The fact that angels don’t really need to eat is an entire aside—one he’s not going to acknowledge right now and will take to the grave on pain of death because, ha, no one needs to know that right now when he’s ready to beg for a bite like a man that’s been starved for millennia.

Alastor’s at the stove, stirring a pot of something—some sort of pot of pure bliss, humming softly to himself, static a familiar crackle around him, and yet the kitchen absent of music even though he can see one of those giant radios set up in the corner, capable of playing whatever kind of tune the demon may want to fill the space with and yet silent in favor of a soothing hum, gloveless hands working quickly and precisely, moving within the space like it’s second nature.

It’s—humbling.

“You can stop your gawking—your gaudy kitchen will be returned, so do cease your fretting. You’re ruining the atmosphere.”

“I—no—” he stumbles, feeling off-kilter as he drifts further into the room, door shutting behind him, and there’s a charm to this—to this room, to what Alastor has made it into, an attractiveness that the bar certainly lacked—although he can’t help but wonder now, as well, if maybe that had also been transplanted from another era, ill-fitting with the rest of the hotel but potentially charming if it had existed in its own place. The kitchen, after all, doesn’t match the hotel at all either, much too dated and colorfully schemed to be anywhere sort of close to the apple, royal red, grandeur design of the hotel.

But the door made it separate, its own space, and it—it fit.

It certainly suited Alastor.

“It’s lovely, actually,” he can’t help but breathe, giving up on trying to find a way to craft the sentence in a way that wouldn’t be such a blatant compliment—because that was just weird for them, despite their most recent interactions not being at all hostile—courtesy to his dire efforts, primarily, in drawing closer to the demon before him.

Even his kitchen at the palace pales in comparison, although maybe that’s because the kitchen was never really a *home* there. It was just a place for people to work to get stuff done for them because Lucifer didn’t have the time, and Lilith didn’t—

He swallows.

What is wrong with him today?

He needs to stop thinking of her.

Alastor’s tail—and he’s only noticing it’s out on display now—twitches—which is adorable in its own right—which is just an *objective* truth, really; he’s not weird for thinking it! It’d be even weirder to be aggressively against it! He has eyes after all, and anyone with eyes can tell that it’s actually fucking adorable as shit, small and fluffy and matching the ears so perfectly, especially with such a contrasting personality found in the owner of those extra appendages, and just—

He’s *not* weird for thinking it, not at *all*, watching the little tuft curiously for a moment, as he rounds closer to where the demon is, curiosity eating at him the same way the aroma wafting from the pot has his stomach determined to eat itself.

“What’re you making?”

Alastor’s eyes slip over to him, chin tucking down, and his forearms are bare for his eyes to peruse, gloves gone for the endeavor of cooking, and the ombre of his skin, the glittering of scars, the fine hair—

These are all details he was privy to before, when he healed the man—from his tail to the language of his ears to the scars and the tonal shifts of his skin—and yet it all felt new, seeing it outside of a context that had taken so much out of him. It was new and exciting and like the most base layer of vulnerability that made him think that maybe, perchance, they were getting closer, after all.

How joyous.

He’s certain Alastor heard him approaching after all—he hadn’t even turned when he addressed him; of course he knew he was coming. And yet hadn’t bothered to cover up. That—that had to mean something, right? Even if he was only just reaching the same level as the other hotel tenants that might have already been aware of—*all of this*—it had to mean his little plan was working and Alastor was falling into a sense of security with him or something. Which is *perfect* because then Alastor wouldn’t need Charlie for *whatever* because Lucifer could provide that and *more* with little problem. Which is—*exactly* what he wants! Perfect!

He was slowly but surely inserting himself as the one Alastor would want to turn to, and that—*that*—was success. Fuck yeah.

Good job, Luci’!

Luci—fer. *Lucifer*.

Fuck, has he been letting that nickname stick?

That’s awful—that’s disgusting—had Alastor even said it more than once? He can’t remember.

Fuck—what the fuck—

Whatever—*focus*.

“Oh, just some—” an abrupt growl cuts him off, and both of their eyes flick to Lucifer’s stomach, heat suffusing his face as embarrassment tricks another growl from the organ which is just plain unnecessary because everyone heard it the first time, thanks *very much*, and Alastor’s grin twitches like he wants to laugh but won’t, the humming continuing in the background even as he speaks, stirring the pot absentmindedly. “Would you care for some?”

His stomach answers for him, another little growl that has him pressing a palm to it like it’ll cease its determination to embarrass the fuck out of him because it’s not like it *needs fucking* *food*, the dramatic bitch, ignoring the redness on his face in favor of moving closer, peering at the pot curiously, breathing in the delicious aroma.

“Do you normally cook?”

Alastor shrugs, rummaging through the cupboards for two bowls, every bit the human man, not bothering with his magic to accomplish such a simple task—and, again, with the mundane acts, finding pleasure in the simple, carrying out basic tasks just for the sheer joy of it instead of just getting shit done and over with as fast as possible—he really can’t relate—placing them on the counter next to himself. It’s like watching him button his shirt again, hands moving through an entirely useless and unnecessary task and yet finding comfort in it—finding control in the basic.

He wonders if his inability to even do that is part of the reason he gets so easily riled.

He wonders if he should really be sparing a thought on any of that right now.

He needs to *focus*—he needs to *lock it in*.

It’s been two weeks, and he’s been slipping—is going to lose all the delicious progress the fantastical duck had gotten him, and he can’t *afford* that.

Charlie’s counting on him, after all.

“On occasion, when the mood strikes. Niffty takes care of most of the cooking here, after all—there’s no real need for me to dirty my hands, per se,” he chuckles, although Lucifer distinctly doubts that because the man hardly ever joins them for breakfast, preferring to dine in his room in the early morning, which means the guy is *clearly* in charge of his own meals and cooks quite frequently, unbeknownst to all of them. But he’s kind—a benevolent king, truly—and will allow him this one little white lie—because he’s *gracious* like that.

And also because he wants to eat whatever this is, and he’s fairly certain pissing Alastor off is a surefire way to make sure that never happens.

See, he can also be an opportunistic asshole when the mood strikes.

He’s multi-dimensional like that, ha, ha, ha.

“And yourself?”

He blinks.

He’s sure he’s drooling.

“Huh? And me, what?”

Alastor cocks an eyebrow at him even as he begins to fill the bowls, static a soothing bit of white noise as it fills the air, dyeing the humming into golden tones that are almost enough to make him drowsy if he were to let himself slip into a headspace like that—truly a slice of an alternate universe found in this kitchen where Alastor can just motion to the table with his head for Lucifer to get the message and obediently walk over, pulling out the chair across from his own as well and magic them some placemats and silverware with a small snap of his fingers.

An alternate universe, indeed—one that reads as painfully domestic—a bliss he feels is entirely alien to himself, embarrassed at the way he wriggles in his seat in excitement as the bowl is placed in front of him by a very dangerous busboy, eager to dig in.

When was the last time he had a home cooked meal that he hadn’t had to make for himself?

Hell, when was the last time he cooked for himself, period?

He thinks the last time he stepped foot in the kitchen—it had been when Charlie still lived at home, when she was going through her, uh, *emotional* days and sometimes the best way to bridge the gap between a faux-depressed child and a fully depressed adult was to have a good, simple meal made from the heart.

Even when she was going through those darker days, she was still his sweet girl.

“Do you also cook,” Alastor asks, laying out a cloth napkin on his lap politely, the picture of high society despite the entire lack of an audience to impress, and Lucifer mimics the action, figuring it wouldn’t do to disrespect the chef.

He scoops up some food—and it smells like comfort, spicy and steaming and warm—and shakes his head. “Not really. I, uh—I can’t say I’m the most talented when it comes to, uh, homely affairs.”

Alastor hums, taking a bite of his food, and Lucifer follows suit, eyes rolling as the taste hits his tongue, perfectly seasoned, well-spiced, balanced and so, so, *so fucking good*—a kick right out of hell, virtually heaven sent (if heaven wasn’t full of such pieces of shit)—and he has to cover his mouth to keep from shouting from the rooftops, or to Alastor in the seat across from him, how fucking amazing this is, leaning back with a satisfaction from just one singular bite that had been unmatched by anything else he had ever consumed in hell *or* heaven.

Fuck, seriously, when was the last time he had something home cooked? *Besides* his coffee.

“Enjoying yourself?”

“Fuck, yeah,” he breathes, eyes shining, and Alastor’s smile widens just slightly, tips of his ears twitching, but doesn’t respond otherwise, seeming pleased to just pick up his humming again as he eats, prim and proper, dabbing at the corners of his lips between bites. It’s almost as distracting as the food. “This is—this is really good. Like, *actually*—wow,” so much for not singing praises, “how did you learn how to do this?”

Alastor’s eyes are heavy lidded, spoon swirling around in the contents, grin growing softer for the briefest of moments—“My mother taught me.”—something like pride oozing into his voice, and, for a moment, for a sickening moment, Lucifer feels like a failure, looking at him.

Just—*look at him*—look at the fondness, the clear love, the volume of emotion coming from a man who seemed so absent of them in just four little words because of something as simple as cooking, from the intimacy of spending time with someone, learning a new skill, making mistakes together, developing a preference and style and growing closer to someone who took time out of their day to bond with him, to teach him something new, something that they could do together. When had—

Had he *ever* done that with Charlie?

*Fuck*.

He can’t remember.

Had—had Lilith?

“If you were not the one cooking, did your wife, perhaps?”

The question dispels his angst almost as soon as it builds, moment broken, caught off-guard by the sudden image of Lilith in an apron, acting in any sort of way the role of a doting mother, laughing abruptly, eyes scrunching around the mirth such a ridiculous picture incites—Lilith performing any bit of the act Alastor just put on when he walked in, daring to serve him, *serve* him—*ha!* “What, Lili’? Fuck, no! I don’t think I could get her to want to even change a diaper to save my life. She was—“ his smile slips, fracturing at the edges, a sorrow he never quite got over digging into his ribs, painful, “she was very independent. Some things were just too, uh, beneath her, I guess. Or maybe it was just when I asked.”

Alastor blinks.

He didn’t mean to sound that deprecating—fuck—

“I didn’t figure being a wife or mother to be something beneath anyone,” he comments, and it’s not malicious, not even laced with the intent to hurt on any level, and yet he feels his hackles raise all the same, the desire to defend Lilith against an impression he accidentally incited himself, especially when she’s not in the room to do it herself—his first wife, his first love—of course he’s going to—

Alastor takes another bite of his food.

He’s still humming.

Did Charlie even know how to cook?

The fire goes out under a splash of cold water, shoulders slumping, staring at the meal in front of him, mouth dry despite the mouthwatering scent.

If he—if he was a bad father for never taking the time to do all of that, then—then what was Lilith?

He’d rather not think about it.

They can’t have *both* failed Charlie. That was just—that was unacceptable. That didn’t make sense. Lili’—Lili’ *loved* Charlie, always kept her close, barely let him have time with her when she was around while they were together—together but separated—

They can’t both have failed.

He’s not sure what he’d do with himself if they had.

“What is—*oh!* Hi, Al! Dad! *Together*! How *exciting*!”

And it’s Charlie, beautiful, magical, wonderful Charlie, coming through the door, closing it behind her with a soft little click, practically bouncing over to them, ponytail flopping. She’s so cute—his greatest treasure—his everything. How had he failed her so completely? How had he *let* himself fail her so completely?

Had he—had he made the wrong choice letting Lilith take her away?

Had he—had he taken the easy way out?

Had he left her to suffer?

Bile sours the spices on his tongue, and he feels like gagging.

“What are you guys up to—*ooh*, Alastor, did you cook?”

“I did, indeed,” Alastor laughs, and he’s quick to rise and pull out a chair for her, urging her to sit tight while he grabs her a bowl, and Lucifer’s throat feels tight, some sort of emotion, some weird strangling thing, suffocating him, some ball pressing on everything vitality in his neck as he struggles to draw breath. How was—how was Alastor so *good* at this, so good at taking his place, taking Lilith’s place—*how*—*why—*

“What were you guys talking about,” Charlie sings, nudging him aggressively with her elbow, the widest grin on her face, looking at him with those big, expressive eyes, and they’re just—there’s no real misconstruing the love and warmth in them, the excitement, the happiness. He can’t doubt that she loves him even if he wanted to—can’t doubt that any failing he’s ever committed has been forgiven as easily as breathing. He can see it plain as day, and it—it makes it slightly easier to breathe, no matter how unworthy he feels of it, drawing in a breath slowly as his shoulders lower from their hunched position, letting the smell of the food waft in again, reigniting the childish draw he had felt at the idea of a good meal, stomach rumbling once more, mouth watering.

She is his everything—the one unequivocal *good thing* he’s ever done in his life.

How did she turn out so perfect?

“Dad?”

“Oh, just your mother, dear,” Alastor brushes off as he places a bowl in front of Charlie as Lucifer hurries to manifest a placemat and set of utensils for her in the time it takes for the bowl to make contact with the fabric—and he’s certain he sees Alastor’s expression twitch at the brush of magic against his skin, drawing back quickly once the bowl is out of his hand, the same hand coming up to rub at his chest, over the wound, over the scar Lucifer knows is there, decorating the thin flesh, and he wonders if his magic lingers inside of him somehow—or if maybe it still hurts—

It wouldn’t still hurt, would it?

But with that magic inside of him, that deal—

Was it still bothering him?

Was it—had the healing not been enough?

*“Mom?”* And Charlie sounds almost—almost *offended* by the idea, brow furrowing even as she picks up her utensils to begin digging in. “Why are you guys talking about *mom*?”

Alastor returns to his seat, ever the showman, and it’s only now that Lucifer realizes the man had removed the apron, had probably removed it ages ago when he brought the bowls over and he just hadn’t noticed, settling the cloth napkin back on his lap. “Oh, I was just asking if you and your parents ever cooked together?”

“Oh,” Charlie blinks, spoon lodged into the side of her mouth for a moment until Alastor’s smile twitches and she smartly removes it, chewing and swallowing politely before continuing to speak. Lucifer’s convinced he’s entered another universe, still—truly the most alternate of alternate timelines, watching a fucking deer scold his daughter, princess of hell, without a word for lacking in her manners. What kind of trip is he fucking *on?* “You mean like you and your mom?”

“Quite.”

Charlie knew about that?

What the fuck?

Clearly he had underestimated how close these two are. He would have to reevaluate.

Unless—did everyone else know that about him?

But he had asked around and no one had ever said—

Were they holding out on him?

No, no, that made no sense—they all seemed genuinely confused, trying to recall information about the man and coming up empty. Vaggie had even been the one to put him on this case, too, so it wasn’t like she would’ve withheld information, so what—did no one know this except Charlie?

Or was it that no one except Charlie remembered?

His head hurts.

“Oh, no, mom didn’t cook. I think I saw her in the kitchen once in my whole life.” She says it so lightly, like it’s normal, like it isn’t something that Lucifer himself is turning over in his head obsessively, wondering if maybe they had fucked up on every front imaginable, starting with the most basic of basic things—“I cooked with dad whenever he was in the mood. It was fun! Although I don’t think either of us can keep up with you in the kitchen,” she laughs, and it’s this genuine little thing, followed by ensuing praises as she digs into her food with a ravenous appetite that is all Lucifer’s—Lilith had always eaten daintily with all the appetite of an angel despite being the original sinner, whereas Lucifer ate like a starved man despite the fact that he had no need for food—all but eating out of the bowl itself, face so close to it.

And he should scold her for that, especially since it seems like Alastor’s big on manners at the table, but—he’s also a little stuck on the fact that—that Charlie *remembers.* She *remembers*: the few times he had taken her into the kitchen to try their hands at cooking, the few times they had ended up with something entirely inedible and a new memory in their hearts, the few times they had made a mess and laughed and looked at the recipe and then at each other in complete confusion—because what the fuck did it mean, ‘fold it in’? How the fuck do they fold a liquid?—remembers the few times they spent time together, uninterrupted and free, and she—

She remembers it *fondly*.

And that’s really all he can fucking think of right now.

He’s feeling choked again, eyes shining, and when he looks over across the table, Alastor has one his eyebrows cocked, like he could read every thought that had been going through Lucifer’s head and was just saying, ’See? What are you worried about?’

His grip tightens around the spoon.

“Alastor, this is literally so fucking good—can you teach us how to do it?”

Not ‘me’—*us*.

He wants to cry.

“Name a time, my dear,” is the response, like it’s something simple, like he’s *willing* to take time out of his day to spend with them—not just her—*them*—and *oh*—

Oh, no.

“Do dig in before it gets cold,” Alastor sighs, sparing a final glance at Charlie’s less than upright image, like he’s giving Lucifer permission to do the same, and he obeys only because the food is exceptionally good, and, also, because it allows him to hide his face as realization makes a cruel home inside his heart—a different sort of strangling pain that he is all too familiar with, has a millennia of experience with.

Oh, no.

He has, he’s realizing, severely miscalculated.

---

“Look, uh—can we talk? *Privately?*” Angel cuts a nervous figure at the edge of the bar, gaze flicking around like he’s worried the walls have ears, hands rubbing together—like they’ve lost all their warmth. He looks cold, ashen, like he’s been dunked in a fresh dose of reality, and it’s not a state he’s used to see the man in, even before, his vulnerability always coming out hot and violent instead of whatever this cold, sweat-filled thing is.

Concern grips him faster than he can even take a breath in, nodding his head as he steps out from behind his station.

Truth is, Husk has made his peace, in a large part, with the state of his life right now, with the fact that he’s under the thumb of a creature far more menacing than he could have ever hoped to be back in his golden years—far more menacing than any of the other overlords at the time.

He remembers the first time he had seen Alastor—long before they had ever ended up on opposite sides of the table—a jovial figure at the bar, standing out alarmingly in a sea of creatures that were already pretty fucking odd looking and off-putting, and he remembers thinking the same as his ‘co-workers’.

*What a joke,* he had thought, too tired and old to give much of a fuck about a man who the shadows laughed at, a performer in every way that exhausted him.

It took him too long to realize that the shadows weren’t laughing *at* him, but *with* him, and by then he was desperate enough to beckon those eyes onto him, was already pulling out the seat across the table from him in invitation, cards fanning wide, letting glowing red eyes peer at him from under dark lashes, the very edges of his wide smile able to be seen over the edge of his cards, and Husk’s previous life, his status, was history.

What Alastor was, what he could do, the full scope of his powers than he couldn’t pretend to fully understand even if the little he knew was enough to have him shaking in his seat—it was like nothing he had ever seen before. In a fair game—fair on Alastor’s part, to his knowledge; he couldn’t say the same for himself—he had felt defeat settle on him like a blanket, comforting and lulling and beating away the desperation he should’ve been feeling because this was his soul he was bartering with—his own versus Alastor’s, a battle of wills, and, in the end, his quick hands had stayed and his soul had been gifted over, a seemingly small price to pay at the time when his luck had turned sour and his hand was getting nastier and nastier.

And here he is.

A servant.

A pet.

A thing to be ordered here and there at the whims of his boss—he ain’t calling that asshole ‘master’ *ever*, even in his head—regardless of his own desires, plans, or personal inclinations. Sure, as far as overlords go, Alastor could be on the better end—he was no scum like Valentino; he sure as fuck didn’t go around raping people or actively stalking them like Vox. But the tradeoff came that whenever Alastor’s call came, whenever he summoned them, the favors could range from something simple, something benign like, “Husker, my dear, I find myself bored—do a few tricks for me? I’ll provide refreshments,” which wasn’t *that* bad even if it was annoying, to something life threatening, like, “Ah, my good friend, so nice of you to join us; can you just hold them still? My patience is waning,” as he very casually eviscerates a soul right in front of his eyes, cold gaze and pinned up smile focused on Husker’s barely controlled expression of horror more than the poor soul he just wiped from existence, a silent reminder that—with a mere toe out of a line—Husk could be next.

Any leash, especially held by a man like Alastor, a manipulator, a planner, a fucking masterminding kind of asshole, was a constrictive one. But one where the deal could suddenly switch on him and render him dead, properly dead, no hope for survival, for regeneration, is far more terrifying than what any other overlord could possibly ask of him.

He had tried, once and *only* once, to get out—got his hand on one of Carmine’s weapons, debated asking for Alastor and just ending it all—but Alastor had taken one look at the blade embedded in the couch cushions behind him, Husker heaving, panicked, over him, terrified over the fact that he had dared miss at a time so crucial, or, rather, that Alastor had moved so fast at the last fucking second that he wouldn’t have been able to redirect even if he had wanted to—

One look, and the man’s face had turned pitying, red tipped gloves carding over the hair of his arm, brows furrowed and downturned in something like an apology, like he felt *bad* for Husk—not for being under contract, but for being stupid enough to try—and—

And nothing.

Nothing had come from it, just Alastor’s hand on his head as Husk had slumped into the cushions next to him, convinced his life was over, ready to die, drunk off his ass enough for his shoulders to shake through sobs even if his eyes stayed dry—fear and grief all-consuming—and Alastor hadn’t said a word, simply hummed along with whatever music filtered through the radio and scratched behind his ears, the angelic blade in his free hand, and that had been that.

Simple.

Because Alastor was simple.

He understood that, for some, it was greater punishment to stay alive in a situation like that than be dead, and he wanted Husk to live—be it for his own amusement or something else; he didn’t really know, but, in the end, it hadn’t mattered. It was a punishment all the same.

In the end, freedom wasn’t really an option, and it was just what he had to live with.

In a way, he supposes, he has no one to thank for his current mentality, his current stability, other than Alastor. If Alastor had turned that blade on him, or had put him on his broadcast, he’d have died a sad, depressed, waste of space drunk that couldn’t deal with the consequences of his own choices when he was on a losing streak. Now—now he’s got friends, a weird ragtag family, if he feels so inclined to frame them as such, and a job that isn’t threatening to get him killed at odd hours (battle with angels, notwithstanding)—and all those came in the absence of his previous power, his previous status, something he could have never found on the other side of table or at the bottom of a bottle.

As far as being in deep shit goes, being in Alastor’s type of shit wasn’t all that bad.

But that didn’t mean—

“*Why* would you make a *deal* with him?”

—he wanted it for anyone else.

Angel looks equally as frazzled by the events of the day that have led to him and Husk hiding in a room that’s far too pink for Husk’s tastes, but suits the spider just fine, pacing back and forth for long enough that a part of him is stupidly worried about the carpet even though it’s really not a top priority right now because—what the *fuck*?

Did all their talks mean nothing?

Did he think his soul changing hands meant freedom?

Did Husk somehow mislead him into thinking Alastor was the better, the safer bet out of the two overlords?

Was this—his fault, somehow?

“Look, I don’t—I don’t really understand how it happened. I just—we were filming, and then, out of nowhere, Val’s wearing a stage light for a hat, and I’m fucking falling into some room, I don’t know, and then, he, uh—he—*look*—”

“I’m looking, aren’t I? *Talk—faster*,” Husk grits out, tail lashing, and Angel looks properly angry for a moment, just for a moment, teeth baring before he collects himself, crossing his arms and looking away, pacing stopping in favor of holding himself with a rigidity that is wholly unlike him, lips pursing. Husk sighs, scrubbing a paw over his face, wings drooping, and it’s—it’s not Angel’s fault; none of this is. Alastor is—this is what he *does*. He doesn’t prey on the weak, that’s never been his thing, but offering them outs? Offering himself as some form of a safe haven compared to the other options out there? That’s his thing. That’s what he did to Husk. And now—“I’m—sorry.”

Angel’s own shoulders slump, dropping to sit heavily on the side of the bed, and, after a beat, Husk shuffles to join him, tail swishing to the side and out of the way just to flick irritably against the side of his leg, wings fluttering awkwardly to arrange themselves in the angle they’re shoved in.

Such a fucking complicated body, and for what? And for what? What fucking lesson is he supposed to learn from it? That he’s lazy? He knew that already—the fuck was the punishment of this apart from just irritating the fuck out of him every two seconds?

“Look, I—I heard you—when you said you regret being where you are, but—Smiles hates the work I do. And—he was just being petty. I don’t know what the fuck happened between those three, but dude’s got it out for them. If that means I get out from under Val’s thumb, then I—”

“You don’t know Alastor,” Husk sighs, and, really, that’s the problem. They don’t know Alastor—*he* barely knows Alastor. Alastor’s a fucking figment in all of their minds and all of their hearts, the kind of guy that twists everything to his advantage while leaving everyone trying to figure out if he’s even playing a game to begin with, and it’s fucking exhausting. Alastor isn’t *just* petty—he’s the kind that would wait *years* to get back at someone for trying to humiliate him, the type of guy that would wait *years* to come out on top, the type of guy that would wait *years* to get back at someone for trying to kill him by taking the first thing that seems to be developing some sort of a deeper meaning—

*Fuck*.

“I know how to take care of myself.”

“This ain’t about that,” Husk hisses, and then fucking hisses even more when his stupid fucking wings fluff up in irritation with him, tail lashing, and just—*ugh*—he *hates* this—“You think *I* can’t take care of myself? Or Niffty? Al doesn’t work like that.”

“Well—look, I was in a rush—he distracted Valentino only for a few minutes—not that long. If there was any chance he’d be able to override the deal, it would have to be then, when he wouldn’t notice, and I—”

“What did he ask for?”

Angel’s legs cross, fingers tapping against his knee, one hand raking through his fluff of hair, gnawing on his lower lip. He’s the picture of barely controlled anxiety, and Husk feels like he can relate, barely restrained himself, feeling ready to fucking run out of the room and yell for Alastor, demand an explanation, risks be damned. “For me to not be able to say anything if it didn’t work—he said he wasn’t sure if it would.”

That made sense.

Alastor’s power wheelhouse is a bizarre one—one that Husk had never seen in hell ever before or after. Alastor was a fucking anomaly. What he could do—eliminating souls without needing an angelic weapon, taking down overlords as easy as breathing, doing something as crazy as overriding a contract while the current holder is still *alive*—that was shit that sinners *couldn’t do*. And yet Alastor could.

Of course he fucking could.

“So, what—all you got out of this deal is your soul in his hands instead of Valentino’s?”

“I got *freedom*.”

“Al ain’t freedom,” he sighs, and he wishes he had a drink on him right now because this is really too fucking much to be dealing with sober. Although, really, probably not having anything on him is for the best, if he thinks about it. Drunk him might try to pull something stupid—like track the guy down and give getting free a second fucking chance even if he knows there’s no way in hell he’d win and that it would cost him a lot more now than it would have back then and—

The game keeps getting bigger, that fucking *asshole—*

Always ten steps ahead, the dick.

“Well—it’s a step closer than I was before! If deals can be overwritten—“

“They *can’t*,” and it comes out as this broken little shout, this exclamation he wasn’t expecting, and the silence that follows is tinged by his own shock, ears twitching, claws curling into his palms in self-consciousness. He’s not one to lose his cool—he’s not, but—“The things that Al can do—don’t conflate them with the other overlords. The only ones that might be able to do the same within this ring are the princess and her dad. And, in case you haven’t noticed, Lucifer himself can’t seem to get Charlie out of *her* deal—probably because it involves having to either *kill* Alastor or make a deal with his daughter to override it. Two things he’s not gonna fucking do even if he has full intentions of releasing her afterwards.”

“But, then, how—”

“The biggest mistake you can make here,” he sighs, and he thinks of a gloved hand in his hair, that pitying look, those cold, glowing eyes staring at him as a soul perishes, as if eyeing his next, “is pretending to know what Alastor can or is capable of doing. He plays his cards close to his chest. And not a single one of us even knows what game he’s even fucking playing.”

Angel’s breathing next to him is stuttered, an anxiety-riddled thing, hands fidgeting with each other until Husk finally reaches over and tentatively places his own on top of them to cease their picking, ears twitching when the hands grip his in turn, betraying the emotions his face keeps hidden.

“I asked him…”

No.

“What? What did you ask?”

“If he’d—if he’d let us out of the contracts if it’s a requirement for—for redemption…”

*No*.

“Husk—he said—he said that if that’s what’s standing in the way, he’d do it.”

No. No, no, no, no, *no*.

Alastor is no liar.

But Alastor is a player, a gamer, a well-versed conductor, and there is no freedom where he’s concerned. He’s *looked,* and it *doesn’t exist*. It doesn’t matter how many years Husk spends under Alastor, doesn’t matter how much freedom he has due to Alastor’s flights of fancy hardly ever having need of the souls under his claws, it doesn’t matter how well they can get along, how sometimes those stupid jokes *actually* make him laugh, or that sometimes Alastor puts on the songs that he knows Husk likes the best whenever his mood is low, or that Alastor has never asked him to do anything that didn’t have something in it for him—none of it matters at the end of the day because Alastor *is* the *game*—the embodiment of chess itself—and they’re all just pawns.

He wouldn’t be surprised if his own turning to the overlord for aid was something that the man had long seen coming—if maybe that was why his red cut figure always made an appearance in his casinos, frequently enough that he could never quite forget the man even as the years passed.

He should’ve known—should have fucking known—that his actions from long before would come back to bite him in the ass at some point. He should have *known.*

After all, there’s really nothing else this could be.

It’s not like Alastor was gonna find anyone with the power necessary to break his own leash, so this—this was just him playing a game, turning around and reminding Husk that he is *owned*, that his soul and everything it attaches to is, ultimately, *his*—and that his choices, as always, have consequences.

And if Alastor said that he’d give up their souls so that they have a fair shot at a redemption then—that means there’s something about redemption that he knows that they don’t, and maybe—maybe there’s no actual fucking way for sinners like Angel, like Husk, to be redeemed.

Maybe that’s the fucking punchline.

The greatest mistake he can make, after all, is to assume he ever truly knows the scope of Alastor’s information and connections.

“Does that—not count for something…”

And Angel—Angel’s gone ahead and started hoping. And, maybe, for a brief fucking moment, Husk had started hoping with him. But now—

He tries to comfort himself with the knowledge that at least Alastor doesn’t have all the cards in his hands right now, that as long as he’s on a leash, there’s still a chance that the game will play out in their favor, tries to comfort himself with the knowledge that even if Alastor is looking for a way out his deal, there’s no one in hell with the ability to get him free, not anyone they have access to in the Pride Ring, because—

*“Charlie…”*

*“What?”*

*Lucifer inhales deeply, as if stealing himself, shoulders by his ears, before it escapes him in a* *rush with the words, “I’d like you to have it.”*

*“I think your dad has a crush on Alastor.”*

No.

Oh, no, no, no—

Fuck—*no*—

A laugh escapes him, and it’s empty and brittle and the beats of it fracture in the air. He keeps seeing glowing eyes peering over the wall of cards, feeling sharp claws in his fur, keeps seeing that pitied furrow in his brow—keeps hearing the songs he loves on repeat in the shadows—

“Husk?”

He wants to cry.

“Nothing at all.”

---

Officially, out of the hit list of all the greatest mistakes Lucifer himself has ever made, giving humanity free will was at the top of it.

Of course, it was at the top for many reasons—so many fucking reasons, like an *embarrassing* amount of reasons—but the top reason *right now* is because it gave Alastor too much *fucking* wiggle room when it came to doing exactly what Lucifer wants and needs him to do. What did Alastor need all this free will for, anyway? Couldn’t he just fucking sit still for two goddamn seconds so that they can have a conversation and Lucifer can put Niffty’s grand little plan into motion and just get this show on the road?

Could that just please happen already?

He can only be pathetic for so long before he gets self-conscious about even that. He needs to get this fucking show on the road already.

“We need to talk.”

Holy shit—the fuck?

Is someone really up there listening to him right now?

Has he been bitching to someone unbeknownst to him?

Well, that’s just fucking embarrassing.

Add another one to the list, please and thanks! What’s he at now—over a thousand? It’s definitely over a thousand. How fucking funny, ha, ha, ha—*hilarious*.

The things they’ve heard—oh, boy; they must be traumatized. So sorry.

“Your highness?”

“Hm? What—oh! Yes! Sorry! Um, yeah, ahem,” he whirls, looking at the deer demon that has been occupying an embarrassing amount of his thoughts, reaching to tug on the lapels of his coat as if to manually pull himself together—only to remember that he took it off earlier, so now he just looks like an idiot with his hands up, grasping at nothing, stomach dropping for a moment at just the sheer intensity of his fucking plight right now, wondering idly what the fuck is wrong with him. He’s clearly on route to setting a fucking record for most embarrassments in eternity. Clearly his life is a fucking joke.

“Apologies for dropping in unannounced, but it seems to be rather tricky to find time away from prying ears,” the deer jovially declares, stepping further into the room with a twirl of his cane that disappears, shadows enclosing around Lucifer’s phone on the bedside table with a polite, “Just a precaution,” tossed his way in response to Lucifer’s raised eyebrow, the overlord at long last settling himself into one of the chairs by the windows, motioning for Lucifer to sit with a wave of his hand. He complies with the silent request after a moment, smoothing his hands down the front of his waist coat as he remembers what the fuck movement is, reclining in the available chair, crossing one leg over the other, adopting the same serious air Alastor holds around himself even though his smile remains in intact.

It seems his plans and embarrassment will have to go on the back burner for now if Alastor is approaching *him* for once.

“What did you need to discuss?”

“Charlie.”

His gaze narrows, fingers tapping a single irate row of beats against the armrest, defense already raised at the mere mention of his daughter’s name, but Alastor’s expression doesn’t change, fingers steepled in his lap.

“*What* about her, specifically?”

Alastor leans back, posture relaxed, like he’s intentionally trying to put Lucifer at ease, show he’s not there to cause harm, but all it does is put him more on edge, nostrils flaring around an irritated breath, muscle in his jaw flexing.

“Her and heaven, of course. This little redemption game.”

“Game?”

“Yes, Luci’, *game*.”

*Ha*, he did fucking say it again! Okay, maybe he’s not entirely crazy. That’s good, that’s good—really good news for him, actually. He feels marginally better now. Fuck, yeah!

He glares.

“You and I both know that’s what this is—don’t give me that look.”

“Aren’t you the one that’s been supporting her the most or something,” he drawls, dragging his gloves off finger by finger with a sharpened gaze that never leaves Alastor’s angled figure, casually leant against one of the arm rests, weight resting primarily on one hip, legs crossed at the ankle instead of at the knee like Lucifer’s, the very picture of a respectable gentleman—a gentleman’s ruse at subservience.

Lilith would sit like that, too—although it never came off as an attempt at subservient or demure. No—nothing about her was ever like that. He had loved that about her.

Had?

Had…*had?*

His brow twitches slightly in irritation, the corners of his eyes tightening just slightly, setting his gloves to the side on the armrest next to him—just for in case he has to strangle the demon in front of him. He wants to feel it, wants to feel every second of it as he squashes the life out of that thrashing body if it comes down to it. He won’t hesitate. Not when it comes to Charlie.

He can worry about the ‘had’ and Lilith later.

“You’ve certainly been making up for lost time—don’t sell yourself *short*.”

He glares.

Yeah, definitely will worry about Lilith later.

He’s got a deer to focus on right now.

“Because it’s not a *game*.”

Alastor leans forward now, just to wave his hand and summon a notebook, holding it out for Lucifer to take, his expression pensive and smile shrinking. “I never said it was a game for us,” he assures, reclining his chin on the palm of his hand, turning his face to the vision of hell outside the windows, bright eyes catching the light and seeming to almost glow—and yet it’s not from the glow of magic. No. It’s just the light, just a—just a trick of the light—and yet it’s distracting all the same, leaving him stalling for a moment before the look drifts to him, a little thing out of the corner of his eyes, and he jolts, turning his eyes to the notebook. “It *is*, however, a game to heaven, and you know this as well as I do.”

The writing is neat and cursive, a calligraphic masterpiece, and it takes a second of looking at it for the words to process in his brain, but once they do, he’s frowning, expression stripping of every remaining ounce of good will, eyes burning.

“What is this,” he whispers, hands shaking, but Alastor simply looks at him evenly, head tilting down, hair swaying with the motion, eyes half-lidded. “Alastor, *what* is this.”

The page flips.

“Since redemption is such a fickle thing, heaven will string Charlie along for as long as they can. They’re dragging their heels, and you *know it*.”

Now the demon leans forward, eyes bright, grin tremulous, and there’s something wild in his eyes, shadows spinning in his pupils, and he looks crazed, deranged, and yet that makes the words on the page even more damning, his shoulders shaking.

*“You know I’m right.”*

“*What* is this,” he roars, flipping the journal around to face Alastor, clawed fingers accusing, but Alastor’s eyes slam shut the second the book flips, shadows swirling at his feet.

“That book,” Alastor says, with finality, eyes persistently shut, a vulnerability that Lucifer could take advantage of right now if he wanted to—could just march over there and strangle him, could just try and force his eyes upon the page, could fucking shove his hand through the wound he had healed only but a couple of weeks ago and *tear*—he *could*—“is for your eyes only, if you please.”—if he wanted to.

Fuck.

*Fuck*.

“What does—what does this mean—why do you—”

Why does he know this?

*How* does he know this?

“Charlie needs your help, your Highness,” Alastor whispers, beseeches, and his gaze, when his eyes open, is a thin thing, focused squarely on his lap until he hears the book snap shut, flicking up to take in Lucifer’s bulging form, anger and confusion and betrayal a hazardous thing swirling around inside of him. “And, regrettably, my hands are *tied*.”

Motherfucker.

Mother*fucker*.

“What do you want?”

Alastor’s lips twitch.

“To cause the least amount of damage possible,” the man answers, and Lucifer doesn’t believe it for a *second,* and yet—embarrassingly—dangerously—he also does.

“*Really*,” he scoffs, turning away to drop the book onto his chair, falling into it with a huff, leveling Alastor with a flat look, the most collected he’s been in Alastor’s presence since the start of this. “*You*, not wanting to cause damage? You’ll have to forgive me if I don’t believe you.”

“Believe what you will, but I am no liar. This ceased being fun awhile ago. I’ve grown weary of it.” Alastor rises, ears flicking, arms clasping behind his back, moving towards the door. “I am not fond of being *used*. I’m sure you can relate.”

He freezes.

Alastor’s tone holds no malice, but the book burns a brand into his thigh all the same, horror sloshing hot and heavy in his guts, hands curling tight into fists. How did he know—*why* did he know—what was—

The truth burns against his thigh, and he doesn’t want to read it, doesn’t want to gift it with any level of consideration, doesn’t want to—doesn’t want—doesn’t *want*—

*“Charlie needs your help.”*

It doesn’t matter what he wants.

He needs to.

“Why,” he chokes out, fingers twitching around the spine of the notebook, and he doesn’t care if he looks like a mess right now—if a three second conversation had left him looking every bit like the used, depressed loser he very much is, feeling ruffled and unkempt, expression tired and tight around thousands of years of grief and secrets and lies—he doesn’t care, “why give me this?”

“You gave me a gift,” Alastor breathes out, a soft admission hanging in the air, reminiscent to that night a couple of weeks ago, exhaustion making lips loose. There’s no exhaustion right now—just a truth he has to debate with himself over whether or not he believes it—*wants* to believe. “I am offering to do the same.”

“This—this isn’t a *gift*, Alastor, this is—this is—”

“Read,” Alastor interrupts, glancing over his shoulder. “Then it’s your choice. Regardless,” the shadows grow, “*I am done with this game.”*

It’s the only time, the whole conversation, that his voice has held a shred of a threat, and he can’t help but swallow at the venom in it, the hatred, the *loathing*.

Alastor is an animal—cornered and threatened—*afraid*—muzzled, held back, *sealed*.

*“Charlie needs your help.”*

And there was nothing more dangerous than a cornered animal, prey or predator.

*“Charlie needs your help.”*

And the predator was clearly quite done being prey.

Fuck.

His phone clatters onto the desk as the shadows release it, the door shutting with a click, leaving him adrift with his own thoughts and a notebook within which was scribed the most heinous of accusations, and he hates himself for believing every single one, knees drawing closer as he clutches it tight against his chest and wills himself to get his shit together and just—do what has to be done. But, fuck—

*Had?*

*“Charlie needs your help.”*

Had.

*Fuck*.

### End Notes

and THAT, lads, is why we don't trust me to ever have a funny fic haHaHA fml am a walking joke
