## Chapter 5: blind opposition

### Summary

When facing an opponent that knows so much about you, it's only logical to start gathering information in turn. Right? Right. So, it's, really, only logical for him to make his way down to the television station for a little chat with the most obvious source of free information. Right? Right.

### Notes

yooooooo it's been ages

soooo how y'all been....

okok honestly, i am low-key struggling. like, the plot bunnies are bunny-ing, but, unfortunately, i'm also toying with an idea for ANOTHER fucking fic that i wanna write for these two (like, am obsessed with this idea even though idk if it'll ever see the light of day), and it's making keeping my thoughts straight for this one difficult. also, just, life happened. shit got busy and i had to check out for a bit and then take a breather afterwards. but i am back! again! with another chapter! even longer than the last!

so please enjoy!

### Text

Going to Vox seems like the only logical course of action, truly—this game with Alastor having become much too complicated, much too convoluted, much too motherfucking concerning for the minute information he has on the demon to be going off of. If Alastor is so well-versed in his own life, in his past,  in details so intimate he himself avoids thinking of them under pain of death—if he knows all of this, it’s only logical, only fair, to seek out the only person in hell that he knows *knew* Alastor with any sort of intimacy while simultaneously having no contract for his soul so he doesn’t have to worry about getting them in trouble—*and* knew him enough to hate him with the same passion of a scorned lover and the freedom, the desire, to *talk* about it—

The Lilith to Alastor’s Adam.

And seeing as Vox was butt-hurt enough to fucking air out his clearly hurt feelings, bruised ego, and intimately personal struggles to the entirety of hell to chime in on--which, uh, talk about embarrassing (which he was privy to seeing as he did find the video of that broadcast, thanks, Angel; it was wildly entertaining, even if it was taken on someone’s phone to be able to hear Alastor’s retort)—he was truly the best bet.

Purely logical.

Purely strategy.

Yes.

Pure.

Absolutely.

He shudders.

The tower of Vox Tech is a looming thing, grandiose and gaudy and fucking gruesome in its ode to wealth and technology, cameras trained on him from every angle—just going off the ones he can see—as yet another ode to the creator’s massive hard-on for watching, the creep. It’s an excessive display and yet truly befitting of someone who Alastor presumably used to do business with, a showmanship of power more than providing any sort of convenient use—an unspoken but heavy-handed, *’I see you’.*

He walks through the doors, cane tapping along the floor, every bit the king of hell with how he carries himself, no room for his true self to leak past because he has his hands full enough as is playing a game with one overlord. He doesn’t need to give incentive or invite curiosity and doubt in any more. He is every bit the fallen seraphim right now, every bit the lord of the damned, lip curling subconsciously in disgust at the sinners around him, and the secretaries are helpless to stop him as he marches past, intentions clear.

Not even Charlie’s success and boundless enthusiasm can quite cure the abhorrence he holds towards these people—the most ugly and brutal that humankind has to offer.

Just because he believes in his daughter doesn’t mean he believes in these stains against humanity.

Charlie was an exceptional and *good* young lady.

These sinners were exceptionally good—at getting on his last fucking nerve.

He doesn’t really *have* to walk through the building, truly—the act of it being more of a show of power than anything else when he could just easily make a portal and walk in on the dude taking a fucking shower if he wanted to (which he *didn’t*—because *ew,* gross, *bleh*—)—and the elevator doors open without him needing to press a single button, an acknowledgement of his presence from the owner of all this *stuff*, rocketing him up to the uppermost floor without a second of delay or pausing to onboard other passengers, the doors only opening at the penthouse floor—a cozier version of the over-compensation the outside of the building is and yet no subtler about its flaunting.

At least, in this, he can admit that Alastor has more taste.

He didn’t think anyone could be worse at interior design.

No wonder these two used to be friends.

Distractedly, he thinks of the homeyness of Alastor’s kitchen—remembers urging him to keep it like that, absolutely taken with this new, soothing, cohesive design that screams of a time he’s never lived himself and yet feels as imbued with it as its environment when he enters the room—thinks of Alastor’s face, bewildered as Charlie positively beamed where she hovered between them, stare eery in its lack of blinking for a solid five minutes straight while Lucifer progressively lost his confidence and Charlie became more and more concerned.

The fucker clearly just likes to see him sweat.

*Or* he was genuinely taken aback which is also just—

He doesn’t want to call it *sad* because that makes Alastor sound a little too *human*, and he’s supposed to be manipulating the guy—he doesn’t *want* to think of him as human, thanks very much—but—

It also is really the only word for it.

Of course, assuming it’s true.

And that’s a massive fucking assumption with Alastor.

It is infinitely easier to just assume he’s being toyed with.

Yeah—there’s comfort in that. It’s expected. It’s *Alastor*.

Whatever mushy soft feelings he’s deluding himself into having are decidedly *not* Alastor, and he needs to nip them in the bud before they can continue burgeoning as they have during the entirety of the past month that he’s been sitting on this newfound realization, this newfound perspective, this newfound notebook and the endeavor it’s led him on to be in this fucking room that he doesn’t want to be in, in a situation he doesn’t want to be in, having to talk to someone he doesn’t want to talk to, and all of that about the one man that has derailed his and his daughter’s life in a way he distinctly did not want him to and yet struggles to get mad at because he’s so fucking *good* with her, and he’s not, and *she’s* not, and *it’s* not—it’s—

It’s not a business meeting room, more of a personal living room, but there shouldn’t be a shred of doubt that this—this little excursion of his that he has a sneaking suspicion will be readily added to his long, long, long, long, *long* list of blunders in his even longer life—is purely business.

Even if he is going to be delving into personal slights.

Oops.

Oh, well. Needs must and all that.

He’s the king of hell, anyway. If they were expecting kindness and sensitivity from him when he’s literally stuck here because of them—watching them bastardize his dream and good intentions—they’ve got another thing coming. He has very little sympathy in his heart for them, and whatever he does have is thanks, solely and purely, to his daughter.

His sinners should truly be bowing down and praising her for any shred of respect he manages to dredge in the depths of himself for them.

This is why Lilith used to do all the hell stuff while Lucifer faded into the background. She had found common ground with them, common ire, a common enemy—hatred at those that had cast them down but allowed her ex-husband to remain, allowed the woman she was replaced with to remain by his side for a bit, have a family, when Lilith herself barely—

He swallows, scrubs the thoughts from his mind with a gritting of his teeth, pressing his tongue against the back of them, feeling the hard ridges and calming the racing of his heart.

He needs to focus, he reminds himself aggressively, stepping out of the elevator and wandering into the interior nightmare this man calls a living room—although he’s sure it’s tasteful to *someone,* such as the said man who *lives* there, unlike that bar that Alastor made which was tasteful to absolutely fucking no one, no matter if it would’ve fit somewhere else, and he’s fairly certain, would bet fucking money on it, that Alastor knew it, too, and had deliberately added something that fucking horrendous just for shits and giggles. It was seriously an offense on the senses in that hotel, and he will except zero criticism on that front, thank you, *Jesus*—holy, fuck, *focus—*

Why is he so *bad* at that?

Vox emerges from one of the rooms on the end, smile wide and welcoming, even if his posture screams dismissive, and Lucifer gives him a small smile in return even if he doesn’t really care to exchange pleasantries, leaning against his cane as the overlord approaches.

It shouldn’t surprised him at this point really, he thinks even as he feels a brief flare of irritation, that the guy is so fucking tall, roughly around Alastor’s height, yet another fucking sinner that towers for no good fucking reason. It shouldn’t surprise him at this point, and yet it does, and it’s just—it’s offensive—horrendous—tremendously abhorrent—absolutely *diabolic*—and someone should really fucking report this to the *authorities* because, seriously, this was getting out of hand—

Wait, shit, *he’s* the authorities.

*Fuck*.

Well, whatever, he’s on a hunt for information, not a friend.

He’ll live.

Well, survive.

Not one second spent in this abomination of a room can truly be called living.

He supposes that’s why one of his sinner’s designed it.

*Ha!*

That’s *hilarious.*

He should go into comedy. He’s certain people would laugh. Or he could—well—add it to his growing list of things Alastor should find him pathetic and pity him and offer his friendship for, which is, really, a win-win scenario, considering his current objective. Maybe he should look into it—

“Sire,” the television head drawls, arms spread wide, screen almost verging on too bright, the very edges of his pixels shaking around a myriad of colors, like he’s barely restraining his emotions—or is just too fucking close for polite conversation with a head made out of a fucking screen—one of the two—“To what do I owe the pleasure of this unannounced visit?”

“Unfortunately, not for niceties,” he sighs, faux-forlorn, eyes sharp, taking in the demon before him. He’s transparent—far more than Alastor is—smile twisting across his pixels, the corner twitching, eyebrows furrowing just a touch. *So transparent.* Like, seriously, it’s like looking through fucking glass—what the fuck was Alastor *on* when he was friends with this guy? Lucifer wasn’t pathetic enough, but this sorry excuse for a businessman was? Damn, way to add insult to injury, Al, *thanks*. “I want information.” He smirks. “You *will* give it to me.”

Vox’s smile becomes strained—again, *seriously,* Al?—screen glitching for a brief second as he releases distorted little laugh, as awkward and fake as all of Lucifer’s worst laughs combined that it has him cringing in embarrassment on both of their behalf. This transparent mess had been close to *Alastor*?

Again, *seriously?*

*What the actual* fuck?

Why the fuck was he struggling so hard for, then?

This should’ve been a piece of cake!

Unless this guy was the one that fucked that all up for him—what a *dick*. He’s struggling this hard because this guy was just *so bad* at being fucking *awkward as shit* that now Lucifer has to pay the price when all he wants to do is protect his *daughter?*

Again: what—a—*dick*.

“And after this we can go our own separate ways and I’ll indulge myself with forgetting you ever existed, uh,” his eyes survey the figure in front of him, brow twitching, “box."

“It’s Vox—”

“That’s nice—*anyway*—” He spins his cane, striding further into the room, settling himself on the single armchair next to the couch. It smells like smoke—fucking *gross*, ew, disgusting, he hates his sinners, ugh, what the fuck—“let’s chat, shall we?”

There’s sparks coming from teal claws as they curl into his palms, smile falling into practiced showmanship even as his eye buzzes around the lamest hypnosis show that Lucifer’s ever seen in his long years of life—truly these sinners have forgotten who he is; he’ll have to rectify that—but Vox walks over, heels clicking harder than they have any right to, begrudgingly sitting down.

Lucifer smirks.

“A man of sense,” he drawls, and with barely a twitch the chair under him is clean and smelling pleasantly of smoked applewood. “Wonderful.”

Vox’s teeth grit.

---

“My, my, what has you so down in the dumps?”

*“Alastor!”* Charlie jumps with all the grace of a toddler, heel slipping against the ground, elbow slamming into the table in an attempt to catch herself, chair knocking over and smacking into her knee in a painful thud that hurts as bad as it sounds, and hair getting caught against the corner of the table, pinched between her back and the wood that when she tries to straighten, it yanks her head back with a pinch, and she ends up in an ungainly heap on the floor.

So much for trying to catch herself.

Honestly, she shouldn’t have bothered.

Alastor’s smile is twitching when she peers up through the veil of her hair, rubbing at her knee with a wince, a sporadic little thing like he’s doing his damnedest to hold back the biggest laugh in an attempt to spare her dignity and is failing at it harder than if he had just let it out to begin with, lips pressing tight and then slicing open only for him to try and press them shut again—just to be sawed open once more, helpless against the mirth of schadenfreude in his eyes—

*Yeah*, definitely shouldn’t have bothered.

Her face burns in response, quickly scrambling up onto her feet in an even less graceful display, shoving her hair back over her shoulder even though one of the hair ties has snapped and her knee hurts and her elbow smarts and the chair is still on the floor, and—

She feels like a wreck.

She probably looks it, too.

She *really* should’ve just let herself fall the first time—like, *wow*, she could’ve been spared so much embarrassment, oh, geez—

“My apologies,” Alastor finally speaks, the inklings of laughter still coloring his voice, the overlord helpless to stop it. His eyes are glimmering around silent joy, and, even though it’s at her expense, the sight is enough to make her own lips twitch into a helpless, bashful sort of smile, raking her fingers through her already mussed hair self-consciously, unable to help the little sputter of an awkward laugh that comes out of her, scrunching her nose around the swell of embarrassment. “I hadn’t meant to startle you—*”*

She cocks an eyebrow.

“—*this* time.”

“Apology accepted,” she laughs, and she almost startles as he rights her chair for her and ushers her to sit down, hands on her shoulders, undoing the ties in her hair without a word, tutting under his breath softly about how she needs to look presentable even though she looked *fine* before he decided to give her an impromptu *heart attack*—thank you, Alastor.

Ugh, she sounds like her dad. They’re spending too much time together, clearly. That’s the exact kind of thing he would say. Wow. Embarrassing.

*Not* that her dad was embarrassing! No, no, he was great, the best, a top-notch guy, truly ten-out-of-ten. Just—she’s her own person and all that, and, really, she hadn’t realized how similar they are till now and, *wow*, truly the apple didn’t fall far from the tree—even though the apple was kept really far from the tree for a really long time because the tree didn’t want her—or *did* he want her? Did the apple even leave the tree? Was there even an apple at all? Or were they just branches of the same tree growing in the same direction with the same roots but different leaves, and—

She’s taking this metaphor too literally.

It can’t possibly be that important.

Yeah...

Yeah! She should just stop thinking about it. Perfect!

“So, my dear, what has you so out of sorts that you were lost in this lively little head of yours?”

*“Ugh,”* she can’t help but groan, head thunking down onto the table, only to jerk back up as the air shrieks for a moment, whatever work Alastor’s doing on her hair ruined, mumbling a soft apology under her breath as he begins again, gloves carding through her hair and leaving the briefest prickling of static as the fabric rubs. “It’s just—*ugh, heaven*.”

“Yes, I’ve gathered they do tend to elicit that reaction.”

She snorts.

“It’s just—” she huffs, anger prickling anew along with the defeat she had briefly forgotten. “They’re just not *listening*. They don’t want to hear what I have to say, and they just—they’re so *stuck* on the method that they’re not thinking about the *possibility*, and I’m just,” she trails off, shoulders slumping, looking at her notes in front of her, yet another plan she’s certain will get shutdown by the time the next meeting rolls around, “tired…” At this rate, she wouldn’t be shocked if they just suddenly decided to spring another extermination on them with no warning and are just dragging her along by the tie just for the fun of it.

Even if she still believes in her dream of redemption—and she does, so fiercely and wholly that her entire being aches with it—she can’t help but wonder if the politics of heaven are anything she wants to send her recently reformed people to deal with. What kind of crap would they have to put up with up there? Would they be allowed to enjoy their life in heaven? She knows that they’re probably watching Pentious like vultures just going off of how they’re acting in the meetings, circling above him and just waiting for him to mess up, waiting for him to step one slithery little scale out of line so that they can chuck him back down or—or worse—

Poor little Pentious, all alone up there, only his eggs and Emily for friends…

And Emily—Emily’s busy, as is.

It’s not fair.

None of this is fair!

If they would just *listen* to her—if they would just—just give this a *chance*—but—it’s been *months!* She’s been arguing on behalf of her people for a month, and heaven just *won’t* *help*. They had been so eager to slaughter them before, but now—

“Well, your father did mention that heaven is impossible.”

“But they’re not! There’s good people up there, too! There’s Emily!”

Alastor ties off the bottom of her hair, stepping away, and the heavy weight of a five strand plait thuds softly against her back, and she turns her head like she’ll be able to see his work like this, instead just managing to catch him as he moves to sit in the chair next to her, crossing one leg over the other and regarding her with the same sharp smile as always.

She wishes she could do that—face the world with an infallible smile. Maybe then this whole heaven thing would be *easy*—maybe they would listen to her if they saw that nothing they said could phase her and that she wasn’t going to back down or be frustrated into a corner.

“I just—ugh, how did Vaggie put up with them for so long? They’re just—they refuse to even give it a chance! It worked, and they’re still just—” she strangles empty air, teeth bared, imagining the entirety of heaven between her hands to just *crush* at will because there’s no fucking way that they could be this bloodthirsty, this obtuse—they proved that the exterminations were unnecessary! They *proved* it! Not just that a soul could change, not just that a soul could be redeemed, but that killing them for so long senselessly was *not needed*—and that there are good people down here, too, good people that they had killed mercilessly for so long.

“My dear,” Alastor begins, brow furrowed pensively, smile just marginally smaller, softer, “heaven is scared right now.” His hand rubs idly at his chest, red tipped claw tapping at the center of his bowtie. “They’ve just had a cruel wake up call that they are not invincible. They fear an uprising, and suddenly they’re not certain they’d survive it.”

“But we don’t even *want* an uprising—“

“Yes, I understand that, but do you think they’re thinking rationally right now?”

She blinks.

“It’s been months, though…”

“Months where they’ve been debating behind closed doors how to justify raising arms against hell.”

No.

No, there was just no way.

They wouldn’t.

Emily would’ve said.

She’d have told her.

She would have.

“No.”

Alastor’s eyes roll, expression pitying, leaning forward and resting his chin on the hand he’s got propped on his knee with his elbow. “My darling, do you believe that everyone is allowed into the room? How many were kept out of the loop the first time around?”

That can’t be.

They’ve shown that it works.

Is she—is she just not doing a good enough job? To show that they’re serious? That they mean well? That they just want a—a better afterlife for people who want to try to be *better*, to make a difference in themselves and be *more* than what they were in life, more than what they got the opportunity to be? They had sympathized with Angel, after all. They had—they had felt for him, a man trying to do better despite the world around him making it so impossibly hard. He moved their hearts—she *knows* it.

And Sir Pentious—he’s a *success*.

Do they know *how* they did it? No! But that’s—that can be figured out later! What they need is a hotel full of people that *want* to be better, that *can* be better, and then they can figure out how to ship them to heaven afterwards because what were they even going to figure out if they didn’t even have a suitable amount of subjects to figure it out *with*? It’s not like Niffty or Alastor were going to try and be redeemed any time soon, probably—although maybe Alastor would? With the whole thing going on with her dad?

Oh, but her dad’s in hell—confined to it. How would—how would that work?

Oh, dad…

Oh, no…

What would happen if Alastor ever got redeemed?

No. No, no, no—they’d figure it out. They would! Her father wouldn’t lose another partner—he deserved to be happy! Even if he—

No, but he was doing better now! So, it didn’t matter! The past didn’t matter! Not anymore!

“If—if that’s the case, how do I—how can I convince them that we’re not—that we aren’t a *threat*? We just want to—to have a chance, an opportunity—*time*—to be better.”

There’s a crackle as Alastor hums, head cocking to the side. “There’s only one way to cull a scared threat.”

She’ll focus on his choice of words later.

She’s a little busy being desperate right now.

“A—*how*?”

“Give them something bigger to be scared of.”

Well—that was—resoundingly unhelpful. “I don’t have anything to scare them with!” She’s at the end of her rope, the next meeting with heaven looming, and she’s got nothing—fucking *nothing*. And they won’t listen even when she does have something, so now that she’s out of material—they’ll laugh her out of the room, stop negotiations, close the doors and send the exterminators and it’ll be game over all over again and they’ll have to fight again and she might lose *everyone* this time around, and she doesn’t *want* that—she doesn’t—

“Patience is a virtue,” Alastor sings, head cocking, the tips of his ears twitching. “Give me time. Despite our lack of open doors, I am still the host of this hotel. Its success is my job as much as it is my entertainment.”

“Al.”

“Trust that I’ve been hard at work to find you your leverage, and I am proud to say that I almost have it. But, in the meantime, I need you to keep doing what you’ve been doing.”

“What—failing?”

“Heavens, no—stalling, my dear! Buy time for us to get the leverage we need to make them *listen*.”

“But—we’re not going to hurt anyone, right?”

Alastor’s brows fall flat, edges of his smile twitching, ears pressing back. “Charlotte—”

“Charlie.”

“*Charlotte.”*

He’s annoyed. She can tell. She’s intuitive like that.

Oops.

“While I know my more well-known wheelhouse is a little outside of the range of your preferences, I do, in fact, know how to, how do you say? Work the system?”

“Right, right. I—sorry. I’m just—” she tugs the end of the braid over her shoulder, eyeing the intricate work of what she can make out with awe. Alastor had done that—unprompted and for no reason other than wanting to. He had braided her hair—just *because*—just to be nice—just to help her feel better somehow. He’s—

Her cheeks are warm, her heart thudding in her chest, and she stokes a hand over the length of her hair and chews on her bottom lip, thinking that if anyone would reject redemption just for his own heart, it would be Alastor. He had always spoken louder with actions rather than words, despite being a radio host. It was why—why it was hard to not trust him, to not fall for his charms. He wasn’t just empty words, wasn’t just a happy face as Vaggie liked to say—he was *someone*, had once been human, had loved deeply and profoundly and made connections and *cared*—he had a heart, just like the rest of them. Regardless of what he had done or would do, he was still one of the gentlest souls she had come across in hell, and that was—

That counted for more than she knew how to put into words.

“I’m sorry. I’m just stressed.”

“Quite alright,” the man dismisses easily, ears perking right back up, smile back in full force, and she can’t help but smile back, his joviality, however manufactured, as contagious as her own optimism. Sometimes a smile is all it takes to make someone feel better, she thinks—just a smile and a steady presence.

“I’m—I’m actually a little surprised.”

His head tilts, eyes wide, that persistent gaze whenever he waits for her to continue to speak. It’s unsettling and yet reassuring—undivided attention in a way she feels she never really got until Vaggie. And from a male figure in her life? Even less. Not that her dad was—

They were better! They were better.

Her dad—he wasn’t anything like her mom kept saying he was. He seemed—*sad*. Sad and lonely, abandoned and left in disrepair. He seemed—to be grieving—although she’s not sure *what*. His dream? His family? Her? She doesn’t know. But—

“I thought you might be with my dad right now.”

—he seemed to zero in and focus when it came to Alastor in a way he didn’t anything else, come alive in a way he seemed to only do around her, almost adrift in their absence, quiet when not acknowledged, expression disinterested and eyes roving but unseeing. But with Alastor—

“Why would you think that,” he crackles, chin tucking down.

She can’t decide if he’s playing coy, or if he’s maybe embarrassed, or if it’s just him being irritated at being sussed out. Maybe—maybe the crush wasn’t mutual yet? But he certainly had a level of fondness for her father—he had to in order to indulge in a meal with him, right? Cooking was—it was important to Alastor; that much, she had deduced. Food was culture; food was family. There was no greater declaration for his affection for them (despite his lack of belief in the complete success of this place) than the fact that he made them food, more than once, just *because*—just to be *nice*, just to make them *feel better*—

“I don’t know, I just—you guys spend more time together recently, right? I just thought—since I hadn’t seen him around…”

“Your father went out on business today, to my knowledge,” Alastor responds with after too long of a time to think, looking off to the side, and—*oh*—does he—does he miss him? Does he—is he missing her dad? Was he also looking forward to spending time with him? Was this—was this *more*?

Oh.

*Oh*.

*Oh*—this was *wonderful!*

Oh, it really couldn’t be any better!

What else could that look mean, his ears twitching just a little against Alastor’s enviable control, smile close lipped for a moment, brow furrowed—it’s—

It’s so *obvious*.

How had she never noticed it until Angel pointed it out?

It had been months, and yet, after that first incident, nothing had been destroyed, no real arguments had burst out—they were—

Oh, wow.

How long had this been going on? How long had she been oblivious? Was he—were they—

Her curiosity is almost strangling.

“I’m sure he’ll be back soon,” she offers, an attempt at comforting, reaching over and placing her hand on the side of the table closest to Alastor in lieu of grabbing his hand, mindful of his boundaries—she had to be when he had taken the time to help her just now, not because he *had* to, but just because—just to be nice, just to make her feel better—and his brow cocks, gaze assessing the limb before flicking up to her, confusion evident in his expression, and oh—

Oh, my goodness.

Did Alastor not *know*?

Did he not realize the expression he was pulling, this heart aching thing that made her want to wrap him up in a hug and rub his back and tell him everything would be okay? Did he not realize that his affections were so obvious, gaze so longing, looking at a door and waiting for her father to walk through as if on cue at the mention of his name—“Speak of the devil and he will come.”—that whole thing—did he not *realize*? Did he not *know?*

Oh, wow.

Did that mean—

Did her dad know?

Did either of them?

Oh, no.

Oh, *dad*.

Okay—okay, no problem. She would help them. She could help them.

“I’m sure he will.”

She beams.

They desperately need it.

---

If he had to summarize his relationship with Alastor, it would be, quite simply put:

A fucking train-wreck.

He’s certain that it must’ve been the same from Alastor’s perspective—he’s *positive*. After all, it’s not like *he* cares—he’s fucking fine, totally over it, honestly quite happy to call it done and over with. He never liked that asshole *that* much. They had been—business partners—yeah, just business partners…

Business partners with a little something extra on the side between the sips of whiskey and long looks he’s certain didn’t mean anything, either at the time or now.

Yeah, they were just business partners.

And, really, that was all they needed to be because anything more than that would’ve just been ludicrous, tasteless, fucking hopeless and pathetic and probably the reason they broke up *anyway*—and they didn’t *break up* because there wasn’t anything to fucking break up *with* because he and Alastor were *nothing*—they were *nothing*—

Alastor was an overlord by the time he had died, by the time they crossed paths.

He was an eccentric, a character, polite and comedic, charming in every way that Vox found himself struggling to be, at times, too emotional behind closed doors for the political plays of hell. And Alastor—despite not being a man of politics, a free spirit in many ways—took to it like a duck to water, dismantling the previous regime on a fucking whim, taking their collective spots at the top, seamlessly adopting the role of overlord despite not being there for very long. He was *everything*—uh, everything Vox wanted to be upon getting to hell.

Power-wise, anyway.

The rest was, uh, not his style.

*Yeah*.

Absolutely not.

Not at all.

It was—too much—for him—too much, uh, old-timey, stuck in his ways, can’t move forward to save his fucking life even though the future is now, why the fuck did he insist on being caught in the past, a sanctimonious prude despite being in fucking *hell* of all places, incapable of proceeding forward and expanding into new markets and fucking opening the doors for them so that they could be in power *together*, rule together, fucking *own* hell together, the next generation of overlords, a system at their feet, if he’d just stop doing shit for the thrill of it, the enjoyment, the *passion*, like a fucking *artist* instead of the *business* that hell was, that fucking piece of shit radio shit-lord—*fuck*—

*Anyway*—

They were business partners.

And Al was cool, or whatever.

He seemed impressive at the time.

But that was ages ago, a bona fide has-been, a fucking snooze fest of a show to watch now, absolutely dull and boring and achingly dated—

Alastor was no longer cool, no longer impressive. He was a *was*. That was the best way to put him.

But, he can admit, that when they had met, first met, back when he was new to hell and his head gave him a crick in the neck and the world of television was still achingly weak and barely settling a foundation—Alastor was a *sight*.

And then it all ended in a horrible, terrible fight that he *totally* would have won if he hadn’t gotten fucking injured and needed to run, but that’s whatever, that’s the past, he’s totally over it, absolutely, ha, the end, story over, thanks for listening, *bye*—

Besides, he got to witness Alastor finally being forced to run, too, so it’s—it’s full circle. He’s moved past it—he *has*.

Except that despite being the most dated fucker to ever grace hell—and that’s saying something considering the fact that Zestial was still a fucking thing and that bitch was *old*—who the fuck knows why the guy is even still alive because there was no way Alastor’s comments about just actually liking the dude are anywhere *close* to true—no fucking way—that heartless bitch ditch deer can’t feel a single genuine emotion if it got on one knee in front of him and asked him to be his partner in power for the rest of their afterlife if he could just say yes, pretty please say yes, he picked out a nice ring for him, if he would, please, even though they have their differences, just say yes, please, say yes, say yes, say yes, say fucking yes, piece of shit fucking dated ass diarrhetic asshole, say *yes*—but *anyway*—

Despite being the most *dated* fucker in hell, Alastor was still—*is* still, begrudgingly so—*eye-catching*, show-stopping, a true *showman*.

He is a *dream*—a perfect vision of heaven in hell, a being so perfectly designed for hell that he thrives in it in a way that Vox can admit he still struggles to do—in a way the literal king of hell visibly struggles to do.

He doesn’t get beaten down by it, swallowed by it, morphed by it—no. Alastor is himself, same as he was back when they met, truly archaic, a walking fossil with the way he’s frozen in both being and time, unchanged despite the change of the century, despite the pull of one hundred years, despite a reign that he’s clung to for far longer than he’s had any right to with that backwards, weak ass ideology of seeking for joy and *passion*—of daring to say that Vox lacks it, that he’s more seduced by fishnets and bright sets and writhing bodies and the ability to stalk, search, *watch* every being and every *thing* in hell and past, from the sets to the clubs to the streets to the *homes*—

*“You are a voyeur, my darling. Everything about you comes from somewhere else, so much so* *that you twist yourself into a new shape every time there’s a new fad. I can’t be doing with* *that.”*

The fucker.

*“You lack passion.”*

The asshole.

*“You think you love what you do, but you don’t, my dear. You love the power.”*

The *dick*.

*“I can’t be doing with that.”*

The colossal fucking dick smear of a dog fucking, mother loving, horse shit shoveling, hoof stomping, pompous pompadour, deer sniffing mama’s boy piece of *shit*—daring to accuse *him* of not loving what he does when he’s willing to get *lost* in it, willing to *lose* himself in it, willing to *morph* with it, to keep *following* it because he fucking *loves* it—and yet that asshole couldn’t do the fucking same with *him?* Even though they were *partners*? Did they mean *nothing* to him? Were they a fucking *joke*?

And then he says—and then he says—

*Ugh*—he can’t *stand* the guy—

They were business partners.

Business partners who drank whiskey together sometimes and talked about a future neither of them saw and got into a fight that ended messy that he’s totally over, absolutely over, one hundred percent over, and—

And Alastor is eye-catching.

And he caught the eye of the king of hell.

Because, of fucking course, he did.

Why the hell wouldn’t he?

*Ugh.*

To be accosted in his own tower, in his own home, about that fucking prick is just—how unbelievable.

*Intolerable.*

Was that fucker seriously so important as to have Lucifer himself asking after him—to have Lucifer himself finally talking to his subjects after *years* of silence because he couldn’t be fucking bothered to be the king? And then he shows up, acting like he’s still the big boss of hell itself despite being fucking missing in action for *ages*, to fucking ask after Alastor? His least favorite person? In his own fucking home? *Daring* to act like he cares less about the fucking radio head than Vox himself?

Because no one cares less about Alastor than him.

*No one*.

He’s the president of the, ‘I don’t give a fuck about the prissy radio demon,’ club, and he takes his title very seriously—like, really, who *gives* a shit?

Apparently, the literal king of hell.

What the fuck?

How unfair.

The guy gets his ass handed to him, and now he’s got Lucifer on his couch, chasing away the remnants of Val and Velvette in the fabric with a flair of golden light that barely makes his nail twitch, asking about him? What in the fuck kind of fucked up fairy tale *bullshit* was that? Alastor didn’t—he didn’t deserve the attention—he didn’t deserve the respect, the precaution, the *acknowledgment*—he didn’t deserve to be *viewed*. He deserved to have his arm torn off, ripped from his body, the one with the finger that refused to don his proclamation of affection, left to bleed and cry and fucking clutch as his severed appendage, the image staying fresh in his mind even as he’d been forced to run away—he deserved to be *humiliated*, ignored, fucking relegated to the back fucking burner that no one uses so it remains pristine because it’s no one’s favorite, no one loves it—it’s inconvenient and pointless and very rarely does a house with *Alastor* in it fucking fill up enough to beg the need of that one lone burner—he deserves to be fucking *forgotten*—

Alastor is a *relic* of a time *long*, long passed.

They should fucking let him *stay* that way—just let him—just let him fade away, off into the background, be the fucking radio in the car that no one ever uses because the bluetooth paired to their phone is *so much* *better*—they should just let him *stay* a relic.

Let the archaic, eyesore, piece of shit be the has-been he was always meant to be.

And let Vox remind him what passion truly looks like.

That is what he would have advocated for, of course, if the king of hell would have given him a fucking say.

Oh, well.

Everyone gets burned by Alastor in the end, anyway.

Not him! *Ha*, of course not—not *him*!

But everyone else—yeah, everyone else—they all got burned.

Lucifer would only have to wait and see.

---

He wants to say that his little talk with Vox earlier in the day had helped him in any sort of way, but, really, if he’s being honest, it didn’t—it hadn’t. There was so much there that he—he didn’t know *how* to address, didn’t know if he *wanted* to address it. He had stumbled—unwittingly, unknowingly, entirely intentionally and yet without a proper understanding of the exact scope of what he’d actually end up hearing—into a veritable Pandora’s box of Alastor’s past, the television demon way too excited to try and slam his apparent ex—despite his vehement denials that they were ever, in any way, romantically involved, even though the evidence *clearly* suggested otherwise—to the king of hell, probably in an attempt to goad him into killing the demon for him. Not that Lucifer could be easily goaded—or even *needed* to be goaded.

He had gone in for information—tactical information—and ended up with a stomach turning level of knowledge into the private life of a man who knows—too much about him, *actually*, and yet—

It doesn’t make him feel like any less of an asshole to know Alastor’s a: “Prudish fucking thing, pretends he’s all chaste and pure, like, okay, how the fuck did you end up down here, then? Fucker never put out—*ever*. And, like, I don’t know if it was a power thing or what, but it was—oh, of course, this is all purely business. I didn’t mean put out like *put out*, you know? Ha, ha, ha, can you imagine? Ugh, I’m getting chills just thinking about it, ha, ha, ha…”

(The blush on his face is fooling absolutely no one.)

He ended up in a therapy session he hadn’t actually wanted to be in, expecting to get more hassle from the overlord than he ended up receiving, had gone in ready to stretch his wings and crack his knuckles against some glass, maybe set fire to a thing or two just for fun—only to spend most of the time mourning the decor, getting his ear talked off, and wondering if it would still be okay to break the dick head’s screen because he would be *livid* if Lilith ever talked like that about him to *Charlie*—if she ever dared to degrade him with such ease, with such pleasure, when he’s not in the room to defend himself, when he can do no more than suffer the repercussions of a hate driven tirade.

It didn’t matter that the person being bad mouthed was Alastor.

It was a dick move to just—*overshare*—to put it mildly—about someone to such a vulgar level, especially to someone so much higher up on the food chain than them, with the intent of souring the image they hold of them.

It’s not to say that he *likes* Alastor.

He doesn’t!

Not—not entirely, anyway.

He’s still very much aware of the sword hanging over his dear daughter’s head in the form of that vague ass deal, but—he can admit the guy is—*pleasant?* Okay to be around? Not totally terrible? Actually kind of charming if he squints and tilts his head and only half listens while imagining his head on a platter? Yeah—yeah, maybe.

He can cook, at least.

Which is nice.

He likes a good home cooked meal.

That was—that was, honestly, the best thing he had in—in a *while*.

But—it wasn’t like he was *compromised*.

No, no, no—not yet. He hadn’t gotten lured in like Vaggie, twisted into affections unbeknownst to him, seduced into caring for a demon too unpredictable, too volatile, to dare to cradle closely on any degree of fondness. He still had his eye on the prize! He needed to get Charlie out of her deal! See? He was *fine*. He had just—maybe he had just invested a little, just a little, too much into this endeavor. Maybe—maybe he was enjoying the game just a *touch* too much. But—but that was fine, too!

All he had to do was *extract* himself a little, put some distance between himself and Alastor, refocus in on his daughter—the daughter that Alastor was appallingly good at dealing with, warm affection and firmness blending together in a balance so perfect that he felt unworthy to witness it, knowing full well he and Lilith had resoundingly failed at that front—refocus on the deal—the deal that was open-ended, vague, and yet held a promise to not cause harm to anyone, and Alastor himself had admitted to not wanting to cause too much damage, so maybe he meant it, maybe the deal would be simple, maybe he was panicking over *nothing* and this deal would be *fine*—refocus on the *plan*—to get Alastor to turn to him for a favor, to be the one the deal gets struck with, to make Alastor *want* to choose him, to be *close to him*—

So why the fuck—*how* the fuck—did he end up being the one wanting to choose Alastor?

That made no fucking sense.

No, no, no—he wasn’t—he wasn’t cracking. He wasn’t *failing*.

He wouldn’t ever fail Charlie again—not while he’s still alive—he’ll never—he’ll *never*—

And yet—

Fuck.

Fuck, fuck, *fuck!*

What is he going to *do*?

The roof between his room and the radio tower is perfect for viewing hell with the outside air, feeling the breeze tickle his face, ruffle his hair, cool his clammy skin—and yet none of that feels enjoyable right now in the face of the burgeoning panic inside of him, in the face of this hollow emptiness that keeps getting bigger and bigger in his chest, swallowing everything in his path until all he can hear, all he can feel, pumping through his veins, burning against his skin like a brand is: failure, failure, *failure, failure, failure—*

Fuck.

How could he—

How *could* he—

Did he?

Did he really—

Fuck.

Fuck, fuck, fuck.

That was impossible. It was literally impossible.

He *loved* Lilith.

Over the years, the *years,* that she had cast him aside, taken Charlie and left, he had never wavered, never faltered in his affections, and yet—and yet—

Had he just been waiting? Biding his time for another, equally toxic option? Had he been deluded into a picture of loyalty when turns out he is nothing more than scum, turning his head and chasing the next pretty, spunky tail too out of his league despite the fact that it went against his best interests and those of his *daughter*? Is something actually, seriously *wrong* with him? Is he *ill?*

What the fuck, Luci’?

Lucifer!

His name is fucking *Lucifer*!

*Fuck!*

“Okay, it’s okay, it’s okay—you’re okay. You’re not—you’re fine, you’re fine, you’re *fine*—”

His hands are shaking and his eyes are wet and the game suddenly feels too big, too big, too big for him to handle even though—even though he’s literally the *king of hell* because his stupid heart had started swaying, and it’s—

It isn’t—it’s not like he *loves* Alastor. He barely *likes* the guy. But—

The problem is that he *likes* the guy—is enjoying what he’s seeing, is finding things in him that make him think that, oh, maybe—maybe he’s not so bad? Maybe he’s not so dangerous? Maybe he’s an okay fucking dude?

But then he remembers those low words, those dangerous eyes, that smile—the ease with which he turns, quick as a dime, searching for his best interests, comforting one second, making a deal the next—

He can’t be *trusted*, and yet he’s fucking *swaying*—

“Your highness?”

*“Fuck!”*

The spurt of fire is unexpected, wings flaring out in threat, whirling to take in the startled—fucking *startled*—figure the radio demon cuts against the background of his tower, horns sprouting and fire igniting before the blind rage and anxiety stalls his hand that lunges forward on instinct, reacting to a threat where there is none—there’s *none*—there’s no threat—Alastor’s not a threat, not a threat, not a threat—

Not because he’s nice because he’s *not*—he isn’t—but because there’s no one in hell that’s a threat to *him* to begin with.

He’s the fucking king, dammit!

He needs to act like it.

Fucking *focus!*

“I—hadn’t meant to startle you.” A wide eyed blink accompanies the words, ears pulled back, Alastor’s smile strained, pulled tight enough that the corners almost threaten to dip into a grimace, holding onto their upward curve through sheer force, and it’s insane—insane, baffling, fucking humiliating, how the tone, how the smile, how the hand that had instinctively moved the microphone in front of his body in defense and is now rubbing the knuckle of his thumb against his chest where Lucifer *knows* the scar is, Adam’s mark is—how it has his wings flopping in an instant, regret filling him up so rapidly that it leaves him dizzy with it, swaying on his feet, and oh—

Oh, no.

He’s—

He can’t be trusted, can he?

He can’t.

He didn’t think he could fall further, but—here he is—back again, with his pickaxe, and *digging*.

*Fuck*.

“Your daughter has been asking about you all day. I figured I would check on what’s keeping you.”

Shit, *Charlie*.

*Shit*.

How much lower can he sink?

How much further can he fall?

How much more of a failure can he be?

He sinks to his knees, pressing the heels of his palms to his eyes, and he doesn’t care—doesn’t care what kind of visage he cuts right now against the backdrop of hell’s horizon, wings wrapping around himself, fire burning out, horns scraping against his claws as he tries to stave off the tears because—it’s not like this is information that’s *new* to Alastor.

He has nothing left to hide, nothing left to shield from prying eyes—not in front of this demon—because he *knows*, he knows *everything*, and yet—

*Fuck*.

Fuck—how foolish can he get?

Didn’t he do this song and dance already?

Didn’t he hear this track before?

“I see,” the radio demon exhales into the air, voice absent of the filter that’s always over it, and his steps click against the floor as he walks closer, gait slow, like he’s worried Lucifer will lash out again—

Lucifer’s more worried about the fact that he’s pretty sure he *won’t*—

Before they pause beside him—his steps coming to a halt, only for him to lower himself down to sit next to him, legs crossing at the ankle once more, back leaning against the railing.

He won’t look. He refuses to look, refuses to deign him with even a glance or a glimpse of his puffy face, overly emotional and out of control, a thing to be pitied and doted and *abandoned* because it was just too much—

Reading the notebook had been a trip down memory lane he hadn’t wanted to take, had been a trip he had been avoiding taking because he—he wasn’t fit to raise a child. He knew that; Lilith knew that. It was why she had taken her and gone. It was why he had let her. It was why—

*“I think—I think dad was right—about me.”*

His head jerks up, eyes wild, searching for his daughter, but the rooftop remains empty outside of them, the breeze blowing cold against his heated cheeks, and he looks next to him, expression wild, to see Alastor with his eyes closed, crackling faintly, brow furrowed as different voices begin to filter through the air between them.

“Stop that.”

Alastor’s eyes open, flicking to him, the voices growing in volume.

“*Stop that*.”

The voices fall into a hush as the smile creaks.

“I could hear you wondering.”

“I wasn’t.”

“It was accurate?”

He sniffs, turning away, glaring at the ground past his feet as he draws his knees to his chest, wings fluttering agitatedly. “Do you always ask stupid questions?”

“I find it a good way to lighten the mood.”

“So you admit it was stupid.”

“I admit you need a distraction.”

He scoffs, tears falling quiet and unbidden.

*“I think—I think dad was right—about me.”*

Why did she—*why* did she—

*He ended up in a therapy session he hadn’t actually wanted to be in, expecting to get more* *hassle from the overlord—only to spend most of the time wondering if it would still be okay to* *break the dick head’s screen because he would be* livid *if Lilith ever talked like that about him* *to* Charlie—*if she ever dared to degrade him with such ease, with such pleasure, when he’s* *not in the room to defend himself, when he can do no more than suffer the repercussions of a* *hate driven tirade*.

He swallows around a sob he won’t let escape, no matter how much the deer is privy to, no matter how much of his personal life the man *knows*—however skewed.

Maybe—maybe he wasn’t as lost as he thought. Maybe he was—he was—what was it Charlie called it? Projecting? Fucking projecting onto Alastor and his little tryst with Vox. Maybe he felt a kinship in between them from this newfound realization that they’re the same—similar in a way he hadn’t know and hadn’t anticipated—

Maybe he wasn’t swayed at all—maybe he was just—sensitive to the similar plight being suffered by another—or feeling protective and wanting to seek retribution on behalf of the demon because it’s not like he could do it for himself in his situation.

Alastor didn’t have kids. Alastor didn’t need to fear the repercussions his actions would have on his family. Alastor *could* get revenge. Hell, *he* could get revenge for him. There would be no consequence—not for him, not for Alastor—none at all.

It would just be—*free*.

*“I think—I think dad was right—about me.”*

But *why—*

What was the purpose? What was the *point?*

“Charlotte wants to do a pizza night tonight,” Alastor brings up suddenly, and the mention of her has his lungs shaking. “She would like you to come.”

He bangs his head against his knees, ignoring the dull pain from where his horns hit, and grits his teeth hard around the air strangling him, the locked bars on a cage of emotion he won’t let out, no matter how much it wails against the iron grate.

It’s bad enough he’s already crying in front of the guy!

He highly doubts this man has cried a day in his life—with that smile always on his face. Is he even capable of crying? He’s probably the type that only sheds a tear when his eyes get dry—which seems to not be often enough seeing as the dude has a serious staring problem, like—he peeks out of his arms, glancing through his wings to see that wide eyed, crimson gaze still trained on him, not a blink in sight—a *serious* staring problem.

The eyes blink slowly, asynchronous, deliberate, like he knows what he’s thinking.

*“I think—I think dad was right—about me.”*

Shit, maybe he does.

“You read minds or some shit?”

The air crackles. “Oh, goodness, no! Can you imagine? Where would be the fun in that?”

Fun.

Right.

*Fun.*

Thanks, Alastor, for the much needed reminder of what an incredibly shitty person he is. Thanks so much. He needed that. He genuinely needed that.

His breath shudders.

He needs to get back on plan—back in the game.

It’s not over yet, if he could just *stop* his *sniveling*.

The game, he’s realizing, though, is getting too big for him to handle. It’s—it’s more than just his game with Alastor. *That* he could handle, no problem—no matter how compromised he becomes, his daughter will always be first. But *Lilith*—

“Can I ask about your—your time—on earth,” he breathes, weak and broken into his knees.

Alastor is nowhere close to his degree of undone, of course, just simply enjoying the night air while Lucifer falls to pieces next to him, shattered and broken and left with nothing to comfort himself except the presence of the one that did the shattering. How fucked was that? How fucked was this whole situation? His wife—the person he got sentenced to eternal damnation with, his co-conspirator—the very thing he needs to protect his daughter from after failing to do so for *years*, his daughter none the wiser, and the man his daughter has a deal with the only one that can possibly help him because he’s nowhere close to emotional strong enough to handle *any* of this, and it’s just—how *fucked* is all of that?

Alastor was right.

Fuck focusing.

He needs a distraction.

Alastor hums, soft jazz filling the air around them, a sigh drifting light over the breeze, and he hears the rustle of fabric as Alastor settles more comfortably onto the floor, hands folding in his lap.

“Hm, well, let’s see,” he begins, voice soft, and Lucifer peeks out of the barrier of his arms to catch the smallest glimpse of a soft smile and hooded eyes, expression intimately fond in a way he’s never seen before, a charm and longing for a past to never be beholden again. It’s a fractured thing, a vulnerable thing, more raw than Alastor has ever been around him, even when he had been literally cut open, heart bared quite literally, and yet this—this smile—it’s more revealing than any of that, a smile full of love in the way the literal vision of his heart had seemed absent of. “It was hot.”

New Orleans, Louisiana was hot—a wet heat that made even the most slightly warm of temperatures feel ten times worse, cotton the best friend when it came to clothes, dust a familiar beast to wrangle with, and the fever pitch of emotions from the balmy sun an every day bear to contend with. It was a haven, an amalgamation of a variety of cultures and experiences, the poverty-stricken and the mentally anguished—creative and sorrowful souls finding community within the rhapsody of a city that changed as it breathed, that lingered with the populace as sticky as the humidity in the air.

“It was a love letter to humanity,” Alastor breathes, drawing a knee up to wrap his arms around it, bracing his chin upon it as he gazes out into the night.

He’s—

The city was a thriving melting pot of a variety of people, cynical and beaten by life during an era that was considered to be the most thriving years of the American populace, a place where people came together. It was jazz and blues and literary prose that shook a nation and defined the written word for decades to come. It was the home of brilliance and naïveté and a newfound zest and drive for life during the lowest of points. It was—a home like no other, a city unrivaled and unmatched, a scene that encapsulated the human experience with an ease that no other place at no other time could ever hope to replicate because—it was *human*. New Orleans was human as human could be—the embodiment of free will where it was just...

—*beautiful*.

Fuck.

“Death was not something to mourn, but something to celebrate. *Life* was to be celebrated. It was—the kind of place where you missed it even when you were in it. It was a community so well-knit that even as the city breathed, you moved with it, with the ebb and flow. It was *soul*—as pure as it could possibly be.”

His eyes are wide, listening to words spoken softly amidst the swinging sounds of what he can only imagine is the music of Alastor’s time, of Alastor’s home, feeling enthralled in the image, in the imagination of a place like that, of a place where people found—found *meaning* in the mundane, in the simple—celebrated life in the face of death and became a lighthouse for the beaten down to find a home to pick themselves up from again. The idea, the image, the concept—

It’s—it really is beautiful.

“I guess it rubbed off on you, huh?” He comments wetly, wiping at his face, startling when a handkerchief is held out to him, maroon and well-loved, gently taking the soft fabric and wiping at his eyes, embarrassedly at his nose, sniffing the worst of it back up, cheeks coloring.

Alastor laughs, breathily and lightly, a brief stuttering of air as he brushes at his hair, an idle gesture that makes him seem more human than anything else has—something so simple, so uncontrolled, an absentminded little gesture—

His heart pounds.

The game really has gotten too big for him.

“Appearing in hell, I found it much the same, actually. Hell is—home.”

His breath escapes his lungs, muscles seizing, jaw falling appallingly slack, helpless to the way his emotions surge in a torrent inside of him, strangle him, because—*home*. Alastor had called it a *home*. This creation of his, this project turned punishment, this *thing* that has become the cage for the worst of the worst of humanity, taken those that bastardized his gift and confined them here, caging him the same way this ring is caging them, forcing him to watch the consequences of his dreams for eternity—

Alastor had called it a *home*.

Hell—a *home*.

He feels faint.

“It’s a celebration of life—in its own twisted little way.” A soft gaze slides over to him, his cheek now pressed into his knee, smile warm, hair spilling down and cupping his cheek, stroking under a bright eye, taunting in its following of a path that Lucifer can only trace with his eyes, expression smooth. “You just have to know how to look.”

Not *where*.

*How*.

*Not ‘me’—*us*.*

His heart stutters.

*Fuck*.

He looks away, ears hot, and he knows his tail is out, thankfully hidden by the railing behind them where it hangs in the night air, twitching with every embarrassed pulse of the traitorous organ occupying the cavity of his chest, cheeks flushing.

“And, uh,” he clears his throat fruitlessly, feeling like it’s terribly dry all of a sudden, pressing the cloth in his hands to his nose in a desperate bid to hide whatever embarrassing expression his face is undoubtedly pulling, looking over at the serene face staring at him, nostalgia turning the air fuzzy around him, limbs loose—humanity leeching into the demon before him and turning him soft—turning him into the Alastor that he had been before coming here, or revealing the side of himself he wasn’t at liberty to showcase often—he’s not sure, but, whatever it is, he’s hooked. “What about—what about you? What were you like? As a—as a kid, as an adult—I don’t know, just—*you*, I guess…”

Another hum.

“Quiet, I suppose,” Alastor breathes, and his fingers twitch where they’re wrapped around his leg, like he wants to hug himself but won’t allow himself a further expression of humanity than he is already revealing, turning his face away—hiding—like it’s easier to delve into the past under the illusion of no audience, just himself and the city below.

Alastor was quiet child, born to a barren mother. Her miracle, she would call him, a gift from rue—born from her suffering and her grief and all the pain and anguish after loss, after loss, after loss. He wasn’t supposed to make it to term, but he had. And he had been quiet, an easily startled child, nervous and concerned, clinging to his mother whenever possible.

He loved his mother—loved her more than the life they celebrated so intensely itself. She was his everything.

“My mother was very accommodating,” he muses, lips twisting in amusement. “Perhaps because I was such a gift to her.”

With his mother came their culture, the lifeblood of their people rooted in the rhythms of the city, and Alastor had taken it and prospered, had gone and become a voice of the city, walking the swinging beats of New Orleans with the flair and familiarity of those with roots there, and his mother had been so—proud. He was her star, her everything as much as she was his.

“Your father?”

“Sometimes—a birth parent isn’t all they should be.”

His gaze is pointed, and Lucifer swallows, sick with the knowledge that Alastor isn’t even referring to him—*knowing* with *certainty* that Alastor isn’t referring to him, but to Lilith—she had tricked them, played both of them, led them on to be separate for years for her own ends, and—and all that she had *done*, to him, to his relationship with Charlie—and he had *let* her, blindly or not, he had *let* her—

“I died before my mother,” Alastor sighs, still looking at Lucifer, no longer hiding, expression severe. “Early thirties—what a time to pass. She was barely sixty.” His fingers twitch again, and this time he lets them squeeze, hugs his leg with digits alone, edges of his smile trembling. “All this time apart, but you know—I never doubted her affections for me.”

He feels like he swallowed a rock.

“I’m sure the papers said something awful about me—I’m certain she read it. Why, if she saw me now, she’d probably give me a talking to. I’m ashamed to having ruined her gift so completely, but—I know she loves me, *will* love me, continues to love me. No matter how much I let her down, no matter how long we are apart.”

His lips tremble.

“Your daughter loves you, Lucifer.”

“Stop.”

“She will understand.”

*“Stop.”*

He shakes.

Alastor’s gaze is a quiet thing, the swing of jazz fading into violin notes of ‘April Showers’, gentle vibrato ringing through through the air and dulcet strings in accompaniment to an enviable lower register, and Lucifer presses the heels of his palms to his eyes once more, fingers grasping at the damning handkerchief, and tries to shove the tears back through sheer force of will.

“*How* can I—” he cuts himself off, voice thin and reedy, broken. “How can I tell her—how can I *damage*—that’s her mother—no matter what she’s done to me, that’s—that’s between her and I, but Charlie—”

Alastor doesn’t move.

“She loved Charlie—loves—she—she does. I don’t—”

“If what she’s done can be forgiven, Charlie will find a way,” is what he receives in response, turning wet eyes to the demon beside him, ensconced in shadows, red eyes the most visible part of him. “But that’s a conclusion she will have to come to on her own. *Don’t* make that choice for her.” Crimson orbs glow. “In case you have forgotten, she already brought your daughter into it, whether you want to admit it or not. The moral high ground ship has sailed, your Highness.”

“Why do you—why do you care so much?”

“Call it an investment.”

He frowns, handkerchief crumpling in his hand.

“I have seen the extent of madness like hers.” The shadows breathe, and the figure it cuts against the ground behind Alastor is terrified, a curled up thing, the holes of its eyes scrunched into thin lines. “I had wanted to do this without having to reveal anything at all—I have no interest in becoming involved in familial drama. However, I am running out of time and my hands are tied. If she beckons with instructions, I am helpless to refuse."

The word ‘helpless’ has the shadow curdling more, drawing close to Alastor, like it’s trying to find comfort.

"After all, hell hath no fury like a woman scorned.”

Lucifer’s hands grip the handkerchief like a lifeline, wringing the scrap of fabric for all it’s worth. “But why—”

Alastor sighs, turning his face away. “I will never understand what goes on in your fuzzy little minds—it must be truly entertaining.” The wind blows, loud in the quiet, ‘April Showers’ echoing faintly on a loop. “I will always return kindness with kindness. Regardless of your *intentions*,” and his eyes flash briefly into radio dials on the word, and Lucifer stills because—*oh shit*, he knows—he *knows*—*how* does he *know*—“a kindness should be repaid. And this *is* a kindness—whether you understand it or not.”

The radio demon rises to his feet, dusting off his suit and gazing down at Lucifer whose eyes are still trained where the shadow had been previously curled, looking defenseless and terrified and everything that the physical form of Alastor did not give off.

“This—this isn’t because of the duck—is it?”

Alastor’s eyes shine, smile pursing.

“Talk to your daughter. Or burn the book.”

He feels haunted.

“It’s your choice.”

He loved Lilith.

Alastor’s heels click as he walks away, stepping into the shadows once more.

No matter how he looks at it, he thinks, looking down at the maroon handkerchief his hands, pressing the stained fabric under his nose, breath rustling it against his lips, he’s lost.

Whatever game this is, regardless of the fact that he’s going to protect his daughter with all he has in him no matter what, he’s lost.

*Fuck*.

Loved.

## Chapter 6: checkers, chess, go

### Text

After their little rooftop interaction, Lucifer can quite proudly say that he’s been avoiding Alastor like the plague—plan be damned.

*Listen*—at first, he tried very hard to, well, *not* do that, tried to confront the problem for once like he probably should’ve been doing for *years*, tried to be the fully functional—fully *grown*—being he would very much like to actually be. But, eventually, that turned into feeling like every time he and Alastor were in a room together, Alastor was silently judging him, goading him into discussing the contents of the notebook with Charlie when he, really, just wants to forget that it even exists.

The worst part, though, really, is that at no point is Alastor *actually* trying to force him into doing anything.

No—instead he’s just *waiting*—never bringing it up, never hinting at it, never fucking saying a damn word, not even giving a pointed look—just *waiting*, like a *lunatic*, and it’s—it’s even *worse* than if he would just fucking say something.

Just because he gave free will to humanity doesn’t mean he wants to be given it in turn!

It would be so much easier if his hand was forced, if he didn’t have to think about this and the consequences and what it might be like every time he looks at Charlie and if maybe she wouldn’t look at him the same anymore if she knew, well, *everything*, and then what will he do if that happens, what will he do if she looks at him and sees a stranger, sees someone he isn’t, sees someone he *is*, sees exactly how broken and damaged and incompetent—he can’t take that—he can’t—

Except isn’t he already taking that?

Isn’t he already enduring that?

Didn’t Lilith already ensure that?

*“I think—I think dad was right—about me.”*

Fuck. He is, isn’t he?

That’s such bullshit.

This whole thing is bullshit!

Seriously, what the fuck did he ever do to her? He was the best! Hell, he was literally *the* best thing to happen to her. Adam hadn’t wanted her—there had literally only been two people on the human plane at the time, and it was Adam and Lilith and Adam didn’t fucking want Lilith (not that Lilith wanted him either), and then Adam got a new wife and Lilith was *alone*, and—

And then Lucifer came along.

And—and he changed *everything*.

And he stayed by her side—he *forgave her* for what she did—he accommodated for her. He created a life for them down here, watched her thrive, did his best in their home, as her partner, even as he felt himself slipping, watching the gift he gave be tortured and ruined, watching worse and worse scum fill the depths of hell until the very idea of breathing the same air as them made him sick. He let himself be directed, let her take control, gave her everything—gave her anything she could possibly desire and more, anything in his capabilities—

He *forgave* her!

He forgave her, and she bought his silence, his acquiescence, his jubilance—however short of a period of time—with a small bundle of happiness that meant more to him than he could ever vocalize. He allowed them to return to normal. He allowed them to build a home, a family, together with Charlie, allowed her to give his life meaning once again even as he felt his will to continue slipping—

And then she decided she was done.

And then she left.

And then she fed Charlie lie after lie after *lie*, and he—

And he’s still forgiving her, isn’t he?

He’s still justifying—still making excuses, still spinning tales, still trying to convince himself that the woman he loved was still there, still loved him, still cared about him, still wanted the best for him even though she left the second the opportunity presented itself, even though she fucking took Charlie and bailed on *him* and then bailed on *her* and then bailed on *hell* and then—

*Fuck*.

Lilith always wanted—

Fuck!

Was Alastor right? Was there no avoiding this? Was he going to—ugh—was he going to have to actually confront—ew, fuck, *ew*—confront the *past*?

Gag.

Literally—*gag*.

Ugh.

Disgusting.

Nope, there was no way he was doing that. Ha, ha, ha! No fucking way. The past should stay the past. Past hurts should stay past hurts—even if he’s still muddling through the consequences today. At the end of the day, he hadn’t made any effort to reach out to Charlie either, anyway, so did it really matter what happened in the past or what Lilith had said? In the end, he also had a part to play, and he took the easy way out and didn’t fucking do it. He let himself become an estranged father—he had *wanted* to be one.

He had wanted to be forgotten.

If Lilith had managed to put any thoughts in Charlie’s head, it was because he let her—by disappearing.

Yeah.

Yeah?

*Yeah!*

This was all *his* fault! That’s right! Just another fucking one for the long list of fuck ups that he has been obsessively adding to since the start of this whole fucking thing—this complete fucking mess—and *yeah*! He *is* the problem! He’s finally cracked the fucking code! *Ha!*

*He’s* the problem.

Anything bad that has ever happened—this whole notebook worth of shit—this entire colossal fuck up of a situation—this is all *his* fault. He can cry and blame Lilith all he wants, but, at the end of the day, the one who gave Lilith that power was him. He’s the reason his daughter has neither parent. That’s—that’s on him. That’s—

That’s *his* fault.

“Why do I get the feeling that every time I take my eyes off of you, you’re managing to think of something entirely illogical,” comes sung from beside him, a figure emerging smoothly from the shadows to perch on the cushion next to him, startling him badly enough that he throws the nearest cushion on instinct, Angel’s cackle from behind the perfect background to accompany Alastor’s frozen smile as the pillow slides down his face and lands in his lap, hair fluffed and mussed from the impact, ears twitching.

“Nice aim, your majesty,” Angel crows from the bar, and Alastor’s smile twitches, strains, gaze flicking over to the bar where the spider demon resides.

It’s as close to annoyed as he ever seems to be around an audience—interactions with Lucifer himself, notwithstanding.

Ha! He’s special.

*Awesome.*

“My good friend, do you not have a job to get to?”

The spider demon scowls, shoulders slumping, nail flicking the empty glass in front of him as he pushes himself reluctantly to his feet. “Yeah, *about that*—”

Static crackles.

“Any update on when I get to, y’know stop—doing that?”

Huh?

The fuck—why’s he asking *Alastor* of all people?

Was he planning on barricading himself in the hotel or something and trust Alastor to protect him if whatever demon it was that owned his soul—Pal, was it? Gal? Mail? *Ha*! Mail.—showed up? Sounded like a shitty fucking plan, honestly. Wouldn’t the consequences be even more severe in the aftermath if Alastor fell for whatever reason? Or, like, chose not to bar the overlord access because, like, wouldn’t overlords getting in the way of other overlords’, uh, ‘property’ be, like, well, frowned upon? Or something? Not that Lucifer himself wouldn’t help, but if he were to start meddling in the soul exchanging affairs of denizens—*well*, it was a slippery slope, and not one he particularly wants to entertain considering the long list of consequences that could crop up if he went down that road.

Red eyes light up. “Quite soon, my good fellow! I’m just ensuring a few last things for the security of this hotel for that particular style of—*altercation*. It wouldn’t do to have a threat show up we’re not prepared for.”

*“Right.”*

“In the meantime, I’ll keep sending Husker to you. Just keep your head down, dear; it’ll be done with soon.”

Lucifer blinks.

Sending Husk? To—what? Guard Angel? Like—expending his own resources, putting one of his souls at risk, for—for *Angel?*

*Yeah*, he’s pretty certain he’s missing some points here—or, like, the whole plot.

Angel sighs, put upon and reluctant even as he drags out his phone and peers at the time. “Right, yeah, okay—just—you promised. We had a deal.”

His skin prickles.

“Yes, yes—I *do* honor my deals, believe it or not.”

His nose twitches.

“Right.”

“Run along now! Don’t do anything I wouldn’t do.”

“Everything I do is something you wouldn’t do.”

Alastor’s head tilts.

Angel stares.

Husk sips from the current bottle he’s working on.

“I’m not sure I follow.”

Husk snorts.

“My job is *literally* sucking dick.”

Husk guffaws.

Static squeals.

Lucifer snorts.

“Ah.”

Damn, now he’s thinking this guy’s funny? He’s got to talk to Vaggie soon because this is truly unacceptable—like, actually, what the fuck—he can’t do this, clearly. He can’t honor this objective, can he? And—more importantly—*deal?* What deal? Angel—

Oh.

*Oh.*

Oh, *no.*

Alastor’s roots in this hotel run deeper than he thought they did.

The *fucker*.

How many times—*how many times*—

Every time he thinks he’s actually starting to understand the guy, every time he thinks the guy is *nice*, every time he thinks that maybe he’s not *all that bad*—every fucking time—

Always with the secrets.

His eyes narrow into slits, tail lashing, sharp gaze shifting to the red clad figure propped up next to him, still watching the door Angel had walked out of with some hazy level of interest, more like he zoned out after the man left and hadn’t quite kicked himself into gear yet. Well, no matter—Lucifer’s happy enough to do it for him, reaching over and grasping a gloved wrist, ignoring the sharp feedback the action induces, standing up, sharp point of his tail ripping a line through the fabric of the couch, a whip crack echoing through the room.

“We need to talk, actually.”

Alastor hums, looking at where his hand is grabbed, ears twitching, the fingers on his other hand spasming.

“Were we not doing that?”

“Not yet, we weren’t.” And that’s the last cue he gives him before he’s tearing a portal into the air and yanking the man through, little care for decorum or whatever semblance of a friendship, relationship, something-ship they’ve got going on between them to be any sort of gentle because, oh, the pieces are falling into place in his head now, and he feels like *such* an idiot.

It’s so like him to be seduced by a pretty face and a charming demeanor and forget everything else underneath.

Truly, he’s just iconic for being able to pick the worst of the worst to have a degree of fondness for, isn’t he?

“My, is there a need to be so rough,” Alastor grouses, tearing his hand out of Lucifer’s iron grasp with no small amount of violence, joints cracking, rubbing at the bruised appendage with the closest his face comes to a scowl, lips downturned everywhere except the corners, brow pulled tight in the center, and his ears are twitching in annoyance—in hurt—and it gives him a brief pause, much like the last time they had been in a situation painfully similar to this, something inside of him panging with something like apology—

Fuck.

He swallows his concern down.

His head finally has some clarity for the first time in a *month*, and he’s not letting it slip away to something like *feelings* right now. He’s getting to the bottom of this, whether he himself likes it or not.

And, truthfully, he doesn’t like it.

He doesn’t like it at all.

Fuck.

“Start talking. *Now.”*

“Whatever about?”

Lucifer’s teeth bare in irritation, eyes blazing, and Alastor looks all manners of caught off guard, smile strained painfully tight, fingers still rubbing over his wrist, more self-soothing than to actually reduce any lingering ache, ears pressed to the side. He looks—well and truly alarmed—maybe scared—and it—

Fuck, he *just* said he wasn’t going to get caught up in feelings again, and yet—

*Shit.*

“I—“ *Fuck.* “I’m sorry. I didn’t—” *Shit, shit, shit, fuck, shit—*“I shouldn’t have been so rough with you. I’m sorry.”

The air crackles and pops between them.

A beat passes.

Then, two.

Then, three.

Then—a nod is all he receives for his efforts, Alastor’s expression not becoming any less strained, but at least he walks further into the room than he was dragged, moving to the same seat he had sat in last time—the last time they discussed something serious. Lucifer almost wants to tell him to sit on the bed instead, if only to foster the same quiet atmosphere that had enveloped them back when he had healed Alastor—back when they had been so close, physically close, and Alastor had trusted him, fully and completely, to heal him and to do it well, to take care of him and not harm him.

He restrains himself.

“Now,” Alastor begins, leaning back into the seat, his weight once more leaning on one side, except this time he’s crossing his legs at the knee, arms crossed with his hand—the one he had grabbed—placed against his chest, over the scar from Adam, and Lucifer tries his best to not drown in guilt he shouldn’t be fucking feeling right now even though he very much does, “what is it you wanted to discuss here that we couldn’t discuss out there?”

He blinks, world coming back into focus, realizing finally that he’s still standing dumbly after his apology, staring at Alastor with an expression he’s sure would appall him if he were to look at it in the mirror—embarrassing and remorseful and too fucking open for the persona he should be occupying for this whole fucking situation—and he remains standing in a desperate bid to seem less like he was zoning out and more like he *meant* to, but he’s pretty fucking sure he misses by a mile. “A deal? With *Angel?”*

The room plunges into darkness.

---

Husk is at the bar when the entirety of the hotel begins to quake, chandelier’s jangling ominously, lights flickering, ceiling cracking.
