There’s fractures of green bursting through, the walls lighting up with eery light—golden, holy light—bursting through the seams, the walls splintering and dust coming down in plumes, and he has a mere second to duck and cover as the light bulbs burst in a piercing shatter of glass, snagging Niffty as she runs past and cradling her to his chest as he cowers beneath the bar, pressing his eyes shut, wings drawing tight, and feeling dread twist his stomach harder than the shaking foundations of hell itself is, realizing, as the chandelier crashes down and shatters, that Alastor truly has a way of getting everything he’s ever wanted.

---

“Private discussions should be truly held in private, don’t you agree?”

Lucifer swallows.

Alastor’s not a threat. He’s not anything verging on one. Even now, he doesn’t even give off the energy of being any sort of a threat, any sort of an ominous being, any sort of a fucking imposing overlord. Here, in the dark, swallowed by his shadows, he’s a man—a—a *deer* man, but a man, nonetheless. His posture is no longer a play at subservience, but a mere lounge, a play at effortless dominance, entirely unperfected and unrefined and yet more impactful than any sort of facade he has ever put on in the just over a handful of months that Lucifer has been acquainted with him for.

Alastor is not a threat.

And yet Lucifer feels hunted, all the same.

He says nothing.

“Now, you asked about Angel?”

He swallows—cracks his neck—licks his lip—debates exiting this little shadow bubble of theirs and pretending he never asked anything at all.

The cushion of the chair he had sat in last time is equally as forgiving, a makeshift throne, and his magic rushes up along the sides of the shadowed sphere Alastor has trapped them in, from ceiling to floor, reinforcing with his own barrier, eyes hooded, leg kicking up and crossing over his knee as he leans against the armrest. In a purely opposite image to the figure he had painted on the rooftop, he is the picture of control now, even more than he had been in front of Vox, attention laser-focused in a way he seldom remembers it ever being prior to his bid for free will. It’s as if his every cell is focused on the being before him, in whatever words may slip out of his smiling lips, in picking out what dishonesty of half-truth he may spin even though he’s been unsuccessful in his attempts previously.

He’s focused—focused on analyzing if Alastor is truly a being deserving of his reluctant trust, or if Lucifer just *wants* to believe him—believe *in* him.

He needs to know if he’s really, truly, severely misjudged absolutely everything—if he’s dug himself and Charlie into a hole he can’t hope to get them out of without ruining everything he’s been working towards with her in the process.

That’s the true consequence of this game, isn’t it? The fact that, as much as Alastor claims his hands to be tied, Lucifer is similarly restrained. Alastor is too deeply entrenched in his daughter’s life, in the life of this hotel, in the lives of the people in the hotel—if Lucifer were to deal with the threat through blunt force, he would be jeopardizing everything. *Everything*. His relationship with Charlie, the success of her hotel, the trust and comfort of the people in it—*everything*.

This was a game of manipulation, of brains, of chess—and Lucifer has truly never been the greatest at playing his cards right, case in point being hell itself and everything that led him to this point.

He wants to be distracted.

He’s never been more focused.

“You made a deal with him.”

Alastor’s smile is sharp, biting in the dark, the golden glitter of Lucifer’s own magic dappling along his face in an ominous speckle, lighting up his eyes sporadically and casting odd shadows along the sharp, curved edges of his face.

“I did.”

“Why?”

“To see if I could.”

*“Why?”*

Another tense smile is his answer, and his own expression doesn’t waver, amicability lost in the face of his greed for knowledge, for the inside scoop, for the *truth*. He wants the truth, more than anything else right now—game, be damned. He wants to *know*, wants to see inside of the mind he’s been playing this haunting little game with, wants to understand what is motivating this creature that he can’t hope to understand—has failed to do so, thus far—and yet has ensnared everyone around him so tightly into his net that not even the most violent of disputes could uproot him from the minds of those he lives rent free in, that not even a little under a century of verging inactivity could assuage the fear that clogs the throats of any denizen that encounters the infamous Radio Demon.

He wants to *understand*—understand what game Alastor’s playing, and how he fits into it, how his *daughter* fits into it.

He’s not foolish enough to not understand that he’s a pawn in this man’s eyes. He’s not foolish enough to delude himself fully into thinking that not one single part of this hotel isn’t a part of a much larger game none of them know they’re dancing across the board of. He’s not foolish enough to convince himself that his daughter wasn’t walking around on a leash attached to this man *long* before the two had ever made a deal.

He’s not foolish enough.

He wants to be distracted.

“As you are already aware,” Alastor begins with a small sigh, like he had hoped to avoid having to relay the longwinded exposition on the script as if he had given any hints or clues about where the fuck the story was going when Lucifer can firmly attest to the fact that he *hadn’t*, “I made a deal.” Red tipped fingers trail up and brush along the edges of the collar of his shirt, long neck cracking as one finger taps lightly at the jut of bone, eyes unfocused, caught up in the throes of a memory that he can’t even pretend to see. “I would very much like to be rid of this deal. Understandable, don’t you agree? I do so hate playing to the tune of others.”

“Yeah, uh-huh, pick up the pace.”

That gets him an annoyed crackle, but he doesn’t even care, lent forward, invested, hungry—practically salivating—for an answer, both feet firmly planted on the floor now, elbows braced against his knees, fingers steepled, gaze piercing.

For all that he felt hunted at the start of this, there’s no mistake under the unwavering fierceness of his unfaltering gaze that *he* is the hunter in the room right now.

Alastor is every bit the prey he manifested in hell as, no amount of calmness can undo that—not when faced with Lucifer himself.

Truly, hell had forgotten who the apex predator amongst all the seven rings of hell was.

That’s his own fault.

“You do not rush a good story, your highness.”

“What can I say? I’m an impatient man.”

“You’ve lived for millennia.”

“And been bored out of my mind for most of it.” More like depressed, but *meh*—potato, tomato, desecrator—same deal. *“Entertain me*.”

Alastor’s smile thins into something close lipped, eyes narrowing, but his neck rights itself after a moment, hair wavering around an imaginary breeze, and it’s silent for a moment, two forces facing off, but they both know who holds the cards right now—a feeling so heady that it calls back to the very first time he ever got an expression of fear out of the overlord, back when he had first started this little plan of his, when everything was hazy and tentative and fragile—

—when he didn’t feel like a piece of shit over some mildly rough handling of a fucking *sinner*—

“Very well,” Alastor hums, a distracted thing, like he’s playing off the concession as something that doesn’t matter all that much, but those simple two words are enough to have him lacing his fingers together with a crack, shoulders rolling, eyes flaring red. Truly victory, however minor in the grand scheme of things, was an addictive thing. He had almost forgotten what it felt like, to *fight*, to play the board and pray to come out the winner, to be filled with the ambrosia of pride over a battle of wits. He hadn’t indulged in such an opportunity since heaven had killed his dream—once, then twice—

He wants to be distracted.

“I do not have access to the person who holds my contract,” Alastor states, plain and simple, fully lounging now, pomp and circumstance truly gone, stripped bare even as the plot of flippancy remains.

He’s a man.

Just a man.

He doesn’t want to remember that.

He doesn’t want to think about that.

“Short of the contract holder perishing or willingly relinquishing the soul in their possession, there are precious few ways to get one’s soul back.”

There are no ways.

There are *no* ways to get one’s soul back.

Except—

“I broke Angel’s contract with Valentino.”

*That’s* his name! *Val*! Not *mail.*

Huh.

Mail was a lot more fun.

Wait—

He did *what* now?

Impossible.

“I own his soul now.”

*Impossible.*

“How?”

Alastor grins.

*Impossible!*

“Through no small amount of energy and power on my part—for all that the V’s are simple up-and-comers, they, regrettably, do not hold minimal power. They are quite formidable in their own right, I will admit. It was fortunate that this hotel provided me with such access to such a disenchanted individual under someone with such power.” Alastor’s lips quirk. “Pure coincidence, I assure you, but convenient, nonetheless.”

And the pieces start to fall into place.

“No other overlord will help you.”

“My dear, no other overlord holds a candle to me at full strength. And certainly not any to the individual on the other end of this particular noose.”

Lilith.

That’s—that’s what this whole thing has been about. This whole—this whole *time*—it’s all falling into place now.

Alastor’s showing up at the hotel. Alastor’s original hostility towards him, entirely unfounded and unprompted. Alastor’s deal with Charlie. Alastor’s alarming amount of information on the details of his personal life and what occurred in his absence. Alastor’s goal.

He understands now.

He gets it.

“Angel was just your guinea pig,” he breathes, feeling dumbfounded and yet enthralled all the same, delighted by the mind presented in front of him even as every fiber of his being openly denies it, abhors it, spits at the sight of it. To play people that trust him so profoundly—how *dare he*—

He pauses.

He had thought it before to himself, gazing at the man before him in every instance that they had ended up in his quarters. He had thought it again and again and again—and yet it had never succeeded at truly repelling him—that Alastor is a cornered animal, a cornered *predator*, and he was *dangerous*—*is* dangerous. He’s an active threat, a feral animal willing to claw and bite and render flesh clear from bone of the hands that reach out to help him if it means he gets out of this precarious situation he’s found himself in for reasons still unbeknownst to him—

Alastor’s dangerous—he’s *been* dangerous—he *continues* to be dangerous—

He had thought all of this before, knew all of this long ago, and yet—

“You want Charlie to break your contract.”

Alastor is not a threat.

He can’t tell if he’s lying to himself, or if it’s the truth persistently niggling at the base of his skull like the start of a migraine he can’t quite pinpoint. He hates more fiercely that he can’t trust himself more than he hates the idea of believing that Alastor may have been telling the truth when he said he wants to cause as little harm as possible. Is it—would it truly be so unthinkable to think that the man before him had formed some level of attachment, of fondness, to the people surrounding him so constantly, who were so delighted by his survival? Could it be that he’s right—for *once*—to believe—to trust—could it be—

“No.”

He pauses.

*“No?”*

“No,” Alastor echoes simply, and *there’s* the feral light in his eyes again, that manic little gleam in swirling glowing voids, eyelids pulled high, grin stretched to such a limit that his face seems to tear, cheeks folding unnaturally under the strain. “You’ve played the game, your majesty. Consider this your victory.”

What?

“What.”

“I do not want your daughter to break it.”

Uh-huh, yeah, that much he got.

“I want *you* to.”

*And* that’s where he lost him.

“What.”

Alastor’s wild-eyed gaze is prying.

*Consider this your victory*.

He had—he had won? What had he won? What was his reward? What had he gotten? Is the victory in the room with them right now? Where?

He had—he had wanted this, hadn’t he? That had been the goal? Get the favor called due on himself, yes? This had—this was the goal, no? This was everything he’d been aiming towards. It was literally the whole point, and yet—

It made no sense.

He didn’t feel like he’d won anything.

He felt bereft.

“Why?”

Alastor reclines back again, eyelashes fluttering back into his normal half-lidded look of apathy, smile softening, gazing off into the distance of sparkling gold against black backdrop like it’s any level of entertaining when it, really, is as dull as it fucking sounds—“Because I want to help.”

Yeah, he’s still fucking lost.

“I’m sorry, what?”

“*Help.* My, my, are your ears working?” A finger flicks against his skull, so abrupt that he doesn’t even have time to react, blinking baffled up at his own forehead that’s vaguely smarting, staring cross eyed upwards. “Is this thing on?”

“The fuck?”

“Ah, yes, perhaps it is running a little slower than usual today. Perhaps it has a short battery life?”

“Are you seriously making a short joke right now?”

“Oh, there it is!” Alastor’s hands clap. “Finally booted up, have you? Do keep up.”

“I’m going to dye your hair the next time you go to sleep.”

“Can you even reach?”

“What—why the fuck wouldn’t I be able to—how the fuck are you sleeping? Hovering in the air?”

Alastor snorts.

It’s entirely inelegant—very much unprofessional, not at all gentlemanly, honestly kind of an ugly sound. It’s appallingly endearing—a common occurrence every time he’s left alone with this headache, clearly. It shouldn’t be endearing. It shouldn’t make him want to smile. It shouldn’t make him forget the entirety of the truth bomb that’s been fucking dropped on him right now, and yet—

He’s become distracted.

*Fuck*.

*Consider this your victory.*

Yeah, clearly he hasn’t won *shit*.

Not when he’s *this* bad at the game.

“What do you want to help with?”

“This hotel, of course!”

“Aren’t you already doing that?”

“Not of my own free will.”

Oh.

*Oh*.

Oh, Lilith.

What the fuck has she done?

*What has she done?*

Everything—oh, everything makes so much sense now. Alastor is here under instructions, under orders. He’s here to—to—well, considering where Lilith is, the deal she probably brokered, the fact that she’s up there and he’s still down here and she went through great pains to desecrate his image in his daughter’s eyes, went through great pains to disappear and leave their child in the wind with no one to turn to, went through great pains to leave Charlie *alone* with *no* *help*—

He’s here to sabotage.

He was *sent* here *specifically* to *ruin* this hotel.

Seven years gone—same as Lilith—and then suddenly shows up? As soon as Charlie gets eyes on her hotel for real? Suddenly?

Oh, it all makes so much sense.

“Charlie said you had a plan.”

“Indeed, I do.”

“And yet you’ve left her stalling,” his mind is running a mile a millisecond, eyes flicking around as he watches the pieces of the puzzle he hadn’t realized he’d been collecting fall into place before his very eyes, “because you literally can’t actually help as long as you’re under this deal.”

“Precisely.”

“*Why* do you want to help?”

Red eyes glow and sharp teeth gleam, hair cascading over his cheek as his head tilts demurely, fingers brushing along his bottom lip, like he’s imagining the most delicious little morsel. “I am a facilitator of entertainment, my dear Luci’. As such, I require a source of entertainment. And what better entertainment in hell than *this?”*

“So, what—you get a good story out of this to blab about on your show, then? What happens when it stops being entertaining?”

Alastor’s gaze is a fixed thing, peering deeply into his soul, and he feels almost vulnerable under its scrutiny, peeled open and put on display for eyes that see far more than he ever wanted to reveal. “I cannot see the future. Perhaps my reasons will shift in the future. Perhaps they won’t and the flame with fizzle out and die, much like yours did. However, in the here and now, in the present, when our passions are highest, I want to help and have the ability to do so—to do what even *you* cannot.”

His breath stalls.

“All I need is a simple deal.”

Well—

“Plan, first. Then, we can discuss a deal.”

—he’s certainly not bored anymore.

---

Angel is at the studio when the entire building shakes, the studio lights toppling over, sandbags ill-equipped to keep the top heavy items from tipping and shattering against the floor in the face of such ferocious tremors, electricity sparking from exposed fuses and strained wires. The entirety of the crew is ducking, cowering, and Angel is no better, grabbing at the sheets and shoving himself as close to the wall as possible, cowering as the building creaks ominously, eyes wide and terrified. There’s no windows in the studio, nothing to explain what the sudden shaking is—if it was earth, he’d assume an earthquake because, well, *duh,* but this is *hell.* Hell is on a whole different fucking plane than earth! There’s no way it could be an earthquake, no fucking way, and that fact helps him absolutely not at fucking all because *what is happening, then*—

The spotlights mounted on the ceiling creak.

It’s familiar. The shake is familiar. The instinctual fear is familiar. The sudden dawning sense of doom he feels is familiar.

He had felt this before, not that long ago, this same shake—maybe to a slightly lesser extent, and it certainly hadn’t reached this far, but—

*Alastor*.

That’s what it was last time—that’s what it had to be this time. But—

To make the entire hotel shake, what—what had he done—what did he—*do*—

Fuck.

Fuck, fuck, *fuck*—

He springs up on shaky legs, Valentino’s rage be *damned*, stumbling on a trembling ground to his dressing room, his perfumes and makeup toppled and spilled all over the floor, powders cracked and glass shattered, plastic similarly fractured, flailing clumsily into his bag to grasp at his phone and pull it out, praying for a signal, praying for a battery, praying for everyone to be okay, dialing the first number that pops up that he knows will be there if he calls because his shoot wasn’t close to done yet, was nowhere near done—he had virtually just gotten here—

The floor gives a thunderous rumble, and he topples to his knees, hands slamming against his dressing room table, crawling underneath in a desperate bid for safety should the whole studio decide to come down on top of them suddenly. The phone rings, connection spotty at best, but, after a few beats of silence and a few stalled rings, it connects, Husk’s swearing voice coming through the line, crackly and distorted but *there* and—

*“Niffty*, fucking stay *put*—*shit*—is he bringing the *whole place* down? *Niffty—*”

“Husk? Husk!” His desperation would be embarrassing if it wasn’t for the fact that his makeup table is literally juddering against the floor, the quakes so violent that it’s shifting its way across the room, pressing aggressively against him, and he has to brace himself against the floor helplessly in vain hope that it’ll stall its forward motion and continue to serve as some sort of fragile protection.

*“What?”*

“What the hell is going on? Is this—is this fucking smiles?”

“*Shit*, you’re feeling it over there?”

“The whole fucking studio’s threatening to come down! What the shit is going on right now?”

The line crackles, connection flickering, and he’s left hollering down it to a blank line, tears pricking his eyes when the connection flares back to life only to grace his ears with the sound of a violent crack and a thunderous thud, the hotel fucking imploding in his mind’s eye—a much more violent version of the same song and dance it went through when Alastor had made his deal with Charlie. And if *that* had been powerful, then this—

This was unparalleled—power unparalleled, unmatched, unattainable by anyone else in fucking hell, except—

“He fucking—made a—”

“Made—made a what—*Husk*!”

“*Fuck!* Niffty, we gotta move, we gotta *move*—fuck—*fuck*—knock it off, Alastor!”

“Husk!”

“He made a fucking deal with the devil, A—get fucking *down*, both of you, *are you insane*?”

*“Husk!”*

The table knocks into him, violent in its shakes—tremors no longer the right word to classify this as the fucking ground groans and heaves and fucking upchucks the denizens resting against it, the very air seeming to shake in answer to whatever deal Alastor just managed to seduce the literal king of hell into, and *fuck*—

Fuck, fuck, fuck—

What the fuck does this mean—what does this mean—

*“Get down! Niffty—”*

The line blanks.

The table topples.

He hears his mirror shatter.

He squeezes his eyes shut and prays for this nightmare to end.

---

Shit—shit, shit, shit—she should’ve known—she should’ve *known*—

She had seen the signs, the same signs she had observed in herself—even more *severe* than the ones she had been giving off herself—and yet had done nothing, had blindly trusted, had run away from the problem as she does, as she did with telling Charlie the truth, as she did with having to confront the extermination, as she did again and again and again—taking the easy way out when she’s supposed to be better than that, she *is* better than that, wants to be better than that—

She had trusted he wouldn’t fail, even as she saw him slipping.

She had trusted he wouldn’t fail, even as she saw the web spinning tighter around him.

She had trusted he wouldn’t fail, even as Charlie had whispered in her ear, overeager and far too excited, about a new romance brewing within the hotel.

She had trusted.

She had feigned blindness.

She had been complicit, once again.

*Fuck*.

“Is everyone okay?” She coughs, batting at the air like it’ll dispel any of the plumes of dust wafting through the air, concrete and rubble from the cracked ceiling scattered across the floor. So much for having a new hotel, she thinks bitterly, looking over at Charlie who is standing next to her, expression horrified.

“Husk? Niffty?” Charlie calls, voice tremulous, hands shaking, eyes wet already despite the lack of confirmation of alive or dead—even though they would regenerate even if they had died, so it’s not like it was *permanent*, not like Sir Pentious—not that his ended up being very permanent either, but still—and guilt grips her so tightly that it’s suffocating, reaching out and clasping those shaking digits in her own, attempting to be soothing and missing it by a mile, she’s sure.

“I’m sure they’re—”

“We’re good.”

“Oh, thank fuck—Husk! Niffty!”

Charlie’s hand is tight around hers, running forward through the rubble, stumbling down cracked steps to where Husk is digging himself out from under the cover he had found in the divot of the structure, setting Niffty down, wings covered in powdered concrete and fur streaked with bits of glass. There’s blood on the floor, undoubtedly from him running across its glass strewn surface moments before the bar had gotten destroyed, bleeding sluggishly from his arm that he holds awkwardly from where he had probably thrown himself under the cover of the stairs moments before true disaster struck, Niffty clutched protectively in his arms.

“What was that?”

“Deal with the devil,” Husk grumps, rubbing at his shoulder, Niffty peering up at him worriedly, small hands brushing shards of glass free from his fur from where she can reach, her own hair dusty but otherwise unharmed. “Angel called—felt it all the way in the studio.”

“Not just the studio.”

“The fuck—”

“Angel!”

“How the fuck did you get here through all of that?”

“Sheer dumb luck,” Angel heaves, and, despite the tremors being over, his legs are still shaking, bones virtually liquid, collapsing onto the dusty, obliterated couch with a relief so deep he seems on the verge of bursting into tears. “D’you know what it’s like to be in that fucking building during something like this? Fucking terrifying. No windows, no signal—can’t see fucking shit—Vox got Val and bailed, left the rest of us to die, probably, so I just—ran.” He’s trembling, bona fide shakes like he’s still going through the worst of the tremors even though the ground is finally still, and Vaggie watches Husk limp over to lean against the arm of the couch and offer his presence as some sort of support, Niffty scurrying after them.

“What’s it—what’s it like out there,” Charlie asks, eyes wide, horror painting her pale features, and Vaggie’s stomach turns.

“Hell is—hell is a *mess* right now, toots,” Angel coughs, wiping at his eyes, running his hands up through his hair, seeming to be trying to regain his composure however he can. He’s not doing amazingly at it, but, really, Vaggie can’t fault him. She’s certain her own conscience is showing clearly on her face, and it’s really fortunate that no one is paying attention to her right now because it would be a dead giveaway. “There’s so many buildings in pieces, people are dead left, right, and fucking center—it’s like the entirety of hell just split open! I’m telling y’, connection is spotty as fuck right now, but there’s people in other rings posting about it—it was felt through *all* of hell. Not just us.”

“Shit.”

“Shit.”

“Shit.”

*“Alastor.”*

“Present and accounted for—oh, my. What happened here?”

She whirls, rage a violent thing inside of her bursting forth with the same fury that her wings do, drowning out the guilt for a sweet moment of respite, launching forward to grab at the lapels of that stupid red coat and yank, teeth bared, accusation in her eyes. “What the *fuck* did you do?”

“I assure you—”

“*What* the *fuck* did you *do?”*

“Vaggie!” Charlie shouts, alarmed.

She’s certain the others are alarmed, too. This isn’t—this isn’t advisable, angel or not. They had literally just had an up-close and personal example just a few months ago about how angels are most definitely *not* immortal and that there’s a lot that a man like Alastor can probably do to her. She’s not thinking about that right now, though. No. Right now, she’s thinking that this red stained, silver-tongued, piece of shit has fucking made a deal with the devil and yet looks *fine* which can only mean that Lucifer’s the one that took the shit end of the deal, just like with Charlie, and that means that Charlie isn’t safe, that Lucifer being hurt is her fault, and that, once again, she should’ve known better and steeled her heart against this pompous asshole better so that she could be the protector that Charlie needs her to be because this fucker just—

“Vaggie.”

She freezes.

Her thoughts still.

Leaning at the top of the stairs, looking exhausted but very much alive, stands Lucifer, using the railing—what remains of it—as support, dark bags under his eyes and yet a stern expression on his face. It’s enough to have her hands dropping in an instant, Alastor’s familiar crackle returning from where it went to a high frequency screech that, only now, with a clear head, does she realize was hurting her ears, jaw dropping as she pops them, standing down, ever the good soldier even if her cause has changed.

“Dad!”

“You’re okay.”

Lucifer nods, waving away the concern even as Charlie trips her way up the stairs to grab him into a hug, frenetically checking him over. She doesn’t take her eyes off of Alastor, even as he brushes off and straightens his coat, peering around the room in interest, microphone spinning distractedly in his hand, expression as expressive and yet elusive as always, eyes widening and lidding in syncopated intervals, brow furrowing, the sound of stations switching filling the air with the flit and whimsy of his thoughts.

“Of course, I’m okay—seriously, Charlie—Char-char—honey—okay, *okay*, that’s a little too much, sweetheart, seriously—*seriously!* I’m *fine.*”

“What were you thinking?” And now Charlie’s anger is coming through, looking at her father and then turning her fiery gaze over to Alastor. “*Both* of you!”

Alastor blinks owlishly, ears perked, and Vaggie gets the distinct urge to fucking smack him.

“Look, honey, it was—” Lucifer scrubs a hand over his face, and Vaggie can see just how much of his weight he’s bracing against the bannister, his figure so clearly exhausted, slumped and defeated, expression morose in the face of his daughter’s ire. She—she was at fault for this. This is—this is her fault. If she just hadn’t told him, if she hadn’t asked him to do this because *she* couldn’t—

“A necessary step for the continued protection of this hotel, my dear,” Alastor interrupts smoothly, and his gait is measured, posture straight, shoulders pulled back, head held high. There’s not an ounce of exhaustion in his frame—none of the bone deep tiredness she can see so clearly in Lucifer. Did—did Lucifer really just make a shit deal? Was Alastor—did Alastor somehow siphon is power? Or something? What the fuck was going on?

“A necessary—*how?* Guys, this is not how you *do* this!”

“Do what, my dear?”

Charlie freezes.

Her eyes flit around in panic.

Her cheeks color.

Heavens, no.

Is she—is she seriously still thinking of the presumed romance between those two? Is she *serious?* Charlie—her love, her everything—*fucking seriously?* What about making a *deal* screams romance? Well, obviously, it doesn’t scream any, seeing as Charlie is scolding them for that, but—if they reached that point, isn’t that a clear fucking indicator that whatever the fuck is going on between these two morons *isn’t* love but something a lot more—sinister? Convoluted? Fucking deranged as all hell and not made for people like them to understand? These are—these are heavy hitters they’re dealing with (even if Charlie is *technically* one herself)—they can’t pretend to understand them even if they want to delude themselves into thinking they can. Not one part of these guys will ever make sense to the common people like them.

Whatever this *thing* is between them, it’s not love.

It’s something far worse.

And this game of manipulation—which she can only imagine is what Lucifer got himself snared in—is her fault. She should’ve known—should’ve guessed—

After so long away from the rest of hell, what made her think that Lucifer was in any way ready to deal with a man like Alastor? What made her think that he would be the best bet? Because he’s powerful? She should’ve just gone to him to finish the job for her if she sussed out undeniably that Alastor was up to no good—that would’ve been more convenient. Lucifer would still hate Alastor for breathing—he would’ve had the power and the strength necessary to annihilate him without even breaking a sweat—it would’ve been fool-proof. It would’ve been the smarter way to go about this—the better way, the correct way.

But, no.

She had instead passed off the whole endeavor.

And all because, what? She got soft on the guy? The guy that even now she *knows* is up to no good and yet still can’t muster the same ire she held previously because he’s also just—kind? Manipulatively kind, she’s sure, but *still*—he *helps*, sort of. And he makes Charlie so happy, it’s—

Ugh.

She really took a backseat to this because of *that*?

Looking at the consequences now, that course of action doesn’t make any sense at all. Frankly, none of this does. She should’ve just—she should’ve done it herself, relationships be damned. If Alastor turned out to be here with malicious intent, Charlie would understand why she did what she did. Charlie is literally the best at forgiving—manages to forgive her for every mistake she’s ever made. She should’ve just—

*“Uh.”*

*Por Dios.*

“Charlie, seriously, it’s okay. He’s not lying.”

“But—but *how*? Why? What could possibly make a deal *necessary*? You guys are friends!”

The room is silent.

Vaggie facepalms.

Static crackles.

Alastor blinks, repeatedly, one after another, like he’s fucking buffering, and, really, she can’t blame him because, seriously, Charlie, what the fuck—

“Well, my dear, that leverage I was telling you about,” Alastor continues after a moment, acting as if he hadn’t missed multiple beats in that ensuing silence, skipping up the stairs to stand by the two members of royalty, smile bright as ever. “Unfortunately, finishing acquiring it puts me at a bit of a risk, if I do say so myself, so we made a deal for peace of mind—nothing more, nothing less.”

Charlie squints.

Vaggie is almost certain she doesn’t even need a nose to smell that bullshit from a mile away.

“Really?”

Of course, though—because when is it not?—it’s enough for Charlie.

Fuck—she’s *really* fucked up.

Fuck.

Lucifer, she’s so sorry.

“But—but why does dad—dad, why are you so tired? What happened?”

“I’m fine, sweetheart, I just—need to lay down for a second. I haven’t exactly ever made a deal down here—didn’t realize how much power I’d use on binding, ha, ha, ha!”

Alastor perks at that, ears twitching, staff disappearing as he turns to Lucifer, and there’s something eery in his expression as he looms, something ominous—or maybe it’s a trick of the light? She can’t tell. Everything looks dystopian as fuck right now, hotel in shambles once more. “He is certainly correct on that front. Why don’t you take him to a spare room to get some rest, Charlie, dear? Have some time to connect.” Lucifer’s eyes narrow into a glare. “*Talk*.”

“You fucker—”

“Oh, don’t worry your tiny little head—I will leave the remodeling to you, rest assured. I will simply aid our housekeeper in cleaning up the place a bit more until you have your strength back.”

“That’s not what I—”

“Splendid! Charlie, dear, if you would.”

And with that, Alastor’s skipping down the stairs once more, surveying the damage as he summons his shadows to start taking care of the wreckage while he goes to survey the area where Niffty, Husk, and Angel are sat.

Charlie looks bewildered while Lucifer looks positively mutinous, and Vaggie can’t keep the regret off her face even if she wanted to, peering up at the king with has much of an apologetic look as she can muster. No matter what he says, no matter the reason, a deal is dangerous. And if it had taken him this long to get Charlie out of hers—and she can really only *hope* that he got her out of hers—

She didn’t want him on the hook in lieu of her, though.

That hadn’t been the goal.

Or—or maybe it had?

Her gut twists.

Fuck.

She is an awful person.

---

Lucifer sleeps for three days straight.

*Three days.*

In that time, Alastor cleans up the hotel of all the rubble, stitches together holes in the structure with his shadows and menial supplies, keeping true to his word of not doing any sort of remodeling. Lucifer’s room had been all but destroyed in the aftermath, kaleidoscope of windows blown out, something Niffty had delighted in cleaning up, and the kitchen had suffered quite a bit of damage as well that required immediate fixing. Alongside all of that, Husk’s injuries required tending to, and Alastor had constructed a barrier around the hotel as well in the meanwhile, taking all of their electronics while he was at it and confining them to a bubble off to the side.

“Uh, y’know, we, uh, kind of like our phones, smiles. I know it ain’t exactly your thing, but—we *are* fond of them.”

“Oh, calm yourself. You’ll get them back once his highness awakes. It wouldn’t do to let the king of hell be seen in such a sorry state when he almost just accidentally destroyed his kingdom.”

“You do realize *you’re* the reason he’s in that state, right?” Vaggie had griped, and Alastor’s smile had been cutting when he looked at her, seemingly any and every bit of progress they had made lost within a day, eyes bright.

“And that is why I am taking every precaution to ensure the safety of this hotel and everyone in it. Astonishing, I know.”

It doesn’t take a rocket scientist to hear the bite in the words—or to see the way Vaggie’s face screws up into something like contrition, mixed with the bitter bite of fury, not that it fazes Alastor at all. He simply carries on as if nothing were the matter, banishing the little bubble with their phones, as if he believed that any of them would be fully capable or *want* to attempt at getting their electronics back from him.

Even the television is taken away.

It’s amidst all of this that Charlie finally becomes aware of the deal Angel and Alastor had cut, and to say she’s shocked is an understatement, but, really, it’s the least of her problems. It’s not an amazing thing to find out, really, that someone she’s entrusted the safety of this hotel to has gone ahead and brokered a deal with her one and only actual tenant, but, well, it’s also not entirely unexpected.

Honestly, regardless of the reasons, she’s kind of—kind of *grateful*.

Angel had said before, previously, that if she wanted to get him to be able to stay at the hotel and do the work that was necessary to actively, like, actually work towards redemption fully, she’d have to deal with his boss. And she had tried that—she had—and it had gone, well, *horribly.* Valentino was—is—a monster, the kind that cares little for status or decency and only gives a fuck about getting his dick wet and keeping people underneath him, and it’s—it’s not her wheelhouse. She doesn’t know how to deal with that, how to maneuver with that. The fact that he doesn’t *want* to be a decent person is tricky to handle—especially when he holds the well-being of someone she cares so much for in his long fingered hands.

But Alastor had made a deal.

Alastor’s deal with Angel had *broken* the deal with with Valentino.

Angel was *free*—free in a way that Charlie hadn’t known how to provide him with sooner.

And Al—Al is a great guy—a *good* guy.

No matter what everyone else thinks of him, this she knows—for sure, one hundred percent, feels it in her very bones. He is a good guy, a gentle soul, a kind man—the kind that keeps his word even if it seems it won’t benefit him.

And, honestly, that’s kind of her problem right now.

She looks at the notebook in her hands, standing off to the side from the group that is using the ballroom of the hotel as their current hangout spot where Alastor has happily provided amenities for them while her dad rests and recovers from the aftermath of the deal, the entrance room still trashed despite Alastor and Niffty cleaning away the glass and dust and broken items. They had even collected all of the pieces of Dazzle’s statue so that it could be fixed first thing instead of needing to be made anew, Razzle staying by its side in a mournful state, hurting once more for the other half of itself that would never be returned.

She frowns, looking down at the notebook in her hands, again.

Walking her dad to a spare bedroom that hadn’t been damaged by the quakes had been an ordeal in and of itself.

*“Dad, seriously, are you okay?” She pries, concern palpably obvious in her tone, in her face,* *in the way she keeps reaching out to him like she feels he’ll topple over—and, truthfully, he* *looks like he would.*

*“Charlie, I’m fine,” her dad sighs, and yet she can’t ignore the way he slumps atop the bed* *that was, blessedly, clean and in order—no doubt thanks to Niffty—as if he was grateful for* *the support, scrubbing a hand over his face. He looks tired—exhausted—not just physically,* *but mentally, and the sight makes worry bubble up in her even more because she’s never seen* *him like this, not truly. Sure, from the sidelines, she can observe that he seems a little aimless,* *a little sad, a little out of touch and despondent, but—never like this.*

*This was a different kind of exhaustion.*

*This was a centuries long exhaustion, a cell deep exhaustion, the kind of exhaustion that* *makes even breathing seem like a chore, the kind that makes someone into a shell of* *themselves and keeps them from operating—*

*The kind she fears ever falling into.*

*“Are you sure?”*

*“*Yes*, Char’, honey, I’m* fine.*”*

*She doesn’t really believe him.*

*“Well, um, is there—something—anything—that we should talk about? Anything at all? I* *mean, I don’t want to pry, and I’m sure you would tell me anything I needed to know, but* *Alastor made it sound like—“*

*“That fucker—”*

*“Dad!”*

*“Look, honey—” her dad starts, stops, draws in a breath, looks away—looks at the ceiling* *and then at his knees, adjusts his position on the bed so that he’s actually properly on it,* *ready to lay down and sleep, scrubs a hand over his face and up through his hair, grasps at* *the back of his neck then swipes his hand over the breadth of his throat and presses his* *knuckles to his lips. He’s stalling—stalling, stalling, stalling—it’s painfully obvious—and her* *gut circles at whatever it could be that he needs to say that has him so clearly reluctant to* *divulge.*

*Her lips purse, hands wringing each other anxiously, taking a small step forward. “Dad?”*

*She gets a notebook dropped into her hands for her troubles, startling so badly that she* *almost drops it. It’s heavy and bound, and, when she steadies it in her hands, she catches* *sight of swirling calligraphy, and it’s—it’s not his handwriting, and certainly not her mother’s* *and yet—she doesn’t recognize it right off the bat, but it doesn’t really matter because her* *dad’s got a pained look on his face, and that’s more worrying than anything.*

“*He called it a* gift,” *he sighs, looking away, crossing his legs, hands collapsing into his lap*, *shoulders slumped. He looks haggard, scared, wrung out to dry, beaten and profoundly*, *deeply, exhausted—he looks like a shell barely clinging to the life that fills him*.

*It makes her nauseous.*

*“A gift?”*

*“Just—read it. And we’ll—we’ll talk after—after I—you know, rest.”*

*“Wait, but—this is—a gift? From Alastor?”*

*Her dad’s shoulders shrug, posture slumped, chin cupped in his hand, elbow propped on his* *knee, and he doesn’t look like he’s there—doesn’t look like he’s in the room with her, and she* *remembers her mother’s words, her grated, irritated whispers—*“He doesn’t care about anything anymore.”—*and wonders if maybe they were true—if they were still true.*

*They couldn’t be—they* couldn’t.

*He was so passionate—he seemed so passionate—he seemed to love being here—how—*

*He wouldn’t just—not want—to be here—right?* Right?

*“Look, just—can you promise me that you’ll—that you’ll wait until—until after we talk to* *decide how you feel?”*

*“Dad, you’re scaring me.”*

*“It’s just—” he groans, flops back onto the mattress, hiding his face behind his hands, and he* *seems so young like this—so lost—and she realizes, suddenly, maybe for the first time, that* *even though her dad is this amazing, powerful, angelic being, he’s also just—just a dad, just* *a man, just a person, fumbling through eternity like the rest of them with his own set of* *struggles and troubles and—she’s seen souls down here that have been here for centuries,* *millennia, and make no effort to change or address their past traumas and hurts. Is it so* *unlikely to think that maybe her father is in the same situation? “Look, honestly, I didn’t* *really want to talk about this with you, but my—my hand’s kind of been forced—“*

*“What—dad—if he’s forcing you to do something you don’t want to—”*

*“That’s not it!”*

*She flinches.*

*He sits up abruptly, expression contrite, eyes wide and open and vulnerable—apologetic in* *every sense of the word. “I—I’m sorry, Char’, I’m—sorry. Fuck—it’s just—he’s not—” he* *looks down. “We’ll discuss this with Alastor after I’ve rested. Just—read it over and be ready,* *and—”*

*His hand is cool around hers, but his gaze is so warm, and she squeezes his fingers, notebook* *held limply in her free hand, stepping closer to embrace him because, really, he looks like he* *could desperately use a hug—he looks like he’s been needing one for the last few thousand* *years, truthfully. She wonders how accurate that is—wonders how little it is she knows, how* *little it is she understands.*

*“—please just—just wait for us to talk to form an opinion. Please? I’m—”*

*“Dad—I love you. I care about you.” She falls to sit on the mattress, notebook set aside as* *she cups his hands in hers and looks deeply into eyes so similar to her own. “Nothing—*nothing*—is going to change that.”*

*His eyes are wet.*

*Hers are, too.*

*“Charlie.”*

“*Nothing, dad*. Nothing.”

*She squeezes his hands.*

*He nods.*

*Her stomach twists.*

*She smiles.*

*He looks like he needs one of those, too.*

And now—now her dad is awake—or, at least, had been when she had checked on him just a few hours ago, looking to still be getting his bearings. He had been getting ready, stretching, trying to wake himself up fully, and she had just rushed him and given him a hug, promised she still loved him even though she really isn’t quite sure what she’s feeling inside, and then left him to get started on the repairs for the hotel so that they could get back to business as usual. And now—now she’s playing the waiting game—waiting for a window of time where she and her father and Alastor can sit down and discuss the damning contents of a notebook she doesn’t understand how Alastor got his hands on to be able to gift it to her father.

The contents are—they’re sensitive, private, intimate—things meant and made for her ears only, things that hadn’t even been breathed out loud but her father clearly held as true if he gave her the book to read, things she never wanted to know and never wanted to see and left her feeling all twisted and nauseous and like she didn’t know how to go about her life in the aftermath of.

And it’s all—*all*—because of Alastor.

And she doesn’t know what to do with the information.

What was—what was the angle? What was the goal?

And what was wrong with her for thinking like that?

She—she *trusts* Alastor, trusts him as much as she trusts Vaggie, as much as she trusts her father. If she’s honest with herself, she can admit that he’s become her closest confidant, listening to things she hasn’t even said to Vaggie, privy to information she didn’t even know she possessed because he’s just—he’s good at listening, good at giving advice, good at finding what’s hurting her and making it feel not as bad. He doesn’t *fix* things for her—no, he doesn’t even try to; not like Vaggie, not like her dad. He just listens and reflects and he comments and makes her look deeper inward to find a way to manage the problem herself.

With the hotel—with things like this ordeal with heaven—he helps. He helps as much as he can. But with her—her, as a person, as a being with a heart and a spirit and aches and pains and insecurities and trauma and feelings she doesn’t know how to deal with—he holds her up to a mirror and asks her to look deeper.

She can see why he and Rosie are such good friends.

And now he’s gone and given this—this—this—ugh, she doesn’t even know the word for it! Intimate? Obtrusive? Voyeuristic? She doesn’t know. Something so personal—so personal to her, to her *dad*, to her *family*—he’s gone and given this to her dad, and her dad’s given it to her, and she can’t—she can’t see what he gains out of this because it—it doesn’t feel very much like a gift.

It feels like a wound being gouged open that she didn’t know she had.

It feels like her world tilting on its axis and her being asked to stand still through it.

It feels like being pecked at by birds and then doused in salt but told to smile despite the immense agony.

She doesn’t know how to deal with it—doesn’t know how to feel about it.

“Charlie.”

Looks like it’s time she figures it out, though.

---

Three days.

*Three days*.

He’s fucking out of it for *three fucking days?*

What the *fuck?*

He stares at the comatose body before him, scrubbing a hand over his face and pressing his knuckles to his lips. *Three days*. How strong was Lilith that breaking whatever contract she held with Alastor took him out for three days—three *whole days*? That type of bone deep, cell deep, *atom deep* exhaustion was one entirely unfamiliar to him, the type that had made waking, let alone *rising* from the bed—even after three days—feel virtually impossible in a way that was shockingly unknown to him (and he knows quite a bit about the act of simply getting out of bed being impossible), body aching so profusely he was tempted to just keep lying there if it wasn’t for the fact that he remembers his conversation with Alastor and the fact that they very much are short on time—the fact that they did it then and there in a hurry because there is no time to waste dilly-dallying.

Not to mention, well, the fact that the hotel was kind of, sort of, definitely-of—uh—in shambles—because of him.

Yeah…

So, sleeping in? Not an option

But, of course, everything after the deal moved at warp speed, too fast for him to really comprehend, plowing through every reservation and concern and fucking road block he had up in a heartbeat to the point where he had no idea how the fuck to go about understanding or addressing anything that had just occurred.

He had talked to Charlie—sort of—well, not really. He had—he had taken the coward’s way out and just handed her the book, but it was a step further than he was originally considering, so it should count for *something*. And then there was the afterwards: the actually *talking* to her, Alastor watching them silently in mild judgment as Lucifer haplessly stuttered his way through an explanation and so many apologies that Alastor’s lip was twitching with a desire to curl in disgust—and he knows that’s exactly what it was doing, he can tell, he’s intuitive like that.

And Charlie—Charlie had just been staring at him with those big soulful eyes, with that expression that said nothing had changed even as her horns emerged and her eyes glowed in anger with every word she heard, with every question she asked, with every page in the book she nitpicked. She—she knew so much now, and yet—

She wasn’t angry—not at him—and that’s—that dizzying, relieving, damning. He doesn’t understand, doesn’t understand how she can forgive him so easily, how she can accept the fact that he *had* wanted her even though he made no effort to reach out and contact her—how that could possibly be enough for her to make up for *years* of neglect—how she could easily dismiss any of the words Lilith had fed her, any of the lies, about her father, how she could accept him for what he said instead of ignoring—how she could forgive—

He doesn’t understand, even when she hugged him, even when she kissed his temple, even when she cried with wet, loving eyes and insisted that she still loved and cared for him and that nothing had changed, just like she promised, and that she’s so glad he told her—like it’s something to be *grateful* for when he just went and did the exact same thing as Lilith and *ruined* a good relationship, and—

He doesn’t understand.

He understands this plan even less.

For all that Alastor’s soul is in his claws now, he can’t pretend to understand the man all that well, much less the leverage he continues to speak about to which Charlie’s eyes light up and sparkle, eagerness lining the long lines of her body as she waits excitedly to hear of the progress Alastor’s making on a front they had apparently previously discussed even if Lucifer’s only *really* hearing about it now.

And, like, *rude*.

How dare this stupid fucking pompous ass go giving Charlie promises and assurances he’s not aware about? How the fuck is he supposed to protect her when he knows so little? That makes no sense.

The sense of loss is even stronger now.

To think—he’s reached the point of *owning* Alastor’s literal *soul* and yet he doesn’t feel like he’s won anything at all. He can’t command him to relinquish his deal with Charlie because that wasn’t their agreement. He can’t command him to give him every little detail about everything because Alastor is a silver-tongued demon and any word that comes out of his mouth can be the truth and yet *not,* and he has no way of proving which. He can’t even ask to be kept in the loop because that wasn’t part of their agreement either!

The only thing he had thought, at the time, to include explicitly in the agreement, seeing as the cat was out of the bag and Alastor was aware of his little plan, was add a clause that he had to be informed and approve of whatever favor Alastor asks Charlie for in the future. He had no more power over breaking Charlie’s deal as he did releasing Husk and Angel from their contracts with Alastor.

For all the power he holds with the man’s soul in his hands, it’s all only theoretical.

There’s nothing he can actually act on—not really.

And now he’s here, doing the most crucial part of their deal, the hotel on lock down, barrier firmly fixed around it—which, truthfully, he probably would have had to do anyway considering the fact that he apparently caused a teeny-tiny, itty-bitty, mini-biggie, uh, hell quake? Hell quake. And hell was not exactly enthused about that fact. And it doesn’t really help with the fact that Alastor is at the hotel, the only person that every overlord has their eyes on to make sure *doesn’t* gain more power—and then he goes and makes a—a hell quake.

That’s a gigantic fucking neon sign of, “Look over here! Guess who made a deal with the devil!”

Yeah—real fucking subtle, Luci’.

Really thought that one through.

Good fucking job.

*Bravo*.

He covers his face with his hands and barely restrains the urge to scream.

“Dad?”

He shouldn’t have agreed to any of this.

“Char’, *hey*—what’s—what’s going on? Did you—need something?”

Charlie cuts a hesitant, shy figure, poking in through the crack in the doorway, fingers tapping anxiously where they’re curled around the edge of the door. At his acknowledgement, she hesitantly edges in further into the room before she’s all but running up to sit beside him on the couch he’s manifested close to the bed to keep a careful eye on the occupant in it.

Alastor looks nothing short of peaceful in sleep—or, well, coma—smile soft to the point where it can barely be classified as one, the natural upward curve of the corners of his lips (which is new fucking information, and, wow, he can’t tell if he’s annoyed or delighted by this new little tidbit), eyes shut (which he had to shut himself because the guy fucking left them *open*, the creep), brow smooth. His hands are folded on his chest, microphone staff clasped between them, and he looks more like he should be in a coffin, buried six feet under, than just taking a little mental voyage to the heavenly realm in the greatest fucking act of defiance Lucifer has ever committed in his long, long, *long* life.

And he’s counting the fucking free will debacle!

At least he was actually part of heaven then.

This—this is full-on rebellion, the kind that comes with fully knowing and understanding what he’s doing and the consequences.

He can’t blame anyone else this time if this goes sour.

Just himself.

He really shouldn’t have agree to any of this.

“What’s going on, sweetie?”

“Hi, sorry, I just—” she slumps, dragging her knees to her chest, slumping into his side, her cheek cushioned on his head. He wraps an arm around her and rubs at her side and tries to not think too hard about the wonderment filling his chest over the fact that he’s still *allowed* this, despite everything that fucking happened, despite everything that Charlie learned—he’s still allowed this—inexplicably. “I can’t stop thinking about what Husk said.”

*Yeah*—that makes two of them.

*“Your highness, this really ain’t a thing against you. But you guys are talking about sneaking* *Al up there to get some info you absolutely need—which only he knows what is—and we have* *no clue exactly what that’s gonna do to him and his,” Husk grumps, looking awkwardly to the* *side, the air popping with the crackle of static and odd humming, “skillset. You could be* *sending him to his death.”*

*“I mean—I ain’t one to shoot a guy when he’s down, but wouldn’t that be a good thing for* *y’?”*

*If shut the fuck up was ever to be conveyed so strongly with a look alone, it was in the glare* *he shoots Angel.*

*Even Lucifer feels like he somehow did something wrong even though he’s the one just sitting* *there and listening and getting, apparently, all of the responsibility dumped into his lap even* *though he’s not the one that made the fucking plan to begin with, thanks very fucking much.* *This is all* Alastor’s *mess, not his. Quite frankly, if it wasn’t for Charlie—his sunshine, the* *light of his life, the greatest gift he’s ever been given, he would not even be considering* *poking the massive, threatening, existence ending bear that is heaven.*

*He’s very much in agreement, Husk—if anyone cared to actually fucking ask him.*

“No.”

*“Your concern is touching, Husker, my dear.”*

*“Who said I was concerned?” The cat demon hisses, although, really, everyone would have* *to be blind to believe the denial because, yeah, it seems like maybe, just maybe, he fucking is.* *Lucifer will not pretend to understand what kind of fucking relationship these two have* *because—hey, he’s not fucking blind. The cat had moved to give Alastor a hug, however* *reluctantly, when the man had returned from the supposed dead a handful of months ago.*

*And, holy shit, it’s been months?*

*What the fuck?*

*Anyway—*

*Clearly, obviously, there’s some sort of affection or genuine loyalty there* somewhere *even if* *the drunk doesn’t want to acknowledge it or admit to it.*

*Who is Lucifer to judge anyone for running away from the truth. That’s* literally *his M.O.*

“*The fuck are y’all thinking happens to us here if he gets caught and goes down up there*? *He’s kind of the face of the hotel outside of the princess over there. You can kiss everything* *goodbye then for sure*—along *with the lives of everyone here because if he gets caught, gets* killed—*that’s all the reason they need to launch the full frontal attack on hell they’ve been* *fucking gagging—do not* start, *Angel*—”

*Angel slumps.*

*“—to do that’s probably worse than any extermination we’ve ever faced.”*

*“Exactly!” Vaggie had jumped in, taking her cue, ever the cautious one, playing the role that* *Lucifer himself should be fucking playing if it wasn’t for the fact that he had made this* *fucking deal—for his daughter or not—and that deal involved going along with this wild* *fucking plan that supposedly would help his daughter even though the slightest thing going* *wrong would fuck all of them over colossally. “Heaven may be scared, sure, but that also* *means that they’re on a hair pin trigger. We could lose everything—could lose everyone.”*

He sighs, rubbing at Charlie’s shoulders and pinching at his temples with the other hand, trying desperately to stem the burgeoning headache.

He really fucking shouldn’t have agreed to this.

“Yeah, I know, hon’, I know.”

“I just—I trust Al. I do! It’s just—Husk isn’t wrong? And—and Vaggie isn’t wrong either. I *know* that. I’m—I just—” she sighs, posture curved in defeat, hands folded in her lap, fingers fiddling with each other in a desperate attempt for a distraction. “You were right, dad—about—about heaven not listening. I—I did everything they asked. A soul got *redeemed* and yet—”

“Charlie…”

“It’s just—it’s so annoying! It’s so unfair! Alastor has to risk his life—and all of us have to, again, because of it—just to get some piece of information for us to be able to—to—to *bully* heaven into seeing some fucking sense! That’s—that’s *bullshit*, dad. That’s bullshit.”

“I know, Charlie,” he sighs, feeling equally as tired as she seems—probably even more so, if he’s honest. It’s not his first rodeo with heaven. It probably won’t fucking be his last, assuming they all live past this. Heaven is—relentless—in its adherence to staying absolutely fucking stagnant. They never want to do anything, never want to change, never want to try something new. They’re so worries about remaining perfect and untouched and the literal dream of all of humanity, a veritable haven, that they refuse to even try to do something *new*.

It’s frustrating, irritating, fucking downright incensing, and yet—

There’s nothing to really be done about it.

At the end of the day, none of them can leave hell truly. None of them can fight against heaven’s full might if they dared to come down here with all of their strength—if, goodness forbid, another fucking seraphim came down here.

He had been the strongest when he fell, but that means little with millennia out of practice and a healthy dose of trauma to fuck up any of his might, and, it wouldn’t even matter. Hell isn’t equipped to battle a seraphim—much less the pride ring. And who’s to say they would stop at the pride ring if they came down here. They could—they would—wipe out all of hell, every ring, every hell-born, every sin and sinner. They’re all the same in the eyes of heaven. They wouldn’t care.

And, truthfully, it’s because of that—all of that—that, no matter how stupid, he had to agree with Alastor’s little plan. More than the deal, more than his daughter, more than anything else, he had to agree with Alastor because Alastor’s plan was really the only course of action that made any sort of sense with their current circumstances. It’s a fucking barmy ass idea—absolutely wild, completely insane, and with a risk factor big enough to put all of hell in peril, not just Alastor or the hotel—and yet—

Heaven was nothing if not talented at tying his hands.

Seriously, was it a kink or something? Because he’s gotta be honest—he’s never been super into the whole ‘B’ in the ‘BDSM’ shtick. He much prefers his limbs free and clear, thanks very much. If heaven could just let him use his safe word, that would be fucking wonderful—an even greater fucking thanks. But, of course, hilariously, heaven never seemed to be super big on the consent thing—probably why they hated the idea of free will so much.

*Ha*.

He’s truly come full circle.

“He’s going to be fine,” he breathes, reclines, braces himself to absorb her weight as she falls into him even more, sniffling softly, frustration making her nose hot, and he strokes at her back and gazes at the prone figure lying before them. His shadow is absent.

“I know. I know! I just—”

“I know it’s unfair. I know, hon’. But, it’s like you said—we just have to trust in him. Out of all of us, he’s the best at playing the board, right?”

At this Charlie sits up, brow furrowed, something like confusion on her face when she meets his eyes, and Lucifer meets her gaze hesitantly, not really knowing where he fucked up with what he just said. He didn’t say something weird, did he? He’s pretty certain everything he said was normal, encouraging, ‘I love my daughter and want her to feel better’ crap. Had he—had he not? Was that—not—what he got across?

“Didn’t you, uh, ‘play the board’, too?”

He blinks—*oh*—snorts—shakes his head as he laughs, scraping a hand over his face and feels that building urge to just scream again—or cry—or both—probably both. Him? Play the board? He was playing checkers with Alastor and still somehow fucking lost even though, supposedly, he—won? And she thinks he played the board with *heaven*? Truly, kids see their parents as better than they are because *what*—how did she get this impression of him?

“Dad?”

“Sorry, I—I’m not laughing at you, just—pft—” he turns his face away and desperately tries to muffle his deranged giggles, eyes scrunching, grasping helplessly for a shred of self-control to make the laughter stop. He can see Charlie pouting grumpily out of the corner of his eye, looking so much like she did when she was just a babe and he could lift her with just one hand—well, he can still do that, easy-peasy, more like when she could *fit* in just one hand—that it makes his heart ache, laughter literally strangled out of him by a sudden wave of emotion that reminds him that he is—

He’s *terrible* at playing the game. He *sucks* at it—*beyond* sucks at it—and—and Charlie—Charlie’s on the chopping block this time. He sucks at playing the game, so he’s trusting someone else to play it for them, and he’s—is he trustworthy? Is Alastor worthy of his trust when the consequence if his judgment is wrong is *Charlie?*

He seriously shouldn’t have agreed to this plan.

He inhales.

“When I—when I ‘played the board’, as you said, I—” his lips purse and he draws himself into a more comfortable position, almost flinching when Charlie settles her head onto his lap, peering up at him with those big, big eyes, and she’s so—so much like *him*, so little of Lilith present in her that it’s laughable and yet entirely a blessing, carding his fingers through the fringes of her hair and thinking back to those days he really would rather fucking forget, “—I was a seraphim when I fell. The highest rank amongst the angels.”

Nodding along to information she already knows, Charlie reaches for his free hand and threads their fingers together, toys with the edges of his gloves and runs her fingers along the seams, expression serious—not that he’s present enough in the moment to fully appreciate it or how much of her attention she’s giving him so religiously.

“More than being a seraphim, I was the—strongest?—amongst them all. I wish that was just me being full of myself, but—you’ve seen heaven. You’ve met the seraphim.”

The seraphim’s powers—the current seraphim and all of the current angels and higher powers in heaven—came from bureaucracy. They used politics to get the results they wanted, banded together into some sort of governing body because alone they—they weren’t much, really. Power scale-wise, the seraphim still ranked strongest, of course. But, amongst the seraphim, not one held a candle to him. He was the golden boy, the peak, the apex, zenith, fucking Mount Everest of the seraphim. And he wishes it was just ego talking, but it really fucking isn’t.

Like—*fuck*—he created *hell*.

*Hell!*

For all that it’s a prison now, it hadn’t started as that. It was a world, a *whole world*, separate from Eden and heaven—something entirely his that he had created to have a space in this existence that was well and truly free, where creation could flourish, where creatures of all kind could spawn, where he could escape and be and *breathe*. And it wasn’t enough back then because that wasn’t his home, it wasn’t where he spent most of his time, and he wanted the world he did have to be in constantly to just reflect the dynamic existence in this little dimension he had made—but he wishes it had been enough.

If only it had been enough…

No.

*No.*

It’s good that it hadn’t been. If it had been enough, there would’ve been no Lilith. And without Lilith, there would’ve been no Charlie. And Charlie—for however long he’s been absent from her existence—is still the greatest thing he’s ever had enter his long, long, *long* life, and he wouldn’t trade her for the world. He’d sooner burn hell and everything in it and return to his shackles in heaven than give her up.

The seraphim relied on bureaucracy.

Lucifer relied on pure power.

This little distinction, however irrelevant seeming, made him uniquely entirely unqualified to play the game. He was absolutely *nothing* in comparison to even Adam when it came to the bureaucratic process. He was *strong*, and, until he royally fucked up, that had always been enough. But heaven is politics—heaven is rules—and Lucifer was—he was an eccentric, and, honestly, he was bound to fuck up. And when he did, what had always been enough was no longer.

“I lived so long and yet never learnt to play the game. I’m afraid that, in this, I’m just as unsure as you. Hell is—hell is easier. Sinners are honest about what they want. Heaven is—”

“They’re snakes.”

Lucifer huffs a laugh at her petulant tone and the idea of heaven being the very thing that had been attributed to him for so many years. It’s comedic, just imagining all of them with serpentine bodies and scales and venomous little fangs—useless for creatures whose bark is the only thing they know how to use. It’s hilarious, actually, really.

That Pentious guy must be fitting right in.

“Why would mom want to be *there* of all places…”

Ouch.

*Ouch*.

Fuck.

How could he think he was done having to acknowledge that stupid fucking notebook? ‘One conversation does not healing make’, after all, or something like that. He should know. He’s talked to his ducks *lots* of times, and he sure as fuck isn’t anywhere close to healed. Hell, he even had the little emotional thing, meeting, get-together or something with Alastor a couple weeks ago, and he’s still not doing any better, really, so—well—

He’s kind of better?

He talked to his daughter, at least?

He accomplished his goal?

Sort of?

Admittedly, though, all of that came with new complications he feels entirely ill-equipped to deal with, so…

Well.

Anyway—

He shouldn’t have expected the conversation to be anywhere close to over.

And what the fuck does he say to that? Hell, he doesn’t get it either! He’s been wondering the same thing for ages and come up with zilch, *nada*, nothing, and—well, the fuck is he supposed to say? “Lilith always wanted paradise. She just wanted it on her terms.” It’s not a *lie*, but, honestly, it’s just his best understanding of what the truth *could* be—a guess, to put it politely. He couldn’t actually *know* unless he, well, *talked* to her which, *ha*, was not gonna fucking happen—ha, ha, ha, no, thanks, he’s all good on that.

“Even still…” Charlie mumbles, and—oops, shit, fuck, he said that out loud? He said that out loud.

Yikes.

He seriously needs to control his mouth better. What the fuck was wrong with him, always spouting shit without thinking it through? How many more years would his brain to mouth filter be on vacation, huh? So rude of it to just up and leave with no warning. He had high hopes for himself and all that—or something—but then his mouth would open and just yap, yap, yap every thought in his head and, well—is that something he’s supposed to be proud of?

Seriously, he needs to get it together.

“Dad?”

“Hm?”

“Do you—hate me? Or—or, like, I don’t know, regret having me? Considering…”

What.

“What—no! *No*, never, honey, *never*. I would—I could never—you are—” Well, fuck, here come the waterworks. “You are the the single greatest thing that ever came from that point in my life. You’re the single greatest thing to come out of *any* point in my life. I just—wish I’d been there for the entirety of yours. If there’s *anything* I regret, it’s definitely that.”

Charlie’s eyes are wet and huge and beseeching, and he tugs her prone frame into his arms and squeezes her tightly, tight enough that her bones creak and her muscles spasm and then even tighter still, like he never wants to let her go because he doesn’t. He really doesn’t. And, hey, she looked like she needed a hug—a very tight, very snuggly hug.

Hell, she looked like she needed a couple hundred of them, honestly.

He could only hope they’d be around long enough for him to give her every single one she needed and more.

“You are a gift, Charlie,” he breathes, whispers, weaves reverently into her hair letting her tug away so that he can wipe the tears on her cheeks and give her the most fond, sappy, achingly open smile he can muster with every fiber of his being, gazing deep into her eyes, so much like his own—she’s so much like him—“And the good kind of gift—not the kind that Alastor gives out.”

She puffs out a laugh, startled, nodding shakily, this aborted little bob of her head as her lower lip wobbles and her nose runs.

His heart aches.

He smiles harder.

She looks like she needs one of those, too.

---

When Alastor comes to, it’s to the undoubtedly unflattering image of Lucifer conked out on the couch with drool dripping out of the corner of his mouth and one leg thrown up on the arm rest like it’ll make the couch any comfier when, really, they all know that it’s nothing compared to a fucking bed. And, see, he knows that this is the image that Alastor comes awake to because Alastor’s return from heaven is anything but quiet, his microphone shrieking with a feedback so piercing that his eardrums *throb*, practically curdling into his skull in an effort to get away, startling him so badly that he jumps in spot, choking on his saliva as he falls off the couch instead of, like, *getting up* like a *normal* person because his fucking leg is hooked on the arm rest and he fucking topples instead, and—

*“Release me.”*

And, yeah, there’s also that.

So, all in all, not fucking quiet at all, not even a bit, and, really, he has no idea how to address any part of it while lying upside down, foot hanging awkward and crooked around the armrest, arms splayed, hacking a fucking lung, and the Radio Demon peering down at him bemusedly, big red eyes and fluffy red hair spilling over, large ears perked straight up in interest, blinking owlishly down at him.

“Did you have a nice rest, my good man?”

“I want to say that you did that on purpose, but there’s literally no fucking way that’s possible.”

“My, my, don’t shortchange my credits, dear. I am a man of many skills.”

He scowls, and the deer just moves his head a little further into view so that he can see the full breadth of the shit-eating grin the guy is wearing, all bright and happy and not at all fucking concerned with the alarm he just caused Lucifer for *no good fucking reason*. He maintains that there’s no way it was done on purpose—*no way*. Alastor was out cold, and his shadow was, obviously, nowhere to be found, off gallivanting in heaven collecting the last of their leverage to use against heaven, and—

Oh, shit! Right.

“Did anyone see you?”

“Hm?” Alastor blinks.

“In heaven!” He shouts, scrambling to his feet, whirling to look at the deer demon who is—kicking his legs? Like a fucking schoolgirl? Is the world just delighting in fucking with him or something? What the fuck? Okay, whatever, not the *point*—“Did anyone see you?”

*“Ah,”* Alastor drawls, resting his chin on interlaced fingers, head tilting, legs still kicking, and it’s infuriating that Alastor doesn’t even need to move his head all that much to maintain eye contact—although the bed is unfairly high, in his humblest of humble opinions, “of course! Quite a few.”

*“What?”*

He was a fool.

A fucking fool.

He should have never trusted—he should have never—he shouldn’t have—he really shouldn’t have—

What are they going to do? What is he going to tell Charlie? Is he—would he even be able to fight the seraphim if they came down? Would he have the nerve to even stand up and face heaven again? Fuck, and Lilith—what would happen with her—what would happen with Charlie—

“Oh, do stop panicking. It’s unsightly.”

“The fuck do you mean, ‘stop panicking’? You were seen! That means that *we*,” he makes sure to gesticulate wildly to *truly* get his point across since Alastor’s such a showman—maybe he’ll get the point through his thick fucking skull if he makes it as big as his ego, the *dick*, “are all screwed. And you’re saying, ‘stop panicking’? Are you serious right now?”

“Quite.”

And now Alastor’s getting up—or, well, teleporting, shadow traveling, fucking ink puke maneuvering his way to sit on the recently vacated couch, legs crossed, looking no worse for wear even though this whole endeavor should’ve been a *huge* fucking strain on his powers. Hell, breaking Alastor’s contract had him bedridden for three days and Lilith is *not* as strong as him—there’s no way that Alastor isn’t at least a little winded from—what did he even do? Astral project himself into his shadow and go to heaven or some shit? Fuck, he really should’ve asked for more details before agreeing. Or, really, rather, shouldn’t have agreed *to* *begin with!*

*“Quite?”*

“You forget—I had a contract and received instructions from heaven. How do you suppose those were communicated?”

“Well—I—“

“A portal from heaven opening up or an angel showing up as a messenger—that would not be subtle at all, don’t you agree?”

“I—*what?*” Oh, he is so shaving this asshole’s head the next time he sleeps, the fucker. “What about that whole ‘grave danger’ thing, then? Why the fuck did you have us so worried about sending you up there?”

Bright red eyes stare at him unblinkingly, head cocking, the tips of his ears twitching sporadically, seeming like he’s trying to understand or decode or fucking read the plain English Lucifer just spoke like it’s written in the air because he just can’t seem to *comprehend*—“I never said the danger was going to heaven. The danger was being under contract in heaven. I felt I made that very clear to you.”

He’s going to shave this dude’s head *right* after he’s done tearing his own hair out because, excuse him, *what*? Made *clear?* Made *what* clear, exactly? What part of this was clear? What part of *any of this* has been clear? Alastor is a walking, talking fucking question mark and not one single thing he says can be misconstrued as being something as banal as *clear*. If there are details he’s supposed to pick up every time they talk, then they really need to be trussed up in neon lights and spectacle because he is *not* getting it.

“Are you kidding me right now?”

“No, I don’t think so.”

“What part of *any of that* was clear? You had me worried for *nothing*—I mean—”

“You were worried?”

“—*Charlie* was worried, and I—what was clear? What fucking hint did you drop that I missed? Speak plainly for once, I swear to *fuck*, you—” Whatever else he wants to say is cut off by a frustrated groan as he marches to the other side of the room and then marches back and then marches away—and then smartly puts the bed between them before he lunges over and strangles the man by his tiny fucking throat. How so much noise and annoyance can spew from the world’s tiniest voice box, he’ll never fucking know.

Alastor stares at him, eyebrow cocked in something like amusement or confusion or both, he doesn’t really know. He doesn’t really care to know either. All he knows is that he was so concerned, was thinking of every possible god-awful turn of events that ended with him losing everything because he agreed to this stupid harebrained scheme only to find out that, actually, ha, so funny, there was nothing to worry about at all! Isn’t that hilarious?

Ha, ha, *ha!*

Fake laugh, hiding real pain.

“Alright, then, let me perfectly clear, then, for your ease of mind. As per our agreement, my soul—I want it back.” Alastor’s leg swings up to cross over his knee, chin leaning on his palm, elbow propped on the armrest—every inch of him a replica of Lucifer the last time they had sat in a room together. “Your terms? I’m sure you’ve had plenty of time to rethink them.”

Rethink—*rethink them?*

When the fuck was he supposed to have time to think them over and come up with better ones when he was a little busy fucking *panicking*?

Okay, okay, no problem—he can think of some. He’s just gotta—he’s just gotta stall, that’s all. *Yeah*, just *stall*—he’s great at that.

“Yeah, uh, how much like the first time is this gonna be? Because, you know, believe it or not, I’m don’t really fancy destroying hell. You know, my daughter kind of lives here, so, you know—it’s just a little inconvenient if it just all goes—boom—or something?”

The shadows swell.

“Are you reneging on our deal?”

“What—*no*—no. No. No, no, no. I’m just—*asking*—a question. A simple—simple—question.”
