The light returns to the room—*dramatic*—and Alastor blinks—like he hadn’t just been ready to tear him limb from limb, or, well, make a good attempt at it—ears twitching, resembling more of a puppy than a fucking deer—or maybe more of a doe than a fully grown deer? But—whatever—again, not the point—

“That was breaking a contract. This is renegotiating one. The power output should be nowhere close to the same.”

“Right.”

Right.

Cool.

Okay.

So.

Terms.

And conditions.

Yup.

That thing that no one fucking reads and yet someone gets paid the big bucks to write.

Yeah.

So—what are the terms, Luci’? What’s the plan? What are the conditions?

Alastor stares.

Yeah. Blank. His mind is fucking blank. Of course, it is.

The one time he needs it to be running a mile a minute, and instead it’s on fucking vacation. Unbelievable. He should’ve seen this coming.

“Are the current terms still acceptable?”

“Absolutely not,” he bites, knee-jerk and impulsive, yet again, his mouth moving without filtering the thought from his brain first—he seriously needs to get that under control or, like, looked at, professionally, by a doctor, or a lobotomist—and yet Alastor takes it in stride, ever levelheaded compared to him despite the fact that he is *so* many ranks beneath him.

Wow, he is, in a word, pathetic.

He should get that tattooed—probably on his forehead. Or maybe on his hand? That way he remembers it every time he goes to do literally anything.

Perfect.

“So—”

“Can you give me a second here? I’ve been kind of busy.”

Alastor’s eyes slide over to the cooling wet patch of drool Lucifer had been steadily working into the couch before his rude awakening, eyelids raising into that wide, doe-eyed look that expresses more judgment than any deer looking asshole should ever be possible of showcasing. Self-consciously, he wipes at his chin, turning his eyes away when Alastor’s gaze meanders back to him in a very pointed, *‘Busy?’*, brow furrowing and dragging his fingers through his hair like it would make him look marginally more put together.

He could just fix it with magic, but that would be cheating. And obvious. And even more embarrassing than whatever the fuck it is that he’s doing right now—which is already pretty fucking embarrassing, if he says so himself. No sense in making it worse.

New terms, new terms, new terms… Fuck, why couldn’t he think of anything? Maybe because the first deal had been Alastor’s idea? This was his game they were playing, after all. Although—giving him the chance to renegotiate when he’s the one that owns his soul now—

He blinks, jaw falling slack for a moment.

How—how much power does he hold right now? How had he not realized? How had he been sitting here feeling like a loser and like he didn’t know how to play the game when he *owns* Alastor’s *soul*? He literally has the ultimate bargaining chip! He has the one thing Alastor wants back more than anything, the one thing that he has the power to return or keep at will—the one thing that would allow him to propose whatever the fuck his heart desires right now and Alastor would have no choice but to comply or risk never getting his soul back, or, alternatively, having even worse terms put upon him. No matter what, they’re still going to be bound by a deal at the end, even if he no longer has Alastor’s soul. They’re still binding.

Whatever he proposes now—he’s been given an easy pass through the toll littered maze of Alastor’s game.

Holy shit.

This really was victory.

Alastor hadn’t been kidding.

*And*, of course, this means that he really is a moron because it took him—how long had it been?—way too fucking long to notice.

But, whatever! He’s got the ultimate bargaining chip, so, of course, what he goes with is: “I want your deal with Charlie terminated.”

*Ha!* Take *that*, Vaggie’s depressed, guilty little face that keeps looking at him like the lamb she sent to slaughter! He *can* do this!

Alastor’s grin is a slow, easy thing, more of a Cheshire Cat than Husk could ever play at being—or like if the Cheshire Cat and the Mad Hatter fucked and had a kid and named it Alastor, fucking creepy, loopy, insane as all hell pompous ass deer—a Cheshire Hatter? Mad Cat? Chemad Hatcat? Madshire Catter?—and his eyes flit and roll like he’s considering it even as his head lowers and rises in a slow nod, easy acquiescence.

“And you can’t harm her or this hotel if you decide you get bored with this place and don’t want to help anymore.”

Another slow nod.

“And—and—“ Fuck, one more, one more—he’s gotta have more terms than this, right, what the fuck? Alastor’s had been so detailed, right down to the protection of the hotel and the souls under his contract and the retrieval of his own soul in the aftermath. His—his sound like a toddler trying to argue for getting extra chocolate chips in his pancakes. Pathetic.

“And?”

Fuck.

“And you let me run my design ideas by you!”

What.

Lucifer, what.

Lucifer, seriously, *what*.

What the *fuck?*

Heaven, send down the mightiest soldier and slay him where he stands because, Lucifer, what in the actual what—

Those damnable ears perk higher.

“The duck?”

If there were sparkles, he’s certain they’d be surrounding Alastor right now.

“Amongst—uh—other things. I—design *more* than just the—just the ducks, I just—I like the ducks the best…”

A hum, placent and light, before Alastor was rising in a smooth motion, staff spinning in his fingers before tucking behind his back as he rounds the bed, hand held out. “Anything else?”

Uh? Keep being someone he won’t regret trusting, however little, maybe? Keep being the person he kind of, sort of, maybe likes, just a little? Actually be the helpful person he’s framed himself to be, maybe, just for a bit—just long enough for him to not feel bad about himself for being fooled if he suddenly turns around and stabs them all in the back, maybe?

His hand reaches out.

“Don’t lie to me.”

The hand in his is cold despite the gloves, and the surge of power that blasts through the room is, admittedly, less than last time and yet no less intense in the way it feels like his very atoms are vibrating apart, like he’s going to fracture out of existence, green and gold a brilliant kaleidoscope around them as the room feels like it expands, shadows bouncing and laughing, and then—

There’s something haunting, something damning, with the way hell abruptly goes quiet suddenly as the chain snaps, electronics malfunctioning in a ray of static and colored pixels, music cutting out, the only sounds those of its denizens that abruptly fall silent at the sudden hush. It’s a ghost town, abruptly, not even a sigh let out, everyone waiting on bated breath as the world grinds to a halt with a crack.

And then the static comes alive, the radio waves pumping out a blare of trumpets and horns that has people crumpling and grabbing at their ears, screams rising up in a choir, shadows ripped from every corner, every overhang, every building—like the light of hell blared bright for a moment and washed it all away—contracted sinners falling to their knees, what everyone has and no one can lose going rogue, leaving its owners bereft as they *rip* apart—

Lucifer thinks, distantly, dazedly, head spinning, that he may have just severely fucked up as the world lights up green and, in the chaos, an echoing laugh filters into his ears, drums pounding against his eardrums—a jazz orchestra unlike any other as the being in front of him spins away with a manic gleam, contract nullified and renegotiated—*free*—

Alastor’s a thing of horror, a thing of beauty, disappearing into the cloak of shadows and taking all of the darkness with him, manifesting at the entrance of the hotel, ink skittering towards him from every direction of the Pride Ring, laughing visages, like a family welcoming their own home. His own form is cloaked in the dark, dripping with it, bending and cracking grotesquely, forming large, misshapen, a hulking, massive thing with too many limbs and too many joints, as fluid as the shadows that feed into it, as disjointed as the bones that make it up, as haunting as the laugh that keeps spilling out, Lucifer unable to do anything but take it in from the window, feeling distinctly like he has somehow, inexplicably, irrevocably fucked up, like maybe he’s just let loose a monster he’s not sure he knows how to control, really.

Had he been so arrogant as to let himself get controlled by his own stupid heart?

Did he really let himself fail to see the big picture again, too caught up in the wonder of someone seeming to genuinely like him for the first time in forever?

Did he lose sight of who Alastor really was?

Had he—had he really thought, for a single, damnable, irreversible second that these sinners really could change?

Alastor’s form is a hulking thing, making the hotel appear tiny in comparison, minuscule, so massive that Lucifer can barely imagine where his head is, only able to see the massive antlers from his point of view, and he can’t even imagine what it looks like from the city, blotting out the pentagram and the sun—purgatory, and heaven—plunging the world into a darkness only Alastor himself can control—

His coat is a gaping, giant thing, its ragged ends seeming to make more sense now as they expand, balloon, like a tent, lengths brushing the floor, and it—it looks like a circus tent—it looks like a *circus* tent—it’s a fucking *circus*—

His throat clogs, eyes burning, and it’s—it’s too much—he doesn’t know what to do—doesn’t know what to do—what the *fuck* did he just *do*—

Hell shrieks as power lines go down under a massive clawed hand, gouging the earth beneath it, leaving an indelible mark on the ring and all who inhabit it—the air shaking around the frequencies of the pummeling music, turning brains to liquid, shaking the window panes until a shower of glass powders the ground, the entirety of the ring collapsing around the might of the demon they clearly had seen nothing from yet—

*“Good to be back on the air.”*

A tear slips down his cheek—and he can’t tell if it’s from horror or delight or the fucking wind or the pain in his ears or something else—burning in its heat, scarring in its damnation, listening to the pounding of footsteps coming up to the door and it slamming open, Husk’s shouting voice barely audible over the cacophony of trumpets—

What the fuck had he done—

He had played right into his hands. He had—he had—

*Alastor’s grin is a slow, easy thing, more of a Cheshire Cat than Husk could ever play at* *being.*

Charlie—he’s so sorry.

He really is so bad at playing the board.
