## Chapter 7: loser's gambit

### Summary

She looks at the demon at her feet, crumpled and used, damaged and broken, and yet smile stubbornly persisting despite her best efforts, and thinks that—yes, this was exactly what she needed. And, yes, this was exactly the tool she wanted. And, yes, she would get the smile off his face if it was the last thing she did. And, yes—he did look rather pretty slumped at her feet.

### Notes

okay, in my humble defense, the 3-5 business months thing was supposed to be a joke, okay? it was just a little jokey-joke. it just so happens to be that god has an even greater sense of humor than i do and he saw my jokey-joke and decided he had the opportunity to do the funniest thing ever. genuinely not my fault, i swear.

anyway-

here we are! final installment! so exciting!

despite my many years writing and my plethora of patience, i do want to preface this off with: i have not and, more than likely, will never ask for con-crit. while i can appreciate the good intentions behind it, i do not need or want it. fic writing is incredibly personal and an art form we writers are letting you appreciate in a public space. these fics are written for me primarily, and i just hope that you find a fraction of the joy in them that i do and perhaps get to interact on mutually shared interests and have some fun. while i am more than happy to answer questions, i do not need or want guidance on my work. this is for fun, it is not professional, and, quite frankly, it is the perfect avenue for me to try new things that is not possible in my professional work which means i especially do not want to suffer through peer-review i didn't sign up for. just-

remember: the exit button is there any time that you decide this isn't your cup of tea. you do not need to share with me why this wasn't your preferred flavor of fun. you can just go. it is really quite simple.

do not misinterpret that as hostile. it is simply a reminder on fandom etiquette. recently, it seems to be a forgotten art, as i've noted in both my comment sections and in others. please respect authors. if you want something different, try your hand at writing something yourself. that's the main reason i write and the main reason i post (my preferred flavor of fic is hard to come by, so i do it myself). however, our work is not target practice for your writing class. please realize that not everyone wants your advice.

(while this isn't the main reason this update took so long, it did have me putting this fic on the back burner for a bit until i managed to quell my temper--by which then life got in the way and, well, my joke turned into reality because i truly am a master at aiming for the stars.)

now, with that out of the way: please enjoy the last chapter, my good people! as always, i am terribly sorry for the wait. it really wasn't supposed to take this long.

enjoy!

### Text

Alastor is a bad boy.

Scratch that—Alastor is *the* bad boy.

He’s the baddest baddie of the bad baddie coalition to ever bad in all of baddie world, and anyone that disagrees is *not* a bad boy.

If anyone ever bothered to ask her why she ‘agreed’ to a deal with Alastor—not that she had agreed to much of anything, seeing as it had been *her* idea, much like Husk’s was, not that he seems to embrace that knowledge all that much—why she happily does what he requests and talks to him like they’re equals and climbs all over him without fear—all of which, she would like to point out, she’s allowed to do, so, really, it’s not like it’s outlandish or something that she takes advantage of at all—she would say that, quite simply, Alastor’s a bad boy. And she loves a bad boy. She especially loves a bad boy that’s a bad boy *and* a good boy at the same time because Alastor—

Alastor’s a bad boy.

He’s not a bad guy.

It’s an important distinction and only those less well read than herself would dare to say otherwise—which people do basically all the time, which, as Alastor always tells her as he helps clean up the messes she makes even though she delights in cleaning up after because he’s nice, is a *them* problem, not a *her* problem.

Scrambling outside to see him in all his glory, this amazing bad boy she’s chosen to follow to the ends of the ring and beyond in all of his grandeur and inspiring nature, she’s abruptly reminded of every reason she ever had for deciding to fall into line with him, why she decided to make a deal with him, why she decided to love him. He is, for all intents and purposes, her muse, her art, her knight, her light—he *is* the standard.

And when he comes down from the high—the high she doesn’t know the origin of but worships all the same—dusting himself off like nothing has happened, like buildings haven’t been leveled, like sinners haven’t died, like there isn’t a crater where his hand just was, it’s confirmed even further for her because he is—he *is*—a bad boy.

He is everything.

He is hell itself, bad and good and tortured and lost and fractured and put together and just—he’s everything, everything she’s ever wanted.

(A shame he never expressed an interest in her, but that’s okay. She thinks she likes him more this way. He’s perfect, anyway.)

And when he turns to her, smile wide and beaming, and simply chuckles when she scrambles up his leg onto his shoulder, poking him about what inspired such a sudden show of power that will become fodder for her back when she’s in the privacy of her rooms and her vents and her job and her fun—when she pushes her forehead into his and squeezes her eye shut and just breathes him in, chaos and ozone and black ink, him carefully tilting his head so that his antlers are out of the way—

And when he scoops her up and sets her delicately on the ground as he begins to make his way inside of the hotel as the rest of them come out, shared looks of horror on their faces, asking Niffty what she wants to eat because he always asks her what she wants to eat whenever he plans to cook because he’s a bad boy, not a bad guy, and that makes him a sweetheart with a spine, and she loves him for it, she *loves* him for it—

And when he waves them all off with a smile and steps back into the hotel and lets her follow him to the kitchen and watch and *help*—

She *knows*.

Alastor is a bad boy.

And, despite the misgivings the others have that they think she doesn’t know or understand—like she’s somehow significantly younger than them when she’s been in hell longer than some of them even if she died early (and she would think that maybe they’d take a hint, then, and realize that maybe she’s done some fucked up shit in her time to be here, trusted, under an overlord as powerful as Alastor, when she died barely into her twenties—she knows, she understands, and she thinks that they’re all just pussy-footing around the very big crux of their issue which is that—

Alastor’s a bad boy.

And not everyone knows how to appreciate a bad boy.

But she does—she does.

Because Alastor had saved her from a *fake* bad boy—from almost falling in with the wrong sort, from almost ending up with an eternity ahead of her tied to a man that was so charismatic that he almost had her *deceived*, from almost ending up forfeiting her afterlife because like *hell* would she have stuck around someone like that—had scooped her up and whisked her away and shown her a world next to the side of someone that’s actually worth a shit, and that’s—she has no misgivings. She has no doubts. She has no regrets.

A bad boy does not mean a bad guy, she thinks—she feels, she knows—helping him as he mans the chopping board while she washes the vegetables.

And Alastor’s proof of that—leveled buildings aside.

Or, perhaps, because of.

It was pretty sexy of him, after all.

*Very* sexy.

She giggles, even as Charlie bursts in with questions, questions, so many questions—she’s always asking questions—and Husk lingers by the door, his expression haunted—he’s always so dramatic, the goofball—and Lucifer stumbles through a portal, his eyes wide, his cheeks wet, looking like he had seen a ghost or had the most religious experience of his life—and he’s like her, she can tell; from the way he looks at the baddest baddie of the bad baddie coalition to ever bad in all of baddie world, she can tell that they’re the same—

She giggles.

Yeah—not a bad guy, at all.

---

“How do you do it?”

He doesn’t mean for the question to come out, doesn’t mean to give voice to his thoughts inside that he should know better than to let loose, doesn’t mean for it to hang in the air in between them, a query begging for clarification that he does not want to bestow upon it, his tongue far too loosened by myriad factors and yet no less incriminating. Pinned beneath eyes of ownership, assessed by the maws of a villain in every sensible person’s ledger, he feels stuck, five words transporting him back in an instant to a room, a couch, a blackout night—knife—he wishes he could forget.

The room is silent—a rare occurrence—just them—an even rarer one, still—and he swallows thickly, feeling his hair prickle as he meets those eyes that had spelled freedom so long ago and yet now—

Had it been freedom he’d been lusting after back then? Or had it been something else?

What had he been so desperate for?

Was the pressure under this thumb any different?

Or did he just want to escape the blame of the pressure being entirely self-inflicted?

“Do what, exactly, Husker dear?”

Well—no pretending he didn’t say shit now. He wasn’t being let off the hook that easily.

He should know that by now.

There’s no such thing as being ‘let off the hook’ when it came to Alastor to begin with, the fucking sadist.

His claws tap across the selection of bottles he has behind the bar counter, for once uneasy to grab another, debating types and proofs like it has any fucking bearing on whether or not he’s going to drink it—he’s just feeling a little too unstable to be off his rocker in the presence of Alastor, a level of survival he previously never fucking possessed.

His tail sways.

“Just,” he grabs a bottle, pours out a glass like it’ll slow his gullet, swirls the liquid around, pours another one out for Alastor—not that the guy had asked—and slides it over—wonders vaguely how many fingers it takes to inebriate Alastor to the degree that Mimzy spoke of, if he could get him anywhere close, “how do you always—always—get—what you want?” The words are halting, hesitant, gaze smartly turned away, focused on the swirling amber in his glass like he’s seeing it for the first time, tonguing at the backs of his teeth.

He doesn’t know why he’s alone with Alastor right now, really—doesn’t remember where the others are suddenly, barely suppressing a flinch when Alastor’s hand rises to grab his glass, raising it to his lips and yet not taking a sip, just resting it against his lower lip as his head tilts forward, fingers only just barely holding the glass, like he’ll let it drop free at any moment—like he could take it or leave it, consequences be damned because he just *really* doesn’t give a *fuck*—

Husk knocks his own drink back.

Alastor hums.

He’s too fucking sober for this.

“I assume this is in regards to the little noose you wouldn’t shut up about.”

“I made one comment!”

“Don’t protest so much, Husker, dear. It’s off-putting.”

“I’ll off-put your face,” he grumbles, and he takes a swig from the bottle, watches Alastor’s eyebrow cock, glass tipping towards his lips—just wetting them, not drinking still, gaze flitting off somewhere to the side in a slow, lazy blink, cheek rolling to rest against the wrist that holds the glass, free hand cradling his arm, tapping an idle rhythm on imaginary keys to a tune that only Alastor knows—

To a rhythm that’s all too familiar to Husk.

His eyes are on the couch, fingers tip-tip-tapping away, swinging staccato gasps to a music that isn’t even playing in the air and yet he can hear it all the same, and he seals his mouth around the lip of the bottle and swallows, his own eyes staring, besieged, locked on the couch. It’s been cleaned, courtesy of Lucifer, back to its former prestige of soothing velvet, and he can’t look away from the blood red of it, the golden beading along the arms, the plush of the cushions.

He swallows around nothing and gags as he tastes air again, lungs shivering.

He stares at the couch.

“There’s a common misunderstanding,” Alastor muses, and the glass settles on the counter lightly before abruptly sliding to him, coming to harsh stop just before it can topple off, liquid sloshing, and Husk grabs at it as Alastor rises from the stool, steps light even as they click against the floor, shrugging his coat off in a smooth motion, narrow waist and sharp shoulders—everything’s sharp, sharp, sharp—fucker would die before ever being misconstrued as being soft—

He swallows the drink, willfully ignorant to the remnants of the lord that remain on it.

He stares at the couch.

Alastor’s coat is folded neatly and placed along the arm—long, sharp figure slumping into a curve atop of the freshly pristine cushions, edges and corners smoothing out as they’re pressed into soft foam, legs crossing, head tilting back, hair spilling over the back like an invitation. Even his throat is sharp, he notes, throwing aside the bottle in his claws that he hadn’t even realized he’d emptied, grasping another one that’s inhaled before he even finishes taking a full breath, stumbling from behind the counter, swaying to a tune he still only hears in his mind, the tap of claws absent, even. All that fills the air is Alastor’s hum as his eyes, bright and glowing and enticing—promising—slide over to him, bangs a feathered dusting across his forehead, ears sloped back.

He stares at the red.

It’s night—knife—night—and the entirety of hell feels like its fallen into a hush, his own bumbling steps carrying him closer until not even the firelight can dance upon Alastor’s figure, his bulk looming over him, feathers fluffed, wings spread, shadow every bit the avenging angel.

Ignited coals sweep over his body, over his figure, over his shadow, over his everything—his *everything*—and slide closed.

It’s night—knife—night—and he asked a question he shouldn’t have, thought whispers he shouldn’t have, felt cobwebs he shouldn’t have, felt fissures he shouldn’t have. It’s night—knife—night—and he’s drunker than he’s ever not been—not drunk at all, not sober enough, not anywhere in between, lost in the throes, in the whims, in the waves of actions he knows he shouldn’t take. It’s night—knife—night—and he’s besotted with the thought of the glinting and gleaming and cutting and slicing and the appeal of a light on the edge, on the precipice, on the curve of a droplet as it lands, as it sprays, as it bathes.

It’s night—knife—night—

Night—knife—night—

Night—knife—knife—

Knife—knife—knife—

Knife.

He stares at the couch.

The velvet is soft, plush and warm in a way the body under his isn’t and yet is, as unforgiving and cold as that night, that night, that fucking night—and then abruptly warm as claws bury themselves in his side and pull, yank, drag, a rough bite before a palm soothes away the sting and ache, and he—

“There’s a common misunderstanding,” Alastor begins again, his tone not even shaking at the knife embedded in the couch cushion beside him, pressing cold and lethal to the edge of his neck from where it protrudes from the cushions, blade kissing skin. His hand pets, his arm moves, his breaths waft into the air around them, muscles loose, and Husk shakes. “Want and need are the same species—two sides of the same coin.”

The fingers stroke.

He listens to the heartbeat beneath him, claws falling away from a drive he can’t claim to have any sort of ownership of, and quivers.

“To survive isn’t to live—to live isn’t to survive. Want and need—need and want. Do you understand, my dear?”

His claws flex in the cushions.

The hand curves around his back, picking just between his wings, appendages flapping haphazardly as the prick and pick and pull drags even more unsteady breaths from him, tail lashing.

“If you want something, you have to need it. If you need something, you have to want it. If you lack either…”

The tone is pitying.

He stares at the couch.

His face burns.

“I thought you understood. Did you forget the lesson already?”

There’s music, the music, his music—that song, that great, that gasping staccato swing, tip-tip-tapping away, choked and strangled and heave-heave-ho’d away, out of lungs that don’t bear conscience as crackles and static come to life and join in on his own pathetic mewling attempts at comfort as his mind melts and reforms and fucks off to who knows where because he’s not drunk enough—he’s not—he’s not even close—and that means—that means there’s no excuse, no fucking reason, no shield to hide behind now because this time—

Fingers pluck and pull and twist and take, and he lays himself prostrate to all of it, breathing in death and shadows and iron, ozone and dust and smoked coffee, damp and freshness and dark, listening to a tune he’s too familiar with and a tune he will always long to hear again.

“He hasn’t fixed your feathers.”

He stares at the couch.

“Shut up…”

Did he not—did he not want this? Did he not want his freedom that desperately? Was he content? Had he grown complacent? Had he—

The claws pick, pick, pick—

“Be a lamb; put them to bed.”

His shoulders shudder.

The claws pick, pick, pick.

The world fractures and renews in waves, eyes dry and burning, wet and vacant, taking in velvet, velvet, red, red, red, and the jittering of little shadows in his peripherals that he dares not look at, fearing of what or who they could be and yet incapable of moving from his self-appointed post as a human—cat-bird-thing—blanket.

The radio plays.

He hums.

One of them is broken, off-tune.

He won’t say which one.

“You should let him.”

It’s the nicest he’s been to himself tonight.

“Let him what?”

“Fix your feathers.”

The appendages flop, and the demon tuts, and the claws pick, pick, pick.

“The fuck would I need you for, then?”

His head rolls, cheek smushing into sharp angles, brows pulling tight in the middle, metal vanishing from sight like it had never been there, not even the dress shirt damaged by a blade that had only just missed—just missed, just missed, just missed, just—stopped—

Tempered coals reflect the flames, fanned fast by lazy bats, barely wet lips a shallow curve of a facsimile, and the gentle claws pick, pick, pick—“You do not need me, Husker, dear."

Want and need—need and want.

Two sides of the same fucking coin.

Yeah.

That was the fucking problem.

---

Every time he ends up in heaven, ever since the fall, he can’t help the bile that rises in his throat every time he sees those stupid, fucking, disgustingly bright, shiny gates—the chirpy, cheery, cheeky little chic-chick shit stain that greets them from behind a pointless little podium at the end of the long white pathway to escort them through the gates like Lucifer doesn’t know where the fuck he’s going when he used to *live here*—

Heaven doesn’t change.

It kind of sickens him.

No—that’s wrong. It *does* sicken him—not kind of, but *definitely*. Like, genuinely, it’s gross as all fuck. That had been part of his whole fucking problem. Heaven was *boring*—a complete fucking snooze fest, a desperate cry for help for someone with a little more *creativity* to give it the helping hand it so desperately needed because, again, seriously, fucking *snooze*, so he wanted to make Eden interesting. If he was going to be tasked with watching the humans for the rest of *eternity*, the least they could do is be *entertaining*—he wanted them to be creative not just languish, to make shit instead of just taking one, to be blessed with the same gifts the angels were and make something *unexpected* instead of just sitting around, counting the petals on flowers and trying to figure out how to get off.

Heaven is stagnant—the air stale, the mosquitoes practically an actual thing with the way heaven sits like still water, fermenting and rotting, a breeding ground for the flies and larvae and all of the little inconveniences that spread disease and itchiness and strife. Heaven is fertile ground, a festering wound, antibiotics overused until they’re ineffective and the bacteria is resistant and continues to infiltrate and take rank until the entire organism is indisposed.

It’s rotting.

It boring.

It’s disgusting.

But, yeah—ha, *ha*—sure, heaven’s great, Charlie! A real gung-ho place, absolutely flawless, no systematic changes needed *what-so-fucking-ever*. Do whatever, Char-char! Daddy will always be here for you!

His eyes roll.

He should’ve tried harder to dissuade her.

That’s not to say that hell is somehow *perfect*—like, that’s also kind of his whole fucking thing, his whole problem with what’s become of this wonderful little world he had created. It wasn’t perfect! Of course it wasn’t fucking perfect! It’s fucking crawling with sinners and the damned and it’s a fucking *mess*, especially after Lilith left and he had to start doing everything himself *again* which is—*not* fucking *fun*—he was *not* built for administrative work, thanks very much, he’s a *creator*, not a negotiator, so whoever’s bright fucking idea it was to make him king (which was, well, actually his, but *ignoring that*) could go right ahead and fuck right the fuck off, thanks so much, good day, sir—

Anyway—

Hell isn’t perfect.

But it’s, at least, at the very fucking least—like, literally, bare fucking minimum, the bar couldn’t *possibly* be any lower, levels of *least*—honest.

Not like this—not like heaven—not like this—this—charade—

If anyone wanted an example of what an actual charade was, heaven was the perfect fucking example of a front put on for so long and so well that people actually bought into the hot pile of garbage that it was with all the belief in their hearts only to experience the most ultimate forms of disappointment because heaven was, and always would be, a true demon trussed up in a pretty face.

And people thought *he* was bad?

He’s a veritable fucking saint, thanks very much.

Like, the fuck did he do? Give some people free will? It’s not like he asked them to commit murder or rape or go around touching up babies and using corpses to built chandeliers. He didn’t perch himself on their shoulders and whisper to them, *“You know what would be nice?* *Fucking your mother.”* He didn’t do any of that! He just gave them the ability to think outside of the clutches of heaven, to explore a little outside the rules, to make the world their *own* instead of the dollhouse heaven had designed it as.

The fact that they took it and ran with it in such a colossally fucked up way is *not* his fault.

That’s kind of why his people are, obviously, the *fucking worst.*

He glances discreetly—well, as discreetly as is fucking possible when the face he’s glancing at is so much fucking higher than his own, which is just—seriously, who the fuck decides the heights for these forms? How fucking tall was Alastor when he was alive? Did his mother fuck a tree to be able to conceive him? Was there an Ent up there somewhere that donated his chlorophyll to the cause of bringing Alastor to life? Because this—*this*—is so disrespectful, what the fuck—

(He very casually ignores the fact that his beef with Angel about his height is significantly smaller—no fucking pun intended—double no pun intended. That’s entirely unrelated and is directly proportional with the fact that, uh, Angel was—tolerable? Likable! Something in between? More like—Angel was too young and too childish and too fucking—whatever his deal is—to take his place in Charlie’s life, so who cares about how tall he is? He couldn’t be a better version of Lucifer if he tried—except for, well, the social skills part, maybe. Although, going by the multitude of interactions he’s seen around the others with Angel, he can quite confidently say that innuendo is *not* a universal bonding language, so, hey, his social skills might not be *that* bad, after all. Small wins.)

*Anyway*—

Fucking hell, he’s getting distracted again.

He can’t get distracted here.

He really can’t.

He’s already been there, done that, and he really doesn’t want to find out what punishment heaven would try to come up with now that could somehow possibly *top* their already stellar choice of complete banishment and eradication of his mental faculties and overall will to live.

Honestly, he’d probably just kill himself at that point, except he still had Charlie, so—on the back burner for now!

So, anyway, uh—*discreet glance!* Right.

Alastor’s as chipper as always, giving Saint Peter a run for his fucking money in the pep department, smile bright and tight even though he sticks out like a sore fucking thumb amongst all of the white, garishly red against soft pastels, a splotch of blood and darkness amongst an otherwise pristine and pure backdrop. Not even the gold matches with him here—not the way it does in the hotel. No—here he still stands out, a red spider lily in a sea of soft, untouched daisies. He looks like corruption—he moves like sin—

He doesn’t belong.

And yet he’s still fucking smiling, posture relaxed, spinning his microphone, ever the cavalier, laissez faire attitude while Lucifer’s over here having yet *another fucking meltdown* even though he used to *live* here—because, really, it’s not just heaven. No. That’s not the only problem. No, no, no, see—see, here’s the fucking thing—

“Do you think we’ll see mom this time?”

Yeah, see, *that’s* the fucking thing.

*Lilith*.

Truthfully—really, truly, from the bottom of his bottom and the heart of his lungs or however the fuck the saying goes—he’s not ready. He’s just not ready! He hasn’t seen Lilith since she left—quite successfully, he might add! He didn’t *want* to see her, didn’t want to see anyone, really. And it wasn’t like she reached out so it had been *easy* then—although, of course, now he knows *why* she hadn’t cared to reach out, and that makes his blood boil in a way he doesn’t even know how to process because if he thinks about it, he’ll either scream or cry and this is—

He’s on enemy turf.

This isn’t his domain.

What they’re going to do today—it’s already bad enough as is. Adding his own little temper tantrum on top of that? Yeah, that wasn’t going to help matters at fucking all, and, well, he’d very much like to avoid a full frontal assault conducted by heaven on hell, especially when he’s fucking *in* heaven right now and not down there, protecting his shitty fucking denizens because his daughter *cares* for some godforsaken reason about all of those shitty people even after all they’ve done, and, fuck—

Distracted.

He’s distracted.

He needs to focus.

He just has to—

“You know, I must say—this decor is positively dreadful! Why, I think even you would agree, your majesty, that this is truly horrendous. Where’s the personality? The gravitas? The *passion*?”

—focus.

And Alastor’s talking about the decor.

Because, of course, he is. Why wouldn’t he?

It would just be—too normal or something—to expect him to be focused on literally anything else. Like, their meeting, for example.

Silly him—he should’ve known better.

He fights the urge to humbly introduce his palm to his face—repeatedly.

“I fucking know, right? Even Vox can design something better, and his office was garish as all heaven,” he snorts, looking around derisively—because, hey, he never said he was *better* and a distraction is a distraction, no matter the form—ignoring the scandalized look Charlie shoots back at them—be it about the heaven slander or the mention of Vox’s interior design choices or the comparison of Vox’s shitty design choices to heaven’s even shittier ones or—

Shit.

Shit, shit, shit, fucking *shit*.

He wasn’t supposed to say that.

He hadn’t meant to say that.

*Fuck*.

He wasn’t supposed to say that!

How the fuck—he wasn’t supposed to *say that.*

He had been *so good*, had kept that little excursion locked up tight, hadn’t slipped up about it once, hadn’t even mentioned it at all—and he fucking could have. They’ve been dealing with so much serious shit recently that he really could’ve let it slip and mentioned something, confirmed what Alastor might suspect, put his cards on the table, and yet he hadn’t! He’d been so good! He hadn’t fucked up once until fucking—*interior design?* Is he fucking serious right now?

Fucking hell, Luci’!

He had *one* job—just the one.

Fuck.

“Don’t I know it! My word, it was enough to make my stomach want to upturn my lunch. It absolutely ruined the whiskey, yes, indeed!”

“Yeah—ha, ha—yeah—so—so true—ha—ha, ha—*ha*.”

He stares at Charlie’s hair.

Oh, wait, shit, fucking *score.*

Could it be? Was it his lucky day? Had he—had he not noticed? Had he been spared? Has his little slip not been worth a mention? Not even an eyebrow cock?

No, no—there was no way. Alastor was too sharp for that, too quick witted, too fucking conniving to miss any slip of the tongue. If he knew, if he had any suspicions, if all he wanted was to hear it confirmed from Lucifer’s mouth, he would be looking out for that, listening for it. There would be no getting it past him. But maybe—did he think maybe Lucifer had met with Vox under different circumstances? Alastor wasn’t the type to take prying into his past like a champ. He’d be pissed. Maybe he thought—no, but—wait—

“My, even you would think *my* work is better than this.”

“I—” had he really not noticed? “Well,” he chuckles anxiously, fingers twisting tight around the length of his cane, trying his damndest to portray the cool air of a man that didn’t just shove his whole foot into his mouth and was anxiously awaiting the response of a spurned lover or some such bullshit while gargling his own toes, casting a glance to the side as they move past heavens denizens that eye them—mainly Alastor—with morbid curiosity, “the kitchen is actually really nice. I don’t—I mentioned that, didn’t I? Did I not? Ha, ha, I think I might have—”

Alastor truly sticks out so much in heaven, it’s painful.

“I appreciate the compliment, dear.”

Like, seriously, even Charlie blends in more with her pale skin and platinum locks—so much like Lucifer, so much like him, so much like—

Wait, hold up, back the fuck up—

Dear?

*Dear?*

When the hell did he get upgraded to *dear?*

And when the fuck did he start thinking of that as an upgrade?

Fuck.

Lucifer!

He needs to get his head on straight.

Yeah, seriously, he needs a drink—like, pronto, stat, right this fucking second, in his hand, right now—where the hell is Husk when he needs him—

For fuck’s sake—*focus*—

Saint Peter is as much of a godsend as he is the soreness in the sight for sore eyes, chipper little voice grating as he hands them off with a happy little skip, sly eyes slipping back to take in Lucifer with all of the subtlety of a man that has been living with the gracious good fortune of not having to worry about his life being ripped away from him at any given second for pushing the wrong button on the wrong beast, flicking briefly to Alastor before he’s on his merry way.

He pretends he doesn’t notice the way Alastor’s smile stretches just that one inch wider under Peter’s split second gaze, the most unsettling of beams that probably scared the guy off more than anything else, like, oh, say, having the king of hell, the most powerful seraphim, in his presence? He barely withholds the sigh that threatens to come out as he thinks of his drastic fall from grace—how low he’s truly sunk.

Even his demons that don’t hold a candle to his might are more intimidating than him.

Talk about a new fucking low.

Like, seriously, he’s in fucking heaven right now, physically *higher* than everywhere else, and yet he still feels like he’s falling.

He swears, life is just a permanent fucking elevator that won’t stop going down.

“At least the smell here is quite clean,” Alastor continues, as if there had been no awkward pause while Lucifer battled with—far too many things to name, really—steps continuing on jovially. “I have never been the biggest fan of smoke. Murder on the old pipes—you can imagine.”

He snorts—tries to imagine a world where Alastor’s never-ending chatter, on the occasions he was forthcoming with filling the space with his own inane drivel and trivialities (instances of which were rare and few between), is interrupted with coughs and hacking lungs—and tries to hush the laugh that spills free unbidden, unrestrained, deranged. “Yeah, the smell was pretty fucking thick. I had to clean everything before taking a seat—nearly burned my suit on—my, uh—”

Wide eyes stare—no, *bore*—into him.

“—way—out, *uh*…”

*Fuck…*

“Yes, I do imagine it’s gotten much worse recently.”

Fuck. Fuck, fuck, fuck—

Nothing gets by this fucking guy.

Shit.

He’s never been lucky.

He should’ve changed the topic.

He should’ve talked about something else.

He should’ve just kept his mouth shut. He should’ve just not fucking engaged. He should’ve just not—just not *gone* to Vox to begin with. He should’ve just—stayed at home, thought of something else, talked himself into something, anything, more productive than wasting hours away wiling on a couch while being complained to like a fucking underpaid two-bit counselor about the very private—very *personal*—life of the man he was trying to—was trying to, well—trying to manipulate.

There was a big difference between what Alastor knew about his life—major events, major offenses, major upsets and arguments and pivots—things, information, he also probably got through, well, no actual desire of his own (because, honestly, there was plenty of time, *plenty* *of time*, between Lilith going to heaven and the termination of their relationship that Alastor could’ve dug up info on the king of hell and made a move to either usurp the throne or just fucking laugh at him or some other equally bullshit dick move, and he *hadn’t*—which means he didn’t care to or *want* to) and what he was now privy to about Alastor’s life—his interests, his sexual deviances, his diet, his jokes, his youth, his standards, his romance, his—

It was different.

It was too much.

It didn’t feel equal.

It hadn’t felt equal when he had done it, when he had ruminated afterwards, when he compared the whispering visage of a city he had never once been to the peaking gripes of neon and shock. What Alastor felt comfortable to reveal versus what Vox had veritably vomited all over him—they were two vastly different things, and it had been with a looming sense of doom that he had clung to that conversation, gnawing on it in the space and time between tearing himself apart over *every other fucking thing* going on around them.

It had never felt right, to have gone to Vox, to have deliberately sought out information that Alastor had simply stumbled upon or had foisted on him in comparison.

Of course—that’s assuming he’s right.

And, honestly, he thinks he is because—the guy wouldn’t even *look* at the notebook.

One time, *one time*, Lucifer turned it to him—the first time—and Alastor had refused to look, had said it was for his eyes only. Did that mean that Alastor knew—or had he just taken serous efforts to *not* know? Or—had he been thinking of Lucifer’s comfort, of the contents of the journal, and wanted to give him the illusion of privacy, an acknowledgement of the sensitive details of his life contained in those pages?

Had he just been being nice?

Fuck, he hoped not.

He seriously fucking hope not because, honestly, obviously, really, truly—that just makes him feel worse.

“Yeah—ha, ha—well—ha—I, uh—I—I wouldn’t know—about—about all that—y’know? I just—only met the guy twice, y’know?” Is it hot? He feels hot. Fuck, he’s so hot. Is he sweating? That is—he feels like he’s sweating—he has to be sweating. The suit’s uncomfortable and his tie is too tight and those eyes won’t stop fucking staring at him and he swears they’re about to set him on fire out of sheer determination—

Alastor hums, eyes shuttering in a blink so fast, he feels like it was just a glitch in the person and not an actually meaningful gesture with any level of effect—more done for the personal benefit of getting a point across than anything fucking useful, like, oh, moisturizing eyes, recharging the death lasers inside of them, or trying to give his dear old king a fucking breather—before they’re fixed back on him in a burn that has his mouth dry and every attempt to swallow *hurting*.

“Do relax, Luci’. We must present a united front.”

He wouldn’t be shocked if he’s sweating straight out of his ass right now, insides all twisted up, guilt all-consuming.

He had—in truth, he had forgotten, almost, amidst everything else going on, what it was—when things changed—when his heart started to rattle a little more at crimson in his peripheral, when the game felt too big, when the world and all of its effects, the future and the past, felt like they were swallowing him up and there was nowhere left to turn that didn’t make him *ache*—he had forgotten what that day had begun with before being reminded of *home* in such clandestine detail that it left his breath caught in his chest in a hush and his tears caught in their ducts and his heart caught in a trap.

He had forgotten everything outside of that rooftop meeting, of softly spoken words, of smooth jazz and April showers and a soft smile that appeared in his dreams more and more frequently than he cared to admit, strawberry hair soothing, seducing, in its riveting falls—

He had forgotten, shamelessly, heartlessly—achingly—everything except that suddenly suffocating feeling that he was so, *so* fucked.

His heart’s rattling now.

“And are—” his voice is hushed, a quiet whisper, barely audible, but those big ears cocking towards him almost imperceptibly are cue enough that he’s heard, “—are we? A—a united front?”

Alastor’s twirling of his microphone stops, expression freezing, head staring straight ahead, and every part of him is rattling right now because—fuck—what the fuck—had he—

Had he screwed up?

Had he—

It wouldn’t be a shock if he had. It wouldn’t be a surprise.

That’s always what he does—that’s practically what he’s designed to do. He’s a fuck up—an imperfection, a waste of space, a hurtling asteroid into the scheme of heaven’s perfection that ended with him rocketing all the way down to earth and past—he’s *the* fuck up, the absolute *worst*—

Wait.

Pump the hate brakes, Luci’; get your head on straight.

It wasn’t fair, was it?

Alastor knew so much about his life, didn’t he? And, yeah, okay, maybe he shouldn’t have gotten into the middle of it by *deliberately seeking out* Alastor’s apparent ex, but, in fairness to him, *how the fuck was he supposed to know that?* He would’ve gladly kept it just to the people in the hotel if he fucking could’ve, but seeing as half of the hotel was under his contract, the other half being his daughter and her girlfriend—both of which knew next to nothing useful—and Angel—who was also apparently under Alastor’s contract, although who knows if he even was at that time, considering, except—

How long had the contract with Angel existed?

How long had—no one known?

How long had—had he had Angel keep it a secret?

*Had* he had Angel keep it a secret?

Had anyone else been aware?

No—no, he knows they hadn’t. Charlie hadn’t known—and if Charlie hadn’t known, neither had Vaggie, and if Vaggie hadn’t known—

Had Husk known?

Could he have even shared if he did?

Had he—had he done that—*deliberately?*

Just—on how many fucking levels was Alastor playing this game that he—had he—had he—

Holy fuck, he was covering his tracks—he was—he was fucking cock-blocking—blocking, just blocking—Lucifer easy access. He was spoon-feeding him information. He was—he was deliberately painting himself as—as a—a fucking, how the fuck does he say—a benefactor? A fucking saint? A well-meaning dude? A fucking proprietor? None of that feels right, and yet he can’t think of anything else to describe what the fuck was going on because, truthfully, he doesn’t think he fucking understands because *what*—in the seventh-dimensional chess *fuck*—

The air crackles, switches stations, static again, switch station, crackle, switch, static, switch, static, switch, static, switch, switch, switch—

*“Don’t lie to me.”*

—switch—

Lucifer, for the fucking win.

Rattle.

Now that—this—*this* tasted like a victory. *This* felt like power.

Vox hadn’t been expected—and, of course, why would it be? Vox had attacked the hotel. Valentino had harassed Charlie—and Angel, of course (not that he gave *that* much of a shit at the time because, hey, in fairness, he was really just here for his daughter, but *still*—he liked the guy; he didn’t want anyone going through that kind of shit, honest to goodness—it just—it fell lower on the priority list of people he was capable of giving a fuck about in his own overwhelmed sort of way). The V’s had made themselves public enemy number one to the hotel, outside of the wrath of heaven.

*Why* would anyone think Lucifer would seek them out *deliberately*?

Except—

“For the time being,” Alastor finally lets out, tone dark and liquid and heated honey, trailing on the long, white walk behind them, microphone vanished in a wave far sharper than his usual flare for suave grace, and Lucifer swallows.

It wasn’t a lie. He knows it wasn’t a lie. And yet, such an open-ended answer—he craves the meat behind it.

He can only guess at the truth, traitorous heart that shouldn’t still be struggling valiantly to keep putting its trust in the *wrong* fucking people swaying to one side over the other, playing at the burden of truth being too easy of an acquiescence for someone of Alastor’s swift-tongued esteem.

—Alastor had.

He had to have.

That’s—*that’s* why they interacted on the roof.

*That’s* why Alastor was there that night.

*That’s* why he was so forthcoming with information about himself when he’s so hesitant to even admit to being on the same side as them despite showing up to heaven on the grounds of *helping them*.

How long had he been collecting the pieces and yet was only *just* starting to see the bigger picture? How long had he been sitting on his own Alastor-themed notebook and yet reading Gaelic instead of the simple scribe on the page? He had a veritable chest worth of information, filled to the brim, on Alastor, and yet he had—he had been incapable of seeing anything until the—the *clarity*—of family drama vaguely resolved to—to process, to *see*—

What use was there in telling Lucifer of Lilith’s involvement, if not—

Oh.

*Oh*.

Fuck.

Family drama.

He hates heaven.

Fucking *revelations*.

“You—”

“Catching up?” Alastor drawls, and, *oh*, there’s the return of that suave, debonair attitude, lids lowered, lips quirked into a smirk more than a smile, chin tilted down, hair curving just above his chin, strawberry hair soothing, seducing, in its riveting falls—that graceful curve, that tempting curve, the illustrious curve—

His heart rattles.

“You sure you can’t read minds?”

“My dear—”

*Rattles*.

“—where would the fun be in that?”

Yeah.

Rattle, rattle, rattle—the stupid fucking organ; is it a toddler?—rattle, rattle, rattle.

*Fun*.

That’s exactly what he would call this.

Fucking revelations.

“Could be useful right about now,” he mutters as they slow to a stop outside of the towering doors of the meeting chambers that never seemed so big when he was flying around heaven, carefree and jovial, disillusioned and illuminated, compared to now.

Charlie looks at them, uncertain, big eyes wide—trying to gather her nerve, he’s sure, trying to not imagine another rejection when they’ve risked so much for an approval. They won’t turn back without a yes today—he knows this for a fact. Not after everything—not after all he fucking risked to make sure they got here with a fucking yes for his daughter that’s looking at him with those big eyes, pleading and hoping and begging for something he doesn’t know how to give, how to assure, because he’s never been one to fucking win against the might of heaven.

He’s sorry to say it, but—he’s a loser, through and through, Charlie.

There really isn’t much he can do.

“As if such comely boors have anything *rattling* around up there,” is mused dismissively, red eyes rolling as a gloved hand smooths over the front of his suit—sore thumb, sticking out, all that jazz—and his neck cracks, claws nimbly adjusting the shadow of his bangs.

*Rattle*.

“Stop your silly fretting—both of you. I do not lose easily.”

“But you *do* lose.”

A warning look.

Rattle, rattle, rattle, fucking *rattle*—

The doors open.

“Not today.”

---

“*So*, how sure are we that smile’s is gonna get this done,” Angel drawls into the quiet that sits around them after Lucifer, Charlie, and their ever-smiling heroic raspberry cosplaying doe—or damnation—of their little hotel have left through the portal, all happy, confident faces even though Charlie and Lucifer both looked like they were ready to puke all over themselves and everyone else in the nearby vicinity. So, really, only one happy, confident face, but, still—the other two did try their best, even if it definitely fell short.

Look, it’s not that he magically is suddenly lacking in faith—he’s seen these guys do the impossible, okay? It’s really not that he doesn’t believe in them, but—

He stares at the figure stuffed in a bag that they’ve been tasked with keeping an eye on, screaming up a storm, albeit muffled—obviously gagged, thrashing left, right, and fucking center.

—he’s having a few doubts right now.

“How the fuck should we know,” Husk mutters, and he’s nursing a bottle again today, prickly and standoffish, fur standing on end and feathers fluffed, like he doesn’t really want to be perceived at the moment and would prefer everyone to keep the fuck away—and Angel wonders briefly what the fuck happened that night—the other night—that he had seen only a little of before Niffty had ushered him away at Alastor’s behest and how much it has to do with his behavior today. He—

He’s not worried about anything—not really, not especially.

The fact that Husk was in one piece, no wounds, injuries, and no weird interactions with Alastor after that, was a clear indicator that nothing had happened—as if anything outside of murder *could’ve* happened with Alastor’s aversion to, well, the greater joys of life—like dick, cock, sex, ass, pussy, fucking around and finding out in the yummiest ways, and just all around anything that gave life flavor—and yet the pensive look that wouldn’t leave Husk’s face, no matter how many bottles he went through was a clear indicator that not everything was fully okay, and the start of *that* whole thing could be traced back to that night, right after Alastor went circus mode on the whole of hell.

He hears they’re still rebuilding, the poor bastards.

They’re lucky they’ve got he king of hell on their side to deal with any of their damages because it would be a bitch and a half to keep reconstructing. Had hell even managed to finish recovering from the aftermath of Alastor and Lucifer’s deal before Alastor went and fucked it all up again? Probably not.

He’s guessing from the fact that the TV has been sorely missing any sort of signal that the television demon, and potentially his cohorts, took yet another loss to Alastor’s mayhem.

It’s funny as fuck, honestly, and, really, he’s glad that the flamboyant, pompous little reverse-Rudolph was on their side.

Vaggie pokes the thrashing bag with the butt of her spear.

Another muffled scream is their answer.

Alastor had said to not, under any circumstances, open the bag.

“The plan isn’t all that tricky,” Niffty giggles, and Angel blinks.

He kind of wants to open the bag.

“Wait, wait, wait,” Vaggie coughs, looking alarmed. “You know the plan?”

Her one big eye blinks, her little fingers weaving branches together, making a—honestly, he’s got no fucking clue what that is. He thinks it might be a prop? For one of her shows? But it looks like it’ll end up being too big for a cockroach, so maybe something else? He really doesn’t fucking know. He sincerely hopes she hadn’t caught a rat or something. He’d actually just walk out of that, no offense.

“Of course!”

“Reindeer games just told y’?”

She bounces. “Of course!”

Angel’s eyes flick over to Husk, and the cat just takes another swig of his drink, looking ruffled and begrudging, like he wants to hide out in some damp cave like a hermit or some shit and pretend the world doesn’t exist.

Or a bar.

Probably a bar.

He keeps staring.

Husk’s eyes roll.

“He answers,” he burps, takes another swig, swallows, licks his teeth, “if you just ask.”

“Oh, so, y’ just *choose* to not know what the fuck is going on all the time, then?”

Husk’s lips twist, feathered brows furrowing, gazing down at the bottle like it holds all of the answers, and Angel wonders—wonders if maybe what he saw was Husk asking, was Husk poking, was Husk prying—or, well, the aftermath of it all. Had he asked? Had it ended in—whatever weird fucking power play he had stumbled upon with Alastor’s big ass antlers out in full force, eyes glowing, claws dripping—half a monster, half a regular demon, entirely in control? Was that Husk asking?

Did—did Niffty have to go through something similar?

Or—

“Yup,” Niffty giggles, and she’s wrapping cobwebs around the branches determinedly with her little needles, small feet kicking, and Angel thinks that they, seriously, need to find her some better hobbies—or, at least, better materials to use that, gag, *weren’t* from bugs. “He said all they have to do is get in there, chit-chat about heaven’s lies, show them the big bag, and then they’re done! Easy-peasy!”

Easy-peasy?

The bag isn’t thrashing anymore—instead quivering, more like—muffled sounds still coming from it but a lot more subdued, closer to panicked breaths and sobs than anything violent.

He’s finding his curiosity about the bag is both rising and falling in equal turns because, honestly, at this point, he could really take or leave the reveal of the contents because anything Alastor’s caught—especially as a bargaining chip—is probably not something he actually wants to get involved in. Besides, if the way Husk is barely letting him get close to the bag is anything to go by, like he’s actually worried about the consequences—maybe not from whoever’s in there, but from the overlord that put them there—he probably *really* doesn’t want to know. It would be, essentially, fucking around and finding out in the *least* yummy way, and he really didn’t want to press the wrong button on Alastor right now.

Guy seemed a little bit unhinged at the moment.

“An’, uh, we’re jus’ supposed to…”

“Wait for the portal to open, throw the bag in, go on with the rest of our day,” Husk grumbles, dragging himself to his feet with the mildest sneer of disgust on his features as he makes his way past the bag, bottle in his claws. Angel doesn’t doubt he’s still very focused on making sure nothing happens to the bag. He’d bet money on it. “I need a drink.”

Angel wants to point out the guy has already had *several*.

In a remarkable show of self-control, if he does say so himself, he doesn’t.

Good for him.

---

*Okay*, she could do this. She could—she could *totally* do this.

This was just—just another meeting—just another meeting with heaven in which they’re finally putting all of their cards on the table and if anything doesn’t go well, there’s really nothing else she can possibly do to get this idea green-lit except wage a war with heaven that they would *probably*, almost undoubtedly, lose, and the entirety of hell would pay the price for her incompetence and everyone would burn, and *Vaggie* would burn, and she—she really would like to avoid that, if, like, at all possible, but—*yeah*.

She can—she can totally do this.

“You’ve got this, Charlie,” Lucifer whispers, soft and encouraging, her biggest pillar of support right now as they enter the room, but the only strength in him seems to come from his voice because, when she looks over, he looks about as uncomfortable, unsure, and unenthused as she does, hiding behind the brim of his hat so that he doesn’t have to actually take in all of the angels surrounding them, up on their seats, staring down at them in burning superiority, and she grabs for his hand, desperate and feeble, because, honestly, it seems like they both need the support right now.

They’re not like Alastor, who, somehow, unbelievably, irrationally, looks positively delighted to be there, veritably skipping into the room to his seat, gaze sweeping over the hall without an ounce of fear that he definitely *should* feel, seeing as he’s the only one there that would be assured an incredibly permanent death if anyone felt he stepped even a toe out of line.

And, oh, that was another fucking thing.

She—she understood bringing Alastor. Hell, she wanted him to come. And, honestly, it seemed like her dad appreciated that he came, that he was here, that he could, like, lean on him for support—which, *aw*, so cute, they were so cute, she loves them so much, when the fuck is the wedding, she is *so* ready—but *also*—

Alastor is vulnerable here.

For all that he’s the cornerstone to their defense today, he’s also their weakest asset, and that’s—that’s kind of *terrifying*.

Compared to hell, where he’s a powerhouse, their steadiest pillar, the man they trust when literally anything goes wrong and they need some less than savory tactics deployed—he’s not much of anything here. He’s—he’s open, exposed, at a disadvantage.

She already had to deal with the idea of him almost dying once. She already had to confront that beast for a few days and found herself resoundingly unprepared for the absolute havoc it would wreak on her. She couldn’t—she wouldn’t be able to handle it if anything happened to him in that room. She just wouldn’t. And now—now she’s not sure her dad would either, and that—that’s also fucking terrifying.

And as if all of that wasn’t enough, her mother—her Lilith—could be here, could show up, could come to—to—to—she doesn’t know, but just—if she came, if she appeared, if she—

What would it do to her? What would it do to her *dad*? What would it do to her dad and *Alastor* and whatever fragile, pure, soft little thing they’ve somehow managed to begin to nurture between themselves despite all of the odds and their own obliviousness? What would it do to all of them? If she showed up—would any of them be okay?

She highly doubted it.

So—

Stakes were high.

No pressure, though! No fucking pressure at all, no sir, not at all—just—just normal—normal amounts of pressure that make her feel like her world is slowly imploding, she’s running out of breath, and like anything and everything that she’s ever done and worked towards will all be for naught.

*Yeah…* Shit, is this what her dad had felt like the last time he had been here? Like his entire being was riding on this one fucking discussion?

Yeah—a lot of things are starting to make more and more sense.

She’s not entirely sure *she* won’t crack under this pressure.

Lucifer moves to take his seat, giving her hand a quick squeeze before he’s settling himself next to Alastor, and she wrings her hands together nervously for a moment before steeling herself as much as she can, trying to shake off the nerves that hadn’t been so bad recently as they are today thanks to—well, reasons already covered.

She takes a deep breath.

Fuck, okay.

She can do this.

She can *do this*.

“Uh,” her voice cracks, and Alastor’s brow drops to solidly unimpressed in a split second, and her entire face burns and she wants to die—holy, fuck—

Maybe she can’t do this.

Fuck.

Okay, okay, she’s just gotta—gotta *focus*. She just has to think—think about how angry she was, how much time she spent looking over all of heaven’s indiscretions and complaints, trying desperately to find a way to appease them all. She just has to think about how much frustration has brewed within her over this whole thing over the course of *months*, since back when Adam gave her the countdown of six months, because she just wants to *help*, and they won’t let her. She just has to think of all of the grief and stress this whole ordeal has caused to both her and her friends and her father and *Vaggie*—how much death and pain and destruction.

She *can* do this.

She *will* do this.

She has to.

She clears her throat—tries again.

“It’s good to see you all, again!”

Okay, ouch, maybe a little loud, but getting there—it’s getting there—*she’s* getting there.

She looks behind herself to where her dad and Alastor sit, silent support as she gets this ball rolling, and Alastor’s smiling—as always—but his eyes, the warmth in them looks genuine, and her dad—her dad looks so proud, proud of *her*, proud of her strength. He looks like she’s achieved something wonderful just by saying a greeting, and it—it feels like love, like so much love, like all of the love, like he loves her so much that he’s just—he just can’t contain it.

Alastor’s gaze flicks between the two of them, and she thinks that it looks even fonder still, and something trembling inside of her loosens and her hands still their tremors, and her legs feel steadier, and she thinks that—okay—okay—they’ll be okay.

They’ll all be okay.

“Are we ready to start?”

There’s a rustle of an agreement, and she swallows thickly and draws on the strength she can practically feel behind her like a physical mass and meets the eyes of the angels with all of the steel in her heart and begins. Because they’re going to be okay. If she has any say, they’re going to be okay.

Yeah.

Well.

Turns out—she has no say.

They are not okay.

“None of what you’re saying is any kind of new,” one of the angels scoffs, some pompous little asshole at the top that, admittedly, isn’t *trying* to come off as a dick, but definitely does, and she didn’t think she’d miss Adam so much after that first meeting, but, honestly, it’s always refreshing to have dicks that intentionally act like dicks and don’t care about hurting your feelings or feigning niceties. Adam was a dick and meant it—and that had actually been semi-nice. At least, compared to this, it was.

“I’m *trying*—“

“You do not even understand how you managed to get this done the first time. And you want us to idly fret while you attempt to figure this out and *maybe* work out a suitable plan, but—how long will that take? You had half a year, and your only thing to show for it is a *mistake*.”

“Well, yes, but—”

“And in the time it takes you to figure out and replicate your mistake, how many more sinners will have joined your ranks? How much bigger will Hell’s population be? How much—”

“Will the threat to Heaven grow?”

And it’s Alastor piping up behind her, taking a cue she hadn’t even realized he’d been given to rise and walk over to her, placing a supportive hand on the jut of her elbow, looking around, once more, like nothing is wrong even though he’s just—in the middle of a room filled with all the beings that could kill him in one fell swoop like Adam almost did.

“Who are you?”

It’s Sera’s voice booming down, dignified and proper, and Alastor’s background static freezes for a moment at the voice—just a brief stutter that she thinks maybe only she picks up on, looking at him in worry—before he’s placing a hand delicately on his chest, every bit the showman, bowing his head in a gesture that is anything *but* submissive, all but leering.

“Alastor! Pleasure to be meeting you, quite a pleasure. See,” and he tosses his hair back, tilting his head, grin cold and calculating, and it feels like—it feels like he’s being protective of her, like he had been restraining himself this whole time, trying to let her fend for herself, but could no longer in the face of heaven’s obstinance, and she—she’s touched—she’s really touched, “you want a timeline.”

“Yes,” the angel from before stumbles hesitantly, sending a questioning look to Sera who—hasn’t looked away from Alastor? “That’s—it’s completely reasonable.”

The look in her eyes makes her skin crawl.

She locks eyes with Emily, and she looks equally as confused.

“Why, yes, of course,” Alastor crows, walking slowly across the space, hands locked behind his back. “Humans are so indulgent—the rate of sinners is many times the rate of any of yours. You want to know how long you’ll have to sit and wait for hell to amass its population, consolidate its strong, and rage war on the heavens for their unjust subjugation into an eternity of torture *just because*. Is that right?”

“Those people *knew* what was coming. That’s on them.”

“Are you saying hell wants to go to war?”

“So this *is* all some *ploy*.”

“Uh, Alastor,” she hisses, nerves spiking, but he waves her off with a look, corner of his smile twitching.

“My, my, so quick to jump to conclusions and yet so slow to answer my question.”

Sera’s look could freeze nitrogen, she’s sure, let alone Alastor’s poor, defenseless demon-ly self that is *so ill-equipped* to be fucking *fighting* with angels right now when he’s *grossly* *outnumbered*—oh, my god—

She sends Emily a panicked look.

“I—”

Oh, bless Emily. She loves her so much.

“—I do believe that is what the court is implying to believe, yes.”

“Wonderful,” Alastor sings, and his steps come to an abrupt halt, and her dad’s hand on the small of her back has her nearly jumping, whirling around to meet his gaze, and his expression is tight, his touch cautious, as he beckons her away from the center of the stage, like he knows something’s coming that she doesn’t, and it has anxiety stirring in her gut because what—what could possibly be about to happen that her dad looks so worried for her—for her safety? “In that case, let me ask you all—what do you think makes a person get into hell?”

The room falls into a frantic murmur, Alastor’s crackle blending in almost seamlessly as he waits patiently, stock still, not even seeming to notice that Charlie’s edged back to her father’s side where she’s been guided, focusing up front.

“I don’t like how she’s looking at him,” she whispers, practically mouths with how low she’s trying to keep her words, cautious gaze trained on Sera, whose stare hasn’t moved from where it’s boring into Alastor, even as she leans to the side to hear something Emily is murmuring to her, her lips tight. Her expression looks strained, surgical, like she’s barely containing quite a few thoughts and that none of them are good. It’s alarming, to say the least.

Lucifer’s eyes look up, and, for the briefest of moments, that cold gaze flicks to him, and his grip around her wrist tightens minutely, wings agitatedly flapping behind him, teeth gritting.

She doesn’t know if it’s a protective instinct that he feels, looking at Alastor up there with all of that bad energy focused on him, but the look on her father’s face is anything but subdued, expression steely, brows furrowed, lips pursed into a thin line, and his grip is tight around her as he edges her behind him, like he can shield her with just himself.

“Yeah—neither do I.”

“Well, then, shall I offer up some suggestions and you all can just tell me if you agree?” Alastor chimes, chippy and tinny and tiny—so, so tiny in a room that big with all of those people and just—just them down there, just the three of them, just the two of them for back up—so, so, tiny. “Shall we start with an easy one? Let’s say: murder. Can we all agree on that? Yes? Wonderful!”

Sera’s eyes narrow.

Charlie swallows.

“What about, say, sexual misconduct? I would think abuse is pretty frowned upon, no?”

The room chatters in agreement, the rustle of movement as people nod.

Alastor’s tone darkens, grin widening.

“What about *knowing* about it?”

The room quiets.

“Surely knowing someone is doing something awful, something reprehensible, something *hellish* to someone else and then—simply—doing nothing about it yourself is *equally* as bad as being the one doing the crime. Can we agree that would get people into hell?”

There’s a creaking silence, awkward and stilted, like the sudden grey area has made them unsteady.

“What about—*hm*—fighting in a war?”

She shivers.

“Wars cause mass destruction, mass death—soldiers kill each other, they kill civilians—they save lives and end them in unequal measure. Would *that* grant someone the illustrious one-way ticket to hell?”

Someone coughs.

She’s not sure who.

Alastor hums, seemingly disappointed.

“Shall I continue?”

“What is your point, *Alastor,*” Sera cuts in, expression thunderous, and, *oh, shit*. She looks so mad—like—deranged levels of mad. Nothing much has happened on her face, still the stoic picture of diplomacy, and yet, Charlie can feel it in air, can feel it in her bones. She knows, she *knows*, that Sera is mad beyond a rational sense, knows that she’s beyond *pissed*, because—

“Well—let’s propose a hypothesis, shall we?”

—because Alastor’s *winning*.

She clutches her dad’s hand.

In a few sentences, just a handful, a fraction of what she’s said over the long, long, long course this whole thing has taken, he’s already *winning*.

“Let’s say someone knew of a group of people being tortured, abused, raped, maimed—the whole nine yards. And then let’s say they did nothing about it. Let’s say that they sat there, with all of this knowledge, and decided to leave these people to suffer when they could have stopped it. Is this person guilty?”

She thinks her nails are digging into her dad’s hand.

*“Alastor.”*

Right now, she doesn’t have the ability to care.

“And, then, let’s say, someone sends a group of people off to fight a war, to slaughter hundreds, thousands, of a defenseless civilian population all over a territory dispute—because there’s more of them than they would like, so they believe they should be wiped out. Is this person guilty? Are their soldiers guilty?”

She hears her father swallow next to her, and when she manages to tear her eyes away from Alastor, from the stunned, horrified faces of the angels around them, to look at him, he looks—

Her breath catches.

He looks terrified.

And it’s not at the words being spoken—no, that much she knows. This is an image they’ve already had painted enough times themselves—it’s the reality they live. No—*no*—he looks terrified—for *Alastor*.

He stands in the center of the room, a blot of red amidst the white, proof of sullied goods, and her father looks terrified, horrified, like he’s watching a death happen in front of him when everything is for once going splendidly, and, *oh*—

The terror she felt walking in here—she’s terrified based on a hypothetical, on a potential. Her dad—he’s terrified—he’s terrified based on past experiences. The last lover he had that had stood by his side as he rallied against heaven—they had been cast out into hell, from heaven and Eden. That had been the greatest punishment at the time. That had been his cross to bear. But now—now there is no Eden. There is no Eden and there is no heaven, and his lover—almost lover, potential lover, hopeful lover—*lives* in Hell. There’s—

There’s no casting out if something goes wrong this time, is there?

There’s only Lucifer—her dad, her father, her greatest parent who deserves to find love again in this life and get his happily ever after, dammit, because she loves him and he deserves it—stuck having to watch as the man he loves gets murdered before his eyes, unable to do anything.

Sera rises, and Lucifer flinches, eyes flicking up to her, expression almost desperate, pleading, like he can stop anything bad from happening with a look because he has to pick one—he’s restricted in heaven; he has to pick *one*—

He’s terrified.

Her heart aches.

—and he’s chosen her.

“*What* is your point,” Sera demands again, and she cuts a regal figure above them, but Emily’s looking at her hands, looking at her profile, and Charlie can see, she can tell—Emily looks as terrified as her father does, like she’s also fearing something is going to happen that shouldn’t because Sera—Sera’s not acting like herself.

“My *point* is that, well, if we can all agree that these are serious transgressions that would guaranteed get someone into hell, then,” the shadows giggle, “aren’t you pots calling the kettle black?”

The room bursts into a roar of noise, angels and officials raging against the accusation, the audacity, the implication that this, in any way, shape, or form could possibly be true—that they, holy beings, could possibly fall to the level of devils when they occupy heaven for a *reason*. That was *impossible*—ludicrous—a sin to even *mention* it—

“How dare you make such claims here,” someone shouts—she can’t tell who—everyone’s standing and shouting, fighting to be heard over the others—clamoring to spit their own level of vitriol at Alastor who is—

He’s still smiling, still unmoving, still maintaining his position perfectly still, looking entirely uninterested as he wipes his monocle on his sleeve, peruses the state of his gloves, counts the pillars in the room, and all around just does not give a fuck about the potential danger he’s facing being up front and fucking center in the middle of all of this.

Her dad’s hand on her arm is *tight*.

It’s only at the peak of the communal tirade that Alastor seems to grow bored and clears his throat lightly, seeming to barely suppress rolling his eyes—and she knows he wants to roll them, she *knows*—speaking once, his voice at normal volume, at yet catching the attention of everyone in a heartbeat.

“And if I can prove it?”

“You can’t,” Sera breathes, and Alastor bares his teeth.

“Your Highness—if you will.”

Her father’s hands leave her in an instant, and she blinks, confused and bereft, as he schools his expression, steps forward, wings flaring wide, and then, in probably the greatest act of treason he can possibly commit upon heaven as someone of his station, tears a portal open.

---

“Guess it’s showtime.”

“In y’ go—oh—*fuck*—stop *fighting*, fuck—”

“Angel, are you okay?”

“‘m fine—fucker just got me in the eye—fuck.”

“Don’t kick it!”

“It hit my eye!”

“That doesn’t mean kick it! We don’t even know what *it* is!”

“Well, what else am I supposed t’ do? I ain’t just taking it.”

“Oh, for fuck’s sake.”

“Husk!”

“It’s not our problem now.”

“*Por Dios.”*

“Pour one out for me, handsome.”

“Pour it yourself.”
