“That’s—that’s not possible.”

The answering grin is sharp, predatory, a gleaming, flesh-rendering thing, glistening with the saliva of a beast that caught the sent of fresh meat. This was—this wasn’t the demon she owned—this wasn’t the demon she had forcibly quelled into submission—this wasn’t the demon whose chain rests in her hand.

No—no, she had no control here, as the man walked—stalked, slinked—closer, rounding around the kneeling figure of Adam—except it couldn’t be Adam, it wasn’t possible for it to be Adam, how could it be Adam—Adam was *dead*. Adam died. Adam was killed by angelic weapons. He was *dead*.

She had no control here over this—this—this *thing* masquerading as the demon she owned with a manipulated approximation of the first man and first angel barely managing to keep his back straight before them, all angelic grace, pomp and pride, bereft in this hollow little—puppet—that couldn’t even stand.

Adam was dead.

“Is it not? Well, then, my dear fellow, you heard her. There’s no chance you are who you claim to be, so I suppose I can just—”

The shadows expanded

Ah.

“Wait, wait, wait—”

“—take care of this little *impostor*.”

Yes.

“—*wait*—you son of a bitch, wait—I can—”

This was exactly according to plan, wasn’t it? This was exactly—exactly what he was supposed to do, make the hotel look like fools, make it impossible for heaven to consider endorsing them, make it clear that there was nothing to be redeemed.

“Well, you heard her. It’s not possible. You can’t possibly be who you claim you are, and I, to your deepest regrets, don’t take lightly to *liars*.”

She had nothing to worry about!

Her hands shook.

Nothing to worry about at all—

Masses of shadows grabbed at the kneeling form that was struggling valiantly to free itself, desperation an odor sitting thick on the air, filling her lungs—the only reason for her trembling figure, she’s sure—gasped protests falling on deaf pointed ears as the shadows leered and the microphone twirled through the air, darkness painfully stark against all the white.

—right?

Could a demon be so terrifying? A simple, rogue demon on her leash? Could he—could *it* be so terrifying?

Had she—had she made a grave error? An error—*her?*

“Wait, Alastor, wait—we didn’t come up here to *murder*—”

“Al—*stop*—think this through—”

“This death will not be on my hands. She said it can’t be so, so it shan’t. Any last words?”

“Alastor!”

“Al!”

Her breath caught, chair shuddering as it’s knocked into, noises of outrage clamoring around her, and yet she can hear nothing past the wailing in her own head, second guessing, wondering, screeching—is she right? Is she *right*? Was this—could this be—could it—

*“I can prove it!”*

The shadows pause.

Her breath shakes.

“Ah, well, isn’t this lovely? A most productive development—he can prove it!” The shadows disappear as if they had never been there, microphone twirling, long, clawed fingers, tapping at the hooded cover, lashes fanning low as a narrow chin tips up, and she swears she can see death cloaked in green in the spinning pupils of his eyes, like a record going around and around and around—no end in sight—if only the needle could stop picking it up—can the needle stop picking it up—can it please stop picking it up—“If it would please the court gathered around us today, do hear him out. The poor fellow did travel quite far.”

“You’re a fucking ass,” Adam—not Adam—yes, Adam? *Adam*?—spits, and his form, a far cry from his previous prestige, long robes traded for loose, baggy rags, horns curling out of a dark blue visage, golden eyes piercing—he looks so similar, so *similar*, so familiar, and yet so different, so alien, and it isn’t possible, it isn’t—it *isn’t*—and yet—

Alastor hums. “Fairly certain I am missing the key features for that, but I suppose we can’t all be well-read. Now,” and the butt of the microphone stand taps at a shoulder as the figure tries to climb to its feet, sending him stumbling forward, the click of Alastor’s heeled boots echoing throughout the space as he walks around to join his two alarmed partners—and why the *heck* is *Lucifer* looking uncertain? This is his—this is his whole *thing*, his *daughter*, his daughter’s *helper*—why, for goodness’ sake, does he look so horrified—“your proof.”

Feedback squeals.

“Dad?”

“I don’t know, hon.”

Emily’s gaze, out of the corner of her eyes, looks horrified, fingers white knuckling the edge of the table, standing up, wings flared wide. She was what had knocked into her chair, she’s realizing.

Adam’s—if that even is Adam—gaze is cutting, murderous, as he looks back at the Radio Demon who’s smile is so beatific, it was as if they were all ants to him, despite the fact that he was the one that didn’t belong, staring back with a delirious curiosity, like he was addicted to the mayhem he was wreaking. He spits, and there’s blood on the ground, before those golden eyes—so much like Adam’s eyes, so very much like his even if the pupils are different, bright and haunted, the same look he always had, the debauched depression—lock on her.

She freezes.

“Eight years ago—”

No.

“—I went down for an extermination.”

*No.*

“I was given orders specifically—”

Her breath rushes out of her.

“No.”

That predator’s grin gets wider.

“—to get—in contact with—*Lilith*.”

“That’s enough.”

“Sera?”

Adam’s—not Adam, can’t be Adam, wasn’t possible to be Adam—teeth grit, and his eyes are baleful, but the crackle of a radio seems to loosen his mouth, and the words just *keep coming*—

“I was to bring her back. She had a package—a payload—to bring to Sera. A *trade*. A *deal.*”

“This is madness.”

“It’s the *truth*!”

“Impossible,” she hisses, rising from her seat, eyes wild, smoothing the front of her dress, hoping the desperation she can feel escaping her in cold sweat wasn’t obvious. “What proof have you besides this silly little story? Accusing *me?* Adam would *never*—”

“I’m not fucking lying, you *bitch*—you think I want to die *again*?”

She feels like she’s been slapped.

“You have no evidence. How is this proof?”

“Oh, but, ladies and gentlemen, hold tight to your seats.”

“Stop.” Her hands are shaking, her wings flaring out, rage festering.

“We have some new and important information coming in for our hot ticket story—is Adam telling the truth? Let’s take a look over here—”

“Alastor,” she grits out, feeling like she’s burning up from the inside out.

“—front and center, heaven’s very own spy camera, is it not? Let’s see, let’s see—”

“Alastor!”

“—and, oh—”

*“Alastor!”*

Her hand lashes out, grasping at a chain and *yanking*—

*“Oh.”*

A laugh track echoes around her as a hush falls around the room, echoing and cackling, like she’s surrounded on all sides by him in the silence as he delights over her, and she freezes, staring ahead at those hooded eyes, that grin—that *smile*—a predator gazing at prey—

Someone gasps.

Her hand is empty.

“Oh, dear, well, isn’t this embarrassing?”

Adam’s shoulders are slumped, life spared, as he’s shoved backwards by a gloved hand that wipes itself on a suit jacket seconds after in disgust, colliding into Lucifer and Charlie where they stand off to the side, barely managing to catch his heaving frame and keep themselves upright at the same time, but she’s not paying attention to that, she’s not focused on that—she’s barely aware of them at all, if she’s honest. All she can see, all she can hear, is that mocking voice, those glowing eyes, that gleaming smile, the red—so much red, red, the red, the *red*, it’s all *red*—

Her hand is empty.

*“Looking for something?”*

“No,” she stumbles, fingers flexing, and yet nothing will manifest—the chain splintered and jagged and connecting to nothing, nothing, nothing—there’s nothing, there’s *nothing*—“No, that’s not possible—that’s not—that’s not *possible*—”

“That son of a bitch,” she hears hissed, and yet she can’t tell from who it’s from, because now—now all she can see are eyes, horrified eyes, staring up at her, from next to her, from beneath her, from all around her, staring at the chain that leads nowhere, staring at the trembling vision she cuts on her high peak, Emily edged away from her to better stare at her horrified shock, like she hadn’t thought she could get worse—and she doesn’t understand—

She’s too young to understand—Emily’s just—she’s just a girl. She doesn’t know what it means to make the hard decisions, what this ridiculous plan would mean, the horror it stirred up in her when Lilith had raised the alarm on what her daughter was planning on enacting and letting take place. Emily didn’t know what it meant to make the hard call, to see how poorly this would all work out, the can of worms such a decision would unleash—

To look at her with such horror—did none of them see? Did none of them understand? This couldn’t—this couldn’t be allowed to *happen*—it couldn’t—

And there’s Lilith—lying on a beach in heaven, Lute beside her, and it’s—it’s all so damning—it’s convicting, it’s—

“Well, then,” is drawled, slow, purposeful, crackling, dragging her heart to beat at a sharply erratic tempo, pulled to the whims of this demon that she had—she *had him*—she fucking *had him*—under control, under her hand, under her lead, under her feet—she *had him*—*how*—“it seems we’ve gotten our proof.”

Her fingers curl, nails cutting hard into the skin, gold marking a path through the crevices, teeth gritting.

Goddammit, she *had him*.

“That’s not *possible*!”

All she sees is those glowing eyes, that gleaming smile, the red, the red—so much red, red, the red, the *red*, it’s all *red*—

She lunges.

---

That *motherfucker*.

*“Sera!”*

“Fuck!”

Holy fuck—holy shit, holy fuck, oh my fuck, what the fuck—seriously, what the *fuck*—

“Oh, dear.”

Oh, dear? *Oh, dear?*

This fucking pompous, pun-pous, fucking shit stick fuck of a fuck face fucking prick piece of shit—*oh, dear*?

“Is she—is she dead?”

“Sera?”

Charlie is quick to rush forward, the bleeding heart that she is, and yet there’s not much she can do seeing as Sera’s body is still pinned standing, chest pierced through by the angelic sword sticking out of her chest, the hilt clutched tightly in the shaking hands of none other than Emily, her eyes wet, expression horrified, lips shaking around words she can’t seem to form, Sera’s eyes bereft of light.

Holy fuck.

Alastor’s smile isn’t even the mildest bit subdued, handkerchief dabbing at the splatter of gold across his face, walking away from where Emily stands before him, in between him at Sera and potential certain death, and the shadows slither around him, laughter tracking his steps, and Lucifer can feel his heart racing, his hands shaking, his breath stuttering. He—he did this—that motherfucker—*he did this*.

“Emily—Emily, you need to—you need to let go—”

“No—no, I—I killed—I killed my—I—oh, no—oh, no, I—I—”

The sound of retching fills the room, the acrid smell of bile mixing toxically to the comparatively refreshing scent of angelic blood, and the court is sitting in silence, shocked, and Adam is shaking next to him, violent tremors that have him looking over just to see the acute horror on the man’s face, like he’s realized everything that Lucifer has and is suddenly acutely aware of how *fortunate* he had gotten during his battle because—in anything except a head-on collision, perhaps, he would’ve been vastly outmanned, outmaneuvered, outplayed—all by one little fucking, *oh, deer.*

“She—she didn’t—she didn’t even have a weapon—Charlie, I—”

“Emily…”

“—I don’t—I don’t even know—where it came from—I—I just—I thought—”

“Em’…”

Alastor hums as his clicking steps come to a halt besides him, folding up his handkerchief as his eyes survey where Lilith’s image still sits exposed to everyone in the room, the switch of stations enveloping him as the air practically buzzes with static, and he—he can’t be that strong up here—he can’t possibly—

Not even Lucifer’s that strong up here—not anymore.

His domain is Hell now—his powers only at their max when he’s there, and yet—

Was this—

How long had he been planning—

Horror is a leaden weight in his gut—horror at what just transpired, horror at the realization that he fucking *helped* this happen, horror at the realization that he had been *lied to*—*again—the fucker—*

He had said—he *had said*—it was Lilith—it was *Lilith*—that’s what he—that’s what he—

*“Why do you—why do you care so much?”*

*“Call it an investment.”*

*He frowns, handkerchief crumpling in his hand.*

*“I have seen the extent of madness like hers.” The shadows breathe, and the figure it cuts* *against the ground behind Alastor is terrified, a curled up thing, the holes of its eyes* *scrunched into thin lines. “I had wanted to do this without having to reveal anything at all—I* *have no interest in becoming involved in familial drama. However, I am running out of time* *and my hands are tied. If she beckons with instructions, I am helpless to refuse.”*

If she beckons with instructions—if she beckons with—if she beckons—if she—if—

*She.*

Had he—

Had he ever said the name?

*Has* he—*had* he ever said—

Fuck.

Fuck, fuck, fuck—

Crimson eyes cut to him, glowing in the low light his hair casts, carving a shadow across his face, lashes fanning low, smile smoothing into something like a pleased smirk, cherry strands cradling his cheek, brushing at his chin, caressing the very top of his neck, kissing his forehead—

He had been played.

All this time—*all this time*—

He had been played.

He had been *fucking played*—

“I never lied.”

His teeth grit. “The fuck you mean—you withheld.”

“That’s not lying.”

“Yes, it is,” he scoffs, dizzy with the indignity of it all, feeling fully off-kilter, heart feeling like it’s being torn apart inside of him, ripping slowly, every letter he had ever transcribed inside becoming nothing but shreds to stoke the flames of his anger, this fire burning inside of him that had him ready to strangle the demon standing before him for daring to—for daring to—to—to use him, to string him along, to fucking make him—make him—make—fuck—*fuck*—

“You never asked.”

He—

“I—”

He—never asked?

He—he had asked. He had to have. He had—he had—he had to have—right?

He casts his mind back, face burning, eyes burning, cheeks burning, everything burning, gaze darting around even as the fire burns along his forehead, even as the fire stokes higher, even as his angelic visage begins to crack beneath the strength, the mass, the sheer weight of his own shortcomings as he realizes, as he thinks and searches and desperately seeks, he never—he never asked.

He—

He’s such a fucking idiot.

Why the hell had Vaggie thought to entrust something like this to him?

He’s—he’s nothing but a screw up, nothing but a good-for-nothing father that couldn’t do one thing right without fucking over someone else—lots of someone else’s—and yet she had trusted him and he had—he had gotten played. And not *just* gotten played—he *let* himself get played—he *let* himself get played all because he was stupid enough to fucking fall for—fall for—fall—fall, fall, fall—fall, *fall*—

How many times did one have to fall before they learned?

How many times did he need to fall before he learned to take a fucking hint?

How many times—

“Would you have told me,” he whispers, and he hates how fucking choked he sounds, how strangled, how *ruined*—all it had taken was one little thing, one little thing, one little fucking thing, and he had been diminished, leveled, fucking destroyed—how *stupid* could he possibly be—he’s pathetic, “if I had asked?”

All he had to do was get Alastor to turn to him for a favor, to be the one the deal gets struck with, to make Alastor *want* to choose him, to be *close to him*—

Alastor’s eyes gleam, gaze searching, smile parted, this small, little breath ghosting out past, brow twitching just the barest bit, a fracture in his mask—or maybe an intentional switch up—he doesn’t know, he can’t fucking know, can’t fucking tell anymore—what’s real, what’s a game, what’s the fucking punchline of this whole fucking joke of a set-up—he doesn’t know, he doesn’t know—he doesn’t know anything anymore.

*“Appearing in hell, I found it much the same, actually. Hell is—home.”*

This creation of his, this project turned punishment, this thing that has become the cage, forcing him to watch the consequences of his dreams for eternity—

*Alastor had called it a home.*

“If you had asked,” was the hush, breathed out words, nearly lost to the static rumbling about them.

—so why the fuck—*how* the fuck—did he end up being the one wanting to choose Alastor?

Whatever else Alastor may have said is lost amidst the static, smile firmly in place, subdued gaze evaluating, observing, dissecting—taking him apart with his eyes like he can see exactly what has Lucifer so bent out of shape about the whole thing when he hadn’t lied—and he fucking *hadn’t*, and that was honestly part of the fucking problem—words stayed by Charlie’s trembling voice behind them.

“Dad—dad, can you help? Can you—can you do anything? Dad? Dad!”

Glowing eyes peer at the fallen figure of the seraphim on the ground, a pool of gold around her crumpled figure. Her wings are pinned beneath her unnaturally, a clearer sign than anything else of her no longer occupying the body before them, and Lucifer’s stomach churns, thinks of the shadows that laughed and slithered and the chain he willed with all his might to snap and overwrite. There’s no remorse in those eyes, no regret in the curve of his shoulder or in the lines of his brows, there’s no sorrow or sympathy in the slope of his smile, in the way the tips of his ears twitch in agitation more than any sort of insane emotion like, oh, he doesn’t fucking know, *guilt*.

“I—“

*“If you had asked.”*

“Dad?”

*“If you had asked.”*

Fuck.

If he had only fucking asked.

“No—I—there’s nothing I can do, Char’. I—I can’t bring back the—*departed*.”

Alastor had read him like a book—

Emily’s sobs echo throughout the room, and he turns his own shaking gaze to the vision of Lilith sipping her tropical drink and relaxing on the beach, long hair flowing despite being pinned beneath her back.

—and he had let him.

“Well, then,” Alastor begins, and it’s really like he’s immune—like he doesn’t understand that someone, multiple someones, are *hurting* right now, they’re *mourning*—someone *died*, and it’s like he doesn’t even fucking *care*, the *psycho*, “shall we continue?”

“What—Alastor—” Charlie’s voice is a broken, pleading thing, every frustration Sera had ever caused her lost to the void of time as she holds a crying Emily, her own eyes wet with tears that roll down her cheeks, as indignant at the cruelty of life—or, well, afterlife—as always, even when it came to sanctimonious assholes like the unfortunate bunch of fucks he used to work with, and it pulls at his heart strings in a way that makes him physically twitch with the pain, his own eyes burning in answer—perhaps more for his own role to play in her tears than any actual disappointment at her death. “We can’t—she just *died*.”

“I understand that, dear, but she *was* trying to kill me, so forgive my callousness. Emotions have never been my strong suit. Now—”

“You said you knew how to play the game. You said you wouldn’t hurt anyone!”

He swears that the feedback somehow hurts more every time it goes off, sharp and high and equally as ear piercing, making everyone in the room flinch as Alastor’s neck snaps, rounding to look at Charlie’s defiant gaze, her tear-filled eyes, her shaking frame, the horns beginning to grow out. He looks—he looks—

Bored.

Alastor’s bored.

“Charlotte.”

“Charlie.”

“*Charlotte*,” Alastor sighs, twirling his microphone in his hand, looking at the prone figure on the floor of the woman he so clearly detested, hair swaying with the breeze. He looks malevolent, magnificent—malignant—

Tired.

“That blade never touched my hands.”

Lucifer’s stomach twists.

Charlie looks stricken.

Emily’s shoulders heave.

“Besides—this is part of the fun!”

“Say what now,” he hisses, marching forwards, shoving Adam back from where he’s apparently decided to take shelter behind him like Lucifer would make any attempt to stop him from being next on Alastor’s list if the fucker decided to try jack-shit, glare furious. He can imagine the heat of Charlie’s own fury behind him without her even having to say anything, making the healthy choice for both of them and making the executive decision to act on his own fury first before she gets the chance to and then ends up internalizing it while going on an around the world guilt trip for hurting Alastor’s feelings or making him fuck off or doing something else that the fucker absolutely *deserves*—“What part of this is fun, bitch?”

“Why,” and there is no fucking reason for that drawl to be so sultry—no fucking reason *at all*—he knows that piece of shit is doing it on purpose; he’s wising up to his games now, mark his words, “the part where she’s not dead.”

The room was silent.

“Say what now?”

Goddammit, Adam—shut the *fuck* up.

“How—how could she not bed dead? I—I killed—I kill—I—how? *How*?”

Alastor’s hum is low, mild, semi-sympathetic and yet entirely apathetic, and his steps are quiet as he walks over, brow furrowed in a convincing slope of guilt as he reaches out—and promptly shoves the body into the ground of heaven, tentacles making quick work of burying the corpse before any of them have the chance to react, ignoring their gaping as he turns to Lucifer, expression of faux-sadness gone, smile at full brightness, something electric in the skip in his step as he waves his hand.

“Your Highness, if you will.”

“The fuck am I doing?”

“Opening a portal, my dear. Another one. Right up there, through the ceiling, quick as you can, get on with it.”

Okay, yeah, he gathered that much, but *where*—

Oh.

*Oh*.

That *motherfucker*.

At this point he’s going to have to just hand his crown over to Alastor because while he’s out here, feeling like he’s playing the equivalent of checkers, this guy is playing chess even *past* the seventh-dimension with the amount of ducks in a row he’s got lined up to win on almost every conceivable level while Lucifer himself has been left scrambling to catch up just so that he can consider himself in the loop, let alone in the *know*. Alastor was—Alastor had seen a death coming, or, well, potentially planned a death to happen of an angel he deemed guilty, an angel that could potentially, like Adam, live and in person—and he’s opening a portal before the thought can even finish—come back as a denizen of Hell.

The portal is open for one, two, three, four, five, six—

Alastor peers at his pocket watch, smile never faltering.

—thirteen, fourteen, fifteen—

Emily sits crumpled, looking up at the portal with a prayer on her lips, sword still limp in her fingers, stained and gleaming, expression hollow.

—twenty-nine, thirty, thirty-one, thirty-two—

Lucifer presses his eyes shut and expands the radius of the portal, whispers a prayer to, well, himself—who the fuck else would he pray to?—that he hasn’t missed her because this would make this entire thing for naught and *definitely* paint a target on their backs—as if Alastor’s casual use of his powers, however limited, hadn’t done that already, along with Lucifer’s own, well, opening a gateway between heaven and hell, not once but *twice*.

“Ah, *there she is*.”

His eyes fly open.

Sera—what has become of Sera—hits the ground with a smack, every bit of grace robbed in the perpetual drop down to hell, figure crumpled and deformed as she lies in a heap, dress ballooned around her as a very poor excuse for a cushion, her hair—no longer as pale as before, but not quite dark either—lies in a tangled nest around her head as she groans, slowly beginning to shove herself up from her intimate and immediate acquaintance with the floor, figure trembling as she stares at the ground, seemingly uncomprehending.

And, honestly, he can’t really blame her.

It’s got to be disorienting to have died—to have felt herself die only moments before—and then to fall flat on her face in the exact room she had *just* died in, the hands facing her not her own, her body not her own, her clothes not her own. For a mortal, it was a given—they expected to either ascend or descend and everything was more about the thrill of somehow still being alive more than anything else. But for an angel? For a seraphim?

Lucifer had gotten the luxury, the joy, the unexpected gift of being cast out—not dying and falling—just *falling*, and that meant he got to keep his features, keep his face, keep his body, keep his *powers*. Sure, hell changed him a little—the fall changed him a little, but—it was nothing compared to the sinners that entered his domain day in and day out and ended up as entirely different people. And for a *seraphim*?

Oh, boy.

At least she got to keep her face—that was more than Adam got. Sort of. His demon form ended up looking more like a butchered form of his helmet than of his actual face, a reflection of where he placed his values in his life—afterlife? Something like that.

“Sera?” Emily croaks, weak and splintered and *raw*.

She’s so much like Charlie, it hurts.

Alastor settles along his side, still managing to not touch him despite being so close, and Lucifer looks over at him to see that he looks—almost at peace. Like—like he’s managed something grand, like he’s accomplished his one goal, like he’s—like he’s finally free.

Rattle, rattle, rattle.

Seriously, he’s got to get that no good, piece of crap organ under control.

Who the fuck thought angels should even have one?

Why not make them incapable of that kind of attachment?

That made a bit more sense, didn’t it?

They were supposed to be impartial observers, after all, weren’t they?

Hell, maybe he’d have fallen in line and not gone off the rails, or however it is that they’re framing his most damned creative burst, if he just hadn’t had the ability to *want*.

Lucifer turns his back on the heart-wrenching display behind him, wings flaring to shield them from view as much as he can manage, staring at the figure of Lilith in the air, seemingly entirely disinterested in whatever Lute was saying next to her, Lute reading from a pad of some sort. She looks—beautiful—as always—as beautiful as the day he first saw her in Eden, and yet—

He loved Lilith—loved? Loves—no—*loved*.

—his heart doesn’t stir for her anymore.

And, sure, there’s a ton of fucking reasons for that—like, for one, she kept his daughter from him, talked bad about him at every opportunity and nearly ruined any chance for him to have a relationship with Charlie again. For two, she fucking *abandoned* their *daughter* for heaven, for a vacation, for a fucking chair on a beach and peace and quiet and *paradise*, and *left their* *daughter*—left her alone and helpless and struggling without guidance, without friends, without family, without *anyone*. Fuck—if it hadn’t been for Vaggie, he seriously worries for what would’ve happened to his little girl while he was off being so lost in his own ass that he didn’t even stop to think about what could be happening with her, what Lilith could possibly be doing, what had become of his daughter while he was drowning in creation, deviation, and ducks.

And for fucking third, she fucking intentionally took part in helping heaven use an overlord—and not just *any* overlord, but one of the most *volatile* ones—to *prevent* Charlie from reaching any of her dreams—of course, assuming he’s finally catching up correctly, which, honestly, he thinks he’s got this one pretty much fully understood now. Good job, Lucifer. Truly, it was the bare minimum he needed to do, but he got there eventually. Go, him!

And for four—for four for four-forty-four—his traitorous little heart has maybe, potentially, fucking bat-shit-insane-ly set its little eyes on someone, *something*, else. On someone that seems to promise him a lot more pain and a lot more strife and a lot more headaches that Lilith ever fucking managed—and she seemed to have been trying her damnedest to be a pain in his ass—and he can think that now because *fuck her*, that’s why. He can fucking think it. She very nearly ruined *everything*.

First love or not, he can at least, maybe, sort of, kind of, with a way too large helping of guilt and reverence and never, ever, *ever* out loud because he’s *better* than her, better than filling their daughter’s head up with lies and personal slights and fucking harsh truths that she doesn’t need to know (but does now because of that book, that book, that fucking notebook—damn, he really needs to teach Alastor how to do gifts) because she is his greatest treasure and needs to know that he *loves* her with all of his being, no matter how she came to be, but—

He can think it, at least.

He can think: ‘I hate my ex-wife. She kind of seriously fucking sucks.’

He’s allowed to!

“I would hazard more than a mere ‘sucks’, but to each their own.”

For fuck’s sake!

He turns his gaze from Lilith, from the alarmed look on her face as Lute freezes and collapses, dagger sticking out of her back, from the skittering of a little demon as it plunges into the shadows once more with a few more enthusiastic little stabs, gritting his teeth against the ill feeling of relief that at least Lilith was okay because she was—she was a monster, more than some of his worst sinners, and yet—

“We are talking about this later,” he hisses, pretending he saw nothing, pretending he knows nothing, pretending he didn’t just watch Niffty commit murder in heaven and then disappear into the shadows, Alastor’s shadows, like he had fucking piggy-backed on Lucifer’s portal to smuggle his contractee in to finish a job he hadn’t even realized they’d started—choosing instead to look up at the fucking, *oh, dear*, of his stupidly useless heart and pretend that maybe a serious discussion would finally work at dissuading his dumbass from making the wrong fucking choice *again*.

As if that had ever worked—ha, ha, ha!

He’s fucking pathetic.

As if knowing something isn’t good for him has ever stopped him—case in point being the *entirety of hell*.

“Have we not already discussed this at length?”

*“Alastor.”*

His laugh is changing stations, shoulders bouncing and chin dipping, eyes squinting into thoughtfully amused little crescents, hand fixing his monocle almost self-consciously, almost shyly, almost softly, and fuck—

Rattle, rattle, rattle.

He’s so colossally fucked.

“Very well, dear. We shall discuss it even further.”

“Get a room,” Adam scoffs, smartly choosing to keep well clear of everything happening in front of them, and yet not so smart as to keep his mouth fucking shut like an intelligent person fucking *would’ve*.

Lucifer glares.

He gets it was useful to bring this guy along—he understood the fraction of the plan he had been let in on, but also—*seriously*. Did this guy *have* to be there?

He was one hundred percent okay with throwing him back down to hell now to serve his eternity in desolation until he, inevitably, discovered he couldn’t fucking hack it, caves, and crawls back to his daughter’s lovely little hotel in search for salvation because, see, the thing is, Adam is *weak*—and that’s coming from an equally mentally weak guy. Adam’s the kind that wanted to die after Eve fucked everything up for him, as he put it. He’s the guy that went into a depressive slump so fucking far, so far that even Lucifer can say *he* hasn’t gone there, that he starved himself to death, endlessly weeping instead and craving an end—

Only to be mercilessly brought back to life because *heaven wasn’t finished with him.*

Yeah, honestly, Lucifer has no idea why anyone would be chomping at the bit to get back here—why Lilith would ever want to get back here.

Another body thumps through the portal behind them.

He looks over at Lilith, at the streak of golden blood leading to the ocean, at her happily relaxing form again, no care in the whole fucking world.

Yeah—no one could ever fucking pay him to come back here.

*Hell is—home.*

He’s quite good where he is right now, thanks very fucking much.

---

“*Que mierda*?” Vaggie shouts as the entourage from heaven returns with their group now *doubled* in size, springing up to immediately envelope her girlfriend in her arms as Charlie beelines to her, body slumped it what can only be taken to be pure exhaustion, her eyes puffy and swollen, lips bitten raw—she could only have been crying, and his stomach swoops, drops, plummets, fearing the worst—

Lucifer’s face is drawn tight, strained and stiff, lips pursed into a thin line, fingers wrapped around the apple of his staff so tightly that it’s a wonder that it doesn’t shatter, shoving the three newest arrivals harshly in front of him, glare vicious. The three figures look equally as tense, devastated, even if two of them are huddled weirdly close together, like they’re seeking comfort from each other, and—was that fucking Adam? That fuck-face—then the one next to him was that one angel, that female one, that had mourned for him. But—she looked different…

Holy shit, had they fallen?

Had they—had they *died*?

Had they been reborn as sinners?

Holy shit.

Holy fuck.

So—it was a two way street. Sir Pentious wasn’t just an anomaly—he was the fucking *key* to toppling down *everything*.

Holy shit.

“I hope you’re pleased with yourself,” the tallest one of the newest additions to the departed group bites, and she’s looking at Alastor—Alastor who has stepped through the portal looking jovial as always, humming a jaunty little tune as his static buzzes like proverbial fucking flowers around him in a reflection of his unending good mood.

“Smashingly so,” he laughs, the radios chortling with him, and his shadow bounces behind him, grin wide.

“You just destroyed *millennia* of stability with this little *farce* of yours, you—you—”

“I did no such thing! You should blame yourself, seraphim—oh, pardon me, *ex*-seraphim. You’re the one that *stooped too low*.”

“I did not—”

“Alastor,” Charlie sighs, and she sounds so, so tired that it’s painful, unusual, fucking scary as all hell, shoulders slump in a defeated little posture, like the day hasn’t even caught up with her, and their—apparent victory? Because that—what the angel, that *seraphim*—and, seriously, Al? A fucking seraphim?—said could only mean one thing, and it was that heaven had decided to listen, finally. And that—that was good news. Wasn’t it? “Can we just—not right now? Just—look.” It looks like it takes physical effort to peel herself away from Vaggie and address the three loiterers. “If—if you want to work towards redemption, you’re welcome to stay here, but if not...”

“Then, you are more than welcome to leave,” Alastor takes over, adopting his role as the hotelier and default security with aplomb, smile stretching just that bit wider, and all three angels look entirely unnerved—if not equal parts murderous—and Adam wastes no time in grabbing both of them and making a beeline for the door, not even waiting for either of them to speak, running, rather smartly, for the fucking hills with everything he has in him, sparing not a singular glance back.

“Well, that was easy,” Angel snorts, and Alastor hums, shadow sagging in—what is that, disappointment? Seriously, Al? Fucking sadist.

“Wonderful little job, Niffty, dear,” he calls, shooing Charlie and Vaggie off with a gentle hand, ignoring the horrified look that Charlie sends him over her shoulder, swept away by her girlfriend that takes the hint faster than she does. Niffty bounces in delight, running around Alastor in circles before she, too, is ushered off to bed, Alastor’s eyes turning to them, wide and commanding, and Husk really doesn’t need to be asked twice to rise to his feet, begrudgingly, stumbling only for a step before Angel’s steadying him and dragging him off to the staircase.

The only ones that remain in the lobby are the king and his boss, and, quite frankly, he’s not entirely interested on whatever they have left to discuss.

He’ll find out the important shit tomorrow.

It’s only when he finds himself toppling face down into pink covers that he thinks that this was an even more catastrophically bad idea because he had—he had *really* wanted to avoid the conversations that he fucking knows has been brewing behind his eyes since the other night, since the morning after *that* night, and he doesn’t want to talk about it, least of all to Angel of all people, and yet—

*Fuck*.

“Never thought I’d see Charlie look that tired,” Angel muses, puttering around behind him, doing something or other—he won’t move his head from where he’s getting lungful after lungful of Angel’s perfume from the fabric, perfectly capable and willing to just smother himself here until a second death bothered to claim him, lost in the delightful aroma and the comfort of the mattress beneath him to bother to look and check. “She looked ready to kick ‘em all out, smiles included.”

His snorts, snuffles deeper, lets his eyes close—not that he’s allowed to avoid the world for much longer because Angel’s then clambering onto the bed, shoving him aside as Fat Nuggets taps his way to his little bed on the floor, knowing already that there was no room for him on the bed, really, with Husk’s bulky form there along with Angel’s mile long legs, and he’s forced to fold his wings up and slump onto his side, tail lashing behind him, to make room.

“Y’ gonna talk to me at all, or am I gonna have t’ go and ask tall, red, and creepy? ‘Cause, I’m a’being honest with you—none of them looked too chipper.”

He’s not. He’s not going to talk. He’s going to stubbornly keep his eyes shut and think about how much easier life was when he was looking for it at the end of a bottle and didn’t have a whole group of people that were, for whatever reason, entirely invested in him and his, ugh, well-being, and—*ugh*. Doesn’t that make him and Alastor a match in fucking hell? There had been—there had been a reason he had sold his soul to him all those years ago, a reason his quick hands had stayed, a reason he had sat himself across from that glowing smile and those enchanting eyes and let himself be seduced and fall.

“Seduced?”

*Fuck—everything.*

“So, that—that night—”

“No,” he grunts, nauseous with the mere suggestion, eyes snapping open so fast that the world frizzles in static and the room spins, stomach swooping. “Fuck, no. Don’t even—do not even, fuck—*no*.”

Angel snorts, hiding a laugh behind his hand that he quickly smooths through his hair, a self-assurance, a comfort, a not-so-subtle sign that he had been worried—for a split second, he had been genuinely worried that Husk and—and *Alastor*—

*Fuck*.

Shit.

Okay—here goes—honesty, just like the princess wants—

“I tried to kill him.”

—just like ripping off a band-aid.

“You did *what?”*

He sighs, shoves himself clumsily to his knees, slumps into a sitting position and debates the merits of having moved at all, mouth filling dangerously with saliva that he barely manages to swallow down, pressing his eyes shut.

Angel is staring at him, all eight eyes wide open—horrified, shocked—mildly turned on?—oh, fucking hell—

“Look, I—it’s not like—it—it wasn’t the first time, I just—”

“Not the first time?”

It comes out as a squeak, although he’s sure that wasn’t what Angel had meant it to sound like, his hands flying to his mouth, eyes stretching even wider, if possible, and yeah—yeah, he can see how that kind of a thing would be alarming to someone like Angel who had only just started standing up the Valentino a little and was still aggressively under his thumb until Alastor came along. But, as Angel—and everyone, for that matter, seeing as he didn’t exactly *flaunt it*—seemed to always forget, Husk wasn’t just some two-bit sinner who got swindled out of his soul.

He had been an overlord.

He had seen things—*done* things—that made taking a knife to another overlord’s throat seem entirely plausible, laughably so. But—

He supposed Alastor was different.

Threatening an overlord was no small feat.

Threatening the Radio Demon was—well, the only one stupid enough to do that was Vox, and, well, he had seen how well that had played out for the man. And that had been *with* backup.

In an empty room, with nothing more than them and a knife, against a couch, back to the shadows—yeah, insane didn’t really cover it.

“I got low—real low.”

Angel swallows.

He had gotten tired—gotten tired at searching for answers where there were none, got tired of seeing sinner’s faces in his dreams as the unthinkable happened, got tired of wondering when he would be next—if he would be next, if he *could* be next—he got low. And then he got drunk. And the rest was really history—until the other night.

“Went at him with one of Carmine’s knives—hoped I’d be set free if he was gone. He—”

Alastor had moved.

He had moved.

He had—

Had he?

He hadn’t moved the other night.

No, the other night, Alastor had stayed stock still, and Husk—Husk had been the one to miss. But that first night—that first night—had he—had he missed, then, too? But—no, he couldn’t have. He couldn’t have. He had—he had been so—so devastated that Alastor had moved, he had been so terrified, so sure that it was the end, he had—

But why—why had he been scared?

What had he been scared of?

Dying?

Ending up on his broadcast?

Getting his soul torn apart?

Hadn’t he felt like anything would be better than being under Alastor at that time?

Had the near certainty of death suddenly startled him into the fear he should’ve had from the start?

Or—

“—lived. And I—tried again, the other night. I should’ve known you saw.”

“I didn’t mean to—I’d just been—”

Husk shakes his head, waves away the excuses, because, really, it doesn’t matter. He hadn’t been in the best states of mind, the whole of hell had just been rattled by Alastor’s show of power, and he had—he had looked for answers in the demon instead of at the bottom of a bottle, and he had—

Some things just don’t change, no matter how sober he is.

“So, he dodged, and you—what? Are living in fear of when he’s gonna get you back or something? Because, listen, I gotta tell y’ that—”

*If fucking only.*

“I missed.”

“—he don’t seem like the type t’—*what*?”

“I missed.”

The words sound like a curse.

“You—you missed? *You* missed? You *missed?*”

Yeah, welcome to the fucking club. He’s equally as lost.

“You missed or y’ changed your mind?”

“I—”

He can see it, in his mind’s eye, clear as night, tempered coals reflecting the flames, fanned fast by lazy bats, barely wet lips a shallow curve of a facsimile, and the gentle claws that pick, pick, pick—*“You do not need me, Husker, dear.”* A purr, an admission, an olive branch offered that he can’t hope to understand because to understand that would be to understand himself, and he doesn’t understand himself because he doesn’t get how he missed, doesn’t get how he changed his mind, doesn’t get how he—

“—didn’t want to.”

*Shit*.

*“You do not need me, Husker, dear.”*

*Fuck.*

“You know how he got our souls—mine and Niffty’s?”

“I mean—yours was a bad hand, wasn’t it? Niff’—well, I don’t know what hers could be, but—somethin’ similar?”

His head shakes, laughter breathless as addled delirium leeches into his bloodstream, and he can only rub a hand over his eyes, up over his ears, down over his eyes, claws pricking everywhere they go, and think that he’s so *fucking stupid*—how could he have not—how had he lived in delusion for so long, how is he *still* living in it—

“We *chose* him,” he laughs, weak and fractured. “There was no coercion, no bargain, no offered trade or deal. No—we *chose* to sell our souls to him. I—I can’t speak for Niffty because her brain’s a ball full of cats, but I,” he looks at Angel now, at his calm face, at his rapt attention, listening and begging to understand, baring his own contrition and conflict for the spider to see, to view and dissect at will, “I stopped playing.”

Angel’s eyes blinked.

“I sat across from him, and I offered a game, and I—I gave in. I handed it to him. He didn’t even *ask*. He didn’t even invite me over. It was all me. And I—”

Angel’s breath hisses through his teeth before he’s clambering closer, wrapping his arms around him, both sets, and Husk sinks into them, into the fluff and warmth, relief and bitter loss staining his tongue, and draws in a deep breath that shakes more than he cares to admit, digs his claws into his palms and breathes.

If it was his choice—from the selling of his soul to the knife in the cushions to the sobs afterwards that Alastor held him through—if it was his choice, why the fuck did he—did he feel like his world was ending every fucking *time*?

“Hey, it’s okay. I get it. I sold my soul to Val’ willingly, too, y’know? I get it.”

“But, see, you actually hated him—as you should. But I don’t hate—”

He freezes.

*“You do not need me, Husker, dear.”*

He doesn’t—he doesn’t hate Alastor.

No—he hates *himself*.

And that—that would be Alastor’s exact angle, wouldn’t it?

*Fuck.*

“So, what I’m hearin’ is—and correct me if I’m wrong, but—your mopin’ about since then is because y’—you’re *scared* you ruined the whole BFF thing you got going on with Al?”

His—*what?*

“What?”

“Because I don’ think he’s holdin’ it against you or nothing. He seems to like y’ a lot.”

Again—“What?”

“What?” Angel draws back, blinking down at him with wide eyes. “Y’ think he was sending you with me everywhere because he cares about *me?* It’s not like Val *knew*—nothin’ was gonna happen to me. He sent you with me because he cares about *you*. Or, well, that’s what it seemed like to me, anyway, whiskers.”

Third time, for the people in the back: *“What*?”

Alastor—Alastor cared—cared about—about *him*?

Laughable—the idea was laughable.

No, there was no capacity to care in that demon, no chance for love or affection. He’s certain that the man wasn’t capable. He’s just—he was sending him because there was a chance Valentino would realize, if he was in a piss-poor mood, if he reached for a chain and found nothing, if—but—he hadn’t stuck around during Angel’s filming, had he? He had—just walked him there and back. He didn’t—

So—so, what? So Alastor—*likes* him? Values him or some bullshit?

His head spins.

Yeah, no—there’s an angle. He’s just not seeing it. Angel, obviously, isn’t seeing it either, and—

The *room* spins.

Yeah, he can figure this out later, anyway.

“I’m too sober for this shit.”

“*Ha*—more like not sober enough. C’mon,” Angel huffs, mirth in every word, and Husk lets himself be coaxed into lying down, wrapped in Angel’s arms, finding comfort in the soft rise and fall of his chest as they lay, in the long fingers scratching behind his ears, in the way he tangles their legs together and lets them just *be*, his eyes slipping shut as his ears tilt towards the tune of Angel’s heartbeat and finds himself slowly drifting off, thoughts of Alastor and like and *BFF*s flitting out of his mind to be addressed by a drunker version of himself.

Or a sober version.

Or a never version.

Yeah, he snorts, burrowing deeper, tail swaying behind himself stutteringly, never sounded good.

---

“Dad?”

Ah, shit.

“Can I talk to you?”

Could she? Could she talk to him?

Because, if he’s being entirely fucking honest right now, his mind is kind of, sort of, maybe melting out of his ears and his nose and every other orifice it can find an exit route through because he’s having to come to fucking terms with the fact that, to whatever almighty there is that still gives a shit about what happens to him—be it to his detriment or benefit, his ring is no longer on his finger and his heart is no longer in heaven and his head is—

Well, it’s still attached to his body, so he’s counting it as a fucking win because, boy, oh, boy, does he need one right now, holy fuck—

“*Yeah*,” he laughs, high and reedy and thin, his mouth moving faster than his liquid brain can process as his thumb clamps around the old-timey radio now taking up space on his fucking desk like it—like it *belongs there*—oh, fuck absolutely everything, holy shit, “of course, you can, hon’. Always—always have, uh, time for you—ha, ha!” He is so fucking screwed.

His daughter is a vision of caution as she edges into the room, closing the door behind her hesitantly, and he watches as she steps further into the space, her expression haggard, body hollow, like she’s been going through the motions and making no real effort, and his heart aches. How had it gotten this bad? How had she not bounced back after the *massive* win that they had in front of heaven? What was wrong?

He had seen Alastor since then in passing, and the man had been walking on air since, positively the happiest Lucifer had ever seen him since meeting the guy, going about his daily job and routine and responsibilities with marked cheerfulness, his radio show—which he had, after thinking long and hard for a grand total of twenty seconds before submitting to the permanent invasion to his privacy that would result from deliberately bringing in one of the demon’s radios into his personal quarters—jubilant and intoxicating, infatuating, leaving him unable to do little else than join in.

To see his daughter in such a comparatively dire straits—

It was a sobering cocktail he hadn’t wanted to drink.

“What’s wrong, honey,” he asks, all anxiety forgotten as he reaches out to her only for her to collapse into his arms in a wail, clutching at his suit and dragging them both to the ground, burying her head into his chest as her shoulders shake around heavy sobs, her heart too big for everything that had occurred up in heaven and since coming back—since before that, even.

“I—I am—an awful—*awful*—person—dad, I—”

“Woah, woah, hold on, hold on.”

Yeah, no, seriously, hold the fuck on. His daughter—this wonderful girl of his that thinks she can redeem the whole of hell if she just gives everyone a big enough hug, who sees the good in people he still only selectively allows himself to see the same in, who found a way to love a serial killer into loving her back enough to risk everything to *help her*—*his* daughter? An awful person? Who the fuck were they talking about?

It sure as fuck wasn’t Charlie.

Who had put such thoughts in her head?

He would kill them.

“Why—how—who—who said you were awful, Char’?”

*“I did,”* she gasps brokenly, fingers fisting the fabric of his suit jacket tight enough that he’s sure it’ll tear.

Well—no killing, then.

Definitely some loving, though.

“Why?”

She sniffs into his chest again instead, shoulders shaking around hiccuped cries, and he tucks her hair behind her ear and strokes her back and calls upon all of his wisdom of the ages—which really boils down to the limited time he got to spend with her as a kid and then an anxious night on a rooftop with someone who is better with his daughter than he is, arguably—which is to say, like, where the fuck is Alastor? Why hadn’t he jumped in as soon as he saw something wrong? He was always so on top of this stuff, wasn’t he? So, where the fuck was he?

Fuck.

Has something happened between them?

Oh, fuck—it would be just his luck that as soon as he starts to get attached, the one person that matters to him more than anyone else would decide she was done.

Fuck everything.

Okay, okay, Lucifer—calm down.

He literally had no information yet.

Everything was fine.

“I—I can’t—I can’t even *look* at Alastor right now, dad. I—I’m *horrible*, I’m awful, I’m so *mean*—”

“Okay, honey, I’m really going to start needing some more context real soon because—ha, uh—I genuinely have no clue what’s happening, and you’re making me a little—well, a lot, actually, ha, ha, a whole fucking lot—worried. Did—did Alastor *do* something?”

*“No!”* That has her scrambling out of his grip, waving her hands around in a show of how emphatic she is, how strongly she feels, how fucking certain she is that, no, Alastor was *not* the problem. But if Alastor wasn’t the problem—which, not as much of a shock as it should’ve been because when has she ever blamed the guy? But, also, still a shock because, honestly, he’s not fucking stupid; the guy was a walking red flag, as the kids say, and quite literally, too—then who was?

*Charlie*?

Impossible.

“I—*I* am the one doing something, and dad—”

Her eyes are welling with tears again, but she swallows against the burgeoning waterworks and clenches her fists, stubbornly pushing down the tears to try and talk, to communicate, to embody everything she tried to teach at the hotel.

“He—she came at him, and I—I didn’t do anything. I didn’t help him. All my—all my power and I didn’t, I couldn’t—he was alone, and he just—”

And, wow, okay, fuck—deja vu much?

*Shit*.

“This is my hotel. This is my idea. And yet, I—“ her lower lip wobbles and her eyes well. “He had to fight my battle for me, dad. I put him in that line of fire, and now—it’s not just his life, dad. Someone *died*, dad. Even—even if she came back, she—she *died*—she died trying to attack him, and I asked him to take on those risks—I—I *put him there* and asked him to pay the price, whatever it could be. Alone. And I should’ve guessed it, I should’ve seen—he probably knew—he didn’t even look shocked. He probably guessed exactly what would happen to him if he took the stage for me, and he did it, anyway, and now—“

His jaw is slack.

“—now I can’t even look at him, dad! I can’t look at him because every time I look at him, I see Emily’s crying face, and every time I look at him, I see Sera lying dead, and every time I look at him, I remember fucking *blaming* him for someone *attacking him* when he couldn’t have known, couldn’t have done anything, and I—I’m a horrible person, dad. He got up there to help me, and I just—“

Double shit.

*Fuck*.

“Oh, Charlie…”

“I treated him like a criminal, dad. I—in front of everyone, I—dad—I—“

“Okay, okay, easy there,” he drags her into his arms with a steady hold and cradles her close, rocks her back and forth and presses kisses into her hair and thinks that he should’ve done more to prepare her even though he really couldn’t have, he couldn’t have *known* that this is what would happen, that this is what would come from this whole fucking ordeal. He should’ve been honest with her from the start and told her that heaven didn’t *want* a status quo change and that shit was going to have to get ugly before they bothered to listen.

Unfortunately, heaven wasn’t run by a bunch of Emily.

And unless the girl suddenly discovered how to asexually reproduce, it wouldn’t be run by her for a long while still.

Damn.

If only.

His life would be infinitely easier, then.

Which would be nice because, hey, then his daughter wouldn’t be crying anymore, he wouldn’t have angels coming into his domain all the fucking time, and he could then just focus on bonding with his daughter and just—living his life? Or something like that, but *anyway*—

It meant—that heaven was never going to go down without a fight—it was never going to go down without it getting ugly, and he had—he had *known* that. That’s why he hadn’t wanted Charlie doing it because heaven was brutal—*brutal* and no one ever expected it, and, always, by the time anyone cottoned on, it was too late.

Genuinely, fuck everything.

He should’ve told her.

“Shush, it’s okay, hon—it’s okay—just breathe—*breathe*—”

The shadows ripple, and he thinks, very much, that the one dealing with this should, surprisingly enough, *not* be him.

Although, he’s kind of understanding *why* Alastor wasn’t already on top of this.

“Dad—how could—how could you face mom, how could you face me, after—after what she did? After what she did—to you—to make me—how—how can you—*look* at me, and not—not feel *sick*?”

Yeah, yet again, he really needs to teach Alastor how to give gifts.

Because, seriously, this gift sucked ass.

Although, it was truly the gift that kept on giving.

Honestly, though, a radio would’ve been a better fucking gift?

Well—except maybe not from the radio demon, seeing as, like, well, radios were kind of his thing and he could, like, eavesdrop through them or something? According to Husk, anyway. Which is like—that’s a weird gift to give to someone, wasn’t it. Like, hello, yes, surprise, here’s a little radio so that privacy is forever and always an illusion. Enjoy!

Yeah—shitty ass gift.

Maybe a bottle of wine would be better.

But *anyway*—

What were they talking about?

Oh!

*Oh*.

Fuck.

Well—

“Honey, what she did—I—okay, that notebook was really the worst way for you to find out because, like, honestly, I was never going to tell you—that was coming with me to the grave, but—in my defense, it’s not like I wrote it! Look, uh—”

Fuck. This was delicate.

“—at the time,” his eyes roll shiftily, focusing on the wavering shadows. He can’t tell if the guy is just nosy or concerned. “I was very much in love with your mother, I just—I wasn’t ready for a kid. I was barely keeping my head above the water, and Lilith was—she was thriving. She wanted to continue as if nothing happened, as if nothing had changed, and I just couldn’t *forget* that everything *had* changed.”

He pulls away, grasping at her hands firmly, and tries to launch his mind back to the past, to the thoughts he tries not to have anymore because, honestly, they’re depressing as fuck, and he’s kind of trying to *not* be a depressed piece of shit for, like, two seconds so that he can actually be there for his daughter, but, *okay*—figures he has to be depressed to help.

Life is fucking hilarious like that.

Refund, immediately, please and thanks.

“When she—when she did what she did, I was—I was asleep at the time. I didn’t know. I kind of—fuck, this is not what you talk to your daughter about, holy shit, look—I was kind of out of it? The days—everything was kind of one big blob, and, honestly, when I woke up in the middle, I was like: ha, *ha*, did I fall asleep during this? And just thought it was normal and moved on. I didn’t put it together until later, and then I was—I was so *excited* for you because you were my light, my little bundle of light, and all I wanted to do was meet you. It wasn’t your fault how you came to be, and I wasn’t going to—I wasn’t going to *blame* you for Lilith rap—rushing to—to conceive you.”

*Nice save, Luci’*.

Real fucking smooth.

He’s pretty sure the shadows are laughing at him.

“Look, honey, it’s not Alastor’s fault that he’s an arrogant asshole—well—actually—that is exactly his fault. He really should work on that. I’d also want to fucking kill him in a crowded room. He kind of brought that on himself.”

Charlie glares.

“*But*,” he scrambles to cover, “it’s not his fault that people actually follow through, or what happens to those people as a consequence of their actions. And you’re not a terrible person for being shaken up about it or for feeling that you need time. But it’s important to realize that, for once—shock of all shocks,” he laughs as her glare narrows more, winking, “he’s not at fault.” Bold-faced lie, but he’ll roll with it, anyway. It’s not *fully* a lie, if he, like, tilts his head and squints. It’s not like he made Sera or Emily *move*. He just—didn’t move himself. “And your dream—Charlie, you’re so close.”

“I—I am?”

“You are! And as loathe as I am to give him any sort of credit,” as if that covered it, “he did help you. A—a *lot*. And I know—I understand that there’s a bit of a, uh, conflict in you right now because you know Alastor can handle himself, but—if it had been Vaggie, or Angel, or Niffty—”

“That’s why I feel so bad, dad… It’s not like I want Alastor to *die*—it’s just—”

“I know, hon’.”

The issue wasn’t that she wanted harm to come to Alastor—it was that Alastor seemed entirely apathetic about harm coming to others.

Sure, he had been convinced that Sera would come back—Adam all but confirmed it, but *still*—his ‘me first’ mentality had—had rattled Charlie. That much was obvious. And yet—

“Well, when you think about it,” fucking hell, he can’t believe he’s doing this, “he already got really close to dying because of Adam, right?” Alastor owes him big-time, he thinks, as Charlie blinks her big wet eyes up at him. “He’s probably just fucking, I don’t know, traumatized? From nearly dying twice? And it’s not like he could do anything this time. We’re representing the hotel, after all. We kind of played our most rebellious card with bringing him to heaven to begin with and then, you know, opening a portal to hell without their say-so.”

“You—you’re right.”

“Exactly!”

Yeah, seriously, the fucking deer owes him. Big time.

How low has he fallen that he’s now defending the manipulative fucker?

*Fuck* everything.

“So, he was just—”

“Maintaining appearances,” Lucifer finishes with a bright smile, and Charlie looks at him—those cursed big wet eyes—and they’re welling up with tears again, and, oh, shit—

“I—I love you so much, dad!”

Oh—*oh*—aw—happy moments.

“I owe—Alastor a *huge* apology. I—I need to apologize to him immediately. I’m—I’m going to go and write him a million I’m sorry letters and then—and then maybe a song—and cook him dinner—and I can—oh, I don’t know what else to do, but I’ll—I’ll figure it out!”

She’s scrambling to her feet before he can even blink, strength returning to her so fast that he’s momentarily stunned, baffled, in awe of the amazing being that his daughter is, has become—this strong, wonderful young woman. He is helplessly beholden to her; his wondrous daughter.

“Thank you, dad. You always know what to say.”

*Ha*—funny! Good one!

But, “Thanks,” he smiles.

He’ll take it.

Hell’s greatest dad needs a win, too.

She did get him the mug, after all.

He watches her leave, smile soft and fond on his face, warming him from the inside out, and he watches the shadows bounce, shaking his head in unbearable fondness.

“Do not let her cook unsupervised—I’m afraid not even Niffty will be able to save that mess.”

He snorts.

“Guess you better find her before she finishes those letters.”

“You do realize I have other matters to attend to, no?”

“So you want *me* to supervise her?”

Static.

He grins.

“Distract her, then. I won’t be long.”

The shadows still.

Shaking his head, he drags himself to his feet, dusting off imagined dirt as he looks down at his damp and wrinkled suit jacket.

He tosses it onto the couch as he makes his way to the door, smile never retreating no matter how much he tries to force the corners into submission.

Huh—what can he say?

Take that depression, he guesses—part fucking three.

---

“A seraphim—I wonder, can you survive the night?”

“You piece of—”

“Ah—redemption will not come easily if you continue to sully yourself like this, your holiness.”

“*Screw*,” she should’ve just cursed—she should’ve just cursed, “your redemption. It’ll never happen. It’ll never pass. They know how dangerous you are now. They *know*. You can’t convince them otherwise.”

The smile—that persistent fucking smile—mischievous, damning, usurping—is a conqueror watching the unconquerable brought down to its knees. And the world—this jagged thing, green and blighting, dragging over the desecration of her being with a leer, salivating at a cut down beast—is wide and swallowing.

“I had been curious,” Alastor muses, rounding the bend, playing at the role of a young man bursting at the seams with a passion for the world—a role she had been intimately familiar with, had watched with morbid curiosity, had stared at with rapture—when, really, he just had a passion for innards, for dissection, for finding the marvel of the secrets that could best be learned in the absence of language, in the physical without mutual sweet release—the carnal without the lust, “if you would come out different since you were cut down unlike his Highness.”

“I am a seraphim—”

“You are a replica.”

“I—”

That haunting man, that haunting figure, that haunting devil incarnate—that consolidated beast of man, the peak-most bastardization of the land of the living, the root of all evil granted physical form—even to the two other demons in the room—the two she was supposed to speak with—

That unsettling demon.

That deviled demon.

That throned demon.

“Will die like a dog.”

“No.”

“Redemption is a fallacy, no? What need do you have of it, then,” and that silver-tongued demon, that devil-tongued deer, that cursed child, steps away with a gallant sweep, like he’s doing a favor, foot scoffing over her head spilled all over the floor, dark ink and jeering, mutilated words spilling in a hush, the shadow reaching towards him, beseeching and pleading, an oxymoron to her actual self.

“I—”

“Carmilla.”

“—wait—”

“A pleasure doing business, as always.”

“—no—wait—”

“You’ve outdone yourself.”

“—*wait*—please—Al—Alastor—”

“I am not one to leave a favor unpaid.”

“—*Alastor, please*—”

“Consider it paid, then.”

“*—please!”*

“Wonderful!”

That haunting man, that haunting figure, that fucking devil incarnate—that consolidated beast of man, the peak-most bastardization of the land of the living, the root of all evil blessed with physical form—

“What of your redemption?” She screams, throat scraped raw and breaking, blood rushing, desperation leaking from every pore until she feels just about soiled with it, legs clamping tight.

*That unsettling demon.*

“My redemption?”

*That deviled demon.*

The air crackles.

The shadows pulse.

Those glowing eyes gleam.

*That throned demon.*

“Who said I want something so boring?”

Her breath catches.

“Then—then *why*—”

That fucking smile.

He’s all sharp angles, all piercing edges, all cutting taunts and burning eyes and soldering heat—

“Sera of the seraphim,” he laughs, jovial and festered, rapture and Ragnarok, void and riddles, “do try to be more interesting.” His steps are swaying, never getting closer and yet feeling like he’s cornering her all the same—and had she really thought she had this man on a leash? Had she really—had she really thought she’d had him under control? Had she—had she been so stupid? “If you’re going to plead for your life, put your *soul* into it. But if it’s sympathy you want,” he trails off—and he’s all the way by the door now, Carmilla—that was her name, she thinks—in front of him, and yet she feels small and insignificant where her eyes are still focused on red, red, red, so much fucking red, all of the red—“I’m afraid I can’t offer you any. You’re—” his eyes squint, trail around, echoes of past words a haunting little screech just for her ears, mouth too dry to swallow and yet wet enough to *drown*, “—just business.”

“No...”

“Afternoon, Carmilla!”

“*No…”*

“Well, then—”

*She grasps the chain in her fingers, watches the torn figure at her feet, dragged to his knees,* *clutching at a hastily sewn back arm, ears pulled back, smile tremulous, brow pulled tight in* *consternation.*

*She wonders if she can get it to slip—*

*“Don’t worry, demon,” she croons, thinking firmly of having to make the hard call, thinks of* *a little project she can’t let bloom, thinks of all the precautions she needs to take place to* *protect heaven, to protect her home, to protect her people from the riff-raff that never learned* *how to kneel properly—aggressively not thinking firmly of how good of a figure the beaten* *man paints at her feet, of how powerful the blood spray had made her feel, of how quelling* *one of the most powerful up-and-comers in hell had been a euphoric rush incomparable to* *anything else in her entire existence, enough to dizzy and spellbind her where she stood, even* *if it had required making a deal with hell’s very own queen to make it happen, “it’s just* *business.”*

*—she vows she’ll try her best.*

“Let’s get started.”

“No.”

“Girls.

*“No!”*

She sees it out of the corner of her eyes—sees it as former gold paints crimson, sees it as everything dyes into garnet hues—all red, red, red, red, red, fucking red, that damnable red, that godforsaken red—

The shadows laugh and jeer and cackle and bounce to her rising screams and cries.

The laugh and jeer and cackle still when she finally falls silent, sightless eyes staring at the beaming darkness and the memory of glowing eyes.

Even then, their smile never ceases.

---

He wants to ask.

He wants to ask.

He wants to ask.

He wants to ask.

The alcohol burns his throat as it goes down, and his tongue stays still.

Alastor is a vision next to him, bathed in the warm lowlights of the hotel lobby, perched close to the fireplace, book open on his knee, fingers of one hand pressing the pages open, swirling a glass tumbler of whiskey in the other—he’s a visage.

Niffty passes him a tissue, and he blinks at it, flustering as she mimes dabbing her mouth, face burning as he presses it to his face even though it comes back dry, stained with his humiliation and nothing else. She giggles and scurries away, ignoring the way he glares after her in confusion, her hair bobbing all of the way, like she’s delighting in the embarrassment she’s caused without him even understanding *why*.

When his eyes turn back, Alastor’s amused gaze is trained on him, leaving him flustered and hot, eager to turn his face away and yet too stubborn to give in.

Like hell is he letting the demon embarrass him into submission—he’s the fucking *king,* dammit.

“Penny for your thoughts,” Alastor croons, taking his cue with remarkable levels of acquiescence, smiling softly as he marks his page and lets the book fall shut, drawing a sip from the glass Husk had so lovingly—read: reluctantly—poured ages ago that he entirely has been ignoring, using more as decoration than as an actual fucking drink, humming softly at the taste.

Lucifer holds out his hand, and Alastor gives him a baleful look.

“You said a penny.”

“I do not care *that* much.”

“Ah, but you *do* care.”

Those ears twitch and his expression is flat, smile notwithstanding.

Lucifer snorts.

A draw from his drink is quick to sober him, though, and he mulls the alcohol over in his mouth for a beat, lips pursing, brow furrowing.

He wants to ask.

He wants to ask.

He wants to ask.

He wants to ask.

Fuck it, he’s going to ask.

“Why did you have Niffty kill Lute?”

Out of everything that happened that day—that some odd week ago—that was the one thing he hadn’t been able to parse together yet.

He understood virtually everything else—from the notebook to the deals to the rooftop to the Lilith to the lies—he had placed the pieces together well-enough to understand the game and look back and appreciate the mastery, seeing as everything had played out okay for both of their sides (truly the losing the battle to win the war on Alastor’s part, which was, admittedly, impressive as fuck), but Lute—

“Oh,” Alastor laughed, drawing another sip, lounging back comfortably. “That was a simple deal with Adam.”

“You made a deal with Adam?”

“Why, yes, of course. How else could I have ensured his cooperation?”

Lucifer’s brow cocks.

“Getting him there was no issue, your Highness, but ensuring that what he said was *precisely* what needed to be said and didn’t get muddied up by previous loyalties—that was *essential*.”

“So, what? You made a deal that you’d give him his favorite angel as company if he played nice?”

Alastor’s smile is answer enough, bright and beatific, like he’s done something grand even though this was the smallest possible thing he had done out of, well, everything, and *yet*—

His chuckle is helplessly fond, and he’s suddenly very scared that he’s exposed far too much to the demon in front of him, suddenly very scared that he’s opened himself up to a world of hurt, and yet—

The fear is fleeting, flitting, floating up to the heavens to terrorize some angels instead because hell is—home.

And looking at Alastor’s amused little smile, bathed in warm lights, suit jacket set aside, legs crossed, whiskey in hand, strawberry red bathed soft in the embers and crackling sparks of fire, he thinks that, yeah—he is home.

The sinners are fucking disgusting, and if he ever has to talk to Vox again, he’s pretty sure he’ll end it all, and he’s not in any hurry to try a cup of Joe again because he’s quite content with having Joe and his coffee *separate*, but—

“You think they’ll come here anytime soon?”

Alastor hums, bright eyes turning towards the flames for a moment, head tilting, hair swaying. “Who’s to say?”

“Maybe they’ll bring Sera.”

It’s definitely home.

The flames dance in Alastor’s eyes.

If he was going to be tasked with watching the hotel with this demon for the rest of *eternity*, the least he could do is be *entertaining.*

*“Don’t lie to me.”*

He grins.

“Not likely.”

Well—he’s definitely not bored now.
